## I. Dymchurch-Under-the-Wall / 一　海墙下的迪姆彻奇

===Original===
To those who have small knowledge of Kent let me say that the fishing village of Dymchurch-under-the-wall lies on the south coast midway between two of the ancient Cinque ports, Romney and Hythe.

In the days of George III, with Trafalgar still unfought, our coast watchmen swept with keen glasses this broad bend of the Channel; watched not for smugglers (for there was little in Dymchurch to attract the smuggler, with its flat coastline open all the way from Dover cliffs around Dungeness to Beachy Head), but for the French men-o’-war.

In spite of being perilously open to the dangers of the French coast, Dymchurch was a happy little village in those days—aye, and prosperous, too, for the Squire, Sir Antony Cobtree, though in his younger days a wild and reckless adventurer, a gambler and a duellist, had, of late years, resolved himself into a pattern Kentish squire, generous to the village, and so vastly popular. Equally popular was Doctor Syn, the vicar of Dymchurch: a pious and broad-minded cleric, with as great a taste for good Virginia tobacco and a glass of something hot as for the penning of long sermons which sent everyone to sleep on Sundays. Still, it was clearly his duty to deliver these sermons, for, as I have said, he was a pious man, and although his congregation for the most part went to sleep, they were at great pains not to snore, because to offend the old Doctor would have been a lasting shame.

===Chinese===
给对肯特郡（Kent）所知不多的读者交代一句：这座名叫“海墙下的迪姆彻奇”（Dymchurch-under-the-Wall）的渔村，坐落在南部海岸，正处于两座古老的五港同盟城（the Cinque Ports）——罗姆尼（Romney）与海斯（Hythe）——之间。

时值乔治三世（George III）在位，特拉法尔加（Trafalgar）一役尚未打响，我们的海岸瞭望员正用锐利的望远镜扫视英吉利海峡（the Channel）这道宽阔的湾弧；他们盯防的并非走私贩子（迪姆彻奇这地方实在没什么能招来走私贩——此处海岸线平缓坦荡，从多佛（Dover）悬崖起，绕过邓杰内斯（Dungeness），直到比奇角（Beachy Head），一路敞开无遮），而是法国人的战舰。

尽管毫无遮拦地敞向来自法国海岸的危险，那时的迪姆彻奇却是个安乐的小村庄——是啊，还挺兴旺。因为乡绅安东尼·科布特爵士（Sir Antony Cobtree）虽在年轻时是个浪荡无忌的冒险家，又好赌又爱决斗，近年来却修心改性，成了肯特乡绅的楷模，对村中慷慨大方，因此极孚人望。同样深得人心的还有迪姆彻奇的教区牧师辛医生（Doctor Syn）：这是一位虔诚而开明的教士，既热衷于撰写令满堂会众在礼拜日昏昏欲睡的长篇布道文，也同样热衷于上好的弗吉尼亚烟丝和一杯热酒。话说回来，布道分明是他的本分——如前所述，他是位虔诚的人；会众纵然大半都听睡着了，却也都极力忍着不打鼾，因为冒犯了这位老医生，那可是终身洗刷不去的耻辱。

===Original===
The little church was old and homely, within easy cry of the sea; and it was pleasant on Sunday evenings, during the Doctor’s long extempore prayers, to hear the swish and the lapping and continual grinding of the waves upon the sand.

But church would come to an end at last, as most good things will, although there was a large proportion of the congregation—especially among the younger members—who considered that they could have even too much of a good thing.

The heavy drag of the long sermon and never-ending prayers was lifted, however, when the hymns began. There was something about the Dymchurch hymns that made them worth singing. True, there was no organ to lead them, but that didn’t matter, for Mr. Rash, the schoolmaster—a sallow, lantern-jawed young man with a leaning toward music—would play over the tune on a fiddle, when led by the Doctor’s sonorous voice, and seconded by the soul-splitting notes of Mipps, the sexton, the choir, recruited entirely from seamen whose voices had been cracked these many years at the tiller, would roll out some sturdy old tune like a giant paean, shaking the very church with its fury, and sounding more like a rum-backed capstan song than a respectable, God-fearing hymn. They felt it was worth while kneeling through those long, long prayers to have a go at the hymns. The Doctor never chose solemn ones, or, if he did, it made no odds, for just the same were they bellowed like a chanty, and it was with a long-drawn note of regret that the seafaring choir drawled out the final Amen.

===Chinese===
小教堂古旧而亲切，离海不过一声呼喊的距离；礼拜日傍晚，在医生冗长的即兴祈祷声里，听海浪刷过沙滩的嘶响、拍岸的轻吟，还有那不住研磨沙粒的绵绵涛声，实在是件乐事。

然而礼拜终究要散，好事大多如此——尽管会众里有相当一部分人，尤其是年轻一辈，认为好事未免也可以太多。

而一旦赞美诗开唱，长篇布道与没完没了的祷告那副沉重拖累便顿时卸下。迪姆彻奇的赞美诗自有一种滋味，让人觉得值得一唱。诚然，没有风琴领唱，但这不打紧——学堂先生拉什先生（Mr. Rash）是个面色蜡黄、两颊瘦削、偏爱音乐的年轻人，会先用提琴把调子拉上一遍；待医生洪亮的嗓音领起，教堂司事米普斯（Mipps）那裂魂碎魄的调门再一相帮，唱诗班——清一色是些在舵柄旁喊哑了嗓子的水手——便轰然推出一支雄浑的老调，宛如巨人的赞歌，震得整座教堂嗡嗡作响，那气势与其说是庄重虔信的圣诗，不如说更像是掺足了朗姆酒的绞盘号子。会众都觉得，为了放声高唱赞美诗这一口，跪熬那些长而又长的祷告也值了。医生从不挑肃穆的曲子；就算挑了也无所谓，反正到头来照样被吼成水手号子，末了，那支海上唱诗班总会拖长腔调，带着一声悠长的遗憾，吟出最后那句“阿门”。

===Original===
Very often when a hymn had gone with more spirit than usual the Doctor would thump on the desk of the three-decker, addressing the choir with a hearty, “Now, boys, that last verse once again,” and then, turning to the congregation, he would add: “Brethren, for the glory of God and for our own salvation we will sing the—er—the last two verses once again.” Whereat Mr. Rash would scrape anew upon the fiddle, Doctor Syn would pound out the rhythm with a flat banging on the pulpit side, and after him would thunder the sea salts from the choir with an enthusiasm that bade fair to frighten hell itself.

When they had hardly a note left in their bodies, the service would be rounded off by Doctor Syn, and the congregation would gather in little groups outside the church to bid him a good night. But Doctor Syn would take some minutes changing his black gown for his cloth surcoat; besides, there was the collection to be counted and entered into the book, and a few words of parochial business with the sexton, but at last it would be all finished and he would come forth to receive the homage of the parish. He would be accompanied by Sir Antony, who was warden as well as squire and a regular churchgoer, as the well-thumbed pages of a large prayerbook in the family pew could prove. Bestowing a cheery word here and a kindly nod there, the gentleman would pass on to the courthouse, where, after a hearty supper, Doctor Syn would metaphorically lay aside his robes of righteousness, and over a long pipe of his favourite tobacco and a smoking bowl of bishop, with many an anecdote of land and sea, make the jolly squire laugh till his sides ached, for he possessed to a lively extent that happy knack of spinning a good yarn, having travelled far and read much, albeit he was a parson.

And while the vicar entertained his patron at the courthouse, Mr. Mipps in a like manner held court behind the closed doors of the old Ship Inn. Here, with his broken clay pipe asmoke like a burning chimney and with eminent peril of singeing the tip of his nose, he would recount many a tale of wild horror and adventure, thoroughly encouraged by Mrs. Waggetts, the landlady, who had perceived the sexton’s presence to be good for trade; and thus it was that by working his imagination to good effect Doctor Syn’s parochial factotum was plied with many a free drink at the expense of the Ship. The little sexton was further encouraged into yarning because it gratified his vanity to see that they all believed in him. It was exhilarating to know that he really made their flesh creep. He felt a power and chuckled in his heart when he saw his audience swallowing his exaggerations for gospel as easily as he himself could swallow rum, for Mipps liked rum—he had served for a great part of his life as a ship’s carpenter and had got the taste for it—and so as a seasoned traveller they respected him, for what he hadn’t seen of horrors in the far-off lands—well, the whole village would have readily staked their wigs was not worth seeing.

===Chinese===
每当有赞美诗唱得比平日格外起劲，医生便会在三层讲坛（three-decker）的经台上砰砰一敲，冲唱诗班热络地招呼：“来，小伙子们，把最后一段再来一遍！”随后转向会众补上一句：“弟兄们，为了上帝的荣耀，也为了我们自身的救恩，我们把——呃——最后两段再唱一遍。”于是拉什先生重新拉响琴弓，辛医生在讲坛一侧用手掌平平地拍出节拍，跟着，唱诗班里的海上老盐们便雷鸣般轰然跟进，那份热情简直能把地狱本身都吓上一跳。

待到他们浑身连一个音符都不剩时，礼拜仪式便由辛医生收尾，会众则三三两两聚在教堂外向他道晚安。不过，辛医生得先花上几分钟把黑色法袍换成呢料外袍；再说，奉献金要清点入册，还有些教区事务须同司事交代几句；等这一切终于办完，他才走出来，接受全教区的致敬。陪在他身边的总有安东尼爵士——他既是乡绅，又是教堂执事，还是礼拜的常客，家族长椅上那本翻得起了毛边的大部头祈祷书便可为证。这位绅士这里赏一句爽快话，那里点一个和善的头，便径自往议事厅（courthouse）去了。在那里，一顿丰盛的晚饭过后，辛医生便算是把那身“义袍”脱了下来：他叼着长烟斗，装上心爱的烟丝，就着一钵冒着热气的“主教”酒（bishop，一种热香料红酒），把陆上海上的奇闻轶事一桩桩抖搂出来，直逗得那位快活乡绅笑得两肋生疼。别看他身为牧师，讲故事的妙手他可是练得炉火纯青——走的地方远，读的书又多。

而当教区牧师在议事厅款待恩主之时，米普斯先生也在老“船”酒馆（the Ship Inn）紧闭的门后自开一坛。在这里，他那只破陶土烟斗烧得像座着了火的烟囱，鼻尖险象环生，他便会讲起一桩桩惊悚骇人的冒险故事；酒馆老板娘瓦格茨太太（Mrs. Waggetts）对他百般撺掇，因为她早看出这位司事大驾光临能招财进宝。就这样，辛医生手下的这位教区杂务总管凭着想象力大显神通，“船”酒馆的公账便一杯接一杯地请他喝起了免费酒水。这位小个子司事讲故事的劲头之所以愈发高涨，还因为看见众人都对他的话深信不疑，虚荣心得到了极大的满足。一想到自己真把大伙儿吓得毛骨悚然，他便觉出说不出的痛快。他感到一股威风，心里暗自窃笑：瞧他的听众把他的夸大之词当成福音吞下，就跟他自己咽朗姆酒一样容易——因为米普斯爱朗姆酒；他大半辈子在船上当木匠，这口嗜好就是这么落下的。而众人也把他当个见多识广的老行旅敬着：远方异域的种种恐怖，但凡他没见过的——哼，全村人都会乐呵呵地押上自己的假发打赌：那些压根不值得一看。

## II. The Coming of the King's Frigate / 二　国王的护卫舰来了

===Original===
Now Doctor Syn was very fond of the sea, and he was never far away from it. Even in winter time he would walk upon the seawall with a formidable telescope under his arm, his hands thrust deep into the pockets of a long sea-coat, and his old black three-cornered parson's hat cocked well forward and pulled down over his eyes. And although the simple old fellow would be mentally working out his dry-as-dust sermons, he would be striding along at a most furious speed, presenting to those who did not know him an altogether alarming appearance, for in tune to his brisk step he would be humming the first verse of an old-time sea chanty that he had picked up from some ruffianly seadog of a parishioner; and as he strode along, with his weather eye ever on the lookout for big ships coming up the Channel, the rough words would roll from his gentle lips with the most perfect incongruity:

===Chinese===
话说辛医生（Doctor Syn）生性酷爱大海，从来不肯离海太远。即便在冬天，他也照旧在海堤上散步，腋下夹着一支威风凛凛的大望远镜，双手深深插在长身航海大衣的口袋里，头上那顶旧黑三角牧师帽向前压得很低，直遮到眼睛上。这位朴实的老人家心里尽管在推敲他那些枯燥得掉灰的布道文，脚下却迈着大步、走得飞快，叫不认识他的人瞧着不免心惊：因为他总合着轻快的步点，哼一支老式水手号子的头一节——那是从教区里哪个刁悍的老海狗教民嘴里学来的；他一路阔步前行，那只阅尽风浪的眼睛始终留神着驶上英吉利海峡（the Channel）的大船，粗野的字眼便从他温文的唇间滚滚而出，说不出的不协调：

===Original===
"Oh, here's to the feet that have walked the plank,
Yo ho! for the dead man's throttle,
And here's to the corpses floating round in the tank,
And the dead man's teeth in the bottle."

===Chinese===
“哦，这一杯，敬那走过跳板的脚，
哟嗬！为死人的咽喉干杯，
再敬那漂满水舱、团团打转的尸首，
和瓶子里死人的牙。”

===Original===
He was as proud of this song as if he had written it himself, and it was a continual source of amusement to the fishermen to hear him sing it, which he frequently did of an evening in the parlour of the old Ship Inn when he went there for a chat and a friendly pipe; for Doctor Syn was, as I have said, broad-minded, and held views that would certainly have been beyond those of the diocesan dignitaries. The very daring of a parson drinking with the men in a public inn had a good effect, he declared, upon the parish, for a good parson, as a good sailor, should know when he has had enough. The squire would back him up in this, and there they would both sit every evening laughing and talking with the fishermen, very often accompanying some crew down to the beach to help them launch their boat—and of course all this added to their popularity. But on Sunday nights they dined at the courthouse, leaving the field open for the redoubtable Mipps, who, as has been said, took full advantage of it.

===Chinese===
这支歌他引以为傲，倒像是出自他本人手笔；渔民们听他唱这支歌，也总能乐上一阵——傍晚时分，他常到老“船”酒馆（the Ship Inn）的店堂里去聊聊天、随和地共抽一斗烟，兴之所至便唱上一曲。因为辛医生，正如我方才所说，是个心胸开阔的人，他持的那些见解，恐怕远远超出了教区神职显贵们的境界。他宣称，牧师敢在酒馆里同汉子们对饮，单这份大胆就对教区大有好处；因为一个好的牧师，正如一个好的水手，应当知道几时算喝够了量。乡绅在这上头也总替他帮腔，于是每天傍晚，他们俩便坐在那里同渔民们有说有笑，还常常跟着哪一伙船员下到海滩，帮他们把小船推下水——这一切自然叫他们大得人心。可是每逢礼拜天晚上，他们要在议事厅（courthouse）用餐，便把整片场子让给了那位了不得的米普斯（Mipps）；前文说过，他把这个便宜占了个十足。

===Original===
Now the ungainly little sexton had a great admirer in the person of Mrs. Waggetts, the landlady of the Ship. Her husband had been dead for a number of years, and she was ever on the lookout for another. She perceived in the person of Mipps her true lord and master. He was enterprising, he had also money of his own, for he was parish undertaker as well as sexton, and ran from his small shop in the village every trade imaginable. You could buy anything, from a bottle of pickles to a marlinspike in that dirty little store, and get a horrible anecdote thrown in with your bargain from the ready lips of the old fellow, who would continue to hammer away at an unfinished coffin as he talked to you.

But the burning passion that smouldered in the breast of the Ship landlady was in no way shared by the little sexton.

===Chinese===
话说这位笨手笨脚的小个子司事，有一位热心的仰慕者——“船”酒馆的老板娘瓦格茨太太（Mrs. Waggetts）。她丈夫死了好些年，她时时刻刻都在物色下一任，并在米普斯身上认出了自己真正的主人与当家人。此人有闯劲，手里也自有几个钱，因为他不但是司事，还兼着教区的殡葬承办，村子里那间小铺子在他手上做着天底下想得出来的各行买卖。从一瓶腌黄瓜到一支穿索铁锥，你什么都能在那间又脏又小的铺子里买到；成交之余，这老家伙那张利嘴还会饶上一段骇人的奇闻，他一边同你说话，一边还在一口没完工的棺材上敲个不停。

然而“船”酒馆老板娘胸中那团闷烧的烈火，小个子司事却半点也无。

===Original===
"Missus Waggetts," he would say, "folk in the death trade should keep single; they gets their fair share of misery, Lord above knows, in these parts with the deaths so uncommon few."

"Well," Mrs. Waggetts would sigh, "I often wish as how it had been me that had been took instead of Waggetts. I fair envy him lying up there all so peaceful like, just a-rottin' slowly along in his coffin."

But the sexton would immediately fly into a rage with: "Waggetts' coffin rottin', did you say, Missus Waggetts? Not mine. I undertook Waggetts, I'd have you remember, and I don't undertake to rot. I loses money on my coffins, Missus Waggetts. I undertakes, ma'am, undertakes to provide a suitable affair wot'll keep out damp and water, and cheat worm, grub, slug, and slush."

===Chinese===
“瓦格茨太太，”他总这么说，“干死人这一行的，就该打单身。老天爷知道，在这块地方死人不常死，咱们受的晦气已经够份儿了。”

“唉，”瓦格茨太太会叹口气，“我常常巴不得当年被带走的不是瓦格茨，倒是我。我真眼馋他，安安静静躺在坡顶上，在棺材里慢慢地、慢慢地烂着。”

可司事一听立刻火冒三丈：“瓦格茨的棺材在烂，你是这么说的吗，瓦格茨太太？绝不是我的活儿。我提醒你记着，瓦格茨的丧事是我承办的，可我从来没应承过叫它烂。我做的棺材是赔本的，瓦格茨太太。我承办的，太太，我承办的是一件像样的物件——挡得住潮气、隔得开雨水，叫蛀虫、蛆虫、蛞蝓和烂泥统统占不到便宜。”

===Original===
"Nobody would deny, Mister Mipps," the landlady would answer in a conciliatory tone, "as to how you're a good undertaker. Anyone with half an eye could see as how you knocks 'em up solid."

But Mipps didn't encourage Mrs. Waggetts when she was pleased to flatter, so he would take himself off in high dudgeon to avoid her further attentions.

This actual conversation took place one November afternoon, and the sexton, after slamming the inn door to give vent to his irritation, hurried along the seawall toward his shop, comforting himself that he could sit snug inside a coffin and cheer himself up with hammering it.

===Chinese===
“谁也不能否认，米普斯先生，”老板娘会用缓和的口气答话，“你这殡葬承办人的手艺是没得挑的。谁只要长着半只眼，就看得出你敲打出来的东西瓷实。”

可米普斯并不领受瓦格茨太太高兴时的奉承，他往往怒冲冲地抬腿就走，躲开她进一步的殷勤。

上头这场对话，就发生在一个十一月的下午。司事摔上酒馆的门，出了出这口恶气，便沿着海堤急匆匆往自己的铺子赶，一路安慰自己：待会儿可以舒舒服服坐在一口棺材里头，敲敲打打，给自己解解闷。

===Original===
On the way he met Doctor Syn, who was standing silhouetted against the skyline with his telescope focused upon some large vessel that was standing in off Dungeness.

"Ah, Mr. Mipps," said the cleric, handing his telescope to the sexton, "tell me what you make of that?"

Mipps adjusted the lens and looked. "The Devil!" he ejaculated.

===Chinese===
半路上他遇见了辛医生。医生立在海堤上，身形衬着天际，望远镜正对着一艘大船——那船正打邓杰内斯（Dungeness）外海朝岸边驶来。

“啊，米普斯先生，”牧师一边把望远镜递给司事一边说，“给我瞧瞧，你看那是什么？”

米普斯调了调镜片，看了一眼。“见鬼！”他失声骂了出来。

===Original===
"I beg your pardon?" said the Doctor. "What did you say?" One of the King's preventer men had come out of his cottage and was approaching them.

"I don't make no head nor tale of it," replied the sexton. "Perhaps you do, sir?"

"Well, it looks to me," continued the parson, "it—looks—to—me—uncommonly like a King's frigate. Can't you make out her guns on the port side?"

===Chinese===
“你说什么？”医生问。“你刚说什么来着？”原来替国王当差的一名缉私员（preventer man）正从小屋里出来，朝两人走来。

“我是一点头绪也瞧不出来，”司事答道。“兴许您瞧得出来，先生？”

“嗯，照我看，”牧师接着说，“它——照——我——看——活脱脱是一艘国王的护卫舰。你瞧不见它左舷那排炮吗？”

===Original===
"Yes!" cried the sexton; "I'll be hanged if you're not right, sir; it's a damned King's ship as ever was."

"Mr. Mipps," corrected the parson, "again I must ask you to repeat your remark."

"I said, sir," replied the sexton, meekly handing back the glass, "that you're quite right: it's a King's ship, a nice King's ship!"

===Chinese===
“瞧出来啦！”司事喊道，“先生，您要是说得不对，我情愿上绞架；这是一艘十足的、该死的国王军舰。”

“米普斯先生，”牧师纠正道，“我还得再请您把方才那句话重说一遍。”

“我说的是，先生，”司事乖乖地把望远镜递还回去，“您说得一点儿不错：这是一艘国王军舰，一艘漂亮的国王军舰！”

===Original===
"And she's standing in, too," went on the parson. "I can make her out plainly now, and, good gracious! she's lowering a longboat!"

"Oh!" said Mr. Mipps, "I wonder wot that's for?"

"A revenue search," volunteered the preventer.

Mipps started. He hadn't seen the preventer.

===Chinese===
“而且它正往岸这边驶来，”牧师又说。“这下我看得清清楚楚了——哎呀，我的老天！它放下一条长艇了！”

“哦！”米普斯先生说，“不知这是要干什么？”

“缉私搜查，”那缉私员自告奋勇地搭了腔。

米普斯一激灵。他压根没瞧见这缉私员。

===Original===
"Hello!" he said, turning round; "didn't know you was there, Sir Francis Drake. What do you make of that there ship?"

"A King's frigate," replied the preventer man. "She's sending a boat's crew ashore."

"What for?" asked the sexton.

"I told you: a revenue search; to look for smugglers."

"Smugglers," laughed the parson, "here in Dymchurch?"

===Chinese===
“哟！”他转过身来说，“没瞧见您在场呢，弗朗西斯·德雷克爵士（Sir Francis Drake）。您给瞧瞧，那条船是什么名堂？”

“国王的护卫舰，”缉私员答道。“它正派一船水手上岸。”

“干什么？”司事问。

“方才说过了：缉私搜查；查走私贩子。”

“走私贩子，”牧师笑了，“在咱们迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）？”

===Original===
"Aye, sir, so they say. Smugglers here in Dymchurch."

"God bless my soul!" exclaimed the parson incredulously.

"How silly!" said the sexton.

"That remains to be seen, Mister," retorted the preventer.

"What do you say?" said the sexton.

"I say, Mister, it remains to be seen."

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛，先生，人家都这么说。走私贩子就在迪姆彻奇。”

“我的老天爷！”牧师难以置信地惊呼。

“多傻呀！”司事说。

“那有待分晓，先生，”缉私员回敬道。

“你说什么？”司事问。

“我说，先生，这事有待分晓。”

===Original===
"'Course it does!" went on the sexton. "Let's have another blink at her. Well," he said at length, closing the telescope with a snap, and returning it, "King's ship or no, they looks to me more like a set of mahogany pirates, and I'm a-goin' to lock up the church. King's men's one thing, but havin' the plate took's another, and one that I don't fancy, being held responsible; so good afternoon, sir"—touching his hat to the vicar—"and good afternoon to you, Christopher Columbus." And with this little pleasantry, which struck him as being the height of humour, the grotesque little man hopped off at high speed in the direction of the inn.

"Odd little man that, sir," said the preventer.

"Very odd little man," said the vicar.

===Chinese===
“那还用说！”司事接着说。“再让我瞭它一眼。”末了，他啪的一声合上望远镜，递还回去，说道：“管它是不是国王的军舰，我看那伙人倒更像一伙红木脸皮的海盗，我得去把教堂锁上。国王的人马是一码事，教堂的银器被抢是另一码事——还要我担这份干系，我可不愿意。那么，先生，下午好。”——他朝教区牧师碰了碰帽檐——“也祝您下午好，克里斯托弗·哥伦布（Christopher Columbus）。”说完这句在他自己看来妙到毫巅的俏皮话，这古怪的小个子便一蹦一跳，飞快地朝酒馆去了。

“那小个子可真怪，先生，”缉私员说。

“怪得很的小个子，”教区牧师说。

## III · The Coming of the King's Men / III · 国王的人马驾到

===Original===
Meantime the little sexton had arrived, breathless and panting, at the inn. Here he was accosted with a breezy, "Hello, Mr. Mipps, where's the Doctor?" The speaker was Denis Cobtree, the only son of the squire.

This young worthy of some eighteen summers was being prepared in the paths of learning by the vicar with a view to his entering the university; but Denis, like his father before him, cared very little for books, and the moment the Doctor's back was turned, off he would slip to talk to some weather-beaten seaman, or to attempt a flirtation with Imogene, the dark-haired girl who assisted the landlady at the inn.

"Just been talkin' to the vicar on the seawall," said Mipps, hurrying past into the parlour and calling loudly for Mrs. Waggetts.

"What do you want?" said that good lady, issuing from the kitchen with a teapot in her hand. Tea was the luxury she indulged in.

===Chinese===
与此同时，小个子教堂司事已经气喘吁吁地赶到了酒馆。一进门，就有人神清气爽地招呼他："喂，米普斯先生（Mr. Mipps），辛医生上哪儿去了？"说话的是丹尼斯·科布特（Denis Cobtree），乡绅的独子。

这位十八岁上下的少爷，眼下正由牧师领着在学问的路上修习，预备将来进大学；可是丹尼斯和他父亲当年一个样，对书本毫无兴致，只要辛医生一转身，他就溜出去找哪个饱经风浪的水手闲扯，或是去找伊莫金（Imogene）调调情——那是个黑发姑娘，在店里帮着老板娘做事。

"刚在海堤上跟牧师说话来着，"米普斯应了一声，匆匆走进堂屋，一边高声唤瓦格茨太太（Mrs. Waggetts）。

"你有什么事？"那位好太太手里端着一把茶壶，从厨房里出来问道。喝茶是她给自己留的一点儿奢侈。

===Original===
"A word," answered the sexton, pushing her back into the kitchen and shutting the door behind him.

"Whatever is it?" asked the landlady in some alarm.

"What's the time?" demanded the sexton.

"A quarter to four," replied Mrs. Waggetts, turning pale.

"Good!" said the sexton. "School will be closing in a minute or two, so send Imogene round there to ask Mr. Rash to step across lively as soon as he's locked up. But no"—he added thoughtfully—"I forgot: Rash is a bit struck on the girl and they'll linger on the way; send young Jerk, the potboy."

===Chinese===
"有句话说，"司事答道，把她推回厨房，随手带上了门。

"到底出了什么事？"老板娘有些慌了。

"现在几点了？"司事追问。

"差一刻四点。"瓦格茨太太答道，脸色刷地白了。

"好！"司事说。"学校再过一两分钟就放学了，让伊莫金跑一趟，请拉什先生（Mr. Rash）一锁上门就麻利地过来。不过——不行，"他又想了想，"我忘了：拉什对这丫头有点儿那个意思，两个人路上准得磨蹭；派跑堂的小杰尔克（Jerk）去吧。"

===Original===
"Jerk's at school hisself," said Mrs. Waggetts.

"Then you go," retorted the sexton.

"No," faltered the landlady. "It's all right, I'll send the girl; for she can't abide Rash, so I'll be bound she won't linger. And while she's gone I'll brew you a nice cup of tea."

"Throw your tea to the devil," snarled the sexton. "One 'ud think you was a diamond duchess the way you consumes good tea. When shall I knock into your skull that tea's a luxury—a drink wot's only meant for swells? Perhaps you don't know what a power of money tea costs!"

"Come, now," giggled the landlady, "not to us, Mister Mipps. Not the way we gets it."

===Chinese===
"杰尔克自己还在上学呢。"瓦格茨太太说。

"那就你去。"司事顶了回去。

"不，"老板娘支支吾吾，"没关系的，我打发丫头去；她见了拉什就腻味，我敢打包票，她绝不会在路上耽搁。趁她跑腿这工夫，我给你沏一杯好茶。"

"把你的茶见鬼去吧，"司事咬牙切齿地说。"瞧你喝好茶那副做派，人家还当你是位满头钻石的公爵夫人呢。要我讲多少遍才能敲进你这颗脑袋——茶是奢侈品，是专为阔佬们备下的玩意儿？你该不会不知道，如今茶叶贵得要花多大一笔钱吧！"

"哎哟瞧您说的，"老板娘嘻嘻一笑，"咱们可花不着那个钱，米普斯先生。照咱们弄到茶的那个路子嘛。"

===Original===
"I don't know what you means," snapped the wary sexton. "But I do wish as how you'd practise a-keepin' your mouth shut, for if you opens it much more that waggin' tongue of yours'll get us all the rope."

"Whatever is the matter?" whimpered the landlady.

"Will you do as I tell you?" shrieked the sexton.

"Oh, Lord!" cried Mrs. Waggetts, dropping the precious teapot in her agitation and running out of the back door toward the school. Mipps picked up the teapot and put it on the table; then lighting his short clay pipe he waited by the window.

In the bar sat Denis Cobtree, making little progress with a Latin book that was spread open on his knee. From the other side of the counter Imogene was watching him.

===Chinese===
"我不懂你在说什么胡话，"警觉的司事抢白道。"可我求你学一学把嘴闭严实点儿，你再这么张嘴下去，你那条摇来摆去的舌头早晚要给咱们大伙儿都套上绞索。"

"这到底是怎么了嘛？"老板娘带着哭腔说。

"你到底照不照我说的办？"司事尖着嗓子嚷道。

"哦，天哪！"瓦格茨太太叫了起来，慌乱中把那把宝贝茶壶都摔了，从后门直奔学校跑去。米普斯捡起茶壶放回桌上，点起他那支短陶土烟斗，守在窗边等着。

店堂里坐着丹尼斯·科布特，膝上摊着一本拉丁文书，看了半天也没挪几行。柜台那一头，伊莫金正望着他。

===Original===
She was a tall, slim, wild creature, this Imogene, dressed as a fisher, with a rough brown skirt and a black fish blouse, and she wore neither shoes nor stockings. Her hair was long and her eyes black. She had no parents living, for her father—none other than the notorious pirate Clegg—had been hanged at Rye—hanged publicly by the redcoats for murder; and the mother—well, no one knew exactly who the mother was, Clegg having lived a wild and roving life; but it was evident that she must have been a southerner, from the complexion and supple carriage of this girl—probably some island woman of the Southern Seas. Imogene was a great favourite with all the men on account of her good looks and her dauntless courage when on the boats at sea; for she loved the sea and was wonderful upon it—her dark eyes flashing, her hair blowing wild, and her young bosom heaving with the thrill of fighting the waves.

Imogene liked Denis because he was nice to her, and, besides, he made her laugh: he was so funny. His ways were so funny, his high manners were so funny, but his shyness attracted her most.

He was shy now because they were alone, and the boy knew that she was watching him; so he made a feint of studying his book of Latin, but Imogene could see that his mind was not on his reading.

===Chinese===
伊莫金这个姑娘，高挑、匀净、天性狂野，一身打渔人的装束：粗料的棕裙，黑色的渔家罩衫，光着脚，鞋袜一样也不穿。她头发长长的，眼睛乌黑。她父母都不在了：父亲不是别人，正是恶名昭著的海盗克莱格（Clegg），在拉伊（Rye）被红衫军以杀人罪公开绞死；至于母亲嘛——谁也说不准她母亲究竟是谁，克莱格一辈子放浪漂泊；不过从这姑娘的肤色和柔软的身段看，她母亲显然是个南方人——多半是南海某座岛屿上的女子。船上的男人们个个都宠着伊莫金，一来她模样标致，二来在海上行船时她胆气过人；她爱海，而且在海上身手了得——黑眼睛闪闪发亮，长发迎风狂舞，年轻的胸脯随着搏击浪涛的兴奋一起一伏。

伊莫金喜欢丹尼斯，因为他待她好，再说，他总能逗她笑：他实在太有趣了。他的一举一动有趣，他那副绅士做派也有趣，不过最招她动心的，还是他的腼腆。

他这会儿腼腆，是因为两人正独处，这小伙子知道她在望着自己，便装出用功读拉丁文的样子；可伊莫金看得出，他的心思根本不在书上。

===Original===
"You don't get on very fast, Mr. Denis," she said.

Denis looked up from the book and laughed. "No," he said, "not very, I'm afraid; I'm not very fond of books."

"What are you fond of?" said the girl, leaning across the bar on her bare elbows.

"Oh, what a chance to say 'you'!" thought the young man; but somehow the words wouldn't come, so he stammered instead: "Oh, nothing much. I like horses rather; yes, I like riding."

"Is that all?" said the girl.

"About all," said the boy.

===Chinese===
"丹尼斯先生，您念得不怎么快呀。"她说。

丹尼斯从书本上抬起头，笑了。"是啊，"他说，"恐怕是快不了；我不大喜欢书。"

"那您喜欢什么？"姑娘说，光着的胳膊肘往柜台上一撑。

"哦，这可是说'你'的好机会啊！"年轻人心里想；可话不知怎么就是出不了口，末了只结结巴巴地说："哦，没喜欢什么。我倒挺喜欢马；对，喜欢骑马。"

"就这些？"姑娘说。

"差不多就这些。"小伙子说。

===Original===
"Mr. Rash, the schoolmaster, tells me that he likes riding," went on the girl mischievously; "he also likes books; he reads very fast, much faster than you do."

"Not Latin books, I'll be bound," said young Denis, starting up scarlet with rage, for he hated the schoolmaster, in whom he saw a possible rival to the girl's affection. "And as for riding," he cried, "a pretty fellow that to talk of riding, when he doesn't know the difference 'tween a filly and a colt. He sits on an old white scrag-bones, jogs along the road at the rate of dyke water, and calls it riding. Put the fool on a horse and he'd be skull under the hoofs before he'd dug his heels in. The man's a coward, too. I've heard tales of the way he uses the birch only on the little boys. Why, if they'd any sense they'd all mutiny and kick him round the schoolhouse."

"You're very hard on the schoolmaster, Mr. Denis," said the girl.

"You don't like him, do you?" asked the boy seriously. "You can't!"

But the girl only laughed, for into the bar-parlour had come Mrs. Waggetts, accompanied by the gentleman under discussion, and followed by young Jerk, the potboy.

===Chinese===
"拉什先生，就是那位教书先生，告诉我他也喜欢骑马，"姑娘带着促狭接着说；"他还喜欢书呢；他看书快得很，比您快多啦。"

"我说那绝不是拉丁文书，"小丹尼斯腾地跳起来，气得满脸通红，因为他恨那个教书先生，把此人看成可能跟他争夺姑娘芳心的对头。"还骑马呢，"他嚷道，"他那种人也配谈骑马？他连母马驹和公马驹都分不清。他骑的是一匹老掉牙的白骨瘦马，在路上颠啊颠，慢得跟沟渠里的积水一个样，还好意思管那叫骑马。把那蠢货放到马背上，不等他把脚跟扣紧，脑袋就得先到蹄子底下。那家伙还是个胆小鬼。我听说过，他抡桦条专拣小个子的学生抽。哼，那帮孩子但凡有点儿血性，早该一哄而起造他的反，绕着校舍把他踢上一圈了。"

"您对教书先生也太苛刻了，丹尼斯先生。"姑娘说。

"你不喜欢他，对吧？"小伙子一本正经地问。"你肯定不喜欢！"

可姑娘只是笑，因为瓦格茨太太已经走进店堂来了，同行的那位正是方才议论的主角，后面还跟着跑堂的小杰尔克。

===Original===
Jerry Jerk, though only a lad of a dozen years, possessed two excellent qualifications: pluck and a head like a bullet. He had got through his schooling so far without a taste of the birch: not that he hadn't deserved it, but the truth was—Mr. Rash was afraid of him, for he once had rapped the little urchin very severely on the head with his knuckles, so hard, indeed, that the blood had flowed freely, but not from Master Jerk's head—oh, no: from the teacher's knuckles—upon which young Jerry had burst into a peal of laughter, stoutly declaring before the whole class that when he grew up he intended to be a hangman, just for the pleasure of pulling the bolt for the schoolmaster. So ever after Jerry went by the name of "Hangman Jerk," and whenever the pale, washy eye of the sandy-haired Mr. Rash fell on him, the schoolmaster pictured himself upon a ten-foot gallows with that fiend of a youngster adjusting the running noose around his scraggy neck.

This young ruffian, entering on the heels of the schoolmaster, and treading on them hard at every step, took over the bar from the fish girl, Mr. Rash remarking with a show of sarcasm that "he hoped he didn't interrupt a pleasant conversation, and that if he did he was more sorry than he could say to Mr. Denis Cobtree."

Denis replied that he shared the schoolmaster's sorrow himself with a full heart, but the door being open, he—the schoolmaster—could easily go out as quickly as he had come in. At this young Jerk let fly a loud guffaw and doubled himself up behind the bar, laughing. Upon this instant the conversation was abruptly interrupted by the head of Mr. Mipps appearing round the kitchen door, inquiring whether it was their intention to keep him waiting all night.

"Quite right, Mr. Mipps, quite right!" retorted the schoolmaster, and then turning to Imogene, he said: "Mr. Mipps wants us at once." Denis was about to make an angry retort, but Imogene passed him and went into the kitchen, followed by Mrs. Waggetts and the sandy-haired Rash, that gentleman carefully shutting the door behind him.

===Chinese===
杰里·杰尔克（Jerry Jerk）虽是个才十二岁的少年，却有两大过人的本钱：胆量，外加一颗炮弹似的圆脑袋。他上学至今还没尝过桦条的滋味：倒不是他不该挨打，而是实情如此——拉什先生怕他。因为有一回，先生用指关节狠磕这个小顽童的脑袋，磕得着实不轻，血一下子涌了出来——可不是从杰尔克少爷的脑袋里涌出来的，哦，不是：是从教书先生的指关节上涌出来的。小杰里见了放声大笑，还当着全班同学的面斩钉截铁地宣布，他长大要当刽子手，就为了享受亲手替教书先生拉下活门闩的那份乐子。打那以后，杰里便得了个"刽子手杰尔克"的名号；每当沙色头发的拉什先生那双淡而无光的眼睛落到他身上，教书先生眼前就会浮现出自己站在十英尺高的绞架上的模样，而那个小魔头正在他干瘦的脖颈上调试活套。

这小无赖紧跟着教书先生的脚后跟进门，一步一踩地踩着人家的鞋跟，从渔家姑娘手里接过了柜台。拉什先生故作挖苦地说，"但愿自己没搅了一桩愉快的谈话，若是搅了，他真不知该向丹尼斯·科布特先生怎么道自己的歉。"

丹尼斯回敬说，这份遗憾他自己也一样满心感同，不过门既然开着，他——教书先生——大可以照着进来时的速度原路出去。听了这话，小杰尔克放声狂笑，笑得在柜台后面直不起腰。就在这一刻，谈话被猝然打断：米普斯先生的脑袋从厨房门边探了出来，问他们是不是打算让他等上一整夜。

"说得对，米普斯先生，说得对！"教书先生应道，随即转向伊莫金："米普斯先生要我们马上过去。"丹尼斯正要恼怒地回敬，伊莫金却已从他身边走过进了厨房，瓦格茨太太和沙色头发的拉什跟在后面，那位先生还小心翼翼地随手带上了门。

===Original===
Denis now found himself alone with young Jerk. The would-be hangman was helping himself to a thimble of rum, and politely asked the squire's son to join him; but Denis refused with a curt: "No, I don't take spirits."

"No?" replied the lad of twelve years. "Oh, you should. When I feels regular out and out, and gets fits of the morbids, you know, the sort of time when you feels you may grow up to be the hanged man and not the hangman, I always takes to myself a thimble of neat rum. Rum's the drink for Britons, Mister Cobtree. Rum's wot's made all the best sailors and hangmen in the realm."

"If you go on drinking at this rate," replied Denis, "you'll never live to hang that schoolmaster."

"Oh," answered Jerry thoughtfully, "oh, Mister Denis, if I thought there was any truth in that, I'd give it up. Yes," he went on with great emphasis, as if he were contemplating a most heroic sacrifice, "yes, I'd give up even rum to hang that schoolmaster, and it's a hanging what'll get him, and not old Mipps, the coffin knocker."

Denis laughed at his notion and crossed to the kitchen door listening. "What can they be discussing in there so solemnly?" he said, more to himself than to his companion. But Jerry Jerk tossed off the pannikin of rum, clambered on the high stool behind the bar, and leaned across the counter, fixing Denis with a glance full of meaning.

===Chinese===
这下丹尼斯发现自己和小杰尔克单独待在一起了。这位未来的刽子手正给自己倒上一小盅朗姆酒，还客客气气地请乡绅少爷也来一份；丹尼斯一口回绝："不，我不喝烈酒。"

"不喝？"十二岁的少年应道。"哦，您该喝。等我实在没精打采、犯起了郁郁症的时候——您知道，就是那种觉得自己长大多半要当被绞的人、当不成刽子手的时候——我总要给自己来上一小盅纯朗姆。朗姆酒才是不列颠人的酒，科布特先生。全国顶好的水手和顶好的刽子手，全是朗姆酒泡出来的。"

"照你这么个喝法，"丹尼斯答道，"你活不到绞死那位教书先生的那一天。"

"哦，"杰里若有所思地答道，"哦，丹尼斯先生，我要是觉得这话有几分真，我就戒了它。对，"他加重语气接着说，仿佛正在斟酌一桩无比英勇的牺牲，"对，为了绞死那个教书先生，我连朗姆酒都可以戒掉。再说了，到头来送他上路的会是绞架，可不是老米普斯那个钉棺材的。"

丹尼斯笑他这番高论，走到厨房门口侧耳去听。"他们在里面这么郑重其事，到底在商量什么呀？"他说，与其说是对同伴讲，不如说是自言自语。杰里·杰尔克却把那一小罐朗姆酒一口干掉，爬上柜台后的高脚凳，隔着柜台探过身来，用满是深意的目光盯住丹尼斯。

===Original===
"Mister Cobtree," he whispered fearfully, "you are older than I am, but I feel somehow as if I can give you a point or two, because you've got sense. I'm a man of Kent, I am, and I'm going to be a hangman sooner or later, but above all I belongs to the Marsh and understands her, and them as understands the Marsh—well, the Marsh understands them, and this is what she says to them as understands her: 'Hide yourself like I do under the green, until you feels you're ready to be real mud.' I takes her advice, I do; I'm under the green, I am, but I can be patient, because I knows as how some day I'll be real dirt. You can't be real dirt all at once; so keep green till you can; and if I has to keep green for years and years, I'll get to mud one day, and that'll be the day to hang that Rash and cheat old Mipps of his body." And to encourage himself in this resolve Jerry took another thimbleful of rum.

"I'm afraid I don't follow you," said Denis.

"Don't try to," replied the youngster, "don't try to. You'll get it in time. The Marsh'll show you. She takes her own time, but she'll get you out of the green some day and ooze you up through the sluices, and then you'll be a man of Kent, and no mistaking you."

===Chinese===
"科布特先生，"他神神秘秘地悄声说，"您年纪比我大，可不知怎么，我觉得自己还能指点您一两句，因为您是个明白人。我是肯特（Kent）汉子，不折不扣，早晚要当上刽子手；不过顶要紧的是，我属于这片湿地，也懂得她的脾性，谁懂得湿地——湿地也就懂得谁，她对懂她的人是这样说的：'像我一样把自个儿藏在绿皮底下，等到你觉得自个儿准备好变成真正的烂泥了再说。'我就听她的话，真的；我如今就在绿皮底下待着，真的，可我耐得住性子，因为我知道，总有那么一天我会成为真正的泥土。谁也没法一下子就变成真泥土；所以先当着绿皮，能当多久当多久；就算我得绿上一年又一年，总有那么一天会烂到泥里头，那一天，就是要绞死拉什、叫老米普斯占不着他那具尸首的日子。"为了给自己这番决心打气，杰里又灌下一小盅朗姆酒。

"恐怕我没听懂你的意思。"丹尼斯说。

"别急着懂，"少年答道，"别急着懂。到时候你自然明白。湿地会指点你的。她有她自己的时辰，可总有一天，她会把你从绿皮里捞出来，让你顺着水闸往上渗涌，到那时你就是个地地道道的肯特汉子，谁也不会认错。"

===Original===
Denis, not able to make head or tail of this effusion, laughed again, which brought Jerry Jerk with a bound over the bar.

"See here, Mister Cobtree," he hissed, coming close to him; "I likes you; you're the only one in the village I does like. Oh, I'm not wanting anything from you; I'm just speaking the truth—you're the only one in the village I haven't hanged in my mind, and, what's more to the point, you won't blab if I tell you (but there, I know you won't), you're the only one in the village I *couldn't get hanged*!"

"What on earth do you mean?" said the squire's son.

"What I've said," replied the urchin, "just what I've said, and not another word do you get from me but this: listen! Do you hear that sexton in there a-mumbling? Well, what's he mumbling about? Ah, you don't know, and I don't know (leastways not exactly), but there's one who does. Come over here," and he led Denis to the back window and pointed out over Romney Marsh. "She knows, that there Marsh. She knows everything about this place, and every place upon her. Why, I'd give up everything I've got or shall get in this world, everything—except that schoolmaster's neck—to know all she knows, 'cos she knows everything, Mister Cobtree, everything, she does. In every house there's murmurings and mumblings a-going on, and in every dyke out there there's the same ones, the very same ones a-going. You can hear 'em yourself, Mister Cobtree, if you stands among 'em. You try. But, oh, Mister Denis"—and he grabbed his arm imploringly—"don't try to understand them dykes at night. She don't talk then, she don't; she does—she just does then. She does all wot the mumbles and murmurs have whispered to do; and it's *death* on the Marsh at night. I found that out," he added proudly. "Do you know how?"

===Chinese===
丹尼斯听得一头雾水，又哈哈笑起来，这一笑，杰里·杰尔克纵身一跃就越过了柜台。

"你听好，科布特先生，"他凑近身来，压着嗓子嘶声说，"我喜欢您；全村我就喜欢您一个。哦，我什么也不图您的；我这是在说实话——我在心里把全村人都绞了个遍，就您一个没绞过；更要紧的是——这话我告诉了您，您也不会出去乱讲（不过话又说回来，我知道您不会）——您是全村唯一一个我*没法绞死*的人！"

"你到底是个什么意思？"乡绅的儿子说。

"就是我说的，"小顽童答道，"一个字不多，一个字不少，再想从我嘴里掏话是没有的了，只有这一句：你听！你听见里头那位司事在咕咕哝哝吗？他咕哝的是什么呢？啊，你不知道，我也不知道（起码说不十分准），可有一个知道的。过来，"他领着丹尼斯走到后窗跟前，指着窗外的罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）。"她知道，那片湿地知道。这地方的一切她全知道，她身上的每一处地方她也全知道。嗐，为了知道她所知道的一切，这世上我已有的和将有的东西，我全都可以舍出去，全都舍——只除了那教书先生的脖子——因为她什么都知道，科布特先生，什么都知道，她真的知道。家家户户都有絮语和咕哝在进行，外面每一条沟渠里也都是这些声音，一模一样的声音在流着。您自己站到沟渠当中就能听见，科布特先生。您试试。可是，哦，丹尼斯先生"——他一把攥住他的胳膊，像在恳求——"夜里可千万别去琢磨那些沟渠。那时候她不说了，她不说了；她做——那时候她只管做。咕哝和絮语嘀咕过什么，她就照着做什么；夜里的湿地就是*死*。这道理是我自个儿发现的，"他不无得意地找补一句。"你知道我是怎么发现的吗？"

===Original===
"How?" queried Denis.

"By going out on her in the day, and gradually getting used to wot she says; that's how; and that's the only way."

Just then a most infernal noise arose from the front of the inn, and before Denis had disengaged himself from the earnest clutches of his guardian angel, and before the murmurs of Mr. Mipps had ceased in the kitchen, the bar was swarming with seamen—sailors—rough mahogany men with pigtails and brass rings, smelling of tar and, much to the admiration of Jerk, reeking of rum, filling the room with their jostling, spitting, and laughing, and their calls on the potboy to serve 'em with drink. But their entrance was so sudden, their appearance so startling, and their behaviour so alarming, that the young hangman was for the moment off his guard, for there he stood open-mouthed and awestruck, watching the giants help themselves freely from the great barrels. To Denis they had come with no less surprise. He had seen preventer men before; he had many friends among the fishermen, but these were real sailors, men-o'-war, who had lived through a hundred sea-fights, and seen hellfire on the high seas—real sailors, King's men. Yes, the King's men had come to Dymchurch.

===Chinese===
"怎么发现的？"丹尼斯问。

"白天到她身上去，慢慢听惯她说的话；就这么回事；也只有这一个法子。"

正在这时，酒馆前头腾起一阵地狱也似的喧嚣，不等丹尼斯从他那守护天使的热切掌握里挣脱出来，也不等米普斯先生在厨房里的咕哝停下来，店堂里已经涌满了海员——水兵——一个个脸膛红如红木的粗汉，梳着辫子，戴着铜环，浑身一股柏油味，还透着冲鼻的朗姆酒气，看得杰尔克好生佩服；他们挤挤撞撞、啐唾沫、哄笑，吆喝着叫小厮上酒，闹得满屋子乱哄哄。可他们来得太突然，模样太骇人，行止太惊心，小刽子手一时竟失了方寸，张着嘴呆立在那儿，一脸敬畏地望着这些巨汉从大酒桶里随意自斟自饮。在丹尼斯看来，他们来得同样出乎意外。缉私员他从前也见过；打渔人里他有的是朋友，可眼前这些是真正的水兵，是军舰上的人，身经百战，在远海见识过地狱之火——真正的水兵，国王的人马（King's men）。是啊，国王的人马开进迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）来了。

## IV. The Captain / IV　上校

===Original===
Just as suddenly as the pandemonium had begun, just so suddenly did it cease, for there strode through the door a short, thickset man, with a bull neck and a red face, a regular rough fighting dog, who, by his dress and the extraordinary effect he produced upon the men, Denis and Jerk at once knew to be an officer.

"Bo'sun," he said in a thick voice, addressing one of the sailors, "in a quarter of an hour pipe the men outside the inn, and we'll see to the billeting. Meantime make 'em pay for their drinks and no chalking. Hi, youngster!" he cried, catching hold of young Jerk by the ear, "if you're the potboy, tumble round behind and look after your job." Jerk, mentally consigning him to the gibbet, did as he was ordered, for his ear was hurting horribly.

"And now, sir," went on the officer, addressing himself to Denis, "is there anyone in this law-forsaken hole who can answer questions in King's English?"

===Chinese===
这场大乱来得突然，散得也同样突然：只见门口大步跨进一个矮墩墩的汉子，公牛似的粗脖颈，红彤彤一张脸，活脱脱一条好斗的猛犬。单看他那身行头，再看他一进门就把这群水手镇得服服帖帖的气势，丹尼斯（Denis）和杰尔克（Jerk）立刻断定，这是位军官。

“水手长（bo'sun），”他瓮声瓮气地朝一个水手吩咐，“一刻钟后吹哨，把弟兄们集合到酒馆外头，宿营摊派的事我来料理。这会儿先叫他们把酒钱付清，一个子儿也不许赊。喂，小家伙！”他一把揪住小杰尔克的耳朵喝道，“你要是这儿的跑堂小厮，就赶紧绕到柜台后头干你的活去。”杰尔克心里已经把他咒上了绞架，无奈耳朵疼得钻心，只好乖乖照办。

“那么，先生，”军官又转向丹尼斯，“这个连法律都撇下的烂窝子里，可有谁能用国王的标准英语答我的话？”

===Original===
"Certainly, sir," said Denis proudly, "if they are asked with a civil tongue. I am Denis Cobtree, and my father, the squire, is the best-known man on Romney Marsh."

"Then," ordered the seadog curtly, "fetch him along here quick!"

"Really, sir," retorted Denis hotly, "I do not think you would afford him sufficient interest. He has not the honour of your acquaintance, and I am bound to consider that he'll have no great zeal to make it!"

===Chinese===
“当然有，先生，”丹尼斯不无骄傲地说，“只要您客客气气地问。我是丹尼斯·科布特（Denis Cobtree），家父是本地乡绅，罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）上顶有名望的人物就是他。”

“那就快去把他给我叫来！”老海狗劈头下令。

“恕我直言，先生，”丹尼斯火辣辣地顶了回去，“我怕您还引不起他多大兴致。结识您这份荣幸他还没缘分享有，依我看，他老人家也就未必急着来攀这个交情！”

===Original===
"Nor I, neither," said Mr. Mipps, who had been looking round the kitchen door. "I don't like his looks."

The infuriated officer was inside the kitchen like a hurricane, glaring at the little sexton with all the condensed fury of the British navy.

"What's this?" he said, addressing himself again to Denis, who had followed him into the kitchen to be quit of the crowd of seamen. "I suppose you'll tell me that this shrivelled up little monkey is a squire's son too, eh?"

"A squire's son!" repeated the sexton. "Oh, well, if I is, I ain't come into my title yet."

===Chinese===
“我也不乐意，”米普斯（Mipps）先生打厨房门口探进头来，“瞧他那副尊容我就不来。”

那军官一股旋风似的卷进厨房，瞪着这个小个子教堂司事（sexton），满脸凝着整个英国海军憋出来的怒火。

“这是哪来的东西？”他又冲丹尼斯问——丹尼斯是为了躲开水手们的人堆才跟进厨房来的。“我看你多半还要告诉我，这只干瘪的小猴崽子也是乡绅的公子吧，嗯？”

“乡绅的公子！”司事应声答道，“哦，那敢情好，就算是吧，我这头衔可还没继承到手呢。”

===Original===
"Don't you play the fool with me, sir!" thundered the King's man.

"And don't you try the swagger with me, sir!" volleyed back the sexton.

"The swagger with you, sir?" exploded the officer.

"Right, sir," exclaimed the sexton, "that's what I said—the swagger, sir!"

But the other swallowed his wrath and announced coldly:

"I am Captain Collyer. Captain Howard Collyer, coast agent and commissioner; come ashore to lay a few of you by the heels, I've no doubt."

===Chinese===
“少跟我装疯卖傻，先生！”国王的人马雷吼一声。

“您也别跟我摆威风，先生！”司事一枪弹了回去。

“跟我摆威风，先生？”军官炸了锅。

“一点不错，先生，”司事扬声道，“我就是这么说的——威风，先生！”

可对方把火气硬生生咽了下去，冷冰冰地宣布：

“我是科利尔上校（Captain Collyer），霍华德·科利尔（Howard Collyer）上校，海岸特派缉私专员；这回上了岸，少不得要把你们这伙人里头几个送进大牢，这一点我心里有数。”

===Original===
"Oh! is that all?" replied the sexton with a sigh of relief. "Well, there, I have been mistook. I'd quite made up my mind that you was the Grand Turk or at least the Lord Rear Admiral of the Scilly Isles."

Ignoring the sexton's humour, the captain turned to Denis and said: "Who is this person?"

But Mipps was not so easily crushed, and he cried: "A man to be looked up to in these parts. I undertakes for the district. The only one wot does it for miles round. They all comes to me, rich and poor alike, I tells you; for they know that Mipps knocks 'em up solid."

"Knocks what up solid?" demanded the captain furiously.

===Chinese===
“哦！就这么点名堂？”司事如释重负，叹了口气，“嗐，瞧瞧，是我看走了眼。我起先还一口咬定，您是土耳其大帝，再不济也是锡利群岛（Scilly Isles）海军少将大人呢。”

上校全不理会司事的俏皮话，转向丹尼斯：“这位是什么人？”

可米普斯哪是那么容易给镇住的，他扬声道：“这一带人人敬着的人物。这一片儿的活儿都归我包，方圆几里地，干这个的就我一家。你瞧吧，穷也罢富也罢，家家都来找我，我告诉你；因为大伙儿都知道，米普斯干活敲得瓷实瓷实。”

“敲什么敲得瓷实？”上校怒气冲冲地问。

===Original===
"Coffins up solid," replied the sexton promptly.

"Coffins!" repeated the captain. "Oh, you're a coffin-maker, are you? Yes, you look it. Thought you might be the landlord of this run-amuck old inn here. That's the man I want. Where can I find him?"

Mipps pointed out of the window toward the church.

"Up in the churchyard," he said.

"What's he doing in the churchyard?" demanded the captain.

===Chinese===
“棺材敲得瓷实。”司事对答如流。

“棺材！”上校跟着念了一遍，“哦，闹了半天你是个棺材匠。嗯，瞧着也像。我还当你是这家撒野撒惯了的老酒馆的店家呢。我要找的正是他。上哪儿找他去？”

米普斯朝窗外教堂那边一指。

“在教堂墓园里，”他说。

“他上教堂墓园里做什么去了？”上校追问。

===Original===
Mipps came right up to him and whispered in this ear the significant word, "Worrumps!"

"What?" shouted the captain, who didn't understand.

"Sh!" said Mipps, pointing across at Mrs. Waggetts, who had begun to weep into her apron. "He's a-keepin' 'em out."

"Keeping who out?" snapped the captain.

"I keep telling you," replied the sexton. "Worrumps!"

"Danged if I can make out what you say." The captain's patience was well-nigh exhausted. "Go and fetch the landlord!" he ordered.

===Chinese===
米普斯径直凑到他跟前，贴着耳朵吐出一句意味深长的土话：“沃姆普斯！”（即“蛆虫”的土腔）

“什么？”上校嚷起来，他没听懂。

“嘘！”米普斯朝那头的瓦格茨太太（Mrs. Waggetts）一指，她已经伏在围裙上哭起来了。“他看着它们，不叫出来。”

“不叫谁出来？”上校抢白道。

“我不是一直跟您说嘛，”司事答道，“沃姆普斯！”

“老子实在听不明白你咕哝的什么。”上校的耐性已近耗尽，“去把店家给我叫来！”他下令。

===Original===
"Oh, would that he could," sobbed Mrs. Waggetts. "Oh, dear, oh, dear!"

The captain turned on her with an oath. "What's the matter with you, my good woman?" he said.

"That good woman is the landlord," volunteered Mr. Mipps.

"You exasperating little liar!" shouted the captain, seizing the enigmatical sexton and shaking him violently; "you said the landlord was in the churchyard a minute ago."

"A minute ago!" cried the breathless undertaker. "Why, he's been there a year and a half, and there he'll stop till as wot time as they gets him. Though I must say I gave him the best pine and knocked him up solid with my own hands—

"Released from Missus Waggetts

I left him to the maggots—

and there's Waggetts, and she's the landlord," and the sexton, chuckling with delight at his ready wit, pointed to the still weeping landlady.

===Chinese===
“唉，他要能来可就好了，”瓦格茨太太呜咽道，“天哪，天哪！”

上校咒了一声，冲她转过身来：“你怎么回事，我的好太太？”他说。

“这位好太太就是店家。”米普斯先生热心相告。

“你这气死人的小骗子！”上校吼道，一把揪住这个高深莫测的司事，使劲摇晃，“你方才还说店家在教堂墓园里。”

“方才！”上气不接下气的殡葬承办人嚷道，“哎呀，他在那儿待了一年半啦，还得一直待到人家把他拾掇了去呢。不过说句公道话，我给他用的是顶好的松木板子，还亲手敲得瓷实瓷实的——

“让瓦格茨太太得了解脱，

我把他交给了蛆虫——

喏，瓦格茨就在那儿，她如今就是店家。”司事对自己这份急智得意得咯咯直乐，朝还在抹泪的老板娘一指。

===Original===
"Well, ma'am," said the captain, coming to the point at once, "you must really blame yourself if your scores are not settled. A little potboy who has to stand on tiptoe to look over the bar is not the sort of person to prevent people helping themselves; and that's what my seadogs are doing now—helping themselves."

Mrs. Waggetts, with a scream, rushed from the kitchen, followed by Imogene, the sexton and the schoolmaster being glad enough to follow their example and so escape from the bullying captain, who was now left alone with Denis.

"Now, then, Mr. Squire's son, listen to me," he said.

"My name is Denis Cobtree," returned the young man. "The name Cobtree is well enough known upon the Marsh to be remembered by a sea captain."

===Chinese===
“好了，太太，”上校单刀直入，“这账要是收不齐，您可真只能怪您自己。一个踮起脚尖才够得着柜台的小酒保，可拦不住人家自己动手；我这帮海狗儿眼下干的就是这个——自己动手。”

瓦格茨太太尖叫一声，冲出厨房；伊莫金（Imogene）紧随其后，司事和教书先生也巴不得照此办理，好躲开这个仗势欺人的上校。厨房里就剩下他和丹尼斯两个人。

“好了，乡绅公子，听我说，”他说。

“我叫丹尼斯·科布特，”年轻人回敬道，“科布特这个姓在湿地上响当当，一位舰长阁下总该记得。”

===Original===
"Look here, young fellow," said the officer warningly, "I am here representing the law, commissioned by King George."

"I have heard that the King's taste may be called in question," Denis replied.

"I can prove to you otherwise," returned the captain, "for it so happens that Captain Collyer holds the majority for stringing up smugglers. I have sent more from the coast to the sessions than any of his Majesty's agents. And stap my vitals, I believe I have landed on a perfect hornets' nest here. Now tell me, sir," he went on with that tone of authority that Denis found so utterly aggressive, "what do you know of the smuggling business in these parts? I have small doubt but that your father finds the business a pretty valuable asset to his land revenues, eh? I warrant me half goes to your own pockets and the rest to the lost cause of the Jacobites."

===Chinese===
“听着，小伙子，”军官警告道，“我到此地是代表法律的，奉的是乔治王（King George）的差遣。”

“我倒是听说过，国王的品味还大可商榷呢。”丹尼斯答道。

“这个我可以证明给你看，”上校回敬道，“碰巧说到绞杀走私贩，科利尔上校稳坐头一把交椅。沿海一带，经我手送上巡回法庭的人，比国王陛下手下哪个缉私差官都多。我敢拿性命打赌，我这回是一脚踩进了个十足的马蜂窝。现在你告诉我，先生，”他接着说，那股威风的官腔在丹尼斯听来蛮横透顶，“这一带的走私勾当，你都知道些什么？我毫不怀疑，令尊把这行当作了田庄进项里一笔挺值钱的贴补，嗯？我敢担保，一半进了你们自家的腰包，剩下的都填了詹姆斯党人（Jacobites）那桩注定失败的事业。”

===Original===
The captain was becoming insulting, so Denis took great pains to hold his temper in check. "Let me tell you, sir," he said, "in the first place, my father is no Jacobite, no, nor yet his father before him. My people were instrumental in bringing across William of Orange. Although my father has withdrawn from political strife, he is still a profound Whig; and on that score he and I have but little sympathy together; for I stoutly affirm that the Dutchman had no right whatever in England, and I never lose an opportunity of drinking to our King over the water, and praying for a speedy restoration."

"You just bear in mind, young man," said the captain, "that the '45 was not so very long ago. I am here to look for smugglers, not royalists, but there's still a price on their heads, so you should keep whatever opinions you may hold to yourself."

"If you are really here to look for smugglers," said Denis, scorning his threat, "you must first take pains to curry favour with my father, for he is the head of our jurisdiction. The Marsh has its own laws, sir; and you will find to your inconvenience, I fear, that the 'Leveller of the Marsh Scotts'1 is a big power."

===Chinese===
上校的话越来越带侮辱味儿，丹尼斯只得竭力按捺火气。“我告诉你，先生，”他说，“头一件，我父亲不是詹姆斯党人，他父亲也从来不是。当年迎立奥兰治的威廉（William of Orange）渡海即位，我家先人是出过力的。家父虽已退出政争，骨子里仍旧是个十足的辉格党人（Whig）；单凭这一层，我们爷儿俩就少有同心之处，因为我一口咬定，那个荷兰人在英格兰根本无权称王；但凡有一星半点儿机会，我总不忘为海那边我们的国王——流亡在外的斯图亚特君主——举杯，并祈求他早日复位。”

“小伙子，你给我记住了，”上校说，“'45 那场乱子（一七四五年的詹姆斯党起事）过去还不算久。我是来找走私贩的，不是来找保王党的，不过后者的人头至今还悬着赏格，所以你肚里存着什么见解，最好自己收着。”

“您当真是来找走私贩的，”丹尼斯把他的威吓全不放在眼里，“那您得先下一番功夫讨好我父亲，因为本地司法归他执掌。这片湿地有自己的法度，先生；只怕到时候您会不痛快地发现，‘湿地斯科特人的平定者’¹可是位了不起的大人物。”

===Original===
"I hold my commission from the King's Admiralty, and that's enough for me," laughed the captain, "and for Marshmen, too, as you'll find."

But Denis replied: "Possibly, sir, you have not heard of the old saying, that 'The world is divided into five parts—Europe, Asia, Africa, America, and Romney Marsh.' We are independent on the Marshes."

"The Act of Parliament," retorted the captain largely, "brought in by the late King William against all smugglers will cook that goose, young sir."

"Ah, well," said Denis finally, "it's no odds to me; but let me tell you this: Your King George may rule Whitehall, but my father rules Romney Marsh," and humming an old royalist tune, much to the annoyance of the captain, the young man sauntered out of the inn.

===Chinese===
“我的委任状出自国王的海军部，这对我来说足够了，”上校大笑，“对湿地人来说也照样够用——不信走着瞧。”

可丹尼斯答道：“先生，您许是没听说过那句老话吧——‘世界分成五块：欧洲、亚洲、非洲、美洲，还有罗姆尼湿地。’在湿地这片地上，我们是独立的。”

“国会那道法案，”上校高声驳道，“是先王威廉在位时立下的，专治一切走私贩，管保叫你那套算盘落空，年轻的先生。”

“那好吧，”丹尼斯最后说，“这跟我没多大相干；不过有句话你记下：你那位乔治王也许管得住白厅（Whitehall），我父亲可管得住罗姆尼湿地。”说罢，年轻人哼起一支王党老调，惹得上校好不气恼，自己晃晃悠悠地出了酒馆。

## V. A Bottle of Alsace Lorraine / 五　一瓶阿尔萨斯-洛林葡萄酒

===Original===
Left to himself, the captain rapidly examined the kitchen; then going to the door that led to the bar-parlour, he called out: “Bo’sun, come in here, and bring that mulatto with you.”

The bo’sun answered with alacrity, pushing before him into the kitchen an altogether horrible apparition: a thin mulatto in the dress of a navy cook. His skin was cracked like parchment and drawn tightly over the prominent cheekbones. His black eyes shone brightly, and the lids turned up at the corners like those of a Chinaman. The unusual brilliance of these eyes may have been accounted for by the scrags of pure white hair that grew from the skull. These were bound at the back into a thin pigtail, leaving the sides of the head bare, and it must have been this that gave him that curiously revolting look, for the foreigner had no ears. Another terrible thing about him was that he could not speak, for his tongue had been cut out by the roots. He had evidently suffered much, this cook.

“Job Mallet,” said the captain, when the door was shut, “we have now got this room to ourselves, and as there is no time like the present, turn that white-haired old spider of yours on to the floor and walls. This panelling seems likely.”

===Chinese===
等到屋里只剩自己一人，上校迅速把厨房搜检了一遍；随后走到那扇通往酒吧间的门前，扬声喊道：“水手长，进来，把那个黑白混血儿（mulatto）也带上。”

水手长应声而至，好不麻利，把一个说不出的骇人形象推在身前赶进厨房：那是个瘦削的黑白混血儿，一身海军厨子的行头。他的皮肤皲裂如羊皮纸，紧绷绷地罩着高耸的颧骨；一双黑眼睛亮得灼人，眼角上挑，倒像个中国人。这双眼睛异乎寻常的亮，或许能从那脑壳上生出的一绺绺纯白头发里找出缘由。白发在脑后束成一根细辫，头的两侧光秃秃空着——准是这一头装束，才叫他生就那副说不出的瘆人相，因为这位异乡人没有耳朵。他身上还有一桩可怕的事：他不能说话，他的舌头被人连根割去了。显然，这位厨子吃过许多苦头。

“乔布·马利特（Job Mallet），”上校待门关上后说道，“如今这间屋子归咱们独享了。机不可失，把你那头白毛老蜘蛛放出来，叫它到地板和墙板上爬一爬。这几面护墙板看着就有点门道。”

===Original===
The bo’sun approached the mulatto, and jabbered some weird lingo into his ear-hole, which immediately made the uncouth figure hop about the room, spreading his lean arms along the panels, which he kept tapping with his fingers, at the same time executing a curious tattoo with his bare feet upon the floor. In this fashion he encircled the room twice, apparently without achieving any result. In the corner of the room was fixed a wooden table with a heavy flap which reached nearly to the ground. Upon this table was a large assortment of cooking utensils, while underneath, almost entirely hidden by the flap, there reposed a like collection of buckets, pails, and old saucepans.

The mulatto, after his double journey round the room, turned his attention to this table. He struck the flap up and, pushing aside the pots and pans, uttered a strange, excited gurgle.

“Ha! ha!” said the captain to the bo’sun, “your spider has caught a fly, eh?”

===Chinese===
水手长凑近那黑白混血儿，对着他的耳洞咕哝了几句怪腔怪调的黑话，那副怪模怪样的身影立时在屋里蹦跶开了：两条精瘦的胳膊贴着护墙板一路张开，手指头不住地叩击板壁，一双光脚板同时在地板上敲出古怪的鼓点。他就这样绕着屋子兜了两圈，看上去一无所获。屋角固定着一张木桌，垂下一扇厚重的桌板，几乎拖到地面。桌上摆着一大堆各式厨具；桌子底下，几乎全叫那扇垂板遮没，也蹲着同样的一窝家什——木桶、提桶，还有一干旧炖锅。

黑白混血儿绕屋兜过两遭，便把心思转到了这张桌上。他猛地把垂板掀起来，推开锅盆，喉咙里发出一声古怪而亢奋的咯咯声。

“哈！哈！”上校对水手长说，“你的蜘蛛逮着苍蝇了，嗯？”

===Original===
Job Mallet looked under the table and saw the mulatto pulling desperately at a brass ring that was fixed to the floor. Pushing him aside, the bo’sun had pulled up a trap and was descending a flight of steps before the captain had even locked both the doors.

“What is it?” he whispered; for the bo’sun had entirely disappeared.

“Here you are, sir,” said the sailor, reappearing with a bottle in his hand. “There’s a wine-cellar down there the size of an admiral’s cabin.”

===Chinese===
乔布·马利特朝桌下望去，只见那黑白混血儿正死命拽着一只钉死在地板上的铜环。水手长把他拨到一边，掀开一扇活板门，踏上台阶往下走了——这时候，上校连那两道门都还没有锁上。

“怎么样了？”他低声问，因为水手长已经整个儿没了踪影。

“找着了，先生，”那水手应声重又现身，手里提着一只瓶子。“底下有个酒窖，跟海军上将的舱房一般大。”

===Original===
“Oh!” replied the captain. “Well, like enough it’s the regular cellar.”

“Then why should they be at such pains to hide the entrance, sir?” returned the bo’sun.

“There’s nothing in that,” replied the captain. “It’s natural that they don’t want every Tom, Dick, and Harry going into the wine-cellar.”

“I suppose it is, sir,” agreed the bo’sun, “but it looks a costly bottle, though it could do with a bit of a shine,” he added, spitting on it and giving it a vigorous rub with his sleeve.

===Chinese===
“哦！”上校应道。“唔，多半就是店家惯常那间酒窖。”

“那又何必费那么大的劲把入口藏起来呢，先生？”水手长顶了一句。

“这算不得什么，”上校答道。“人家不愿意随便什么阿猫阿狗都往酒窖里钻，原是人之常情。”

“想来也是，先生，”水手长表示赞同，“不过这瓶子瞧着挺值钱，就是欠擦亮一点儿。”他补了一句，啐了口唾沫，抡起袖子使劲擦了起来。

===Original===
“Let’s look at the label,” said the captain. “ ‘Alsace Lorraine, White, Rare 500.’ What on earth does ‘500’ mean?”

“The date, sir,” ejaculated the bo’sun; “it’s the date. My eye! that’s enough to give a man a bad head. It’s over a thousand years old.”

“Nonsense, my man,” said the captain, laughing. “It means that this bottle is one of a cargo of five hundred.”

“Of course,” said the bo’sun, slapping his knee. “What a thundering old idiot I am, to be sure. You’re right, sir, as you always are, for I see the other four hundred and ninety-nine down below there. But,” he added ruefully, “we’ve got no proof that they’re smuggled.”

“We’ll soon get that,” said the captain, thrusting the bottle into the capacious skirt pocket of his sea-coat. “Put these things back, and summon the landlady.”

===Chinese===
“瞧瞧酒标，”上校说。“‘阿尔萨斯-洛林，白，珍酿500。’这‘500’到底是个什么讲究？”

“是年份，先生，”水手长脱口嚷道。“是年份。我的老天！这酒下肚，准叫人头疼得裂开。它都一千多岁了。”

“胡扯，伙计，”上校大笑道。“这意思是，这瓶酒是一整批五百瓶货里的一瓶。”

“可不是嘛，”水手长一拍大腿。“我可不真是个天打雷劈的老糊涂。您说得对，先生，您向来是对的——底下那四百九十九瓶我可都瞧见了。不过，”他懊丧地补了一句，“咱们还没有凭据说它们是走私货呢。”

“这凭据马上就能到手，”上校说着，把瓶子塞进海员大衣宽大的下摆口袋里。“把这些家什归回原位，去请老板娘。”

===Original===
Then the captain unlocked the door and quietly opened it.

Coming up quickly from a stooping position near the keyhole was Mr. Mipps, the sexton.

“You’re a fine fellow,” he said, not at all put out of countenance by the captain having found him eavesdropping, “a very fine fellow to come lookin’ for smuggling, with a gang o’ blasphemous scoundrels wot kick up more to-do than the Tower of Babel. Look here, sir, are you coming in to keep order or not? I only want a word, Yes or No, for I shall go straight round to the courthouse and report you to the squire. And then p’raps he won’t—only *p’raps*, ’cos I’m dead sure he will.”

===Chinese===
随后上校开了锁，悄没声儿地把门拉开。

正从钥匙孔旁边躬着的身子上直起身来的，是教堂司事米普斯先生。

“您可真是位好汉，”他说，偷听被上校当场撞破，他却丝毫没有失措，“顶顶体面的好汉，带着一帮亵渎神明的无赖来查走私，闹腾得比巴别塔还要凶。听着，先生，你到底进不进来维持秩序？我只要你一句话：管，还是不管。不然我就直奔议事厅，把你告到乡绅大人那里。到那时啊，兴许他不会把你和你那班人马统统塞进那儿的牢房；兴许不会——只是*兴许*，因为我敢十拿九稳，他一定会。”

===Original===
“What are my boys doing?” laughed the captain.

“What are the little dears not doing?” answered the sexton, thoroughly angry. “Oh, nothing, I assure you! Only upsetting the barrels, throwin’ about the tankards, stealin’ the drinks, and makin’ fun of Missus Waggetts.”

“Oh, that’s all right,” said the captain. “Tell Mrs. Waggetts to come here.”

“You tell worse men than yourself to do your dirty work,” replied the sexton. “Do you think I’m a powder monkey that I should fetch and carry Missus Waggetts for you? Fetch her yourself, or send old fat-sides there,” he added, jerking his thumb at the bo’sun, “or that dear old white-haired admiral wot’s lost his yellow earflaps. As for me, I’m a-goin’ to the courthouse, and if you don’t know what for, you’ll soon learn—you and old fat-sides.” The bo’sun made a grab at him, but Mipps slipped through the crowded bar and was running up the high road.

===Chinese===
“我的弟兄们在干什么？”上校笑问。

“那些小宝贝们有什么不干的？”司事答道，怒气冲冲。“哦，没什么，我向您担保！不过是掀翻了酒桶，抡着大酒杯乱甩，偷嘴喝着酒，再拿瓦格茨太太开涮罢了。”

“哦，那没什么，”上校说。“叫瓦格茨太太到这儿来。”

“干这种脏活，你该使唤比你更不济的人去，”司事回敬道。“你以为我是火药猴（powder monkey）——舰上递火药的跑腿小子——该替你把瓦格茨太太搬来递去？要请你自己去请，要不就打发那边那位老肥膘去，”他朝水手长翘了翘大拇指，“要么就打发那位连黄色耳扇都丢光了的可爱白毛老将军去。至于我，我可要去议事厅了；你要是不知道为的什么，很快就会知道——你，还有老肥膘。”水手长伸手就抓，米普斯却从挤满人的店堂里一溜脱身，已经顺着大路飞奔而去。

===Original===
The captain now stepped into the bar. Order was at once restored. “Now, ma’am,” he said to Mrs. Waggetts, “while the bo’sun is seeing that your score is paid, give me a bottle of wine.”

“Port or claret, sir?” said the landlady.

“Neither,” said the captain. “I have a fancy to try a bottle of Alsatian. Yes, a white wine from Alsace Lorraine.”

But before the captain had time to smack his lips Mrs. Waggetts replied: “Oh, we don’t keep that, sir.”

===Chinese===
上校这会儿踱进了店堂。秩序霎时恢复。“好了，老板娘，”他对瓦格茨太太说，“趁水手长在那边照应着把你的账结清，给我来一瓶葡萄酒。”

“波特酒还是波尔多红酒，先生？”老板娘问。

“都不要，”上校说。“我想尝尝一瓶阿尔萨斯酒。对，阿尔萨斯-洛林（Alsace Lorraine）的白葡萄酒。”

可上校还没来得及咂一咂嘴，瓦格茨太太便答了话：“哦，先生，我们这儿不备那种酒。”

===Original===
“No?” queried the captain.

“No, indeed, sir,” said the landlady. “You see there’s no call for it in these parts. And then the customs are so high we couldn’t afford to stock it for the few and far betweens as might ask for it. Why, for my own part, sir, though I’ve been in the business these—well, many years now, I’ve never even heard of it.”

“Really!” said the captain. “Well, it’s a good wine, ma’am. Now, bo’sun, pipe the men outside.”

“Won’t you try a bottle of claret, sir?” said the landlady with persuasion.

===Chinese===
“没有？”上校问了一句。

“真的没有，先生，”老板娘说。“您瞧，这一带没人点这个。再说关税又高得吓人，为着千载难逢的一两位想喝的客人去备货，我们可担不起。哎，就拿我自己说吧，先生，我做这一行也——呃——好些年头了，连听都从没听说过它。”

“是嘛！”上校说。“这可是瓶好酒呢，老板娘。好了，水手长，吹哨把弟兄们召到外面去。”

“先生，尝一瓶波尔多红酒不成吗？”老板娘劝道。

===Original===
“No,” said the captain, “later on, perhaps. I’ll see. By the way, is there any old barn about where I could quarter my men? I’m loath to billet them on the village.”

“No, I don’t know anywhere,” returned the landlady. “Do you, Mr. Rash? Perhaps you’d loan the schoolhouse to the captain?”

“Yes, and give us a holiday for once in a way!” chimed in the potboy.

“It’s not to be thought of,” said the schoolmaster, walking out of the inn.

“No one uses the church on weekdays, I suppose,” said the captain. “I daresay there’s room for them there, in the vestry or the tower perhaps, or even in the crypt.”

===Chinese===
“不，”上校说。“待会儿再说吧，我看看。对了，附近可有什么旧谷仓，能让我把人马安置进去？我实在不愿硬摊派到村里各户借宿。”

“不知道，我不晓得哪儿有，”老板娘回道。“拉什先生，你可知道？要不你把校舍借给上校使使？”

“对呀，破天荒给咱们放一天假吧！”小酒保插嘴道。

“想都别想。”教书先生说着，径自走出了酒馆。

“平日里教堂总没人用吧？”上校说。“我琢磨那儿准搁得下他们——法衣室啦，钟楼啦，要不就连地下墓室也使得。”

===Original===
“Them drunken ruffians in the church!” cried out young Jerk, pulling a horrified face, and indicating the rough sailors who were now outside the inn. “You’d better watch out what you’re up to, or you’ll have the vicar on your track.”

“I’ll tell you where you’ll end, my lad,” said the captain, turning on him sharply.

“Where, sir?” said young Jerk, looking really interested.

“If not upon the scaffold, uncommon near it, I’ll be bound,” the captain replied.

I hope so indeed, thought Hangman Jerk, and I hopes it’ll be a-fixing the noose around your bull neck. But he kept this thought to himself, for he suddenly remembered that the captain could be rather too playful for his liking; so he watched the sailors shouldering their bundles, falling into line, and eventually swinging out of the old Ship Inn, followed by the captain.

===Chinese===
“让那帮醉醺醺的恶棍进教堂！”小杰尔克惊骇万状地嚷道，指了指这会儿已经聚在酒馆外头的那些粗野水手。“你最好留神着点自己在干什么，不然教区牧师可就要盯上你的梢了。”

“小伙子，我来告诉你你早晚的下场，”上校霍地转向他说。

“什么下场，先生？”小杰尔克问，倒真来了兴致。

“就算上不了绞刑架，也保管离它不远，”上校答道。

但愿果真如此，刽子手杰尔克心里念叨，我还盼着到头来由我亲手把绞索套上你那公牛脖子呢。不过他把这念头咽进了肚里，因为他忽然记起，这位上校闹着玩起来有时未免狠过了头，不合他的口味；于是他只好眼睁睁看着水手们扛起行李卷，排成一行，末了浩浩荡荡开出老“船”酒馆，上校押在后头。

## VI. Doctor Syn Takes Cold / VI　辛医生着了凉

===Original===
You can imagine that the coming of the King’s men caused some stir in Dymchurch; for after leaving the Ship Inn they were marched round the village and drawn up in front of the courthouse. Here they waited while the captain knocked upon the front door and asked for the squire.

“Sir Antony Cobtree is out riding,” said the butler. But at that instant a clattering of hoofs was heard upon the highway and the squire himself came along at an easy trot and drew rein before the house. “My faith!” he cried, looking from the butler to the captain, and then at the line of naked cutlasses. “Have the French landed at last?”

===Chinese===
可以想见，国王的人马开到，在迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）激起了不小的骚动；他们离开“船”酒馆（the Ship Inn）之后，列队绕村行进了一遭，随即在议事厅（courthouse）前排开站定。大队原地候命，上校则上前叩响前门，求见乡绅。

“安东尼·科布特爵士（Sir Antony Cobtree）出门骑马去了。”管家答道。可恰在此时，大路上传来一阵得得的马蹄声，乡绅本人一路小跑而来，在宅前勒住了缰绳。“我的天哪！”他嚷道，目光从管家移到上校身上，又扫过那一排亮出鞘的水手弯刀，“法国人到底还是登陆了不成？”

===Original===
“Captain Howard Collyer of the King’s Admiralty, sir,” said the captain, saluting, “and if you are the squire, very much at your service.”

The jolly squire returned the salute, touching his hat with his riding whip. “Indeed, Captain?” he said, dismounting. “And I would prefer to be your friend than your foe so long as you have these sturdy fellows at your back. Is it the renewed activity of the French navy that we have to thank for your presence here, or the coast defence?”

“I should like a word with you alone,” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“国王的海军部属下，霍华德·科利尔（Howard Collyer）上校，先生，”上校敬着礼说，“倘若您就是乡绅，随时愿为您效劳。”

快活的乡绅还了一礼，拿马鞭碰了碰帽檐。“哦？上校，”他一面下马一面说，“只要你身后带着这么一帮结实的汉子，我宁可做你的朋友，不做你的对头。您大驾光临，我们该谢法国海军重新蠢动呢，还是该谢海防事务？”

“我想同您单独谈几句。”上校说。

===Original===
“Certainly,” returned the squire, throwing the reins to a groom and leading the way to the house.

They crossed the large hall, and the squire, opening a door at the far end, invited the captain to enter the library.

There in the recess of the old mullioned window sat Doctor Syn, deep in a dusty tome that he had taken from the bookcase.

===Chinese===
“当然可以。”乡绅答道，把缰绳抛给马夫，领头朝宅子里走去。

两人穿过宽敞的门厅，乡绅推开尽里头的一扇门，请上校进了书房。

只见辛医生（Doctor Syn）正坐在那扇老式竖框窗的窗龛里，捧着一部打书架上取来的积灰大部头，读得入了神。

===Original===
“Ah, Doctor,” said the squire, “they didn’t tell me you were here. No further need to fear the French fleet. The King’s Admiralty has had the kind grace to furnish us with an officer’s complement. Captain Collyer—Doctor Syn, our vicar.”

“Not the Collyer who sank the *Lion d’Or* at the mouth of the St. Lawrence River, I suppose?” he said, shaking hands.

“The same,” returned the captain, highly delighted that the achievement of his life had been heard of by the parson. “Captain Howard Collyer then, commanding the *Resistance*, a brigantine of twenty-two guns. Indeed, sir, the French Government kicked up such a devil of a row over that little affair that I lost my command. So now, instead of sinking battleships, the Admiralty keeps me busy nosing out smugglers; a poor enough game for a man who has done big things at sea, but it has its excitements.”

===Chinese===
“啊，医生，”乡绅说，“他们没告诉我你在这儿。这下不必再怕法国舰队了。国王的海军部大发慈悲，给我们拨来了一位军官带一整队人马。这位是科利尔上校——这位是辛医生，我们的教区牧师。”

“想必您不是那位在圣劳伦斯河（St. Lawrence River）口击沉‘金狮号’（Lion d’Or）的科利尔吧？”他伸手相握，一边说。

“正是在下。”上校答道——毕生功业竟连牧师都有耳闻，他心里着实得意。“当年是霍华德·科利尔，统带‘抵抗号’（Resistance），一艘载有二十二门炮的双桅横帆船。说实在的，先生，法国政府为那桩小小的事端闹了个天翻地覆，害得我丢了指挥权。于是如今，海军部不派我去击沉战列舰，反倒叫我成天忙着缉查走私贩；对一个在海上干过大事业的人来说，这份差事够寒碜的，倒也自有它的刺激。”

===Original===
“So I should imagine,” said the cleric.

“And what have you come here for?” asked the squire.

“To hang every smuggler on Romney Marsh,” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“我可以想见。”教士说。

“那你到这儿来是为了什么？”乡绅问。

“把罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）上的走私贩一个个送上绞架。”上校说。

===Original===
“Do you believe in ghosts?” said the squire.

“What do you mean?” retorted the captain.

“What I say,” returned the squire. “Do you believe in ghosts?”

“Well, I can’t say I do,” laughed the captain, “for I have never yet met one.”

===Chinese===
“你信不信鬼？”乡绅问。

“你这是什么意思？”上校反问。

“就是我说的那个意思，”乡绅答道。“你信不信鬼？”

“这个嘛，我不敢说信，”上校笑道，“因为我到如今还没碰见过一个。”

===Original===
“No more have I,” returned the squire. “But they say the Marsh is haunted at night. They’ve said so so long that people believe it. Whenever a traveller loses his way on the Marsh and disappears, folk say that the Marsh witches have taken him. When the harvests are bad, when the wool is poor, when the cattle are sickly, oh, it’s always the Marsh witches that are blamed. They set fire to haystacks, they kill the chickens, they blast the trees, they curdle the milk, and hold up travellers and rob them of their purses. In fact all the vices of the Marsh, really performed by Master Fox, or Master Careless, or Master Footpad, are all put down to the poor Marsh witches, who don’t exist except in the minds of the people. I know the Marshes as well as any man ever will, and I’ve never seen a witch, and it’s the very same with smugglers. The whole thing’s a fallacy. I’ve never caught ’em at it; and I keep a stern enough eye on my farms, I can tell you. Why, I’m a positive king, sir. Do you know that if a man working in the neighbourhood doesn’t please me, that I can shut every door of the Marsh against him? Why, these farmers are all scared stiff of me, sir. I’d like to see the man who went against the laws of Romney Marsh. I can tell you, sir, that I’d soon mark him down.”

===Chinese===
“我也没碰见过，”乡绅答道。“可人们都说湿地夜里闹鬼。这话说得年头太久，大家便都信以为真了。但凡有旅人在湿地里迷了路、从此没了踪影，乡亲们便说，是湿地女巫把他摄了去。年成坏了，羊毛歉了，牛羊病了——唉，罪责一概落在湿地女巫头上。草垛起火，是她们点的；鸡雏丧命，是她们弄的；树木焦枯，是她们咒的；牛奶结絮，是她们搅的；拦住旅人、劫走钱袋的，也是她们。说到底，湿地里种种劣迹——真正干出来的是狐狸先生（Master Fox）、马虎先生（Master Careless）、剪径先生（Master Footpad）之流——全叫可怜的湿地女巫背了黑锅；而这些女巫，除了活在人们的脑子里，压根儿就不存在。要说对这片湿地的了解，我不输给天底下任何人，可我从来没见过一个女巫；走私贩的事也一模一样。这一整套名堂全是无稽之谈。我从来没当场拿住过他们；跟你说，我盯自家的田庄盯得够严的了。嘿，在这块地方，先生，我就是个地地道道的国王。你可知道，但凡左近做工的有谁不合我的心意，我就能叫湿地对他关死每一扇门？嘿，这些庄稼汉，先生，个个怕我怕得腿肚子发僵。我倒想见识见识，哪个吃了熊心豹子胆，敢跟罗姆尼湿地的王法作对。我可以告诉你，先生，要不了多久，我就会把他盯死。”

===Original===
“You are perhaps too confident, sir,” suggested the captain.

“Not a bit of it, sir,” exclaimed the squire. “Mind you I don’t trust ’em, oh, Lord, no; I just know ’em to be honest, because I don’t give ’em the chance to be otherwise. They never know when or where I’ll be turning up. Why, I have a groom in my stables awake all night in case I want to surprise a farm ten miles away. Smugglers? Pooh! Rubbish!”

===Chinese===
“您这话或许说得太过自信了，先生。”上校委婉地点了一句。

“压根儿没那回事，先生。”乡绅大声道。“你可听好，我并不是信得过他们——哦，老天，绝不；我只是知道他们本分老实，因为我从不给他们不老实的机会。他们永远摸不着我什么时辰、打什么地方冒出来。跟你说，我马厩里养着个马夫整夜不合眼，就防着哪一刻我要突袭十英里外的庄户。走私贩？呸！一派胡言！”

===Original===
“Then you consider that I am here on a wild goose chase?” said the captain.

“Not even that,” said the squire; “for you will find no wild geese to chase. However, I don’t think that you need regret having been sent here, for we can give you really good entertainment; and I’ll bet my head that after you have stayed with us a week or so you’ll be sending in your papers to the Admiralty, and settling down on the Marsh as a good Kentish farmer.”

“I’m afraid not, sir,” laughed the captain.

===Chinese===
“这么说，您是觉得我这趟是来追野雁——白忙一场了？”上校说。

“连白忙都算不上，”乡绅说；“因为这儿连一只供你追的野雁都寻不出来。不过，依我看，你也不必懊悔被打发到这儿来，我们能给你的款待，可都是顶真格的；我敢拿脑袋打赌，等你在这儿住上个把星期，你就要向海军部递辞呈，在湿地上安家落户，做个规规矩矩的肯特农夫了。”

“恐怕不会，先生。”上校笑道。

===Original===
“Oh, yes, you will,” went on the squire. “And I’ll be bound that we’ll have you bothering Doctor Syn to put the banns up for you and some country beauty. What do you say, Doctor?”

“Well,” chuckled the cleric, entering into the joke, “if a man wants to marry and settle down, and live happily ever after, as the saying goes, why, then, Kent’s the place for him. It’s a great country, sir, expecially south and east of the Medway; famous for everything that goes to make life worth living.”

“Yes, take him on the whole,” said the squire, “the King can boast of no greater jewel in the crown of England than the average man of Kent.”

===Chinese===
“哎，会的，准会，”乡绅接着往下说。“我还敢打包票：到头来，准是你少不得要来麻烦辛医生，替你跟哪位乡下美人张榜预告婚事。你说是不是，医生？”

“这个嘛，”教士乐呵呵地凑趣道，“一个人要是想娶妻成家、安顿下来，照老话说的，从此过上幸福日子——嘿，肯特正是他的去处。那是片了不得的好地方，先生，尤其是梅德韦河（the Medway）以南、以东一带；凡足以教人生值得一过的物事，那里样样出名。”

“是啊，总而言之，”乡绅说，“国王尽可以夸口：英格兰的王冠上，再没有比肯特的寻常汉子更璀璨的珠宝了。”

===Original===
“Well,” agreed the captain, “I’ve heard say that Kent has fine clover fields, and it’s evident to me that I’m a lucky devil and have fallen into one. But I must see to the billeting of my men. Perhaps you can advise me?” But the squire wouldn’t hear of business until the captain had cracked a bottle of wine with them and promised to lodge himself at the courthouse, Doctor Syn readily placing the large brick-built vicarage barn at the disposal of the men.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”上校附和道，“久闻肯特有上好的苜蓿田；照我看来，我分明是个走运的魔王，一跤跌进苜蓿田里了。不过，我得去张罗弟兄们的宿营摊派了。也许您能给我出个主意？”可乡绅说什么也不肯先谈正事，非要等上校同他们开了一瓶葡萄酒共饮，又答应自己下榻议事厅，方才罢休；辛医生则一口应承，把牧师宅那座砖砌的大谷仓腾出来，交由水兵们随意使用。

===Original===
So having settled all amicably, and promising to return within the hour for supper, the captain, piloted by Doctor Syn, and followed by the seamen, proceeded to inspect the barn; and it was not long before the sailors had converted it into as jolly an old hall as one could wish to see, with a great log-fire ablaze in the stone grate, and a pot of steaming victuals swinging from a hook above the flames.

“Are you all here?” said the captain to the bo’sun, before rejoining the Doctor outside the door.

===Chinese===
就这样，诸事和和气气地议定，上校许下一个钟头之内回来用晚饭，便由辛医生引路，身后跟着水兵，前去察看谷仓；不多时，水手们已把它拾掇成一处人人称羡的快活老堂屋——石头炉格里架起大堆木柴，火烧得正旺，火苗上方的挂钩上吊着一锅吃食，热气腾腾，晃晃悠悠。

“人都到齐了么？”上校先问过水手长，才走出谷仓，去同候在门外的医生会合。

===Original===
“All except Bill Spiker and the mulatto, sir,” returned Job Mallet. “I sent ’em for rum. Here they are, if I mistake not.” And indeed up to the barn came two seamen carrying a barrel.

“Now,” said the captain to Doctor Syn, “I am ready to return to the courthouse.”

But the cleric’s eyes were fixed on the men carrying the barrel, who were passing him. “Who’s that man?” he said to the captain, shivering violently, for a cold fog had risen with the night.

===Chinese===
“只差比尔·斯派克（Bill Spiker）跟那个黑白混血儿（mulatto），先生。”乔布·马利特（Job Mallet）答道。“我打发他们打朗姆酒去了。要是没看走眼，这就来了。”果然，两个水兵抬着一只酒桶，朝谷仓走了过来。

“好了，”上校对辛医生说，“我们可以回议事厅了。”

可教士的两眼却直勾勾地钉在打他身边经过的那两个抬桶人身上。“那个人是谁？”他问上校，浑身剧烈地打起寒战来——夜里升起了冷雾。

===Original===
“That’s Bill Spiker the gunner,” said the captain. “Do you know him?”

“No—the other, the other,” exclaimed the Doctor, still watching the retreating figures who were now being received with shouts of welcome from the barn.

“Oh, that fellow’s a mulatto,” returned the captain; “useful for investigation work. An ugly enough looking rascal, isn’t he?”

===Chinese===
“那是炮手比尔·斯派克。”上校说。“你认识他？”

“不——是另一个，另一个。”医生失声叫道，眼睛还盯着那两道退远的身影——谷仓那边，正爆出一片迎接他们的欢呼。

“哦，那家伙是个黑白混血儿，”上校回道；“干侦缉差事再合适不过。模样丑得够瞧的，是不是？”

===Original===
“A very ugly rascal,” muttered the Doctor, walking rapidly from the barn in the direction of the courthouse.

“You look cold,” remarked the captain as they stood outside the courthouse door.

“Yes. It’s a cold night,” returned the Doctor. “Why, I declare my teeth are chattering.”

===Chinese===
“一个丑透了的恶棍。”医生喃喃说着，快步离了谷仓，朝议事厅方向走去。

“你瞧着像着凉了。”两人立在议事厅门前时，上校说了一句。

“是啊。今晚真冷。”医生答道。“哎呀，我说，我的牙都打起颤来了。”

## VII. Clegg the Buccaneer / VII　海盗克莱格

===Original===
There was one man who knew Romney Marsh as well as the squire. This was Sennacherib Pepper, and, what’s more, he knew the Marsh by night as well as by day, for he was the visiting physician to the Marsh farms, and his work called him to patients sometimes at night. He had seen curious things upon the Marsh from his own account, hinting darkly about the witches and devils that rode on fiery steeds through the mists. The villagers, of course, believed his yarns, but the squire pooh-poohed them, and, as it was well known that Sennacherib Pepper was a hard drinker, some people put his stories down to the effects of wine. But although he gave no credence to his tales, Sir Antony rather enjoyed the physician, and he was a frequent visitor to the courthouse. He had prevailed upon him to stay to supper this very night, introducing him to the captain as his dear friend Sennacherib Pepper, the worst master of physics and the most atrocious liar on Romney Marsh, for although Sennacherib was a very touchy old customer and was ever on the brink of losing his temper, Sir Antony could never resist a joke at his expense.

===Chinese===
还有一个人，对罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）的熟悉不亚于乡绅。此人便是西拿基立·佩珀（Sennacherib Pepper），何止如此，这片湿地他白天识得，夜里也一样识得，因为他是个给湿地各农庄巡诊的医生，出诊看病人，有时就得摸黑赶路。据他自己说，他在湿地上见过种种怪事，言语间总阴沉沉地暗示：有女巫和魔鬼骑着喷火的骏马穿雾而过。村民们自然对他这套奇谈深信不疑，乡绅却只当耳旁风；再加上人人都知道西拿基立·佩珀是个贪杯的酒徒，不少人便把这些故事归结为杯中之物的功效。话虽如此，安东尼爵士虽不信他的故事，倒挺喜欢这位医生，而佩珀也是议事厅的常客。就在今天晚上，爵士还硬留他吃了晚饭，把他引见给上校，说是他的挚友西拿基立·佩珀——医道最稀松的大夫，罗姆尼湿地最惊天动地的谎话大王；因为西拿基立虽说是个一戳就着、火气随时都会炸开的老家伙，安东尼爵士却从来忍不住要拿他打趣。

===Original===
“Zounds, sir!” he retorted, “if I were presenting you to Sir Antony I should most certainly style him the worst business man upon the Marsh.”

“How do you make that out?” cried the squire.

“My dear sir,” went on Sennacherib to the captain, “his tenants rob him at every turn. Everybody but himself knows that half the wool from his farms finds its way over to Calais.”

“My dear Captain,” said Doctor Syn, who was warming himself at the fireplace, “our good friend Pepper is repeatedly coming into contact with the old gentleman himself upon the Marsh. Why, only last year he informed us that he met at least a score of his bodyguard riding in perfect style and most approved manner across from Ivychurch on fire-snorting steeds. And how many witches is it now that you have seen? A good round dozen, I’ll be sworn; and they were riding straddle-legs, a thing that we could hardly credit.”

“Well, let us hope,” said the physician, “that the presence of the King’s men will frighten the devils away. I’ve seen ’em, and I’ve no wish to see ’em again.”

“You can set your mind quite at rest, sir,” returned the captain, “for if as you say their horses breathe fire, they will afford excellent targets on the flat Marsh. We’ll hail the King’s ship and see what ninety good guns can do for the devils.”

===Chinese===
“见鬼，先生！”他回敬道，“要是我把您引见给安东尼爵士，我准会一本正经地称他是湿地上最不会做买卖的生意人。”

“你这话怎么讲？”乡绅嚷道。

“我亲爱的先生，”西拿基立接着对上校说，“他的佃户时时刻刻都在偷他。除了他自己，谁都知道他家农场的羊毛有一半都流到加来（Calais）那边去了。”

“亲爱的上校，”正凑在壁炉边烤火的辛医生开了口，“咱们的好朋友佩珀在湿地上可没少跟那位‘老绅士’本人打照面。可不是，就在去年，他还告诉过我们，说他遇见至少二十名那位的贴身卫队，骑着喷火的骏马，派头十足、仪态无可挑剔，打伊维丘奇（Ivychurch）那头一路过来。还有，你如今到底见过多少个女巫啦？不多不少，整整漂亮的一打，我可以起誓；而且她们还岔开两腿跨骑，这可实在叫人难以置信。”

“呃，但愿，”医生说，“国王的人马一到，能把这群魔鬼吓跑。我是见识过他们的，可不想再见识第二回。”

“先生，您大可把心放回肚子里，”上校答道，“既然您说他们的马喷火，那在这片平展展的湿地上，正好是再妙不过的活靶子。我们就给国王的军舰打信号，看看九十门好炮能把那些魔鬼‘伺候’成个什么样。”

===Original===
All through supper was this vein of humorous conversation kept up, until when the meal was finished and pipes alight, and Denis had retired to his room with a glum face to steer most sorely against his will upon a course of literature, the conversation gradually drifted into the Southern Seas, and the captain began telling stirring tales of Clegg the pirate, who had been hanged at Rye.

“I should like to have been at that hanging,” he cried, finishing a tale of horror, “for the fellow, as you have just heard, was a bloodthirsty scoundrel.”

“So we have always heard,” said Doctor Syn; “but don’t you think that some of his exploits may have been exaggerated?”

“Not a bit of it,” exclaimed the captain; “I believe everything I hear about that man, except that last blunder that put his neck into the noose at Rye.”

===Chinese===
这路诙谐的谈兴一直贯穿着整顿晚饭；待到席终，烟斗点着，丹尼斯也哭丧着脸退回自己房里，去给自己硬扳着舵，万般不情愿地驶上一条文学的航道，谈锋便渐渐漂向了南海，上校讲起海盗克莱格那些惊心动魄的事迹——此人已在拉伊被处了绞刑。

“那场绞刑，我倒真想亲眼看看，”他刚讲完一桩恐怖的故事，嚷道，“这家伙，各位方才也听见了，是个嗜血成性的恶棍。”

“我们向来也是这么听说的，”辛医生说，“不过您不觉得，他那些事迹里头，有些兴许添了油加了醋？”

“一点儿也没有，”上校扬声道，“关于这个人，我听到什么信什么，唯独最后那桩把他脖子套进拉伊绞索的糊涂事除外。”

===Original===
“That is his only exploit about which there is any certainty,” said the physician.

“It was a mistake murdering that revenue man,” agreed Doctor Syn, “but Clegg was drunk, and threw all caution to the devil.”

“Clegg had been drunk enough before,” said the captain, “and yet he had never made a mistake. No, he was too clever to be caught in the meshes of a tavern brawl. Besides, from all we know of his former life, he would surely have put up a better defence at his trial; of course he would. You don’t tell me that a man who could terrorize the high seas all that time was going to let himself swing for a vulgar murder in a Rye tavern.”

“But it is a noticeable thing,” put in the cleric, “that all great criminals have made one stupid blunder that has caused their downfall.”

===Chinese===
“那可是他唯一桩确凿无疑的‘事迹’了。”医生说。

“杀掉那个缉私员，确实是一步错棋，”辛医生附和道，“可克莱格当时喝醉了，把所有的小心谨慎一股脑抛给了魔鬼。”

“克莱格从前喝醉的回数还少吗，”上校说，“可他从没出过差错。不，他那般精明，绝不会一头栽进酒馆斗殴的罗网里。再说，凭我们对他早年行径的种种了解，到了受审的时候，他准会作一番更高明的辩护；他当然会的。总不能叫我相信，一个把公海搅得鸡犬不宁那么多年的人物，肯为拉伊一家酒馆里一桩下三滥的谋杀案，稀里糊涂地吊上绞架。”

“不过有一点惹人注意，”教士插言道，“凡是巨盗悍匪，末了都犯过一桩蠢不可及的错事，才招致了覆灭。”

===Original===
“Which generally means,” went on the captain, “that up to that moment it was luck and not genius that kept them safe. But we know that Clegg was a genius. I’ve had it first hand from high Admiralty men; from men who have lived in the colonies and traded in Clegg’s seas. The more I hear about that rascally pirate the more it makes me wonder; and some day I mean to give the time to clearing up the mystery.”

“What mystery?” said the cleric.

“The mystery of how Clegg could persuade another man to commit wilful murder in order to take his name upon the scaffold,” said the captain. “It takes some powers of persuasion to accomplish that, you’ll agree.”

“What on earth do you mean?” said the cleric.

“Simply this,” ejaculated the captain, beating the table with his fist, “that Clegg was never hanged at Rye.”

===Chinese===
“这往往意味着，”上校接着说，“在那之前，保他们平安无事的是运气，而不是天才。可我们知道，克莱格是个天才。这话是我从海军部的大人物们嘴里亲耳听来的；从那些在殖民地安过家、在克莱格出没的海面上做过买卖的人嘴里听来的。那个恶棍海盗的事，我听得越多，心里越犯嘀咕；总有一天，我要腾出工夫，把这桩疑案查个水落石出。”

“什么疑案？”教士问。

“克莱格怎么能说动另一个人去干一桩蓄意谋杀，好让他顶着克莱格的名字上绞架——疑的就是这个，”上校说，“要办成这种事，得有多硬的说服功夫，二位总得承认。”

“您这话究竟是什么意思？”教士问。

“意思再简单不过，”上校一拳擂在桌上，脱口喝道，“克莱格压根儿没在拉伊上绞架。”

===Original===
There was a pause, and the gentlemen looked at him with grave faces. Presently the squire laughed. “Upon my soul, Captain,” he said, “you run our friend Sennacherib here uncommon close with staggering statements. I wonder which of you will tell us first that Queen Anne is not dead.”

“Queen Anne is dead,” exclaimed the captain, “because she was not fortunate enough to persuade somebody else to die for her. Now I maintain that this is exactly what Clegg did do.”

“Can you let us have the reasons that led you to this theory?” said the cleric, interested.

“I don’t see why not,” replied the captain. “In the first place, the man hanged at Rye was a short, thickset man, tattooed from head to foot, wearing enormous brass earrings, and his black hair cropped short as a Roundhead’s.”

===Chinese===
满屋静了一阵，几位绅士板着脸望着他。过了一会儿，乡绅笑出声来。“我的老天，上校，”他说，“你放出来的惊人话头，简直跟我们这位西拿基立朋友不相上下了。我倒想晓得，你们两位谁会头一个告诉我们：安妮女王（Queen Anne）还没死呢。”

“安妮女王是真死了，”上校大声说，“就因为她没那个造化，说动旁人替她去死。我可要一口咬定，克莱格干的恰恰就是这件事。”

“能不能把促成你这个想法的理由，讲给我们听听？”教士饶有兴致地问。

“有什么不能讲的，”上校答道，“头一条，在拉伊被绞死的是个矮墩墩的壮实汉子，从头到脚刺满了青，耳朵上坠着老大的黄铜耳环，一头黑发剪得短短的，跟个圆颅党人（Roundhead）的发型一个样。”

===Original===
“That’s an exact description of him,” said the parson, “for, as everybody knows, I visited the poor wretch in his prison at Rye, and at his desire wrote out his final and horrible confession.”

“Is that so?” said the captain. “Oh, yes, I remember hearing of how he was visited by a parson. I thought it a bit incongruous at the time.”

“And so it was,” agreed the parson, “for I have never seen a more unrepentant man go to meet his Maker.”

“Well, now,” went on the captain, his eyes glistening with excitement, “I have it on very good authority that the real Clegg in no way answered this description: he was a weird-looking fellow; thin faced, thin legs, long arms, and, what’s more to the point, was never tattooed in his life save once by some unskilled artist who had tried to portray a man walking the plank with a shark waiting below. This picture was executed so poorly that the pirate would never let anyone try again. Then I also have it on the very best evidence that Clegg’s hair was gray, and had been gray since quite a young man; so that does away with your black, close-cropped hair. And again I have it that Clegg would never permit his ears to be pierced for brass rings, affirming that they were useless lumber for a seaman to carry.”

===Chinese===
