Now to make conversation Jerry was bold enough to interrupt this song by inquiring what exactly was meant by the “dead man’s throttle.” Doctor Syn stopped in his walk and looked at him, filling two tots of rum, one of which he handed to Jerk, tossing off the other himself and saying:

“Ah, you may well ask that, sonny. I don’t know exactly myself, but I suppose if poor Pepper was to come in here now and throttle us, man and boy—him being stone dead, as we both well know—well, we should be having the ‘dead man’s throttle’ served on us!”

“Oh, I see!” replied Jerk with interest. “Then I take it that the rest of the song has some shreds of meaning, too? What’s the ‘tank’ that the corpses float round in, sir?”

===Chinese===
为了没话找话，杰里大着胆子打断了这支歌，问那“死人的扼喉”到底是什么意思。辛医生停下踱步，望着他，斟了两小杯朗姆酒，一杯递给杰尔克，另一杯自己一仰脖喝干，说道：

“啊，小老弟，这一问可问得在行。连我自己也说不确切。不过我想，倘若可怜的佩珀这会儿闯进这屋来，掐住咱们爷儿俩的脖子——他老人家嘛，咱们两个心里都有数，早是个十足死了的人——那好，咱们爷儿俩就算领教着‘死人的扼喉’了！”

“哦，我明白了！”杰尔克兴味盎然地应道。“照这么说，这歌儿剩下的几句也多少有些意思了？那尸首们在里头漂着的‘水柜’，又是什么玩意儿，先生？”

===Original===
“The sea,” replied the Doctor, “the sea; that’s the great tank, my lad, and that there are corpses enough floating round in it, I don’t think you and I could doubt.”

“That’s plain and true enough,” said Jerk, “but I don’t see no sense about the ‘dead man’s teeth in the bottle.’ ”

“That’s plain enough,” said the Doctor, taking a stiff swig from the black bottle itself; “it was in England’s day that I wrote that. He cut a nigger’s head off with a cutlass because the rascal was drinking his best rum on the sly, and the shock, as he died, made the black brute bite through the glass neck of the bottle.”

===Chinese===
“是大海，”医生答道，“是大海；大海就是那口顶大的水柜，我的孩子。至于里头漂着的尸首断然少不了，你我总不至于疑心吧。”

“这句浅显，也在理，”杰尔克说，“可‘死人的牙齿在瓶里’那句，我就瞧不出什么道理了。”

“那句再明白没有了，”医生说着，就着那只黑瓶子本身结结实实灌了一大口；“写下那段词儿，还是英格兰当年当道的年月。他一弯刀砍下个黑鬼的脑袋，只因那无赖偷偷摸摸喝了他的上等朗姆酒；那黑畜生断气的当儿一惊，竟一口咬穿了瓶颈的玻璃。”

===Original===
“Did you see it, sir?” asked Jerk, carried away by the tale.

“Who said I saw it?” demanded the cleric sharply.

“Well, you said you wrote the song, sir, and at the time it happened.”

“Nothing of the kind—I said nothing of the kind. The song’s an old one, an ancient thing. God knows what rascal invented it, but you can depend upon it, a rascal he was. I don’t know why I should hum it—I don’t know what it means; can’t make head or tale of the jargon.”

===Chinese===
“这事儿您亲眼瞧见的么，先生？”杰尔克听得出了神，问道。

“谁说我瞧见了？”牧师厉声喝问。

“嗐，您方才说这歌是您写的呀，先生，还说正是那桩事出的时候。”

“哪有这话——我压根儿没说过这话。这歌是支老歌，老掉牙的东西。天晓得是哪个无赖编出来的；不过你只管放心，编它的人准是个无赖。我不知道自己干吗要哼它——我不知道它是什么意思；这堆黑话，我实在摸不着头脑。”

===Original===
“You explains it very sensible, I thinks,” replied Jerry.

“I don’t—I don’t. I give you my word it’s Greek to me.”

“But Greek’s easy to parsons, ain’t it?”

“Yes, yes—well, Chinese, Fiji—what you will—what you will. Have some rum!” The Doctor’s manner was really very strange indeed. Add to this the shuttered room, the candlelight, and the strong spirits in his head, and it was small wonder that Jerry felt none too comfortable, especially as at the conclusion of the meal the door opened and Mr. Rash entered the room.

===Chinese===
“您这番解释很是明白在理，我看是的。”杰里答道。

“我不是——我不是那意思。我拿人格担保，这歌儿在我就是希腊话。”

“可希腊话对牧师们来说还不容易么？”

“是，是——那好，就是中国话，斐济话——随你怎么说——随你怎么说。再来点儿朗姆酒！”医生的做派实在古怪透顶。再加上这间闩死了窗板的屋子、这一片烛光，还有他脑子里那股烈酒的劲儿，杰里觉得浑身不自在，也就怪不得人了；尤其到用饭将毕，房门一开，拉什先生（Mr. Rash）走了进来。

===Original===
“Well, my lad,” said the vicar, “now you know where I feed, drop in again. Parochial matters to attend to with the schoolmaster: must choose the hymns, you know, for Sunday, or the choir will have nothing to sing.” And in this vein he led the boy out into the hall. He then dropped his voice to a whisper: “You were wrong about the schoolmaster last night, sonny. I’ll explain things to you some day. Meanwhile, here’s a crown piece. You’re a smart lad, ain’t you? Well, keep a weather eye open for that mulatto rascal. There’s more in this ugly business than we imagine. I’ll tell you all about it when I know more myself, but you made a mistake last night, and I begin to see how you made it, but I can’t tell you just yet, because I’m not quite sure of my ground; and it’s dangerous ground we’re treading, Jerry, you and I. Now here’s another crown—that one’s for keeping your eye open—do you know what the other’s for?”

“What?”

“Keeping your mouth shut. Don’t you remember anything about last night till I tell you—you wouldn’t understand if I was to explain. You’re very young, you know, Jerry lad, but smart’s the word that describes you, and no mistaking. You’re smart and bright—as bright as the buttons on that sea captain’s coat—as bright as a thousand new guinea bits just served from the mint—that’s what you are, and no mistake!”

===Chinese===
“好了，我的孩子，”牧师说道，“如今你知道我在哪儿吃饭了，往后常来串门。我还有教区事务，得同那位学堂先生接洽：你明白，总得把礼拜天唱的赞美诗挑选出来，不然唱诗班就没的可唱了。”他就这么一路说着，把孩子领进了门厅。跟着，他把嗓音压成耳语：“昨夜的事，你对那位学堂先生看走了眼，小老弟。这其中的原委，改天我讲给你听。眼下，这儿是一枚克朗（crown）银币。你是个机灵的小伙子，是不是？那好，睁大眼睛，时刻替我盯住那黑白混血的无赖。这桩丑事里头的花样，比咱们料想的还要多。等我自己知道得更多了，原原本本讲给你听。昨夜你错了一处，我也渐渐看出你是怎么错的；可眼下我还不能告诉你，因为我自己还没拿稳脚下的地基；再说，咱们踩着的本就是危险的地界，杰里，你我都是。喏，这儿再给你一枚克朗——头一枚，是谢你眼睛盯得紧——你可知道这一枚是谢你什么？”

“谢什么？”

“谢你把嘴闭严。在我开口对你说明白之前，昨夜的事你什么都别再记起——就是解释给你听，你也听不懂。你要明白，杰里小老弟，你还年幼得很，可若说机灵，‘机灵’二字就是给你写照的，错不了。你又机灵又亮堂——亮得就像那位海军上校外套上的纽扣——亮得就像造币厂里才铸出来的一千枚崭新基尼金币——你就是这么个人，一错不了！”

===Original===
“I hope so,” replied Jerk, stepping out of the front door. “I thinks I am!”

“God bless you!” said the Doctor, shutting the door and returning to Rash, who was waiting in the shuttered room by the light of the guttering candles.

===Chinese===
“但愿如此，”杰尔克跨出前门，应声道。“我看我就是！”

“上帝保佑你！”医生说着关上了门，回到拉什身边去；拉什正在那间闩死了窗板的屋子里等候，身旁的蜡烛已烧得残了，烛光摇曳将尽。

## XV. A Landed Proprietor Sets Up a Gallows Tree / 十五　一位地主竖起绞架树

===Original===
Back to the Ship and to duty went “Hangman Jerk,” with much to think over in his bullet head, and much to digest in his tight little stomach. To make head or tail of the Doctor’s remarkable manner was beyond him, so he dismissed it from his mind and instead fell to contemplating the two silver crowns: one payment for keeping his weather eye open—easily earned; the other—the schoolmaster’s safety—directly against his highest hopes; yes, a crown was poor payment for that, especially as it was now possible for himself to be the direct means of hanging his enemy.

Approaching the bar door, he paused, for he heard voices within, voices that he knew released him from work, the voices of Mrs. Waggetts and the pride of her life—the sexton Mipps.

===Chinese===
“刽子手杰尔克”回“船”酒馆复他的差使去了：一颗炮弹似的圆脑袋里，装满了要想的事；绷得紧紧的小肚子里，还装满了待消化的酒饭。要理出医生那副古怪做派的头绪来，实在超出了他的能耐，他便索性撇开不想，转而盘算起那两枚克朗银币（silver crowns）来：一枚是酬他睁大那双警醒的眼睛的——不费吹灰之力便挣到手；另一枚，是酬他保得学堂先生平安的——这桩差事偏偏正顶在他最大心愿的对头上；是啊，为着这个，一枚克朗银币的报酬未免太薄，何况如今，亲手吊死仇家的很可能就是他自己。

走到店堂门口，他停住了脚，因为听见里头有人说话；一听那嗓音，他便知道可以歇工了——那是瓦格茨太太的说话声，还有她平生顶得意的人物——教堂司事米普斯的说话声。

===Original===
Jerk knew exactly how the land lay with Mrs. Waggetts, and he was always wondering when (if ever) she would succeed in folding that queer little man within the safe bonds of matrimony. Now whatever Jerk’s failings may have been, he was loyal to his friends, and Mrs. Waggetts was not only his friend but his employer, and she had done him one or two very good turns. For one thing, she had given him a money box in which to save a portion of his weekly wage. That doesn’t sound a great deal on the surface, it is true, but her kindness had not ended there, as you shall see. Jerk’s teeth were not sweet, like those of most boys of his age; he never bought sweetmeats, barley sugar, and such child’s trash. No, when he wanted a pick-me-up it was a grown man’s pick-me-up that he indulged in—a pannikin of rum, a whiff of tobacco, and a long-shot spit at the china spittoon that stood in the front of the bar. These indulgences had no effect on his purse, for the cravings of the first two were easily satisfied from the bar store when nobody was looking, and the third he was at liberty to practise whenever he felt so disposed. And thus it was that, although but approaching thirteen years of age, he had through the good offices of the landlady and a systematic use of her money box already become a landed proprietor. When the landlady heard that Jerk wanted to spend his savings on such a very strange thing as land she had exclaimed in some surprise:

===Chinese===
瓦格茨太太那边的形势，杰尔克一清二楚；他一直在纳闷，她要到哪一天（倘若真有那么一天）才能把那个古怪的小个子男人稳稳当当拢进婚姻的保险绳结里。杰尔克毛病再多，待朋友却是一片忠心；瓦格茨太太不单是他的朋友，还是他的东家，况且着实照应过他一两回。头一桩，她给了他一只存钱罐，让他把每周工钱省下一份来存着。诚然，这话表面听来没什么了不起，可她的好处并没有到此为止，往下你便看得分明。杰尔克的牙齿不像他这年纪的大多数孩子那样嗜甜；糖果、麦芽糖，还有诸如此类娃娃才稀罕的废物，他一概不买。不，他要想提提神，享用的是大人的提神物——一小罐朗姆酒，一口烟草，再朝店堂前头那只瓷痰盂远远地啐上一口。这几样享受分文不费他的钱包：头两样的瘾，趁人不留意从店堂存货里解一解便得了；第三样嘛，只要他兴起，尽可随意操练。就这样，他虽然还差着几个月才满十三岁，却已全仗老板娘鼎力玉成，再加上对她那只存钱罐的按部就班的取用，当上了一名地主。老板娘听说杰尔克打算把积蓄花在地这么一样顶古怪的东西上，不由得有些吃惊，嚷道：

===Original===
“Lord bless the boy! Land? What can a boy of that age want with a plot of land?”

“The money’s good enough, ain’t it, ma’am? Very well, then, I wants land. A nice little bit of snug mud-bank where I can hide and learn about the Marsh. If I’ve a bit of mud wot’s all mine on Romney Marsh—well, I’ll be a Marshman, I’ll be, and it’s a Marshman proper I wants to be.”

So Mrs. Waggetts consented, and bought a plot for him situated about a mile and a half from the village and a rough half mile from the sea. As land, it was of no use in the commercial sense—in fact, the farmer had thought the landlady clean crazed to buy it, though the price was small enough as far as prices go on the Marsh. It was more mud than land, surrounded by two broad dykes that slowly oozed round to meet in a sluice channel. This was Jerk’s estate, measuring twelve by ten yards all told, and only solid in one spot near the centre, a patch of about ten square feet which formed a knobby mound surrounded by great bullrushes; but the mound was not such a small affair, for it rose high enough to top the loftiest rush, and that is quite a noticeable height on the flat of Romney Marsh. This mound was given by its owner the dignified name of Lookout Mountain, a name well deserved, for by sitting on the top of it upon the great stone which he had dragged from the seawall and carried a mile across the Marsh for the purpose, he could see from Dover Cliffs to Dungeness, and in the other direction the long line of hills which bind the Marsh inland, with old Limpne Castle frowning from the top. But Jerk wouldn’t have changed his stronghold for any other, Limpne Castle included; it suited him admirably. From it he studied the Marsh and the creatures therein: the great brown water-rat that came out in the evening to hunt in the rushes; the swift-winged dragonfly that could stand in midair stock still, as it seemed, to look at you; the myriad mosquitoes with their fantastic air dance, hunting in tribes along the sluggish waters; the tadpole who looped about in the water below; and more especially the flabby flap of the night-prowling bat who hung all day head downward from a decayed old tree trunk that was rotting on the opposite bank to Jerk’s estate. Now this same tree trunk had put ideas into young Jerk’s head. It was obviously no good to anyone, and yet Jerk found himself regretting that it had not lived and died upon his land, for it was shaped devilishly like a gallows tree, and if he could only erect a gallows tree upon the summit of Lookout Mountain he would be more than ever living up to his reputable name of Hangman Jerk. He half thought at one time of digging it up and replanting it on his own property, but when he had caught hold of a branch one day and it had crumbled away in his hand he considered that, although very nice and weird to behold, it wasn’t much use as a genuine gibbet, and a genuine gibbet he then and there resolved to possess. Now the silver crowns of Doctor Syn would buy the most glorious scaffold, a regular professional affair, fixed snug and firm in the ground, and capable of supporting the weight of a wriggling man. Mipps was the man to undertake the job, for he was a first-rate carpenter, and there was wood and to spare in the yard behind the coffin shop. Yes, if any man could supply him with a gibbet Mipps could; and there he was talking in the bar ready to hand, and here were the silver crowns in Jerk’s pocket. But to buy the gibbet and then to have to keep his mouth shut about the schoolmaster was no good. Mipps would never do the job for one crown, but for two Jerk thought he might. Well, he would see about that, and if he were unsuccessful, he must find a way of raising the money, and then, as soon as the apparatus was ready, he would get Rash condemned, and offer the authorities the loan of a brand-new gibbet. Oh, to watch the murderer swinging from the top of Lookout Mountain, right away on the lonely, windswept Marsh! That, indeed, was a glorious thought. Yes, he must come to terms with the undertaker at once—an undertaker now with a vengeance—Rash’s undertaker. But the little gentleman in question was talking to Mrs. Waggetts, so Jerry had to wait in honour bound, for he was staunch to his benefactress, and would not have interrupted for the world. The conversation going forward in the bar was carried on in earnest tones but low, and Jerk began to think that Mrs. Waggetts was at last drawing the sexton into a proposal of marriage, and his interest in this one-sided love affair made him crouch by the bar door in hopes of gathering up some scraps of the honeyed words. But the few disjointed words he did catch were more akin to passion than to love.

===Chinese===
“上帝保佑这孩子！地？他这么点年纪的娃娃，要一块地皮做什么使？”

“这钱是好好的钱，是不是，太太？很好，那我就要地。一小块舒舒服服的泥滩，好叫我藏起身来，把湿地的门道摸个透。只要罗姆尼湿地上有一小块泥巴归我名下——哼，我就算个湿地汉子了，我算定了，我要做的还是个地地道道的湿地汉子。”

于是瓦格茨太太依了他，替他买下一块地，离村子约莫一英里半，离海不下半英里。作为地，它按生意经来讲一无可取——说实在的，那农场主只道老板娘买下它准是疯了，尽管照湿地上的地价行情，这点价钱本也算不得什么。那地方与其说是地，不如说是泥，四面围着两条宽阔的沟渠，渠水慢悠悠地渗漾回旋，末了在一道水闸渠里汇合。这便是杰尔克的产业，统共十二码乘十码，只有近中央的一处是实的——约莫十平方英尺的一小块，隆成一个疙瘩似的土丘，四周环着高高的芦苇；不过这土丘可并非小玩意：它蹿得比最高的芦苇还高出一头，而在罗姆尼湿地这片平川上，这样的高度已相当惹眼了。这土丘被它的主人赐了一个威风堂堂的名字——瞭望山（Lookout Mountain），倒也名副其实：只消坐上丘顶那块大石头——那是他特意从海堤上拖下来、横穿湿地搬了一英里路去的——他便能从多佛悬崖一路望到邓杰内斯，朝另一头又能望见把湿地拦在内陆的那一道绵绵丘岭，古老的利姆讷城堡（Limpne Castle）正打岭顶上蹙着眉峰。可杰尔克这处山寨，拿哪座来换他也不肯，利姆讷城堡也不例外；这地方正对他的脾胃。他就在这上头研究湿地和湿地里的生灵：傍晚出来在芦苇丛里打食的褐色大水鼠；翅膀迅疾、看着就像能在半空里定住不动端详你的蜻蜓；成千上万跳着奇异空中舞、成群结队沿滞水巡猎的蚊蚋；在丘脚水洼里兜圈子游的蝌蚪；更有顶惹眼的，夜里出来游荡的蝙蝠那软塌塌的扑翅——它整天头朝下倒挂在一截朽烂的老树干上，那树干正在杰尔克产业对岸的岸坡上烂着。偏偏就是这截树干，往小杰尔克的脑袋里种下了念头。这东西明摆着对谁都没有用处，杰尔克却发现自己竟惋惜它没长在自家地皮上、没烂在自家地皮上：因为它那副模样，邪门得活脱脱一棵绞架树；只消能在瞭望山顶上立起这么一棵绞架树，他那“刽子手杰尔克”的名号便越发名实相副了。他一度还半真半假地想过把它挖起来，移栽到自家产业上去；可有一天他攥住一根枝子，枝子登时在他手里酥成了渣，他便断定：这玩意儿瞧着虽然又别致又瘆人，当一副真绞架却不大顶用；而当务之急是非有一副真绞架不可——他当场便拿定了这个主意。眼下辛医生那两枚克朗银币，正买得起一座顶气派的刑架：一副规规矩矩的行家货，牢牢实实扎进地里，吊得住一个挣扎打挺的大活人。这活儿交米普斯办最合适不过：他本是头一等的好木匠，棺材铺后院里木料又有的是。是啊，天底下若还有谁能给他置办一副绞架，那准是米普斯；而此人此刻就在店堂里说话，一伸手就够得着，克朗银币又揣在他杰尔克兜里。可是一面买绞架，一面还得为学堂先生的事守口如瓶，这买卖可不划算。一枚克朗银币，米普斯断不肯接这活；两枚么，杰尔克估摸他兴许肯。好吧，这事且去试探；倘若不成，他还得另想法子筹钱；总而言之，刑架一备好，他就要叫拉什定成死罪，再向官府出借一座崭新的绞架。哦，瞧着那凶手吊在瞭望山顶上荡来荡去——就在那片孤零零、任凭风吹雨打的湿地上！这才真是锦绣念头。对，他必须立刻同那位殡葬承办人谈妥条件——如今这一位，可是不折不扣的殡葬承办人——拉什的殡葬承办人。可我们说的这位小个子先生，这会儿正同瓦格茨太太说话，杰里只得讲体面地候着：他对女恩人一片赤诚，天塌下来也不肯去打岔。店堂里那场谈话，语气恳切，声音却压得低低的，杰尔克渐渐寻思：瓦格茨太太这回总该是把司事劝到求婚的门槛上了；他对这场单恋大感兴趣，便蹲伏在店堂门边，指望拾得几句甜言蜜语的渣儿。可他零星捉到的那几个字，与其说近于情爱，不如说近于激愤。

===Original===
“Alsace Lorraine—one bottle gone! Damn that captain’s soul!”

Yes, there was passion there—not love. “We know how to use the mist—they don’t.”

“It’s safe enough. Lots of it tonight—”

No, there was no vestige of love in that. And presently the conversation was terminated with the most uncomplimentary remark from the sexton.

“You can lay your old topknot, and throw in your face, that there’ll be a good haul out tonight, and a good haul in here,” saying which, with a knowing slap at his pocket, Mipps came hurriedly out of the bar door and fell all a-sprawl over the crouching body of young Jerk.

===Chinese===
“阿尔萨斯-洛林葡萄酒（Alsace Lorraine）——少了一瓶！咒那上校的魂下地狱！”

是啊，这里头有的是激愤——可不是情爱。“我们知道怎么使唤这海雾——他们不会。”

“稳当得很。今儿夜里雾有的是——”

不，这话里寻不出半点情爱的影子。不多时，这场谈话便以司事一句顶不中听的话收了场。

“你只管押上你那颗老脑袋，再饶上一张老脸：今儿晚上运出去的准是一大票，运进这儿来的也是一大票。”说罢，米普斯朝自己口袋心照不宣地一拍，匆匆夺门而出，一头绊在蹲伏着的小杰尔克身上，摔了个四仰八叉。

===Original===
“Why, in the name of all wot rots, can’t you tell me where you was?” cursed the sexton.

“’Cos I prefers to tell you what I wants,” replied young Jerk.

“A thrashin’?”

“A gallows!”

“Aye, that you do, if anyone did.”

===Chinese===
“哎，以一切烂朽之物的名义，你就不能说一声你在哪儿么？”司事咒骂道。

“因为我宁可告诉你我要什么，”小杰尔克回嘴道。

“一顿好打？”

“一座绞架！”

“可不是，天底下真有谁欠揍，头一个就是你。”

===Original===
“Will you make it for me, then?” said the boy.

“What do you mean?”

“What I says—will you make me one?”

“At a price.”

“And that is?”

===Chinese===
“那你肯替我造它一座么？”孩子说。

“你这是什么意思？”

“我就是那个意思——你肯不肯给我造一座？”

“得有价钱。”

“多少？”

===Original===
“Depends on the size. Wot do you want a gallows for now?”

“That don’t concern you,” returned Jerk. “You’ll have all you can do makin’ it, without askin’ questions.”

“And you’ll have all you can do, when it’s made, a-preventin’ me a-stringin’ you up on it, if I has any more o’ your impert’nence.”

But Jerry was in no way put out, and replied:

“If you don’t want to build my gallows, say so, and I’ll soon find some other cove wot does. Come, wot’s your price?”

===Chinese===
“那得看尺寸。你这会儿要绞架做什么使？”

“这就不消你操心，”杰尔克回道。“光把它造出来就够你忙活的，哪还有闲工夫问东问西。”

“等它造好了，你要是再多一句放肆话，你那点本事就全得使在拦着我把你吊上去了。”

可杰里半点不恼，回道：

“你不想接我这绞架的活儿，就直说，我立马能找着别的愿意干的角色。快说，你要什么价？”

===Original===
“And wot’s your game?”

“My business, not yourn,” said the boy. “But you’ll find as how yourn won’t improve by annoyin’ your employers.”

“Employers? And who might they be now?” said the sexton.

“Well, I’m a-tryin’ to be one,” said Jerk, jingling the coins about in his pocket to lend weight to his words. “What price for a gallows, eh?”

The jingle of coins always made the sexton think.

===Chinese===
“那你打的是什么算盘？”

“我的买卖，不劳你问，”孩子说。“不过你记着：惹恼了你的东家，你的买卖只会更糟。”

“东家？又是哪几位贵人大驾呢？”司事说。

“喏，我就正想当一位，”杰尔克说着，把兜里的银币摇得叮当乱响，好给自己的话添些分量。“一座绞架什么价，呃？”

银币一叮当，司事的脑筋就开动。

===Original===
“Wot size?” said he.

“Big enough and strong enough to hang a man on, of course, and allowin’ for a good foot or two of timber in the earth.”

The sexton scratched his head. “Well, I’m cursed!” he said.

“That’s nought to me,” replied Jerry. “Come on! Your price?”

“Well, say two crowns for making and one for fixin’.”

===Chinese===
“什么尺寸？”他说。

“自然是又大又结实，吊得住一个大活人，还得算上往土里埋一英尺上下的木料。”

司事挠了挠头皮。“真是见了鬼！”他说。

“那与我无干，”杰里答道。“快说！什么价？”

“这样吧，造工算两枚克朗银币，竖立费另加一枚。”

===Original===
“One for makin’ and one for fixin’,” said Jerk, holding them out.

“No!” said the sexton, eying the coins.

“Then hang the fixin’!” cried the boy, “for I’ll fix it myself. So it’s one for makin’ and the wood, ain’t it. Mister Sexton?”

“No, it’s two for makin’, and I lose on that.”

“Very well,” agreed Jerk desperately, handing over the money, “and please, Mister Sexton, make it now, ’cos I wants it quick.”

===Chinese===
“造工一枚克朗银币，竖立费一枚，”杰尔克说着把银币递了过去。

“不成！”司事瞅着那几枚银币说。

“那就让竖立费见鬼去！”孩子嚷道，“我自己来竖。那么，一枚克朗银币造工，木料你出，成了吧，司事先生？”

“不成，造工两枚克朗银币，这价钱我已经是赔本了。”

“好吧好吧，”杰尔克豁出去了，一边把钱交过去，“求你了，司事先生，这就动手造吧，我要得急。”

===Original===
So the bargain was struck there and then, and off they both set to the coffin shop to carry it out; and the gallows was made by nightfall and set up on Jerk’s property, the sexton carrying it there himself, digging the hole and fixing it up—a regular professional affair with a jangly rusty chain a-swing through the hook—and all this for the nominal price of two silver crowns, lately received by the purchaser from Dr. Syn.

“Ah!” cried Jerk, as they viewed the completed erection from the other side of the dyke; “ain’t it fust rate?”

“Slap up,” agreed the sexton.

“Quite strong, ain’t it?” inquired the owner anxiously, to which the sexton replied imperiously:

“It were Mipps as knocked it up, as you seed yourself; and when Mipps knocks up, you can lay it’s solid wot’s knocked,” saying which he turned and strode off toward the village, followed by Jerk.

===Chinese===
买卖就这样当场拍板，两人一齐动身去棺材铺兑现；天黑之前，绞架造成了，竖在杰尔克的地皮上，司事亲自扛了去，挖坑、立架——一副规规矩矩的行家货色，钩子上还荡着一根当啷作响的锈铁链——而这一切，只花了区区名义上的两枚克朗银币，那还是买主刚打辛医生手里领来的。

“啊！”杰尔克嚷道，这时两人正打沟渠对岸端详那座完工的架子；“真是头等的活儿，是不是？”

“顶呱呱，”司事表示赞成。

“够结实的吧？”地主焦灼地问。司事傲然答道：

“这是米普斯亲手敲出来的，你亲眼瞧见的；米普斯敲出来的东西，你只管押上脑袋，敲出来的是什么，什么就结实。”说罢，他转身大步往村子走去，杰尔克跟在后头。

===Original===
When they had gone about half a mile Jerk looked back and called to the sexton to do the same. Darkness was already creeping over the Marsh, but sharp and black against the skyline—no toy, but real, weird, and convincing—stood Jerk’s gibbet.

“What do you think of Lookout Mountain now?” sang out the boy.

“That you can better the name of it, Hangman Jerk. Why not call it Gallows Tree Hill?”

“Why, so I will!” cried the singular youngster. “It’s a good name, and so I will—and let’s hope as how the tree’ll bear fruit.”

“As how it won’t,” muttered the sexton.

===Chinese===
走出约莫半英里，杰尔克回过头去，喊司事也回头瞧。夜色已悄悄爬上湿地，可就在天际线上，衬得又清晰又乌黑的——不是玩具，是真物，又古怪，又逼真——立着杰尔克的绞架。

“如今你觉得‘瞭望山’这名字怎么样？”孩子高声喊道。

“依我看，这名字还可以改得更好，刽子手杰尔克。怎么不叫它‘绞架树山’（Gallows Tree Hill）？”

“嘿，就这么办！”这个古怪的小家伙嚷道。“好名字，我就用这个——但愿这棵‘树’将来结得出果子。”

“但愿结不出，”司事咕哝道。

===Original===
“But it will, you can lay to that.” Jerk could already picture the schoolmaster hanging there.

As they neared the village, with sudden fear Jerk said to the sexton:

“I suppose the smugglers won’t take my gibbet as a personal offence and knock it down?” But the wary Mipps disarmed his fears with:

“There ain’t no smugglers, for one thing; ’sides, if there was, how could they knock down wot’s knocked up so solid?”

“Well, dig it up, p’raps,” suggested Jerk, “’cos, Mister Sexton, it do catch the eye some wot, don’t it? Look, you can see it even from here, and it don’t look exactly pleasant, do it?”

===Chinese===
“可它准结得出，这你大可信。”杰尔克眼前已经映出学堂先生吊在上头的光景。

快到村子的时候，杰尔克忽地一阵害怕，对司事说：

“想来那些走私的不会把我这绞架当成存心跟他们过不去，把它掀翻吧？”可机警的米普斯一句话便解除了他的忧惧：

“头一条，压根儿没有什么走私的；再说了，就算有，敲打得这般结实的物件，他们怎么掀得倒？”

“那，兴许把它刨了，”杰尔克出主意，“因为，司事先生，这物件也着实有点扎眼，是不是？瞧，打这儿都望得见它，瞧着可不太叫人舒坦，是不是？”

===Original===
“Pleasant ain’t exactly the word, I agrees, but you needn’t worry yourself on that score. If them damned King’s men had put it up now, I don’t say as how it mightn’t get mobbed and knocked about a bit, ’cos them damned King’s men ain’t wot you might term popular favourites in the village, but as it weren’t, don’t you worry, for I’ll soon pass the word, young Jerry, as how it’s you wot owns it.”

“Thank you,” said Jerry. “They wouldn’t knock it over if you asked ’em not to, I’ll be bound.”

“Asked who not to?” demanded the sexton quickly.

“Why, any of ’em,” replied Jerk innocently: “Marshmen, smugglers, jack-o’-lanterns, demon riders, wot you will; for I’ll lay they’re all a-scared of Sexton Mipps, ain’t they?”

But Sexton Mipps was not to be caught by such dangerous flattery, and he replied:

===Chinese===
“‘舒坦’二字确实不贴切，这我承认，可这一层你犯不上操心。换要是那些该死的国王的人马竖起来的，我不敢说它不会招来一通围殴、挨上几家伙；因为那些该死的国王的人马，在村里算不上什么得人心的红人。可既然不是，你就放宽心，我这就把话递到，小杰里，就叫大伙儿知道这绞架是你的产业。”

“多谢，”杰里说。“只要你开口叫他们别动，他们断不敢推它，这个我敢打包票。”

“叫谁别动？”司事急忙追问。

“喏，随便哪个都成，”杰尔克装出一脸天真答道：“湿地汉子、走私的、鬼灯笼、魔骑，随你点；我敢打赌，他们个个都怕米普斯司事，是不是？”

可这等奉承话暗藏杀机，米普斯司事才不上这个钩，他答道：

===Original===
“There ain’t no such things as smugglers hereabouts, as I thinks I’ve already remarked; and as for demon riders, why, uncanny they be, and I holds no truck with ’em, thank the Lord. Folks wot has dealin’s with ’em has sold their souls for the bargain, and I ain’t a-goin’ to do that!”

“Bein’ such a very good and respectable Christian? Oh, no!” said Jerk winking.

“Why, certainly,” answered the sexton, “and might I ask wot you’re a-winkin’ about?”

“Nothin’—I was only thinkin’!”

“Wot about?”

===Chinese===
“这一带压根儿没有什么走私的，这话我想我方才已经交代过了；至于魔骑嘛，哼，那路货色邪门得很，谢天谢地，我可不同他们沾半点边。跟他们做买卖的，都是拿灵魂换去了好处，我可不干这号事！”

“瞧您这么一位虔诚可敬的基督徒，哪会呢？哦，当然不会！”杰尔克眨着眼说。

“那可不，”司事答道，“敢问方才你眨眼是几个意思？”

“没什么——我就是想点心事！”

“想什么？”

===Original===
“A dream—a nightmare I had last night, that’s all.”

“Wot about?” asked the sexton again.

“Nothin’ particular,” returned the boy casually.

They had now reached the coffin shop, so, thanking the sexton for his assistance, Jerk bade him good night.

“Where are you bound for now?” Mr. Mipps called after him.

===Chinese===
“一个梦——昨儿夜里做了个噩梦，就这么回事。”

“梦见什么？”司事又问。

“没什么特别的，”孩子随口应道。

这会儿两人已到棺材铺前，杰尔克谢过司事出力帮忙，道了晚安。

“这又上哪儿去？”米普斯先生在他背后喊道。

===Original===
“The vicarage.”

“Wot for?”

“To tell the vicar as how I’ve borrowed a crown off of him, that’s all!”

“Wot’s that?” cried the sexton, making as if to follow, but the boy waved him back with a fierce gesture.

“’Tain’t nothin’ to do with you. You’re paid, ain’t you? And it didn’t get stole from the poor-box, neither, so don’t you start a-worritin’.”

===Chinese===
“牧师宅。”

“去干什么？”

“去告诉牧师，我跟他借了一枚克朗银币，就这么回事！”

“你说什么？”司事嚷了起来，作势要跟上去，孩子却凶巴巴地一摆手把他挡住了。

“这跟你不相干。你的工钱到手了，不是么？再说那一枚又不是打施舍箱里偷的，你犯不上这就愁起来。”

===Original===
And thrusting his hands deep into his breeches pocket, Jerk set off for the vicarage to tell Doctor Syn that although he couldn’t accept the silver crown for holding his tongue, he had taken the liberty of borrowing it off him.

And in this way was the gibbet set up on Lookout Mountain, and the name changed to Gallows Tree Hill.

===Chinese===
杰尔克把两手往马裤兜里深深一插，动身往牧师宅去，他要告诉辛医生：那枚叫他闭嘴别言语的克朗银币，他不能收，可他已自作主张，把它借下了。

就这样，绞架竖上了瞭望山，山名也改成了绞架树山。

## XVI. The Schoolmaster's Suit / 十六　学堂先生的求婚

===Original===
It was now dark. Jerk passed through the cluster of quaint little houses that make up the one street of Dymchurch-under-the-wall, and so on to the vicarage. Just at the corner where the courthouse stands amid the great trees he heard singing, and recognized the voice and figure of Imogene. She was carrying a basket from the direction of the Ship and was probably bound, like himself, for the vicarage. But as she passed the courthouse she paused, and to Jerk’s astonishment felt among the ivy that grew around the old front door. There in a certain branch was a piece of paper, which she took from its hiding-place as if she had expected to find it. The message it contained she read by the light of the lantern that hung above the door, and then, thrusting it into the bosom of her rough dress, she went on toward the vicarage gate. But out from the shadows of the trees stepped a man, whom Jerk perceived to be the schoolmaster. Imogene hesitated when she saw him, for he was standing directly in her path, but when she tried to hurry past, Rash stopped her and spoke.

“So, Mistress, now that you have got your lover’s written promise from the ivy there, you think you can afford to pass by such a humble one as the schoolmaster, but you’re mistaken, and I’ll trouble you to show me that letter.”

===Chinese===
夜色已经黑透。杰尔克（Jerk）穿过那一簇古雅的小屋——它们凑成了海墙下的迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch-under-the-wall）仅有的那条街道——径直朝牧师宅（vicarage）走去。行到议事厅被大树环抱着的那个街角，他听见一阵歌声，听声音、看身形，便认出是伊莫金（Imogene）。她挎着一只篮子，从“船”酒馆那头过来，多半和自己一样，也是要上牧师宅去。可她经过议事厅时停住了脚，竟伸手去摸老前门四周丛生的常春藤——杰尔克看得惊诧不已。一根枝条里果然藏着一张纸，她把它从藏处取了出来，仿佛早料到会在那里寻见。她借着悬在门上方的灯笼那点光，把纸上的字句读了一遍，随即将它塞进粗布裙衫的怀里，继续朝牧师宅的院门走去。不料树影里踱出一个人来，杰尔克认得，正是学堂先生。伊莫金一见他便迟疑起来，因为他正正站在她的去路上；她想急忙绕过去，拉什（Rash）却拦住了她，开口说话。

“好啊，小姐，如今你从那常春藤里取到了情郎的亲笔许诺，就自以为可以不把学堂先生这等卑微人物放在眼里、扬长而过了？你可想错了。劳你的驾，把那封信拿出来给我瞧瞧。”

===Original===
The girl’s hand went involuntarily to her bosom, where the note in question was securely tucked away, and she answered back clear and straight: “No, Mister Rash, you’ve no right.”

“Right is might, Mistress, as you’ll find, and I think we shall be able to come to terms now. I want you to come along with me to the vicarage; Doctor Syn is there, and I’ve something to say before you both.”

“Let us go, then,” said Imogene, trying to pass.

“All in good time,” returned the schoolmaster, stopping her. “There’s no immediate hurry, I think, for the Doctor won’t come out of that shuttered room of his till morning, so we can afford to keep him waiting, and I’ve something to say to you first—alone.”

===Chinese===
姑娘的手不由自主地按向怀口，那张字条正稳稳地藏在里面。她回答得清脆爽利：“不行，拉什先生，你无权看。”

“有权便有势，小姐，你往后自会明白。我看，咱们如今可以谈条件了。我要你跟我一块儿上牧师宅去；辛医生（Doctor Syn）就在那儿，我有话要当着你我两人说。”

“那就走吧。”伊莫金说着，想从他身边绕过去。

“急什么。”学堂先生拦住她说。“依我看不必急着走，医生那间下了窗板的屋子，不到天亮他是不会出来的，让他等一等也不妨事。我先有话要对你说——单独说。”

===Original===
The girl tossed her head impatiently, as if she knew what was coming, but Rash continued:

“A few weeks back I asked you to marry me—I, the esteemed schoolmaster, asked you, the daughter of a criminal; you, whose father was a proved murderer, a dirty pirate hanged publicly at Rye for a filthy tavern crime; you who were born in a Raratonga drinking hell, some half-caste native girl’s brat! Ecod! it’s laughable! I offered to make you respectable and put your banns up in the church, and you refused. Now I know why. You think because that young fool Cobtree is pleased to admire you, that you will catch him in your toils, do you? You’re a clever one, ain’t you? I dare swear that sooner or later you’d succeed in getting hold of him—let the young idiot ruin you, eh? Then make a virtuous song about it to the squire, and a settlement to keep your mouth shut, perhaps.”

===Chinese===
姑娘不耐烦地把头一扬，仿佛早知道接下来会是什么话，拉什却继续说了下去：

“几个礼拜以前，我向你求过婚——我，德高望重的学堂先生，向你，一个罪犯的女儿求婚；你，你父亲是个铁证如山的杀人凶犯，是个肮脏的海盗，在拉伊（Rye）因一桩下流的酒馆罪行当众绞死；你，你生在拉罗汤加（Raratonga）的一座酒客魔窟里，是哪个混血土著丫头养下的崽子！老天在上，笑死人了！我好心要让你变得体面，把你们的婚事预告（banns）贴上教堂，你竟拒绝了。如今我可明白缘故了。你只当科布特（Cobtree）那年轻的蠢货乐意爱慕你，你就能把他收进你的罗网，是不是？你可真够伶俐的，不是么？我敢打包票，你迟早能把这小子弄到手——放任这年轻的傻子毁了你，呃？回头再跑到乡绅面前，把自己装点成个贞洁女子哭诉一番，说不定还能讨得一笔封口的安家费呢。”

===Original===
“Beast!” cried the girl, and she struck him sideways across the mouth with her clenched hand.

“Hello!” thought Jerk, crouching in the bushes, “here’s another one having a go at him; well, the more the merrier, so long as I’m the last.”

The schoolmaster recoiled, trying to look as if the stinging blow had not hurt, but the blood was flowing from his lip and from the hand of the girl as well.

===Chinese===
“畜生！”姑娘喝道，攥紧拳头，一拳横扫在他的嘴上。

“哟嗬！”杰尔克蹲在灌木丛里暗想，“又来一个跟他交手的；好哇，人越多越热闹，只要最后收拾他的那个是我便成。”

学堂先生踉跄后退，竭力装出这一记火辣辣的拳头并不疼痛的样子，可血已从他的嘴唇上淌了下来，姑娘的手上也见了血。

===Original===
“So that’s it, is it?” he sniggered, “a real love match, p’haps? The squire’s consent, the wedding bells, and live happily ever after, eh? Ecod! my lady, I think not. Rash is your man, see? and lucky you are to get him; you whose father’s gibbet chains are still swinging in Rye.”

“And yours are swinging a bit nearer than that!” said Jerry Jerk to himself.

“You leave my father out of it,” went on the girl, “for from all I’ve heard of him he was a better man than you, and he was fond of me, too; so it’s lucky for you he’s not here to hear you speaking bad of his child.”

“You know nothing about him—he was a drunken rascal!”

===Chinese===
“原来如此，是不是？”他吃吃窃笑，“一桩两情相悦的真姻缘，兴许？乡绅点头，教堂钟响，从此幸福到老，呃？老天！我的大小姐，我看未必。拉什才是你该嫁的人，明白么？你能捞着他，是你的造化；也不看看你自己是什么人——你父亲的绞架铁链这会儿还在拉伊荡着呢。”

“可你自己那几条，晃荡的地方比那还要近得多喽！”杰里·杰尔克心里说道。

“不许你糟践我父亲，”姑娘接着说，“我听来的种种，都说他是个比你强的人；而且他也疼我。所以算你走运，他不在这里，听不见你糟践他的孩子。”

“你对他一无所知——他就是个醉鬼流氓！”

===Original===
“Doctor Syn knew him well, and he’s told me things. A rough man he was, certain, and none rougher, reckless, too, and brave, a lawbreaker on land as well as sea, pitiless to his enemies, staunch to his friends, but contemptible he never was; and so, Mister Rash, you can afford to respect him, and I say again that I wish he were here to make you.”

“Shouldn’t care if he was,” replied the schoolmaster, “for there’s always the law to look after a man.”

“So there is,” chuckled Jerk, “and that you’ll find.”

===Chinese===
“辛医生深知他的为人，他对我说过他的事。他是个粗人，这自然，还粗得没边儿；也鲁莽，也有胆量；陆上海上都干犯王法；对敌人毫不留情，对朋友却生死不移；可他从来不是个卑鄙小人。所以，拉什先生，你也该拿得出对他的一分敬意。我再说一遍，但愿他此刻就在这里，好来治一治你。”

“他就是在这儿，我也不怕，”学堂先生答道，“反正有法律护着人呢。”

“法律是有的，”杰尔克咯咯笑道，“这你迟早会领教。”

===Original===
“Bah! what’s the good of haggling and squabbling?” said Mr. Rash. “You’re mine, or you’ll have to bear the consequences.”

“And that is?” asked the girl defiantly.

“The rope for your friends when I turn King’s evidence.”

“You wouldn’t dare, you coward, for you’d be hanging yourself as well.”

“King’s evidence will cover me all square.”

===Chinese===
“呸！讨价还价、吵吵嚷嚷有什么用处？”拉什先生说，“你跟了我，不然就得自担后果。”

“后果是什么？”姑娘挑衅地问道。

“等我出面给国王作证（King’s evidence），你那些朋友就得挨绞索。”

“你不敢，你这胆小鬼，因为那样一来，你也会把自己一块儿送上绞架。”

“王室的证词会把我保得平平妥妥。”

===Original===
“So you’re determined to turn it, are you?”

“I am, unless you change your mind.”

The girl didn’t reply to that, so Mr. Rash, thinking that he was making an advance, continued:

“Think, Imogene—this Cobtree fellow will be packed off to London in a month or so, and from there on to Oxford; and after a university career of drinking, gambling, and loose living, with precious little learning, he’ll settle down to the gentleman’s life, marry some person of quality, and you—eh? what of you, then?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你是打定主意要出首作证了，是不是？”

“我是打定主意了，除非你回心转意。”

姑娘没有答话。拉什先生只道自己又进得一步，便接着说下去：

“想想吧，伊莫金——科布特这小子不出一个月就要被打发上伦敦，再从那里去牛津；在大学里喝喝酒、赌赌博、过放荡日子，学问却没长进几分，回头便安安稳稳做起绅士人家，娶一位名门闺秀——而你呢，呃？到那时你又算什么？”

===Original===
“I earn my living now, don’t I?” replied the girl. “Well, what’s to prevent me going on the same?”

“Don’t you want to marry?” went on the schoolmaster. “Don’t you want a house of your own? Don’t you want to be the envy of all the girls in the village?”

“Not at the price of my happiness; and, besides, I’m not so sure that I do want all those things so desperate. I’m afraid the wife of Mister Rash would be too genteel a job for me.”

“Oh, I’d soon educate you up to that,” returned the schoolmaster, looking pleased.

===Chinese===
“我现在就自己挣饭吃，不是么？”姑娘答道，“那好，往后照旧过下去，又有什么拦得住我？”

“你不想嫁人么？”学堂先生又说，“你不想有幢自己的房子么？你不想叫全村的姑娘都眼红么？”

“拿我的幸福作价钱，不干；再说，那些东西我未必就稀罕到那个地步。只怕给拉什先生当太太这份活计，对我说来太斯文了些。”

“哦，这个我可以很快把你调教出来。”学堂先生答道，一脸得意。

===Original===
“It ’ud be a great nuisance to both of us, wouldn’t it?”

“I shouldn’t mind—it would be a pleasant business making a respectable woman of you, Imogene. You see, you’re not common like these village girls, and that’s what attracts me; otherwise, it might have been better for me to have fixed my choice on one of them: one that hasn’t a bad mark against her, so to speak. But I don’t mind what folk say. I suppose they’ll talk a bit and laugh behind my back. Well, let ’em, say I. I don’t care, because I want you.”

===Chinese===
“那咱们两个都得大受其累，可不是么？”

“这个我不在乎——把你调教成个体面女人，倒是桩乐在其中的营生呢，伊莫金。你要明白，你不像村里这些姑娘那样庸常，这正是吸引我的地方；要不然，我倒不如把心思放在她们中间哪一个身上：挑一个身上没背着污点的，可以这么说。可人家说什么，我全不在乎。我料想他们会议论几句，背地里笑话我。哼，随他们去，我就是这句话。我不在乎，因为我要的是你。”

===Original===
“Then it’s a pity that I’m not the same way of thinking, isn’t it?”

“What do you mean?”

“That I wouldn’t marry you—no, not though you got the whole village the rope!”

“You ungrateful wretch, not after all they’ve done for you?”

“You’re not the sort of party to talk to others about being ungrateful, are you now?”

“I wasn’t born of jail folk.”

===Chinese===
“那可惜得很，我可不这么想，是不是？”

“你这是什么意思？”

“意思是我不会嫁给你——不嫁，哪怕你把全村人都送上绞索，我也不嫁！”

“你这忘恩负义的贱货，人家待你那么好，你还不肯？”

“忘恩负义这四个字，还轮不到你来说别人，是不是？”

“我可不是牢狱人家生的。”

===Original===
“No; and you can hope your children, if you’re ever cursed with any, will be able to say the same, for I doubt it very greatly, Mister Schoolmaster. And as to your threats, I set no store on them, for from my heart I despise you; I despise you because you would be willing to betray your fellows, but I despise you more because I know you are too great a coward to do it.”

“We shall see,” said the schoolmaster, “for who’s to stop me?”

“Parson Syn,” answered the girl. “Parsons can bear all manner of secrets and not betray them. That’s their business, and Doctor Syn’s a good man, so I’ll tell him everything, and in his wisdom he’ll find a means of checking your contemptible scheme.”

===Chinese===
“不错；你也只能巴望你的孩子——倘若你命中真受得住那份诅咒、生得出一男半女的话——将来能说同样的话；对此我可大大地怀疑，学堂先生。至于你的威胁，我半个子儿也不放在心上，因为我打心底里鄙视你；我鄙视你，因为你竟肯出卖自己的同伙；我更鄙视你，因为我知道你是个天大的懦夫，根本不敢。”

“走着瞧吧，”学堂先生说，“谁能拦得住我？”

“辛牧师。”姑娘答道，“牧师什么秘密都能守在肚子里，不会泄露，那是他们的本分。辛医生是个好人，我要把一切都告诉他，以他的智慧，自会想出法子来挫败你这套卑鄙的把戏。”

===Original===
“That shows how little you know about things, Mistress Ignoramous; for it’s that very same good man, Doctor Syn, who is going to read out your banns on this next Sabbath as ever is, and it’s Rash who is going to make him, and if you won’t come along with me to church, well, I’ll threaten other parties in this little place who’ll help me to make you. Folk are none too anxious to be exposed these days with King’s men in the village, and so you’ll see—” The schoolmaster stopped talking suddenly.

===Chinese===
“这说明你对世事有多无知，无知小姐（Mistress Ignoramous）；因为就在下一个主日，当众宣读你们婚事预告的，恰恰就是那位好人辛医生，而逼他就范的，正是拉什我。你若不肯跟我上教堂去，哼，这小小地方还有别的人家，我一吓唬，他们就会帮我把你弄到教堂去。如今国王的人马就驻在村里，这地方上的人谁也不乐意被抖搂出来——你瞧着吧——”学堂先生的话头突然打住了。

## XVII. The Doctor Sings a Song / 十七　辛医生唱起一支歌

===Original===
Now, although Jerry had employed all his auditory faculties for the overhearing of this conversation, he had unconsciously listened to something else: a slight noise that now and again came from the direction of the vicarage, a small, whirring noise, the kind of noise that he had heard in Mipps’s coffin shop when a tool was working its way through a piece of wood—yes, a whirring noise with an occasional squeak to it.

He hadn’t bothered to ask himself what it was; he had just gone on hearing it, that’s all. But now another noise arose in the night that not only claimed his immediate attention but made him feel cold all over. It had the same effect upon Mr. Rash, for he stopped talking suddenly and gripped the post of the gate with one hand and with the other pulled Imogene roughly into the denser black of the bushes; and then the noise grew louder and louder. What at first could only be described as a gibbering moan rose into shriek after shriek of mortal terror: a man’s voice, a man scared out of all knowledge; and then over the gate leaped a dark form, agile and quick, that went bounding away through the ghostly churchyard. There was something familiar in that figure to Jerk. He had seen it almost from the same spot the night before. It was the man with the yellow face. The schoolmaster came out from the bushes, followed by Imogene. Quickly they went through the gate and toward the vicarage, and silently Jerk followed, with his heart thumping loud against his ribs; for although the echoes of those drum-cracking shrieks still vibrated in his ears, the gibbering moans still continued.

===Chinese===
话说，杰里（Jerry）方才虽把全副的听觉都拿去偷听这番谈话，却不知不觉还听到了别的动静：牧师宅（vicarage）那边不时传来一丝轻微的声响，一种细细的呜呜声，就像他在米普斯（Mipps）的棺材铺里听见的、工具一点点啃进木料时的那种动静——对，就是那种呜呜声，偶尔还夹着一声吱嘎。

他倒没费神去想那是什么；只是一直听着罢了，如此而已。可这会儿，夜地里又起了一种声响，不但立刻攫住了他的注意，还叫他浑身上下都起了寒意。拉什（Rash）先生也是一样：他话头猛地收住，一只手扒住院门的门柱，另一只手把伊莫金（Imogene）粗暴地拽进灌木丛更浓的黑影里；接着那声响越来越响。起初只能算是一阵语无伦次的呻吟，随即升成一声接一声、吓得人魂飞魄散的尖叫：是个男人的嗓音，一个吓得神智尽失的男人；紧接着，一条黑影敏捷利落地翻过院门，一路蹦跳着穿过阴气森森的教堂墓地跑远了。那身影在杰尔克眼里有几分眼熟。前一晚，他几乎就在同一地点见过它。正是那个黄脸汉子（the man with the yellow face）。学堂先生从灌木丛里钻出来，伊莫金跟在后面。两人急急穿过院门，朝牧师宅奔去；杰尔克不声不响地跟上，心口咚咚地撞着肋骨；因为尽管那几声震得耳鼓欲裂的尖叫，余音还在他耳边嗡嗡打颤，那语无伦次的呻吟却仍在继续。

===Original===
To the back of the house went the girl and the schoolmaster, and to the front went Jerk. It was all dark—indeed no lights were showing from any of the rooms but one, and that was the Doctor’s sitting-room with the shutters still close fastened; but a jagged little hole in the corner of one of the shutters sent a shaft of yellow candlelight straight out into the blackness. Yes, the gibbering moaning was coming from the Doctor’s room. Jerk crossed a bed of flowers and a gravel path and applied his eye to the jagged hole in the shutter. This little hole accounted for the whirring and squeaking that he had just heard, for it was newly cut, and Jerk put his hand upon several little pieces of split wood that had fallen upon the outer sill. It was plain that the awful apparition he had just seen had been looking into the room. He had evidently made the hole for the purpose, and made it with that awful weapon he carried, that same harpoon over which so much talk had been expended at the courthouse inquiry. Now the shutter, being an outside shutter, backed right against the lead-rimmed glass casement, and thus it was that Jerk had to wait for a few considerable seconds before seeing plainly anything in the room, for the candlelight flickered and danced upon the glass. But the very second he had put his eye to the hole the moans within the room steadily rose, and Jerk’s thumping heart increased its already unnatural pace, for he expected the loud shrieks to follow, though he could not understand their motive. But soon his eye got accustomed to the light, and one thing in the room became visible, the form of Doctor Syn. He was sitting in a high-backed chair in the centre of the room, gripping the oaken arms with his long, white fingers, and upon his face was a look of indescribable horror: his neck being stretched up alert and straight, his eyes dilated to a most disproportionate stare, glazed and terrible; his hair unkempt, and his thin legs pressing hard against the floor.

===Chinese===
姑娘和学堂先生绕向宅子后头，杰尔克则奔向宅前。四下里一片漆黑——果然各屋都没有灯火，只有一处例外，那便是医生的起居室，窗板依旧关得严严实实；可其中一块窗板的角上有个毛糙糙的小洞，一道黄色烛光正从洞里笔直射进黑夜。不错，那语无伦次的呻吟正是打医生屋里传出来的。杰尔克跨过一方花圃、一条砾石小径，把眼睛凑到窗板的破洞上。这个小洞恰好解释了他方才听见的那种呜呜声和吱嘎声，因为洞是新钻出来的，外窗台上还散落着几小片崩裂的木头，杰尔克伸手便摸着了。明摆着，他方才撞见的那尊可怕鬼影，正是往屋里窥探来着。那洞分明就是他为了这个目的弄出来的，而且用的是他随身那件可怕的凶器——正是议事厅查讯上众人费过那么多口舌议论的那柄鱼叉。那窗板是装在外头的，背面紧贴着铅条镶边的玻璃窗，因此杰尔克足足等了好几秒钟，才把屋里的情形看个分明，因为烛光在玻璃上闪动跳荡。可他的眼睛刚凑上洞口，屋里的呻吟便一阵紧似一阵地高起来，杰尔克那颗擂鼓的心也跳得愈发失常，他只怕跟着又是一场震天尖叫，虽说琢磨不透这又是为了什么缘故。不多时，他的眼睛习惯了光亮，屋里有一件东西显露出来——那是辛医生（Doctor Syn）的身形。他端坐在屋子正中一把高背椅里，又长又白的手指紧扣着橡木扶手，脸上是一副难以名状的恐怖神情：脖颈直挺挺地伸着，绷得警觉而僵直；两眼瞪得溜圆，大得全不成比例，呆滞而骇人；头发蓬乱，两条细腿死死抵住地板。

===Original===
But his mouth was neither set nor rigid, like the rest of his members—his mouth was loose and hanging open—such a mouth as the madman carries; and from it was coming that inarticulate gibber, that gibbering moan that had arrested the hearing of Jerry Jerk. Straight at the shutter stared the demented Doctor; straight into Jerk’s eye at the jagged hole, and suddenly his hand shot out over the table; he picked up the great plated candelabra, and hurled it, lighted candles and all, full at the window. Jerk started back to the rattle of glass, and at the same time a heavy hand fell upon his shoulder, and another was passed over his mouth, while a familiar voice whispered in his ear: “For God’s sake be quiet!” It was the captain, and he stood holding the boy tightly, keeping his eye on the jagged hole, and with something approaching terror upon his strong face. It was dark now, of course, for there was no light in the house, but presently Jerk and the captain heard low, frightened voices, and a light showed suddenly through the hole. The captain stooped and put his eye to it. Yes, the door of the Doctor’s sitting-room was opening, and Imogene and the schoolmaster came into the room. Imogene came first, with a lighted candle held high above her head.

===Chinese===
可他的嘴却不似身上其余各处那样咬紧僵直——他的嘴松垮垮地张着——正是疯子才有的那副嘴；那含混不清的咕哝，那阵攫住了杰里·杰尔克耳朵的呻吟，正是打这张嘴里发出来的。疯了心的医生直勾勾瞪着窗板；直勾勾瞪着破洞后面杰尔克的眼睛，忽然他的手越过桌面猛地伸出，抓起那座镀银的大枝形烛台，连烛带火整个儿朝窗户掷来。玻璃哗啦一响，杰尔克惊得后缩；就在这时，一只沉重的大手落在他肩上，另一只手捂住了他的嘴，一个熟悉的嗓音在耳边低语：“看在上帝的分上，别出声！”正是上校。他紧紧按住这孩子，眼睛盯住那个破洞，刚毅的脸上泛起几分近乎恐惧的神色。屋里这会儿自然一片漆黑，因为灯已经没有了；可是不多时，杰尔克和上校听见低低的、带着惊惶的话音，一点亮光忽然透过破洞现了出来。上校俯下身，把眼睛凑了上去。不错，医生起居室的门正在打开，伊莫金和学堂先生走进了屋子。伊莫金走在前头，一支点燃的蜡烛高高举过头顶。

===Original===
The Doctor was now kneeling on the floor straight up. He had a black bottle in his hand; the same rum bottle from which he had treated Jerk that very day. He seemed to recognize Imogene, for he smiled as she entered, smiled as he slowly raised the bottle and tilted the contents, neat and raw, down his vibrating throat. And then he saw the schoolmaster. His upper lip twitched, curled, and rose, disclosing his white upper teeth; his underlip stretched down and showed his lower teeth, shining white, that glistened underneath the bottle’s neck. There was a snap and a quick crunching sound. The captain gasped for breath, for Doctor Syn had bitten through the glass neck, and seized the bottle by the broken end. Slowly he dragged one leg from the kneeling position and pushed it out before him; slowly he fixed his other foot like a firm spring behind him. Terrified, Mr. Rash sprang back against the wall, with the blood still trickling from his cut lip, and motionless stood the girl Imogene, with the candle held above her head. Syn was in position to spring. Rash was waiting to be seized, and nothing moved in the room save the slowly oozing blood on the schoolmaster’s lip, vivid against the pale lantern jaw, and the blood and ground glass that glistened in a saliva stream that hung from the cleric’s mouth. Nothing else moved at all, except perhaps the light shed by the flickering candle, which danced shadows of the two weird men upon the whitewashed wall. And then with a hissing sound Syn made a leap, swinging the bottle as he did so, and bringing it down with a sickening crash on the white face before him. Down went Rash, senseless, blinded with blood and the shivered glass. Then Syn laughed, and sang at the top of his voice:

“Here’s to the feet wot have walked the plank,
					

					Yo ho! for the dead man’s throttle.
					

					And here’s to the corpses floating round in the tank,
					

					And the dead man’s teeth in the bottle.”

===Chinese===
这会儿，医生直挺挺地跪在地板上。他手里拿着一只黑瓶子；正是那天他请杰尔克喝过的那只朗姆酒瓶。他似乎认得伊莫金，因为她进屋时他笑了；他一边笑，一边慢慢举起瓶子，把里面的酒原汁原浆、不兑一滴水地倒进自己颤动不止的喉咙。接着，他看见了学堂先生。他的上嘴唇抽搐起来，蜷曲着向上掀翻，露出白白的上牙；下嘴唇向下扯开，露出下牙，白得发亮，在瓶颈底下闪闪生光。咔的一声脆响，跟着是一阵急促的咯吱碎裂声。上校倒抽了一口气，因为辛医生竟把玻璃瓶颈一口咬穿，攥住了瓶子的断口。他慢慢地把一条腿从跪姿里抽出，伸向身前；又慢慢把另一只脚像一根绷紧的弹簧似的拄在身后。拉什先生吓得魂飞魄散，蹦起来退到墙边，割破的嘴唇还在往下渗血；姑娘伊莫金却一动不动地站着，蜡烛高举过头。辛已摆好了扑击的架势。拉什等着被他扑住；屋子里再没有一样东西在动，只有学堂先生嘴唇上缓缓沁出的血——衬着那灰白瘦削的长下巴，红得刺眼——还有打那位神职人员嘴里垂下的一缕口涎，血珠和玻璃碴子在其中闪闪发亮。除此之外，一切纹丝不动，或许只有那摇曳烛光洒下的亮光是个例外，它把两个怪人的影子投在粉墙上舞动。就在这时，辛嘶的一声扑了出去，抡着瓶子，朝面前那张白脸狠狠砸下，砸出一声令人作呕的碎裂巨响。拉什栽倒了，不省人事，血和碎玻璃糊住了双眼。这时辛放声大笑，扯开嗓子唱了起来：

“这一杯，敬那走过跳板的脚板，
					

					哟嗬！为那死人的扼喉。
					

					再一杯，敬那水柜里漂着的浮尸，
					

					还有那死人的牙齿，留在瓶中。”

===Original===
And as he sang he danced, and stamped the senseless face beneath his feet; and then he sang again, roaring new words to the eternal old tune:

“A pound of gunshot tied to his feet,
					

					And a ragged bit of sail for a winding sheet;
					

					Then out to the sharks with a horrible splash,
					

					And that’s the end of Mr. Rash.”

And with diabolical glee he leaped again, and landed with both feet upon the victim’s face.

===Chinese===
他一面唱，一面手舞足蹈，把那张不省人事的脸踩在脚下；接着他又唱起来，用那支万古不变的老调子，吼出一些新词：

“一磅铅砂绑在他的脚上，
					

					一块破帆权当裹尸的衣裳；
					

					扑通一声可怕，抛出舷外去喂鲨鱼，
					

					拉什先生就此一命呜呼。”

他怀着恶魔般的欢喜，又是一跃，双脚齐齐落在了那受害者的脸上。

===Original===
All this time the girl stood still. Like a statue she stood, with the candle high above her head; and the terrible cleric went on with the song: new words, but still a corruption of the same old tune, which he roared and screamed in the very whirlwind of his uncontrolled madness:

“And all that isn’t ripped by the sharks outside
					

					Stands up again upon its feet upon the running tide.”

Taking the prostrate body, he lifted it on to its feet and leered into its face; then letting go of it, he watched it fall and collapse in a heap.

“And it kept a-bowing gently and a-looking with surprise
					

					At the little crabs a-scrambling from the sockets of its eyes.”

===Chinese===
这工夫，姑娘始终站着没动。她像一尊石像似的立在那里，蜡烛高举过顶；那可怕的神职人员却唱个不停：词是新的，仍旧是那支老调子的翻版，他在自己那阵失控疯狂的旋风中心，又吼又尖叫：

“凡没叫外面的鲨鱼撕碎的残骸，
					

					都会踏着奔流的潮水，重新站起身来。”

他抓起那具倒在地上的尸身，把它扶立起来，凑着脸狎邪地端详；随后一撒手，看着它扑通栽倒，瘫作一堆。

“它一个劲儿温柔地鞠着躬，诧异地四下张望，
					

					看那些小螃蟹，正从它的眼窝里爬出，慌慌张张。”

===Original===
The captain then shouted, shouted at the top of his voice, and tore at the fast, firm shutter. The song ceased in the room. The light once more went out of the jagged hole, and there was the noise of a falling body. Probably the girl had fainted. The shutters were strong and wouldn’t give.

“The back door!” shrieked the terrified Jerry. “The back door is open!” And around to the back rushed the captain, followed by the boy. And as he ran he blew three shrill calls upon a silver whistle that he carried on a chain. The whistle was answered with another, and before the captain had found and opened the back door, the captain’s bo’sun had appeared from the bushes, followed by a strong party of the King’s men. The bo’sun made a light from his tinder box, and as they were finding a candle in the back kitchen they could hear someone moving about in the sitting-room.

===Chinese===
上校这时喊叫起来，扯开嗓子拼命喊叫，一面死命去扯那关得又紧又牢的窗板。屋里的歌声停了。破洞里的亮光再一次熄灭，跟着传来一具身子倒地的响动。多半是姑娘晕过去了。窗板结实得很，怎么弄也弄不开。

“后门！”吓坏了的杰里尖叫道，“后门开着呢！”上校飞奔着绕向宅子后头，孩子紧随其后。他一面奔跑，一面把拴在链子上随身带着的一只银哨子吹出三声尖利的哨音。哨音得到了另一声哨音的应答；还没等上校找到后门、把它打开，上校的水手长（bo’sun）已经带着一队人强马壮的国王的人马，从灌木丛里赶到了。水手长用火绒盒打起亮来；他们正在后灶间里寻蜡烛，便听见起居室里有人来回走动的声响。

## XVIII. Behind the Shutters / 十八　窗板之后

===Original===
“Thank God somebody’s brought a light, for I don’t know what hasn’t happened here. Ah, Captain, it’s you, is it?” The speaker was Doctor Syn—he was calmly kneeling over the form of Mr. Rash. He had, in fact, propped his head upon his knee and was dabbing the bleeding face with his clean handkerchief.

“Just get the brandy bottle out of that corner cupboard, will you, my man?” he said to the bo’sun. “The girl there has fainted. Nothing serious, just sheer fright.”

The bo’sun did as he was ordered, and Imogene was quickly restored to consciousness.

The captain for the most part just stared at Syn and said nothing. Suddenly he passed his hand over his brow and wiped away the great beads of perspiration that had gathered there; then taking the brandy bottle from the bo’sun’s hand he took a long pull, and with a sigh sat down in the armchair, still staring at Doctor Syn with unconcealed amazement.

===Chinese===
“谢天谢地，总算有人端了灯来，真不晓得这儿方才闹出了些什么名堂。啊，上校，是你呀，是不是？”说话的正是辛医生（Doctor Syn）——他正从容不迫地跪在拉什先生（Mr. Rash）身边，其实已把那颗脑袋托在自己的膝上，拿一块干净手帕轻轻揩着那张流血的脸。

“劳驾把墙角橱柜里的白兰地酒瓶取出来，好不好，伙计？”他对水手长说。“那边那位姑娘晕过去了。不碍事，纯粹是吓的。”

水手长依言照办，伊莫金（Imogene）很快便醒了过来。

上校大半晌只是直勾勾地望着辛，一声不吭。忽然，他抬手抹过额头，揩去聚在那上头的一颗颗大汗珠；跟着从水手长手里接过白兰地酒瓶，足足灌下一大口，叹了口气，坐进扶手椅里，仍旧直望着辛医生，满脸毫不掩饰的惊愕。

===Original===
“Feeling a bit squeamish, Captain?” said the latter, smiling. “You’re right, it’s an ugly sight. More blood than necessary, though. Merely flesh cuts. Bruised a bit, too! Help yourself to brandy. Good evening, Jerry; pleased to see you. Here’s your poor schoolmaster got hurt. Feeling better, Captain? That’s good. The sight of blood does turn one up. Was it Hannibal or Hamilcar who never could reconcile himself to the sight of blood? I forget. Some great general it was, though. The girl here is the same. Better, Imogene? Surely it was Hannibal, wasn’t it?”

“I am sure I don’t know, or care,” thundered the captain, standing up and turning desperately on the bo’sun. “Job Mallet, what in hell’s name is all this business? I’m dazed.”

===Chinese===
“觉得有点儿发恶心吧，上校？”辛医生笑吟吟地说。“你没说错，这景象是难看。不过血淌得比要紧的多。不过是些皮肉伤。身上也磕碰了几处！白兰地请自便。晚上好，杰里；见到你真高兴。瞧，你们那位可怜的学堂先生挂了彩。好受些了吗，上校？那就好。见血这玩意儿实在叫人反胃。当年那位见了血就始终不能习惯的，是汉尼拔（Hannibal）还是哈米尔卡（Hamilcar）来着？我忘了。不过总归是哪位伟大的将军。这儿这位姑娘也是一样。好些了吗，伊莫金？准是汉尼拔，错不了吧？”

“我压根儿不知道，也不想稀罕知道，”上校吼道，霍地站起身，发狂似的转身冲水手长发作起来。“乔布·马利特（Job Mallet），这满屋的乱子究竟搞的什么鬼名堂？我都晕头转向了。”

===Original===
But Doctor Syn went on speaking in his usual collected tones: “It’s all very horrible, I grant, but there’s no mystery, I assure you. We were all three chatting here quite pleasantly, when in leaps that mulatto of yours, attacks my friend the schoolmaster and all but kills him. I picked up a bottle and landed the brute a crack over the head. The bottle broke, and the madman turned on me, clapped a bit of broken glass in my mouth, which I expect is cut about a bit, and got away. I asked the girl to hold the light, and when she saw the schoolmaster’s face, why, over she went, candle and all, into a dead faint. Never saw such a thing in my life, but I tell you this, Captain: it’s your bounden duty to get hold of that maniac and string him up to the nearest tree, for there’s not a man, woman, or child safe while he’s free.”

Then Doctor Syn helped them to move the still unconscious Rash into his own bedroom, leaving the bo’sun and two seamen in charge, the rest of the sailors returning to the vicarage barn; and finally muffling himself in his great cloak he proceeded to the inn to procure a room for the night. Supporting Imogene, he walked ahead, followed by the captain and Jerry Jerk bearing a lantern.

===Chinese===
辛医生却照旧用他那副四平八稳的腔调往下说：“这光景确实吓人，我不否认，可这里头没有半点玄虚，我向你担保。我们三个原本在这儿聊得好好的，忽然你那黑白混血儿（mulatto）蹿了进来，扑向我的朋友学堂先生，险些要了他的命。我抄起一只瓶子，照那畜生的脑袋就是一下。瓶子碎了，那疯子便掉头冲我来，把一截碎玻璃塞进我嘴里——我估摸着嘴里不免割破了些——随后逃之夭夭。我请姑娘帮我掌灯，她一看见学堂先生那张脸，喏，连人带蜡烛，一头栽倒，昏死过去。我这辈子从没见过这等事。不过有句话我得跟你讲，上校：把那疯子捉拿归案、吊上最近的一棵树，是你责无旁贷的本分；只要他一日逍遥在外，这儿就没有一个男人、女人或是娃娃能得安稳。”

随后，辛医生帮众人把依旧不省人事的拉什抬进他自己的卧室，留下水手长和两个水手看守，其余水手则回牧师宅的谷仓去；末了，他把自己深深裹进那件宽大的斗篷，动身往酒馆去订一间过夜的房间。他搀扶着伊莫金走在前头，上校和提着灯笼的杰里·杰尔克（Jerry Jerk）跟在后面。

===Original===
“Potboy?” said the captain on the way.

“Sir?” said Jerry Jerk.

“Are we dreaming, or what?”

“Blowed if I know; wish I did.”

===Chinese===
“跑堂小厮？”上校在路上唤道。

“先生？”杰里·杰尔克应道。

“咱们这是在做梦，还是怎么着？”

“鬼才知道；真知道就好了。”

===Original===
On reaching the inn they all agreed that it was none too safe to walk abroad that night again, for fear of that sinister mulatto out upon the Marsh, so they ordered the supper and rooms to be got ready, and for an hour or so the Doctor chatted of indifferent things, just as if nothing had happened.

But the captain kept silent that night; he had many things in his head that he couldn’t understand, and the greatest of these was Doctor Syn, that pious old cleric, who was making himself so pleasant over a steaming bowl of punch; and as the parlour clock ticked on, and the room was filled with tobacco smoke which the parson kept sending in thin rings across the fireplace, the captain rubbed his eyes hard, fidgeted and shuffled in his chair, wondering when the dream would stop and he would find himself awake.

===Chinese===
到了酒馆，众人一致认定，那天夜里再出门行走实在太不安全，生怕那个凶险的黑白混血儿还在外头的湿地上游荡，于是吩咐把晚饭和房间备好；约莫一个钟头的工夫，医生尽拣些不相干的话题闲聊，仿佛什么都不曾发生过一般。

可上校那晚一直闷声不响；他满脑子塞着许多想不通的事，其中最大的那桩便是辛医生——那位虔敬的老教士，这会儿正对着一碗热气腾腾的潘趣酒（punch）谈笑得那么自在；堂屋里的挂钟滴答不止，满屋子都是牧师不住吐出的烟，一环环细烟圈悠悠飘过壁炉，上校使劲揉着眼睛，在椅子里烦躁地扭动、挪蹭，心里直纳闷：这场梦几时才肯散，好叫他发现自己原来是醒着。

## XIX. The Captain's Nightmare / 十九　上校的噩梦

===Original===
Presently the captain yawned and Doctor Syn rose and summoned Mrs. Waggetts. The captain yawned again and rubbed his eyes. Was he awake or dreaming? The last thing he remembered was drinking the hot rum punch and listening to a long story that he thought the Doctor would never finish. What a soothing effect that punch seemed to have on his faculties, for after that he was rather vague. He dreamt he was lifted up sleeping, lifted up by two men who had followed Mrs. Waggetts from the bar when Doctor Syn had called her. Was one of those men that insolent Sexton Mipps? He vaguely thought it was, though he wouldn’t be sure. No, he wouldn’t be sure of anything! He thought he had been carried up to bed, but that was too silly, for who would carry him up to bed? Was it Doctor Syn who had said to Mipps on the stairs that he wasn’t going riding tonight for a thousand guineas, and that they must do without him for once? Then Mipps answered:

===Chinese===
不多时，上校打了个哈欠，辛医生（Doctor Syn）便起身唤瓦格茨（Waggetts）太太。上校又打了个哈欠，揉了揉眼睛。他是醒着，还是在做梦？他记得的最后一件事，是喝着热腾腾的朗姆潘趣酒，听一个长得没完的故事——他原以为医生那故事永远也讲不完。那潘趣酒对他的神志，效力真叫人舒坦，因为打那以后，他的记忆便有些恍惚了。他梦见自己睡得沉沉时被人抱了起来——抱他的正是两个人；辛医生唤瓦格茨太太的时候，这两人便跟着她从酒吧间里出来了。那两人当中，有一个可是那个放肆的司事米普斯（Mipps）？他依稀觉得是，却又不敢断定。不，他如今对什么事都不敢断定了！他记得自己被人抬上楼放到了床上，可这念头也太傻了，谁会来抬他上床呢？在楼梯上对米普斯说，今晚就是出一千几尼他也不去骑马了，这一回只好缺他一个的，那话可是辛医生说的？接着，米普斯答话了：

===Original===
“That yellow beast ain’t a-lookin’ out for Clegg’s carpenter, is he? Well, I’ll go, it don’t want us both tonight.”

Then the dream got more confused than ever. There was a lonely reef in the coral seas, and on it was a weird figure calling. The captain seemed to be on a ship that was standing away from the reef, and all the time the figure kept calling. There was a full ship’s crew collected on the deck who were threatening two men. One was a familiar figure, a figure he had not seen often out of his dreams, and so was his little companion, and still the voice kept calling. The crew pushed forward a spokesman: he was a Chinaman—they called him by a nickname—Pete. Pete sheepishly advanced and stammered out to the familiar figure, whom he addressed as “Captain,” to put the ship about, and take up again the lonely form calling from the reef. Pete’s argument was evidently useless, for as he turned to join his fellows, the tallest of the familiar figures stretched out his hand and caught the yellow man—he was clad in the scanty garb of a cook—and broke his naked back with a marlinspike that the little companion of the familiar figure had handed to him. Then the crew were commanded to throw the body overboard or they would be served the same. This they did, and the sharks surrounded the ship, clacking their teeth. Then the breeze seemed to blow off the reef, and the familiar figure ordered the men aloft to unfurl the sails. They obeyed sullenly, and still the voice, getting fainter and fainter, called from the reef, and the breeze increased, and the captain and his mate ordered the men the quicker aloft.

===Chinese===
“那头黄皮畜生该不是在盯克莱格（Clegg）的木匠吧？好啊，那就我去，今晚犯不上咱俩都出动。”

接着，那梦变得比先前越发乱作一团。珊瑚海里有座孤零零的暗礁，礁上立着一个怪影，在一声声呼喊。上校仿佛正在一条驶离暗礁的船上，而那身影始终在不停地呼喊。甲板上聚着全船的水手，正在威逼两个人。其中一个是他熟识的身影——那身影出了他的梦，他也不常见到——他那个小个子同伴也是一样；而那声音依旧在呼喊不止。水手们推出一个出面的：是个华人，大伙给他起了个绰号——皮特（Pete）。皮特讪讪地上前，结结巴巴地对那个熟识的身影——他称他“船长”——进言，求他掉转船头，回去搭救那在暗礁上呼喊的孤零零的人影。皮特的进言分明是白费，因为他转身要退回伙伴中间时，那几个熟识身影当中个子最高的一个伸出手去，一把抓住了那个黄皮肤的人——他身上还穿着厨子那身单薄衣裳——拿一柄索针——那是熟识身影的小个子同伴递过去的——砸断了他光着的脊梁。随后，全船水手奉命把尸首抛下海去，不然就叫他们同样下场。他们照办了，鲨鱼团团围住大船，牙齿咬得咯咯作响。接着，风仿佛从暗礁那头吹了过来，那熟识的身影命水手们爬上桅去张帆。水手们闷声服从了；那声音仍从暗礁上传来，一声淡似一声；风却越刮越紧，船长和他的大副催水手们往上爬，越催越急。

===Original===
“Get up aloft there, you dogs! Get up! Get up! Get up!”

The familiar figure then caught sight of the dreamer (though he wasn’t sure that he was dreaming even yet), and striding up to him ordered him aloft, and when he refused he dragged him up by the arm. The dreamer felt dizzy, for the sails were blowing in his face, and he thought he would let go, it was so like his first experience aloft; and he begged the familiar figure to let him go down, but the voice went on crying: “Up! Get up! Get up!”

Then the sail was pulled from his face, the wind blew through his hair, and he started up, catching hold of a stay (which turned out to be the bedpost), and letting the sail fall below, upon the deck, which in reality was the bedclothes slipping to the floor, and still the voice cried: “Get up! Get up!” And he recognized there the familiar face and form of Doctor Syn, and by him his companion, Sexton Mipps.

===Chinese===
“都给我上去，上桅顶上去，你们这些狗东西！上去！上去！上去！”

随后，那熟识的身影瞧见了做梦的人（虽说做梦的人连自己此刻还在做梦与否，也都不敢肯定），便大步走到他跟前，命他上桅去；他不肯，那人便揪住他的胳膊，硬把他拖了上去。做梦的人只觉头晕目眩，帆布呼呼地抽打在他脸上，他心想自己怕是撒了手，那滋味跟他头一回上桅顶时一模一样；他哀求那熟识的身影放他下去，那声音却一个劲儿地吼：“上去！上去！上去！”

随后，那面帆从他脸上扯开了，风灌进他的头发，他猛地坐起身，一把抓住一根支索——其实原来是根床柱——任那面帆坠落到下面的甲板上，那甲板其实是滑落到地板上的被褥；而那声音还在喊：“起来！起来！”这当口，他认出了那张熟识的面孔、那副熟识的身形——是辛医生——他身旁还站着他的同伴，司事米普斯。

===Original===
“Get up! Get up!” the parson was crying. “What a fellow to sleep you are! Like waking the dead! Upon my soul, it is, Mr. Mipps.”

The captain rubbed his eyes again.

The sun was streaming through the window, which was open, and a good stiff breeze was blowing in from the sea.

===Chinese===
“起来！起来！”牧师还在嚷着。“你这觉睡的！跟把死人叫活一个样！我敢对天起誓，正是这样，米普斯先生。”

上校又揉了揉眼睛。

阳光从敞开的窗口泻进来，一阵又稳又硬的好风正从海上吹进屋来。

===Original===
“What the devil!” said the captain. “Oh, it’s Doctor Syn, is it? What’s the time?”

“Just on ten o’clock,” said the cleric.

“Ten o’ what?” bellowed the captain, leaping out of bed.

“Clock,” repeated Mr. Mipps.

“I’ve overslept. Thing I’ve never done in my life. Been dreaming, too. Nightmares—horrible! But what do you want? Is anything the matter?”

===Chinese===
“见鬼！”上校说，“哦，是辛医生啊。什么时辰了？”

“正要到十点呢。”牧师先生说。

“十点的什么？”上校吼道，一跃跳下了床。

“钟。”米普斯先生重复道。

“我睡过头了。这可是我这辈子从没有过的事。还做了梦。梦魇——可怕极了！可二位有什么贵干？出了什么岔子么？”

===Original===
“I think there is,” said the Doctor quietly.

“And so do I,” said Mr. Mipps.

“What? What’s wrong? What’s happened?”

“I don’t quite know yet, it may be nothing at all, but I don’t like the look of it.”

“The look of what?” shouted the captain.

“The vicarage,” replied the vicar. “Put on your clothes quickly, Captain, and come and see. I think there’s something wrong.”

===Chinese===
“我看是出了。”医生不动声色地说。

“我也是这么看的。”米普斯先生说。

“什么？什么不对？出了什么事？”

“眼下我还说不准，也许根本不算回事，只是那情形叫我不放心。”

“什么情形？”上校嚷道。

“牧师宅的情形。”牧师答道，“快穿上衣服，上校，跟我们去看一眼。我看是出了什么岔子了。”

## XX. A Terrible Investigation / 二十　一场可怕的查究

===Original===
The captain was not long in tumbling into his clothes. Meantime, the sexton sat upon the bed, which neither of the other two seemed to think extraordinary or even familiar. The captain now and then addressed a sharp question to the Doctor, which the Doctor did not answer, nor indeed did the captain seem to expect an answer. The Doctor was standing by the window, his gray hair blowing in the stiff sea breeze that filled the room. Suddenly they heard a little shaking noise upon the bed, and, turning, perceived the little sexton, with the tears rolling down his cheeks, given up to the most ungovernable laughter, and yet it was not laughter, for the sexton made no noise. He just let his body quiver and heave and the tears roll on over his thin cheeks. Yes, he was lost in a fit of unmanageable giggles.

“What the thunder’s amusing you?” roared the captain; and he hurled the bolster at the sexton’s head.

Mipps was himself again upon the instant. “Blessed if I knows,” he gasped, “but thank you kindly for that bolster whack, for if something hadn’t happened I believe I should have bust.”

“But what is it? There must have been something to make you laugh like that.”

“If there was, I’m blessed if I knows wot,” returned the sexton, “for I gives you my word that I never felt solemner than I does now, no, not never in my life.” Doctor Syn took no notice of this extraordinary occurrence.

===Chinese===
上校没多会儿便手忙脚乱地把衣服套上了。这当口，司事坐在床上——对这情形，另外两位似乎谁也不觉得古怪，甚至也不觉得眼熟。上校时不时朝医生抛出一句厉声问话，医生并不作答；上校呢，似乎也压根不指望有人答。医生立在窗前，灌满屋子的强劲海风，吹得他一头灰白头发乱飘。忽然，他们听见床那边传来一阵窸窸窣窣的抖动，回头看时，只见小个子司事满脸泪水滚滚而下，正纵情大笑——可那又不是笑，因为司事没出半点声息。他只是听凭身子哆嗦、起伏，任眼泪顺着瘦削的两颊淌个不住。不错，他是一头栽进了一阵管束不住的闷笑里。

“见什么鬼了，什么把你逗成这样？”上校吼道，抄起床上的长枕头，照司事的脑袋掷了过去。

米普斯（Mipps）登时还了魂。“天打雷劈我也说不上来，”他喘着气说，“不过方才那一枕头，还得好好谢谢您，因为要不是出了那档子事，我怕是就要把自己憋炸了。”

“可到底是什么事？总有个缘故，叫你笑成那副样子。”

“要真有什么缘故，我也是说不上来，说不上来，”司事回道，“我敢对天起誓，我这会儿正经得不得了，活了这一辈子，从没这么正经过，从来没有。”辛医生（Doctor Syn）对这桩怪事毫不理会。

===Original===
When the captain was dressed they all three set out for the vicarage.

“Well, now, what is wrong with it?” said the captain, surveying the little house that looked so pretty in the morning sun.

“That’s just what we want to know,” answered Doctor Syn. “In the first place, short of forcing the door, I don’t see how we’re going to get in. The place is all locked up, and, though we have battered and hammered on the doors and windows for a good hour, we can get no answer from the sailors inside.”

“And my men in the barn, where are they?” said the captain, looking across at the building in question.

“I’m afraid, Captain, that you are too liberal to your men, for their rum barrel is empty and the whole lot of them are still asleep.”

The captain swore and walked to the back door, raised his foot, and with one kick sent the door in, splintered and cracked from the bolt sockets.

===Chinese===
待上校穿戴齐整，三个人便一齐动身往牧师宅（vicarage）去。

“好了，如今说说，这宅子究竟毛病出在哪儿？”上校说道，一面端详着那幢在晨光里瞧上去那般秀气的小屋。

“这正是我们要弄明白的事，”辛医生答道，“头一条，除非破门，我看不出我们怎么进得去。整座宅子锁得严严实实，门窗上我们足足又擂又敲了一个好钟头，屋里那几个水手就是不应声。”

“那我谷仓里那些人呢？都上哪儿去了？”上校说着，朝那座房子望了过去。

“上校，只怕您待手下人太大方了，他们那桶朗姆酒已经见了底，整窝人这会儿还睡着呢。”

上校骂了一声，走到后门前，抬起脚来，一脚把门踹了进去，门板从插销槽那里迸裂得开了花。

===Original===
“Neatly done!” remarked Doctor Syn, “though who’s to pay for a new door?”

But the captain did not heed him, nor care a brass farthing for the door, he was bent on investigating the house, which he did, followed by Mipps and the Doctor and Jerry Jerk, who had appeared from somewhere, nobody quite knew where.

The kitchen was empty, so the captain opened the door of the sitting-room; it was very dark because of the closed shutters.

The captain strode across to the broken window, threw it open, and unbolted the shutters, which, swinging back, let in the light of day. In the corner of the room opposite the window lay the two sailors who had been left to watch with the bo’sun. Both were bound and gagged, and one of them was moving. The captain loosed his bonds with a clasp knife, and the fellow seemed to recover his senses.

“What does this mean, my man?” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“干得利索！”辛医生评了一句，“只是新门钱谁出？”

上校可没理会他，对那扇门半文钱也不心疼；他一门心思要把这座宅子查个究竟，说查便查，米普斯和医生跟在后头，还有杰里·杰尔克（Jerry Jerk）——不知打哪儿冒出来的，谁也说不清他究竟从哪儿来的。

厨房空无一人，于是上校推开起居室的门；因为窗板紧闭，屋里黑得很。

上科大步跨到那扇破窗跟前，把窗子推开，又拔去窗板的插销；窗板向后荡开，天光涌了进来。在窗子对面的屋角里，躺着那两个奉命留下与水手长（bo’sun）一同守夜的水手。两人都被捆了手脚、堵了嘴，其中一个还在动弹。上校用折刀割断他的绳索，那家伙才算缓过神来。

“这是怎么回事，伙计？”上校说。

===Original===
The sailor turned and pointed to the body of his friend. It lay half propped up against the wall, and above it was a large splintered tear in the whitewashed plaster. There were blood marks on this part of the wall. And then the captain saw and understood, for the neck of the propped-up body had been cruelly pierced, although there was no sign of a weapon; but some weapon had transfixed that body to the wall and then been plucked out, so that the body had collapsed amid a mess of broken plaster.

“It’s Bill Spiker, sir,” said the sailor. “He’s dead! He was a good gunner, sir, too. We wanted Spiker, sir, to fight the French—and he’s dead!” And the sailor broke off blubbering.

Just then they all became aware of a moaning overhead.

“What’s that?” said Mipps, beginning to giggle.

Indeed the uncanny atmosphere of the vicarage that morning had upset them all.

===Chinese===
水手转过身，指着他同伴的尸首。那尸首半撑半靠在墙上，上方的粉墙上一大片墙灰迸裂，豁开一道大口子，那一处墙上还留着血迹。上校见状，登时明白过来：那具半撑着的尸首颈子已叫人残忍地扎穿，近旁却不见半件凶器；分明是有件什么凶器把尸身钉在了墙上，随后又拔了出去，尸身这才塌落下来，跌进一片碎墙灰里。

“是比尔·斯派克（Bill Spiker），长官，”水手说，“他死了！他还是把好炮手呢，长官。我们还要靠斯派克去打法国人，长官——他死了！”说罢便呜呜咽咽，话也断了。

就在这时，众人都觉出头顶上有一阵呻吟。

“那是什么？”米普斯说着，又咯咯地憋起笑来。

这天早上牧师宅里那股阴森森的气氛，确实搅得他们个个心神不宁。

===Original===
“I’m sure I don’t know,” said the captain, “for I’ve had my fill of horrors. I don’t mind blood and I don’t mind fighting, but these mysteries are horrible. What the devil is that moaning?”

“That’ll be Job Mallet, captain’s bo’sun,” said the sailor.

“Or Rash, the sick schoolmaster,” said Doctor Syn.

But Mipps said nothing; he had left the room and was now out in the passage, suffering from another attack of giggles.

“Damn that sexton’s body and soul!” ejaculated the captain; “his giggling gives one the creeps. What’s tickling him now?”

===Chinese===
“我可真不知道，”上校说，“这些吓人的事我已然受够了。血我不怕见，仗我也不怕打，可这些神神鬼鬼的谜团真真骇人。那呻吟到底是什么见鬼的声音？”

“那是乔布·马利特（Job Mallet），上校的水手长。”水手说。

“要么就是拉什（Rash），那位病着的学堂先生。”辛医生说。

米普斯却一声不吭；他已出了屋子，这会儿正在外头过道里，又是一阵闷笑发作。

“咒这司事连人带魂都不得好死！”上校猝然骂道，“他咯咯的闷笑叫人汗毛直竖。这会儿又是什么逗着他了？”

===Original===
“Unstrung,” muttered the vicar, as he followed the captain up the dark stairs to the bedroom.

There in the bed, last night occupied by Mr. Rash, lay the fat bo’sun on his back, with his face gagged up and covered with a nightcap. Dreadful moans he was making as he lay there.

The captain pulled the bedclothes off, and discovered that the faithful fellow was tied to the bed. Grateful he looked, though troubled, when the captain cut his bonds and pulled him up; and he owned in a shamefaced manner that he never had endured such a horrible night in his life, and that Parson Syn (saving his presence) must be the foul fiend himself to be able to sleep in such a devil-haunted house.

Doctor Syn went downstairs and fetched the brandy bottle, and administered a good dose to the bo’sun, and also to the other seamen who had followed them upstairs.

“And where’s the schoolmaster got to?” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“绷断了弦，”牧师喃喃道，一面跟着上校摸黑上楼，往卧室去。

拉什先生昨晚睡的那张床上，此刻仰面躺着胖水手长，嘴给堵了个严实，头上扣着一顶睡帽；他在那里躺着，呻吟得怕人。

上校掀开被褥，才见这忠心耿耿的家伙被人捆在了床上。待上校割断绳索、把他拽起来时，他满脸感激，虽则惊魂未定；他羞惭惭地承认，活了一辈子，从没熬过这么骇人的一夜，还说辛牧师（恕小的当着他老人家的面说这句）必定就是那万恶的魔王本尊，不然怎能在这么一座恶鬼盘踞的宅子里睡得着觉。

辛医生下楼取来白兰地酒瓶，给水手长灌了好大一口，也给跟着他们上楼来的其余水手一人灌了一口。

“那学堂先生上哪儿去了？”上校说。

===Original===
“He’s gone.”

“Gone?” they all repeated together.

“Aye, sir, gone! And if ever a man has gone body and soul, I declares he has; for I solemnly and soberly declares that I seed him hoisted up and removed downstairs by a couple of horrible light-faces.”

“Light-faces?” roared the captain.

“Yes, sir, coves with faces all a-shine. Why, I wouldn’t settle down and live within a hundred miles of Romney Marsh for a thousand guineas a year pension, I wouldn’t; for talk about devils, the place stinks of them!”

===Chinese===
“他走了。”

“走了？”众人不约而同地跟着念了一遍。

“是啊，长官，走了！要是有谁走得连皮带骨、连魂带魄都无影无踪，我说就是他；因为我清清醒醒、一本正经地起誓，我亲眼看见他给两个可怕的亮脸家伙（light-faces）扛了起来，抬下楼去了。”

“亮脸家伙？”上校吼道。

“没错，长官，就是那号人物，一张张脸亮晃晃的。嗐，就算一年给我一千几尼的年金，我也不肯在离罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）一百英里之内安家落户，说什么也不肯；要说魔鬼，那地方都叫魔鬼熏臭了！”

===Original===
“Now, look here, my man,” said the captain, “just pull yourself in a brace or two and tell me what happened.”

“Why, so I will,” said the bo’sun, “for queer, most queer it be.”

===Chinese===
“喂，听着，伙计，”上校说，“快给我定定神，把出的事一五一十讲来。”

“好嘞，这就讲，”水手长说，“只因这事邪性，邪性得很哪。”

## XXI. The Bo’sun’s Story / 二十一　水手长的自述

===Original===
“Nothing happened, sir, for some hour or so after you left, and then things made up for lost time, as ’twere, and came fast and quick. I was sitting outside this here room with the door on the jar—outside I was, ’cos I couldn’t bear the sight of that schoolmaster’s face. I think you’ll own yourself, sir, that it wasn’t just exactly wot you might call ‘a pleasant evening face’ especially, a-battered about as it was. Poor Bill Spiker and Morgan Walters here was asleep downstairs, for we’d agreed that I should stand first watch.

“Well, the boys had brought us over our allowance of rum from the barn, and we’d all had a drop, though I kept most of mine to the end of my watch, thinking to use it for a nightcap, as ’twere, but the little drop I did get was making me feel very drowsy, and I began to think the next hour would never go, when I could wake up Bill Spiker. Presently I hears a noise of galloping horses. I goes to the window on the stairs there, and looks out. Right along the road I could see those same riders with lit-up faces wot I’d seed the night before last. I know it was them, ’cos I could see their faces, you understand, when quite sudden I was seized from behind and pulled over backwards down the stairs. I fought the best I could, but there was a sort of overpowering smell upon a kerchief wot had been pulled over my mouth, and I was lifted up on four men’s shoulders, as it seemed. I couldn’t see anything of their faces, but as I went up the stairway on their shoulders I just remember a-seein’ that schoolmaster a-comin’ down in the same fashion as I was a-goin’ up, only that he only required two to hold him. Now, whether this was because I was heavier, I don’t know, or whether ’cos he was only a-comin’ down while I was a-goin’ up, or whether the things wot had got hold of me was real or sham, as ’twere, but certain am I the two things wot had the schoolmaster—and things I must call ’em, though they was a bit like men—had got the same shiny faces all alight, just like wot them demon riders had; and then I don’t remember nothing else till I was woke up by hearin’ a sort of horrible shriek downstairs which I thought was just a dream, but now suppose was poor Bill a-voicin’ his last opinion in this world, as ’twere. After that I went to sleep again; then I was waked up again by a sort of groanin’, which I finds was myself, and then in comes you after a long time and lets me go, as ’twere, and that’s all I knows, so help me God, sir; but quite enough for one night, as I thinks you’ll agree.”

===Chinese===
“您走了之后，长官，约莫个把钟头倒还太平，随后事情就像要把耽误的工夫补回来似的，来得又急又快。我正坐在这间屋子外头，房门虚掩着——我在外头待着，只因那学堂先生那张脸我实在看不下去。长官，您自己想想也认这个账：那张脸挨了那样一顿打、走了相，实在算不得您所谓的‘晚间瞧着舒坦的一张脸’。可怜的比尔·斯派克和这位摩根·沃尔特斯（Morgan Walters）当时在楼下睡着，因为我们讲好头一班岗由我来站。

“嗐，弟兄们从谷仓把咱们那份定量的朗姆酒捎了过来，大伙儿都沾了一口，只是我把自己那份大半留到换岗的时辰，想着拿它当睡前酒；可就沾了的那么一小口，已经叫我昏昏欲睡，我心想这一个钟点怎么也熬不到头，熬到了好去叫醒比尔·斯派克。忽然我听得一阵马蹄疾驰之声，便走到楼梯口那扇窗前，朝外张望。就在那条大路上，我望见了前晚见过的那几个满脸亮光的骑手。我认得准就是他们，因为他们的脸我瞧得真真的，您明白，长官，就在这时，冷不防有人从背后把我擒住，倒拖着往楼下就扯。我拼死挣扎，可一方手帕捂上了我的嘴，帕子上一股熏人的气味，压得人透不过气，跟着仿佛就叫人架上了四条汉子的肩头。他们的脸我一概瞧不见；只记得叫人抬着上楼的时候，正瞧见那学堂先生叫人照原样抬着下楼来，同我一上一下打了个照面，只是架他的只需两个人。这究竟是因为我身量沉些，我说不上；还是因为他不过下楼、我却在登楼；又或是擒住我的那些物事本来就真假难辨；可有一点千真万确：架着学堂先生的那两个物事——物事二字我只得这么称呼，虽说他们长得有几分像人——也生着同样亮闪闪发光的脸，同那伙魔骑一模一样；打那以后我就什么都不记得了，直到楼下一声骇人的尖叫把我惊醒，我还只当是做梦，如今想来，那必是可怜的比尔在人世间发出的最后一番高论。打那以后我又昏昏睡去；跟着又给一阵呻吟吵醒，一寻思，那呻吟竟出自我自己口中；又过了老半天，您才进来把我放了出来。我知道的就是这些，长官，上帝在上；可一晚上经过这么些，也就尽够了，您说是不是。”

===Original===
Morgan Walters then gave his version of what happened in the night, which bore out certain points of the bo’sun’s story.

He had soon fallen into a deep sleep, but was awakened with a feeling that something was wrong. He tried to move but couldn’t; indeed, he could scarcely breathe. The only things that he could see were two dark forms moving about the room, but their faces were lit up by a curious light. These two things passed out of the room, and then for what seemed an interminable time Morgan Walters worked away at his bonds, and presently became aware that his companion was doing likewise. They couldn’t talk, for they found that, just as soon as they tried to, the breath that they took in through the anaesthetic overpowered their senses. Presently Morgan Walters thought that he could hear the sound of horses. It sounded like a regiment of pack-ponies trotting on the high road—*tlip tlop* they went, a slow *tlip tlop*, and a lot of them, too. These were his very words. Then he heard a sigh of satisfaction from his companion, and saw him stand up, for he had partially unbound himself. Whether to let in the refreshing sea air, or whether he had also heard the horses and wanted to locate them, Morgan Walters couldn’t say, but Bill Spiker had got to the broken window and unbolted the shutters. He felt the cold air come into the room with a great gasp, and then he seemed to have dozed off again, but the next thing he heard was a great scream of agony, and turning over he beheld Bill Spiker embracing the wall, and the wall held him up, for there was a weapon transfixed to it through his companion’s neck. The very horror and sudden surprise of the thing caused Morgan Walters to make a superb effort, and he somehow stood upon his feet. Then came a curious thing: He saw between himself and the now repulsive form of his fellow a man—a yellow-faced man—the mulatto seaman. With one hand the creature plucked the weapon from the wall and drew it back through the bleeding neck that held it. This was strangely vivid to Morgan Walters, and he could recall his thought of wonder that the blood in no way stained the yellow hand that drew the reeking steel from the flesh. The body of Bill Spiker fell from the wall and collapsed in a heap, and a hand seemed to strike Morgan Walters at the same time, for he lost consciousness again and remembered little else.

===Chinese===
接着，摩根·沃尔特斯也把他夜里所经的事照自己的见闻讲了一遍，其中有几处恰与水手长的话互相印证。

不一会儿他便沉入了酣睡，却猛地惊醒，只觉事情不对。他想动弹，却动弹不得；说实在的，连气也喘不上来。目力所及，唯有两条黑影在屋里走动，两张脸却被一种奇怪的光照亮着。这两个物事出了屋去，此后仿佛没完没了的一段时光里，摩根·沃尔特斯一直磨着他身上的绳索，渐渐觉出同伴也在一样挣扎。两人说不得话，因为他们发现，刚一张口，透过麻醉药气吸进去的那口气便又熏得他们神志迷糊。过了一阵，摩根·沃尔特斯觉着自己听见了马蹄声。那声音活像一整队驮马在大路上小跑——*踢里·踏喽*地响，慢慢的*踢里·踏喽*，数量还着实不少。这正是他一字一板的原话。跟着他听得同伴满意地舒了一口气，又见他站起身来，原来他已把身上的绳索解开了一半。这一站，是为了放进一口提神的海上夜气，还是他也听见了马蹄、想要辨一辨来处，摩根·沃尔特斯说不上来；总之比尔·斯派克挪到了那扇破窗跟前，拔去了窗板的插销。他只觉一股冷气呼地灌进屋来，跟着仿佛又打起盹去，再往下听见的就是一声痛彻骨髓的惨叫，他翻过身来，只见比尔·斯派克正搂着那面墙，是墙把他撑住了，因为一件凶器穿过他同伴的颈项，钉在了墙上。这般骇人又这般突如其来的景象，逼得摩根·沃尔特斯拼出了超人的力气，好歹站起了脚跟。跟着来了一件古怪的事：他看见自己与同伴那如今叫人憎厌的形骸之间，立着一条汉子——一个黄脸汉子——便是那黑白混血儿水手。那家伙单手便把凶器从墙上拔下，又从那淌血的颈项里抽了回去。这一幕在摩根·沃尔特斯眼前出奇地真切，他事后还记得自己当时暗自纳罕：那手把兀自冒着热气的钢器从肉里抽将出来，黄黄的手上竟不见沾半点血。比尔·斯派克的尸身从墙上跌落，瘫作一堆；同一时刻仿佛有一只手朝摩根·沃尔特斯打来，因为他又一次不省人事，此后的事便记不得多少了。

===Original===
“Did the mulatto *touch* you?” asked the captain, speaking suddenly and rather loud, so that all in the room gave a perceptible start. “Think well, my man.”

“I am quite certain of that, sir. I know he did *not*!”

“And yet you were knocked down!”

“So it seems, sir, but it may have been just losing consciousness again. I’ve never fainted before, so perhaps it was that, or the effects of the smelly stuff on the kerchief.”

“And you remember nothing else?”

===Chinese===
“那黑白混血儿可曾*碰*过你？”上校发问。他话来得突然，嗓门又高，满屋子的人都不觉为之一惊。“好好想一想，伙计。”

“这一点我千真万确，长官。我知道他*没有*碰过我！”

“可你分明是给人打倒的！”

“看着是这样，长官，可那兴许不过是又昏了一回。我从前从没昏过，兴许就是这么回事，要不就是那手帕上臭烘烘的药气闹的。”

“别的你就什么都不记得了？”

===Original===
“One thing, though whether I dreamt that or not I couldn’t swear to, but it seemed that when I come to something like myself the dawn was breaking, for the room was filled with a gray light, when suddenly something came into the room and closed those shutters. Then I fell off into another sort of sleep and dreamt that people were trying to wake me up by banging on the shutters, and then at last—hours after it, it seemed—you came, sir, and freed me.”

“One moment,” said the captain; “this something that closed the shutters—a man?”

“Yes, like a man.”

“Like *what* man?”

“Well, sir, it was like one of them devils that I’d seen leaving the room that night. It also reminded me—yes, it reminded me of that gentleman there, a-standing at that door—that sexton; in fact, now I comes to think of it and look at him, I remembers dreaming a lot about him in the night.”

===Chinese===
“有一桩——是梦不是梦，我可不敢起誓——仿佛等我多少还过点神来的时候，天已破晓，屋里满是青灰的光，忽然有个什么进了屋，把那两扇窗板合上了。跟着我又睡昏过去，梦见有人砰砰捶打窗板要把我叫醒；末了——仿佛过了好几个钟头——长官，您来了，把我解了。”

“慢着，”上校说，“这个合窗板的什么——是个人？”

“是，像个人。”

“像*什么样*的人？”

“这个嘛，长官，就像那晚我亲眼看着出屋去的那几个魔鬼当中的一个。他还叫我想起——对，叫我想起那边那位老爷，正立在门边的那位——那位司事；说实在的，如今我这么一想、再这么一瞧他，我想起夜里净梦见他了。”

===Original===
“Thank you kindly,” said Mr. Mipps, who was indeed listening to the narrative from the door, “but don’t trouble to drag me into it, mate. I gives you my word that we were all as merry as crickets till you King’s men come nigh the place, and as for talks of demons and suchlike, well, there’s always gossip of such, of course, but since you fellows come aboard, the talk’s been of nothing else; and murders, too. Why, we’d never heard of murders, except, of course, in church we’d heard as how there was such things. We was as happy and contented a pleasant-going little village as you could have wished, we was; but now, so help me God! you fellows have turned our little spot into a regular witches’ kitchen, that you have. Two days you’ve been here, and two murders we’ve had—one a day—and if you stays here for a year, as you can calculate for yourself, we’ll have three hundred and sixty-five, at the present rate. Of course it’s good for my trade, so I says nothing. Go on murdering to your hearts’ content, for I can knock up one a day all right, but I ain’t a-goin’ to take any blame about it, and, wot’s more, I object to being dreamt about; so another night kindly leave me out of your adventures, ’cos I don’t like bein’ mixed up with such traffic.”

Saying which Mipps stepped across to the corpse of Bill Spiker, and, producing his footrule, measured him up, and entered the same in a dirty notebook.

The captain then proceeded to the barn and soundly rated his still drowsy men; and putting the bo’sun in charge of the corpse, he asked Doctor Syn to join him for breakfast at the Ship. And as there was no schoolmaster, and consequently no school, Jerry Jerk had the extreme pleasure of waiting upon them.

===Chinese===
“多谢抬举，”米普斯先生说——他确乎一直守在门口听这段故事——“不过劳驾可别把小的拽进去，伙计。小的可以拿话担保，在你们国王的人挨近这地方之前，咱们全村都跟蟋蟀似的欢欢喜喜。至于魔鬼之类的闲话，呃，这类闲话自然向来有得是，可打你们这班人上了门，闲话里就再没旁的了；连凶杀也添上了。嗐，凶杀咱们可是听都没听说过；当然了，在教堂里倒是听讲过世上有这么档子事。咱们原是个人人称羡的快活小村庄，和和美美，心满意足；可如今呢，上帝在上！你们这班人把咱们这块小地方活活变成了一个巫婆的灶间，就是这话。你们来了两天，咱们就摊上了两条人命——一天一条——你们要在这儿住上一年，这账你自己会算，照这个章程，一年就是三百六十五条。这买卖自然对小的的营生大有好处，所以小的什么也不说。你们只管称心如愿地接着杀人，反正一天赶制一口棺材，小的还供得上；可这罪名小的决计不认；再者，平白叫人梦进梦里去，小的也断不答应；所以往后再有什么历险，行行好，把小的撇开，小的可不喜欢跟这路营生搅在一块儿。”

说着，米普斯便跨到比尔·斯派克的尸首跟前，摸出一把木尺，把他量了一遍，又把量得的尺寸登进一本油污的小册子里。

随后上校往谷仓去，把他那班还没睡醒的部下着实申饬了一顿；他吩咐水手长看守尸首，便请辛医生陪他到“船”酒馆去用早饭。既然学堂先生没了，自然也就没了学堂，杰里·杰尔克便捞到了伺候二位的无上美差。

## XXII. A Curious Breakfast Party / 二十二　一场奇特的早餐会

===Original===
During the meal Jerry took good stock of both men. The captain’s manner was sullen and grumpy. He was turning things over in his mind that he was incapable of solving—things altogether out of his ken. Doctor Syn, on the other hand, seemed eager to discuss all these curious events, but underlying his interesting, polished, quiet conversation there smouldered a nameless fear which now and then burst into flames of enthusiastic fury—fury against the captain’s apparent inactivity in taking measures to find and capture the mysterious mulatto.
