Jerk now saw the iron qualities in the sexton that had struck the love spark upon the flinty bosom of Mrs. Waggetts, for as Mipps walked about among his men, from room to room, and in and out of the coffin shop, which was heavily shuttered, he carried a power upon his shoulders that would have done credit to Boney himself. And the company that Jerk found himself among—well, if the young hangman had suddenly found himself in the greenroom of Drury Lane Theatre in the midst of the great play actors, he could not have been more surprised, for there, collected altogether, were the jack-o'lanterns, the Marsh witches, and the demon riders, all preparing themselves as for a country fair. Grizzly old men, fishermen, and labourers, as the case might be, were arranging themselves in torn rags of women's garments, and with a few deft touches of Mipps's hands, lo! the fishermen and labourers were no more, and Marsh witches took their place. Similarly were the big fellows, hulking great men of Kent, metamorphosed into demons, enormous demons upon whose faces Mipps stuck heavy moustaches and hairy eyebrows of a most alarming nature. The grizzled ones likewise used horsehair in long streamers from their conical hats, so that their appearance as witchfolk should be the more pronounced. There were also three little boys and two little girls dressed as jack-o'-lanterns. They were much younger than Jerk, but their rigouts filled him with envy.

===Chinese===
村子若是十点钟便都上了床，棺材铺里到了后半夜半点钟，却是生机勃勃。杰尔克（Jerk）奉了指示，恰在那个时辰去敲后窗，转瞬便让米普斯先生亲自一把拽进屋里去。司事披着一件宽大的骑装斗篷，坠着沉沉的披肩领，一只黑面具遮住了脸上半截；可是杰尔克只朝那两颚瘦削利落的线条一瞥便看得出，米普斯已经把他那副油腻做派、那口尖酸俏皮、那副点头哈腰的自鸣得意统统撇在了一边，正让藏在他身上的那个真人头一回大放光彩——八成是专为他自己的莫大好处。杰尔克这会儿才算看见了这位司事骨子里的那股铁质——当初正是它，在瓦格茨太太那颗铁石心口上敲出了爱情的火星；因为米普斯在他那班人马里走来走去，一间屋又一间屋，出出进进那扇扇关得严严实实的棺材铺，肩上扛着的那份威风，便是拿去给波尼（Boney，即拿破仑）增光也绰绰有余。至于杰尔克发觉自己置身其中的这伙人嘛——嗐，倘若这个年轻刽子手是一下子闯进了德鲁里巷剧院的后台休息室，站到了名伶们中间，他也不见得会更加惊奇：因为聚在那儿的，正是鬼火儿（jack-o'-lantern）、湿地女巫和魔骑，一个个装扮起来，喜气洋洋，赛过上乡间集会。白头老汉、渔夫、雇工——各按各的身份——正往一身破破烂烂的妇人衣裳里钻；经米普斯一双手几下来得心应手的点拨，瞧！渔夫雇工顿时无影无踪，湿地女巫原地现身。同样地，那几条大汉，肯特郡虎背熊腰的壮家伙，摇身变作了恶魔——好大的恶魔，米普斯往他们脸上贴起浓黑的髭须和毛茸茸的眉毛，瞧着煞是吓人。那些白头老汉也照样从尖顶帽沿上垂下长长的马尾毛流苏，好教他们那副巫婆行头越发扎眼。还有三个小男孩、两个小姑娘，扮作鬼火儿。他们比杰尔克小得多，可那一身行头，惹得他满心眼红。

===Original===
"Gentlemen," said Mipps, leading Jerry into this motley throng of eccentrics, "the new recruit. A young man wot has the eye of an eagle and the nerves of a steel blade. Those who quarrels with this young gent'll come off worst, if I'm not mistook, but them wot be his friends can bank on his good faith, for he's as staunch as a dog. Get your brandy flasks out, my devils, and let's drink to our new recruit. Jerry Jerk his name is, but accordin' to custom we drops all mention of private names in this organization; so up with your glasses whilst I rechristen him. We has power, we has, we has devils amongst us of very great power, for we has lawyers, and farmers, and squires, and parsons wot be in league with us, but the greatest enemy we has is not the revenue swabs, nor the Admiralty uniforms, nor the bloody redcoats, nor the Prince Regent—God bless him for a vagabond and a 'rip!'—no, I thinks you knows who we fears more than all that ruck?"

"Jack Ketch! Jack Ketch!" whispered the horrible creatures.

===Chinese===
“诸位，”米普斯说着，把杰里领进这一大群形形色色的怪人中间，“新入伍的弟兄。一个后生，长着鹰的眼，钢刀的胆。要是老汉我没看走眼，跟这位小爷作对的，保管占不着便宜；可拿他当朋友的，尽可以信他的实心实意，因为他忠得像条猎狗。把你们的白兰地扁瓶都掏出来，我的魔崽子们，咱们为新弟兄干一杯。他名叫杰里·杰尔克（Jerry Jerk），不过照咱们的规矩，本堂口里一概不提私人名姓；所以举起杯子来，且听我给他重新施洗。咱们有的是势力，有的；咱们中间有的是法力无边的恶魔，因为咱们有讼师，有农人，有乡绅，有牧师，全都跟咱们一条藤上拴着；可咱们顶大的对头，既不是缉私的蠢货，也不是海军部的号衣，也不是那帮该死的红衫军，更不是摄政王——上帝保佑他，那个流氓，那个‘浪荡胚’！——不是，我想你们知道，咱们怕的比那一窝蜂都厉害的是谁？”

“刽子手杰克·凯奇！刽子手杰克·凯奇！”那些狰狞的家伙齐声低语。

===Original===
"Why, right you are, for Jack Ketch it be," retorted the sexton. "And here's a man wot's goin' sooner or later to be a Jack Ketch. He's got all the gifts of the hangman, he has—just that jolly way with him, he has—and so you'll all be delighted to hear as how he's joined us, for with Jack Ketch as our friend we'll cheat the black cap on the gallows. Gentlemen, Jack Ketch." Therefore they all drank to Jerk with much spirit, and Jerk, having been presented with a flask, pledged them in return and was introduced to all severally by the sexton. "This is Beelzebub, knocked over a good round dozen revenue swabs in your time, ain't you, Beelzebub? And this is Belch the demon, the finest rider we ever had in our demon horse, and here's Satan, and this be Cat'seyes, the weirdest old witch you ever met with in a story book, I'll wager," and so on until such a vast collection of weird names had been rammed into Jerk's brains that he felt quite overpowered. However, when his own particular uniform was produced for him to don, his interests were requickened, and before Mipps had half finished attiring him in the strange rags Jerk would have sworn that it wasn't himself he saw in the old cracked mirror.

"And now, Jack Ketch," said Mipps, "you only has to follow me into the coffin shop to get your allowance of devil's face cream, then I thinks you'll feel real pleased with yourself."

===Chinese===
“着啊，你们说得一字不差，正是刽子手杰克·凯奇（Jack Ketch），”司事回敬道。“眼前站着这么一位，早晚也要做一名刽子手杰克·凯奇。刽子手的看家本事他样样齐全——天生就带着那股子乐呵劲儿——所以听说他入了伙，你们个个都该欢喜，因为只要刽子手杰克·凯奇做了咱们的朋友，绞架台上那顶黑帽子就甭想罩到咱们头上。诸位，敬杰克·凯奇。”于是众人兴高采烈，齐齐为杰尔克举盏；杰尔克得了一只扁瓶，回敬了大家，随后由司事一位一位地引见过去。“这位是别西卜（Beelzebub），想当年你放翻过整整齐齐一打缉私蠢货，是不是，别西卜？这位是恶魔贝尔奇（Belch），咱们魔骑里有史以来最出色的骑手；这位是撒旦（Satan）；这位是猫眼儿（Cat'seyes），storybook 上也数不出的顶古怪的老巫婆，我敢打赌，”如此等等，直到一大堆稀奇古怪的名字通通塞进杰尔克的脑壳，直塞得他晕头转向。不过，等他自己那套特别的制服捧出来给他穿上，他的兴致又陡然回涨；米普斯还没把那身怪里怪气的破烂给他套到一半，杰尔克就已经敢赌咒说，那面裂了纹的旧镜子里照见的根本不是他自己。

“眼下呢，杰克·凯奇，”米普斯说，“你只消跟我进棺材铺去，领你那一份魔鬼脸霜（devil's face cream），我想你自己就会觉着称心如意。”

===Original===
Into the weird coffin shop accordingly Jerry followed the sexton, and there was that black cauldron that he remembered so well. Now he would discover its use. Mipps stirred the contents and with a great brush began daubing Jerry's face. The curious smell made the youngster close his eyes and he felt the brush pass over them.

"Now," said the sexton, "I blows out the candles and you shall see." Jerry opened his eyes as the sexton blew out the lights. "Bring in the mirror!" called the sexton to the other room. And then into the coffin shop came the other members of the company, and the mystery of the demon riders was explained, for in the dark room each diabolical face glistened like the moon, and when the cracked mirror had been held up before him he saw that he in his turn burned with the same hellfire. "It's now time, Satan, to get the scarecrow in, and you, Beelzebub, go and paint the horses with what's left in that cauldron."

===Chinese===
杰里于是跟着司事走进那间古怪的棺材铺，那口他记得烂熟的黑锅果然就在那儿。如今他可要见识它的用场了。米普斯把锅里的东西搅了搅，拿起一把大刷子，开始往杰里脸上涂抹。那股怪味呛得小伙子闭上了眼睛，他觉得刷子从眼皮上扫了过去。

“好了，”司事说，“我吹熄蜡烛，你就看见了。”司事吹灭灯火，杰里睁开眼睛。“把镜子抬进来！”司事朝隔壁屋里喊。于是班子里其余的人鱼贯走进棺材铺，魔骑的谜底就此揭穿：黑屋子里，一张张魔鬼的面孔月亮似的放光；待那面裂纹镜举到他面前，他看见自己也轮上了同样一团地狱火。“这会儿该办正事了，撒旦，把稻草人（scarecrow）搬进来；还有你，别西卜，提上锅里剩下的，给马匹上漆去。”

===Original===
Beelzebub obeyed the sexton promptly and, picking up the cauldron, went to the back of the house, Satan accompanying him on his different errand—namely, that of bringing in the scarecrow, a thing that puzzled Jerry exceedingly.

Mipps seemed to read his thoughts, for he approached and whispered: "Jack Ketch, you're a-wonderin' about the scarecrow now, ain't you? Well, you've noticed him, I dare say, all dressed in black, at the bottom of my turnip field, ain't you?"

"Yes," replied the new christened Jack Ketch; "I've noticed him as long as I can remember, and a very lifelike scarecrow I considers him to be."

"You're right," replied the sexton; "it's the best scarecrow I ever seed, for it's lifelike and no mistake, and if you keeps your eyes open you'll see him a bit more lifelike tonight—you wait."

===Chinese===
别西卜即刻遵命，提起那口锅往宅子后头去了，撒旦陪他同去，办的却是另一桩差事——也就是把稻草人搬进来，这桩事可把杰里纳闷得厉害。

米普斯似乎看穿了他的心思，凑上前来低声道：“杰克·凯奇，你这会儿正琢磨那个稻草人呢，是不是？这么说吧，你一定早就留意过他了，我那块芜菁地头上那个一身黑的物件，是不是？”

“是啊，”新受洗的杰克·凯奇答道，“打我记事起就留意他了，我一直当他是顶顶逼真的一个稻草人。”

“你说得不错，”司事回答；“我一辈子见过的稻草人里数他最好，逼真得没治；今晚你要是眼睛放亮些，还能瞧见他更逼真几分——你就等着瞧吧。”

===Original===
Satan soon reappeared bearing on his shoulder the dead lump of the scarecrow. Mipps indicated an old coffin that lay on the floor behind the counter of the shop and Satan at once pushed the scarecrow into it, and covered him with a lid.

"He'll be there till the work's done," said Mipps, "for you see the great man himself rides out at nights as the scarecrow, and if you keep your eyes open you'll spot him. Now, Beelzebub," as that terror reappeared, "I take it that them horses is all ready; so bear in mind that my friend Jack Ketch is new to the game, and stick by him, and good luck to you devils, and may the mists guard the legion from all damned swabs!" And so the company filed out of the Devil's Tiring House after receiving this parting blessing at the hands of the sexton.

===Chinese===
撒旦不久便回来了，肩上扛着稻草人那堆没有生气的玩意儿。米普斯朝铺子柜台后头地板上卧着的一口旧棺材一指，撒旦立刻把稻草人塞了进去，盖上棺材盖。

“活儿干完之前，他就躺在那儿，”米普斯说，“要知道，那位大人物本人夜里出门，扮的就是这个稻草人；你眼睛放尖些，准能把他认出来。好了，别西卜，”恰在那个煞星转来的时候说，“那几匹马想必都备齐了；记着我这位朋友杰克·凯奇是头一回干这行，你贴身照应着他。祝你们交好运，魔崽子们，愿雾气护佑我们全班人马，叫那些该死的蠢货一个也摸不着边！”于是众人得了司事这场临别的祝福，鱼贯走出魔鬼的化妆室。

===Original===
"Ain't you coming along, Hellspite?" said one of the ghastly crew to the sexton.

"No, Pontius Pilate, I ain't," replied Mipps, "for me and the blunderbuss is a-goin' to watch that damned meddlesome captain."

And so they left him there, Beelzebub leading Jerry by the hand out of the back door of Old Tree Cottage.

===Chinese===
“你不一块儿去么，地狱煞（Hellspite）？”那伙鬼气森森的人里有一个问司事。

“不去，本丢·彼拉多（Pontius Pilate），不去，”米普斯回答，“我和我这把喇叭铳要留下，盯着那个该死的多管闲事的上校。”

就这样，众人把他撂在了那儿，别西卜牵着杰里的手，从老树农舍的后门走了出去。

## XXVII. The Scarecrow's Legion / 二十七　稻草人军团

===Original===
The company found their steeds in the turnip field at the back of the house, guarded so religiously during the daytime by the old scarecrow that now reposed in the coffin—horses and ponies decked out with weird trappings and all tethered to a low fence that bordered one of the dykes. Jerry’s horse, or, rather, the missing schoolmaster’s horse, was brought to him by Beelzebub himself, whom Jerry very soon discovered to be a most entertaining and affable devil. It was fortunate indeed for Jerry that he was a good rider, and had a knowledge of the Marsh, for the cavalcade immediately set out across the fields, breaking into a high gallop, leaping dykes and sluices in such a reckless fashion that it was a marvel indeed to the boy that his old scrag-bones could keep pace with them; for Beelzebub rode at his side on a strong farm horse and kept urging him to a higher speed. It was nothing more nor less than a haphazard cross-country steeplechase, and the young adventurer was caught in the thrill of it. How exhilarating it was to ride through the night with those reckless fellows, but he would not altogether have relished it had Beelzebub not proved himself such an indefectible and capable pilot.

“Heels in hard, Jack Ketch, when I tell you. Now!” And hard went the heels in, and neck to neck went the horses straight at the broad dyke. “Yoikes!” And up they would go, crashing down again into the rush tops on the far side. And in this way they traversed the Marsh for six miles till they reached the high road under Lympne Hill. There they drew rein at a spot where three roads met. At the bend of these roads Jerry could see a man on a tall gray horse.

“That’s the Scarecrow,” whispered Beelzebub. “That’s the great man hisself.”

===Chinese===
大伙儿在屋后那片芜菁田里找到了各自的坐骑——白日里，它们一向由那个老稻草人虔诚地看守着，此刻那稻草人正安卧在棺材里；马匹矮马个个披着怪模怪样的披挂，全拴在临着一条沟渠的矮篱笆上。杰里的马——说得更准些，是那位失踪的学堂先生的马——由别西卜（Beelzebub）亲自牵了过来；杰里很快便发觉，这位是个顶有趣、顶和气的魔鬼。杰里真是福气：他骑术高明，又识得湿地上的路径，因为这支人马随即便出发横越田野，纵马狂奔，飞越沟渠和水闸，那股不管不顾的劲头，叫这孩子暗自称奇：不知他那匹老瘦骨怎么竟跟得上大伙；只因别西卜骑着一匹壮实的农用马走在他身旁，一个劲儿催他再快些。这简直就是一场全无章法的原野越野赛马，小小冒险家叫那份刺激整个儿攫住了。同这班亡命之徒连夜驰骋，是何等痛快的事；不过，若不是别西卜显出那样一副万无一失、精干可靠的向导本事，他未必能尽兴。

“脚跟给我夹紧马肚子，杰克·凯奇，听我号令再动。起！”话音未落，脚跟狠狠夹下，两匹马并辔齐驱，笔直朝宽阔的沟渠冲去。“嘞——嗬！”马儿腾身跃起，又轰然落进对岸的灯芯草丛里。就这样，他们纵马横穿湿地六英里，直到利姆讷山（Lympne Hill）脚下的大路。在那里，他们在三岔路口勒住了缰绳。就在岔路的转弯处，杰里望见一个骑着灰色高头大马的人。

“那就是稻草人（the Scarecrow），”别西卜低声道，“那就是大头领他本人。”

===Original===
One of the jack-o’-lanterns trotted off on his pony toward this figure, and Jerk saw him salute the Scarecrow, who handed him a paper. Saluting again, the youngster came back to Beelzebub, who took the paper from him and read it carefully by the light of the young jack’s lantern. These boys carried lanterns fixed upon long poles, bearing them standard fashion as they rode. As he was reading, Beelzebub kept catching in his breath in an excited manner, and as he tucked the paper away in his belt he muttered: “May the Marsh be good to the Scarecrow tonight!” Jerry instinctively looked down the road to where the Scarecrow had been standing, but horse and rider had disappeared. “Ah! Jack Ketch,” said Beelzebub, “you are wondering wot’s become of him, eh? You’d need an eye of quicksilver to keep sight of him. Here, there, and everywhere, and all at once he is, and astride the finest horse on Romney Marsh, a horse wot ’ud make the Prince Regent’s mouth water, a horse more valuable to the Scarecrow than the Bank of England ’ud be.”

“But where’s he gone to?” asked Jerry.

“About his business and thine, Jack Ketch,” answered Beelzebub.

“I wish I’d seen him go,” returned Jerry, “for I likes to see a good horse on the move. He went very silent, didn’t he?”

===Chinese===
一个鬼灯笼骑着矮马小跑，朝那身影迎了上去；杰尔克望见他向稻草人行了个礼，稻草人递给他一张纸。那小子又行一礼，回到别西卜跟前；别西卜从他手里接过纸来，就着小鬼灯笼的灯笼光仔细读了起来。这些孩子都提着装在长杆上的灯笼，骑在马上像扛旗杆一般高高擎着。别西卜一面读，一面兴奋得不住倒抽凉气；把纸掖进腰带的时候，他咕哝道：“愿湿地今夜善待稻草人！”杰里下意识地顺着大路望向稻草人方才立着的地方，可人马已无影无踪。“啊，杰克·凯奇，”别西卜说，“你正纳闷他上哪儿去了，是吧？你得长一双水银做的眼睛，才盯得住他。他东也在，西也在，处处都在，还处处同时都在；跨下的又是罗姆尼湿地顶好的一匹马——一匹叫摄政王瞧着都要淌口水的马；对稻草人来说，这匹马比英格兰银行还金贵。”

“可他上哪儿去了？”杰里问。

“办他的事，也是办你的事，杰克·凯奇，”别西卜答道。

“我倒真想瞧瞧他走的模样，”杰里回嘴道，“我就爱看好马跑起来的样子。他走得一点声息也没有，是不是？”

===Original===
“You’ll never hear the noise of the Scarecrow’s horse a-trottin’, Jack Ketch, ’cos he’s got pads on his hoofs. Ah! he’s up to some tricks, is the Scarecrow, and, by hell! he’ll need ’em tonight.”

“Why?” asked Jerk.

“Because he’s had word passed from Hellspite that the King’s men are out, and Scarecrow thinks as how we may have to fight ’em.”

“And don’t you want to do that?”

“Why, you see it ’ud be awkward if any of us got wounded, as wounded men ain’t easy things to hide in a village now, is they? and it ’ud be a difficult business to explain. Though, come to that, Scarecrow ain’t never put out for an explanation o’ nothing.”

===Chinese===
“稻草人的马跑起来，你永远听不见响动，杰克·凯奇，因为他那马蹄上包着毡垫。啊！稻草人这就要耍他的把戏了，他这个人呀——凭地狱起誓！今夜他还非得耍不可。”

“为什么？”杰尔克问。

“因为他得了地狱煞（Hellspite）递来的口信，说国王的人出动了；稻草人估摸着，咱们兴许得跟他们真刀真枪地干一场。”

“难道你们不想干上一场？”

“嗐，你想想，咱们要是有谁挂了彩，那可就尴尬了；伤员如今在村子里可不是好藏的物件，是不是？要解释起来也够呛。不过话又说回来，稻草人可从来不为任何事情费心去解释。”

===Original===
As he was speaking, Beelzebub took Jerry’s rein and started off again at the head of the cavalcade. Their way was now along the road the Scarecrow had gone, and when they had ridden for about half a mile they again sighted him, sitting his horse stock-still in the middle of the road, but this time he was not alone, for there were some half-dozen men leading pack-ponies from the road into a large field. Toward this field Beelzebub led his cavalcade, and consequently they had to pass the grim figure called the Scarecrow. Jerry was ambitious to get a near view of this strange personage, for he wanted if possible to pierce his disguise and see if he could recognize the features. But the nearer he got, the stranger the strange figure became. If it was anyone that he knew, then it was only the scarecrow in Mipps’s turnip field, for he was as like that as two peas are alike to each other.

And the voice was not like any voice he could put an owner to, although there was something familiar in it. It was a hard, metallic voice, the voice of a commander.

“The King’s men are watching the Mill House Farm, so, Beelzebub, you will circle the pack-ponies as usual till we get half a mile from the house, then you will cut off and decoy them from the rear. If your attack is sudden and fierce they will have all they can do to defend themselves, and so that will afford the Mill House Farm men time to get their pack-ponies in with the others. I will see that they get them away safely, and when you have shaken off the King’s men pick us up again on the Romney road opposite Littlestone Beech. Understood?”

“Understood, Scarecrow, understood,” replied Beelzebub promptly.

===Chinese===
说话间，别西卜抓住杰里的缰绳，又在队伍头里动身了。他们如今走的正是稻草人方才去的方向；跑了约莫半英里，他们又望见了他：他纹丝不动地骑在马上，停在路中央；只是这一回他并非独自一人，另有五六条汉子，正牵着驮马从大路引进一大片田里。别西卜便领着人马朝那片田去，如此便须打那尊唤作稻草人的冷面身影跟前经过。杰里一心想凑近了瞧瞧这位怪人，指望能看破他的伪装，认出那张脸究竟是谁。可他越凑近，那怪身影便越发古怪。若说他像哪个相识的人，那便只有米普斯芜菁田里的那个稻草人，因为他同那一个，就像两颗豌豆一般相像。

那嗓音也不像他叫得出主人的任何声音，虽然其中自有一番耳熟之处。那是一种生硬的、金属质地的嗓音，是发号施令者的嗓音。

“国王的人正盯着磨坊农庄（Mill House Farm），所以，别西卜，你照老规矩先把驮马带出去兜圈子，等咱们到了离农庄半英里的地方，你就脱开队伍，从背后把他们引开。只要你们攻得又快又猛，他们就自顾不暇，只能保命招架，磨坊农庄的人手便趁这个工夫把他们的驮马并进大队。我自会照应他们把马安全带走；等你甩脱了国王的人，就在利特尔斯通海滩（Littlestone Beech）对面的罗姆尼大路上同我们会合。听明白了？”

“明白了，稻草人，明白了。”别西卜立时应道。

===Original===
“And,” went on the strange man, “you will stick by Jack Ketch as far as possible, and don’t let him get into any needless danger. I want him to see all the fun that is possible, but I don’t want any hurt to come to him. If I alter the plans, I’ll pass the word. Understood?”

“Understood, Scarecrow, understood,” repeated Beelzebub.

“Then off you go!”

And off they did go, the pack-ponies, trotting under their heavy loads of wool, keeping along the edges of the field, and this with a very good purpose, for where the dykes run zigzag over Romney Marsh a thick mist arises some eight feet high, and even upon nights of full moon these mists hang about the dykes like heavy rolls of a spider’s web, contrasting strangely with the rest of the country, which is all bright and easily seen. And now Jerk had to ride even faster than before, for the pack-ponies, entirely hidden by the mist curtains, were circled and circled all the way by the galloping demons and jack-o’-lanterns, these last swinging their pole lights round their heads and uttering strange cries like those of the Marsh fowl, weird and ominous. This accounted, then, for all the ghost tales he had heard, for all the ghostly things those not in the secret had seen upon the Marsh, and a very clever scheme Jack thought it was, and a very good way of clearing the ground of the curious. For there is no power like superstition, and nothing that spreads quicker or is more grossly exaggerated than tales of horror and fear. So on they rode in wild circles round and round the pack-ponies. Beelzebub was the actual leader. He it was who gave the orders, but the mysterious Scarecrow would dash out of the mist every now and again just to see that all was well with the legion, and then as quickly would he disappear, borne away like a ghost upon that spectral gray thoroughbred.

===Chinese===
“还有，”怪人接着说道，“你要尽力照应着杰克·凯奇，别让他陷进不必要的危险。凡是有热闹可看的地方，我都要让他看个够，可不许他伤着一根汗毛。我若变更计划，自会传话。听明白了？”

“明白了，稻草人，明白了。”别西卜重复道。

“那就出发吧！”

驮马队果真出发了，驮着沉甸甸的羊毛，沿着田边小步快跑；这一手自有一番用意：罗姆尼湿地上，凡沟渠曲折蜿蜒之处，便会升起约莫八英尺高的浓雾；即便在月圆之夜，这些雾气也像一卷卷沉甸甸的蛛网，缠绕在沟渠上，同四下里清亮澄澈、一览无余的原野形成古怪的对照。这下杰尔克只得比先前骑得更快了，因为驮马队整个藏在雾幕之后，一路都被疾驰的魔鬼和鬼灯笼兜着圈子；鬼灯笼们抡着长杆灯，绕着脑袋呼呼挥舞，口中发出湿地水禽一般的怪叫，瘆人而又不祥。他从前听来的种种鬼话，至此全有了着落；不知内情的人在湿地上瞧见的种种鬼影，也全有了着落。杰克心想，这条计策实在高明，是清空地面、打发好奇闲人的绝好法子。要知道，世上再没有比迷信更大的力量；也没有什么东西比骇人听闻的恐怖故事传得更快、夸大得更离谱。于是他们继续驱马狂奔，绕着驮马队一圈又一圈地兜。别西卜才是实际领队，发号施令的都是他；而神秘的稻草人则时不时从雾里蹿将出来，看一眼军团诸事顺遂，随即又倏然隐没，骑着那匹幽灵似的灰色纯种马，鬼魅一般飘然而去。

===Original===
Jerry of course knew the terror with which the pallid host could affect the unwary wayfarer—for had he not seen them himself on the night of Sennacherib’s murder?—but had he needed other proof he would have got it in the case of a small encampment of gypsies. They were not a recognized band of gypsies, but a wandering family, tramping from town to town, from village to village, getting what they could here and what they shouldn’t there, to keep the poor life in their bodies. The gallopers came upon them in a ditch. They had lanterns there and a small fire around which three men and a young lad were sleeping. There was an old crone rocking herself to sleep on one side of the fire, and opposite, between two of the sleeping men, was a younger woman. Her garments were tattered and ragged to the last degree, and her shoulders and arms showed bare, for she had wrapped her shawl round the babe that was crying in her arms. The sudden appearance of the awful riders spread instant panic in this little circle. The old crone shrieked to her menfolk to awake, but before they could get to their feet the horses were upon them. Beelzebub, with daredevil precision, rode straight through the wood fire, his horse bellowing with fright as he scattered the crackling sticks. The young mother just avoided Jerry’s horse as he came crashing through after Beelzebub, and the shriek of fear that she gave made Jerry turn heartsick as he reined in his mount.

“An ill-famed baggage, I’ll be sworn,” said Beelzebub. “’Twould have been a good thing had you ridden her down, and as for the brat, such devil spawn should be put out of their misery.”

“Now I should have thought devil spawn would have had rather a way with us.” At which sally Beelzebub clapped Jerk on the back, and declared that he was a good Ketch, a remarkable good Ketch, and as the young recruit had all he could do saving his own neck every minute as they leaped backward and forward over the dyke, this unpleasant episode was forgotten, or, rather, slid back into his brain like the memory of a nightmare slides when we dream again. On they dashed, but stopping at numerous farms on the way, where they always found more pack-ponies waiting to join the cavalcade. And the Scarecrow was always somewhere. As soon as any little hitch occurred—as one frequently did when the men placed the temporary bridge over the dykes for the transit of the pack-ponies—the Scarecrow would suddenly appear in their midst, giving sharp orders, whose prompt obedience meant an instant end to the difficulty, whatever it chanced to be. But it was the laying of this same temporary bridge that caused most of the delays, for it was a cumbersome thing to move about, and it had to be built strong enough to support the weight of the pack-ponies. These ponies, too, caused considerable bother at some periods of the march, as their packs of wool would sometimes shake loose from the harness, and the cavalcade would have to stop while this was being remedied. But although the pack-ponies stopped often, the demon riders were never allowed that luxury. Beelzebub untiringly flagged the horse round and round, now in large circuits, now in small circles, always ringing in the pack-ponies from any prying eyes. It would have meant death to anyone who got a view within that sweeping scythe of cavalry. And as murders on the Marsh were all put down to Marsh devils, except in the case of Sennacherib Pepper—for there was then a likely assassin known to be at large upon the Marsh to lay the deed to—and because of the dreaded superstition that had grown in the minds of Kentish folk, the smugglers were utterly callous as to what crimes they perpetrated, for they were as safe from the law as the most law-abiding citizen, for those who didn’t credit the existence of murdering hobgoblins at least possessed sufficient fear of the smugglers themselves to leave them alone; for, after all, it was no business of anyone but the revenue men, and so to the revenue men were they left, and in nearly every record it may be seen that the revenue men got the worst of it.

===Chinese===
那支面色惨白的人马能给粗心赶路的人带来怎样的惊吓，杰里自然心中有数——西拿基立遇害的那一夜，他不是亲眼见过吗？——倘若还需要别的凭证，一小伙吉卜赛人扎营的遭遇便是现成的例证。他们并非正经成帮结伙的吉卜赛人，只是一户流浪人家，走镇串村，这儿能捞的捞一点，那儿不该拿的也拿一点，好叫那条可怜的性命苟延下去。那队疾驰的人马在一条沟渠边撞见了他们。他们打着灯笼，燃着一小堆火，三个男人和一个半大孩子正围着火睡觉。火堆一侧，一个干瘪老太婆摇着身子打盹；对面，两个熟睡的男人中间，是个年轻些的女人。她衣衫褴褛到了极点，肩头和胳膊全露在外面，因为她把披巾裹在了怀里啼哭的婴孩身上。可怕的骑手猝然现身，这一小圈人登时乱作一团。老太婆尖声叫喊，要自家男人快醒，可他们还没来得及站起身，马已经冲到了眼前。别西卜精准得近乎玩命，径直从那堆篝火上冲了过去；柴枝噼啪四散，他的马吓得嘶声悲鸣。杰里紧跟着别西卜冲将过去，年轻母亲堪堪避开了他的马；她那一声惊恐的尖叫，听得杰里心头翻搅，勒住了坐骑。

“我敢起誓，这准是个名声狼藉的贱货，”别西卜说。“你方才若能纵马把她撞倒，才叫痛快；至于那小崽子，这等魔鬼的孽种，早点送走，倒是免了他们受罪。”

“咦，我倒还以为，魔鬼的孽种见了咱们该更亲热些呢。”听了这句俏皮话，别西卜在杰尔克背上啪地拍了一记，宣称他是个好样儿的凯奇，呱呱叫的好凯奇；再说，这新入伙的每分钟都得使出浑身解数保住自家脖颈——他们正前前后后地在沟渠上腾跃——这段不愉快的小插曲便也就撂下了，或者说，退回了他的脑海深处，就像我们重新做梦时，噩梦的记忆便悄悄滑了回去。他们继续飞驰，途中在好些农庄歇脚，每处总都有更多的驮马候在那里，等着加入队伍。而稻草人总在什么地方。一有半点岔子——汉子们往沟渠上架设临时便桥、好让驮马通过时，岔子是常有的——稻草人便会突然出现在众人中间，发出一串干脆利落的号令；号令一经雷厉风行地照办，不管眼下是什么麻烦，霎时便烟消云散。不过，耽搁得最多的也恰恰是架这座便桥：那物件挪动起来笨重得很，又必须搭得够结实，吃得住驮马的分量。行进到某些路段，这些矮马也添了不少乱：驮着的羊毛包有时会从鞍具上晃松散落，队伍便只得停下，等收拾妥当了再走。驮马固然频频停歇，魔骑们却从不获准享受这份清闲。别西卜不知疲倦地挥鞭策马，转了一圈又一圈，忽而兜大圈，忽而兜小圈，始终把驮马队围护在当中，不让任何窥探的眼睛瞧见。谁若瞧见那把骑兵大镰刀扫掠之内的情形，谁就没命了。而湿地上的凶杀案，一概记在湿地魔鬼的账上——唯有西拿基立·佩珀一案除外，因为当时湿地上恰有一个众皆知闻、在逃未获的杀手，这桩血案正可栽到他头上——再加上肯特郡百姓心里那股越积越深的可怕迷信，走私客们对自己犯下的种种罪行便全然无动于衷；他们同最守法的良民一样不受法律的追究：不信吃人妖魔的那路人，至少也怕走私客怕得够呛，轻易不敢招惹他们；说到底，这本是除了缉私员（revenue men）谁也不该管的事，于是便留给缉私员去管；而几乎每一页旧档上都写着：吃了大亏的，正是缉私员。

## XXVIII. The Fight at Mill House Farm / 二十八　磨坊农庄之战

===Original===
Mill House Farm was the last on Beelzebub’s list, and in the dyke facing the house, but on the other side of the high road crouched the King’s men, commanded by the captain’s bo’sun. They were as still as mice, for the captain had given strict orders to the bo’sun on that score, but they need not have put themselves to such pains, for owing to the extreme vigilance of Sexton Mipps the smugglers knew exactly where they were and what they were going to do.

Now it is depressing to the most seasoned fighters to have to crouch for hours in a soaking muddy dyke waiting for an outnumbering enemy; for it was common knowledge that if smuggling was carried on upon the Marsh, it was well manipulated and relied for its secrecy upon the strength and numbers of its assistants. So the bo’sun had no easy task in keeping his men from grumbling; for whatever Captain Collyer’s opinion may have been with regard to maintaining the law according to his duty, it was pretty evident that his men had no great relish for the task, and the bo’sun heartily wished that the captain had not left him responsible, for his absence was having a poor effect upon the men, and the unfortunate bo’sun was greatly afraid that they would fail to put up a good fight when the time came. It is one thing to fight an enemy, but quite another to shoot down your own countrymen, and although every man jack of them was itching for the French war, they felt no enthusiasm for this suppression of smuggling, for the whole of the countryside would have taken the side of the lawbreakers, and who knows how many of these same King’s men had not themselves done a very profitable trade with the illegal cargoes from France.

These were the feelings that existed as the King’s men lay in the dyke opposite Mill House Farm, listening to the noise of ponies’ hoofs in the yard, and waiting to fire upon anyone who presented himself.

===Chinese===
磨坊农庄（Mill House Farm）是别西卜（Beelzebub）单子上最后一处地方；正对农舍的水沟里，就在大路另一侧，趴着国王的人马，由上校的水手长（bo’sun）统领。他们屏息静气，纹丝不敢动，因为在这上头，上校给水手长下过严格的命令；其实这般辛苦大可不必——多亏司事米普斯（Sexton Mipps）眼观六路，走私贩子对他们身在何处、将要干什么，早已一清二楚。

再有耐战的沙场老手，也得在泥水浸透的沟里一连几个钟头蹲着，眼巴巴候着人数占优的敌人，这滋味总归够丧气的；因为湿地上的事人人心里有数：走私既然干得起来，必然调度有方，其机密全仗帮手的人多势众才保得住。所以水手长要弹压住手下的牢骚，着实不是件轻省差事；且不说科利尔上校（Captain Collyer）奉职守法抱的是何等主张，他手下那班人对这桩差事没有多大胃口，却是一眼就能看明的。水手长打心底里巴望上校没把这担子撂给他一个人；上校一走，弟兄们的劲头眼见着往下塌，这倒霉的水手长真怕到了节骨眼上，他们打不出一场像样的仗。跟敌人开打是一回事，朝自己的同胞开枪可是另一回事；他们虽然个个都巴不得去打法国那场仗，对这档子镇压走私的营生却提不起半点热忱——四乡的百姓本就都向着犯法的人那边，再说，谁知道这些国王的人马里头，有多少不曾跟法国来的违禁货色做过大赚其钱的买卖呢。

国王的人马趴在磨坊农舍对面那条水沟里，听着院子里小马蹄声杂沓，候着朝任何露头的人开枪，心里翻腾的便是这些滋味。

===Original===
But the order “Not to kill, but to fire low,” also damped their spirits, for what chance would they have against desperate fellows keeping their necks out of the rope, who would not hesitate but would rather aim to kill?

The bo’sun had great difficulty in preventing one old seadog who lay next him in the ditch from voicing his opinion of the proceedings in a loud bass voice, but what he did say he after all had the good grace to whisper, though a whisper that was none too soft at that.

===Chinese===
而那道“不许杀人、只许朝低处放枪”的命令，也一样浇得他们心头发凉；对面那伙亡命徒是脖子上拴着绞索讨生活的人，开起枪来断不会迟疑，只会一心往死里打，跟这样的人对峙，他们又能有多大胜算？

水沟里，一个老海狼紧挨着水手长趴着，非要扯开他那口低音嗓子，对眼前这番举动发表高见；水手长好一番拦阻，才没让他嚷出来；不过他到底还算识趣，改用了耳语——只是那耳语实在也算不得轻。

===Original===
“What the hell’s the sense, Mr. Bo’sun, of sending good seamen like we be to die like dogs in this blamed ditch? Ain’t England got no use for seamen nowadays? ’Tain’t the members of Parleyment wot’ll serve her when it comes to fighting, though they does talk so very pleasant.”

“They don’t talk as much as you do,” was the hushed retort of the bo’sun.

“Look ye ’ere, Job Mallet,” went on the seadog, “you’ve been shipmate o’ mine fer longer than I well remembers, and you be in command here. Well, I ain’t a-kickin’ against your authority, mind you, but I’m older than you be, and I want to voice my opinion to you, which is also the opinion of every mother’s son in this damned ditch. Why don’t we clear out of this and be done with the folly? We looks to you, Job Mallet, I say we looks to you as our bo’sun, and a very good bo’sun you be, we looks to you, we does, to save us bein’ made fools of. We wants to fight the Frenchies and not our own fellows. The Parleyment’s a-makin’ a great mistake puttin’ down the smugglers. If they only talked nice to ’em they’d find a regiment or two o’ smugglers very handy to fight them ugly Frenchies. For my own part I don’t see why the Parleyment don’t put down other professions for a bit and leave the smugglers alone. Why not give lawyers a turn, eh? They could do with a bit o’ hexposin’! Dirty swabs! And so could the doctors wot sell coloured water for doses. Bah! dirty, dishonest fellows! But, oh, no! It’s always the poor smugglers who be really hardworking fellows; and very good fighters they be, too, as we’ll soon be called upon to see.”

All this time Job Mallet tried to silence him, but threats, persuasions, and arguments were all alike useless.

===Chinese===
“水手长先生，这他妈到底算个什么道理，把咱们这号好水手打发到这该死的水沟里来，像狗一样送死？如今英格兰就用不着水手了不成？真到了打仗那日，能替她效命的可不是那些国会议员老爷，别看他们如今话说得那么中听。”

“人家说话，总没你这么多，”水手长压着嗓子顶了他一句。

“你听着，乔布·马利特（Job Mallet），”老海狼接着说，“你跟我搭伙做船伴，年深日久，我自己都记不清年头了，如今这地界归你管。好，你放心，我不是成心顶撞你的权威，只是我痴长你几岁，有句话要对你说，这话也是这条该死水沟里每一个爷们儿的心里话。咱们何不趁早抽身，省得再犯这份傻？乔布·马利特，咱们都指望着你，我说，咱们都指望着你这个水手长，你也真是个顶好的水手长；咱们指望着你，千真万确指望着你，好歹救救咱们，别叫人当猴儿耍。咱们要打的是法国佬，不是自家人。国会拿走私的开刀，可是铸成了大错。他们只要好言好语相待，就会发现，拉出一两团走私的来对付那帮丑八怪法国佬，顺手得很。依我看，国会怎么不把旁的行当也整治一阵，单单饶过走私的？怎么不让律师也轮上一回，嗯？他们正该给曝光曝光！腌臜的拖把！还有那些拿五颜六色的药水当汤药卖的大夫，也该曝光曝光！呸！腌臜、不老实的货色！可是，哦，不成！遭整治的总是可怜的走私贩子，人家才是真正卖力气干活的人；而且打起来还都是把好手——这一点，咱们很快就要有幸亲眼见识了。”

这段时间，乔布·马利特一直想叫他住口，可是吓也好、劝也好、讲理也好，一概白搭。

===Original===
“Old Collywobbles thinks the same as wot we does.”

“I’ll have you to remember,” whispered the bo’sun stiffly, “that I bein’ in command in this ’ere ditch don’t know as to who you be alludin’ when you say Collywobbles. I don’t know no one of that name.”

“Oh, ain’t you a stickler to duty?” chuckled the seadog. “Still I respec’s you fer it, though p’raps you’ll permit me to remind you as how it was you in the fo’csle of the *Resistance* as gave the respected Captain Howard Collyer, R.N., the pleasant pet name of Collywobbles. Though p’raps that’s slipped your memory for the moment.”

“It has,” answered the bo’sun.

===Chinese===
“老科利闹肚（Collywobbles）心里的盘算，跟咱们一个样。”

“有句话你得给我记住，”水手长硬邦邦地低声道，“如今在这条水沟里当家的是我，你说什么科利闹肚，我不明白你指的是哪一位。叫这个名字的人，我压根不认得。”

“哟，瞧你这忠于职守的劲儿！”老海狼哧哧地笑。“这个我照旧敬你；不过，也许容我提你一句：当年在‘抵抗号’（*Resistance*）的船头水手舱里，把备受尊敬的皇家海军霍华德·科利尔上校奉送了那个顶受用的诨名叫‘科利闹肚’的，不是别人，正是你。当然了，这话兴许一时想不起来了。”

“是给忘了，”水手长答道。

===Original===
“Very well, then, but you can take it from me as how it was, so there, and a very clever name it be, too; but there, you always was one of the clever ones, Job Mallet.”

“I wish I were clever enough to make your fat mouth shut, I do,” muttered the bo’sun.

“Now, then, Job Mallet, don’t you begin getting to personalities. But there, now, I don’t want to quarrel with you. You’ve always had my greatest respec’s, you has, and as we’ll probably be stiff ’uns in a few minutes, we won’t quarrel, old pal. But I give you my word that I don’t like being shot down like a rabbit, and I’m sorry as how it’s you as is in command, ’cos if it was anyone else I declares I’d get up now and walk home to bed.”

“If Captain Collyer was here, you know you’d do nothing of the sort.”

===Chinese===
“好，就算这样，可这事儿有我作保，错不了，就这么着；再说这名字起得也真叫绝；不过话说回来，你一向就是能人里的一个，乔布·马利特。”

“我倒巴不得自己能聪明到把你这张油嘴缝上，真恨不得，”水手长咕哝道。

“哎，行了行了，乔布·马利特，你可别扯到人身上去。不过说真的，我不想跟你吵。我一向顶敬重你，这是实话；再者，咱们过不了几分钟多半就得变挺尸，咱们就不吵了，老伙计。可我跟你说句实在话，我不乐意像只兔子似的给人撂倒；只可惜这会儿当家的是你，换个旁人，我实说了吧，这会儿我就爬起身，一路走回家睡觉去了。”

“上校要是在这儿，你自个儿心里清楚，你才不敢来这一套呢。”

===Original===
“Why, ain’t he here? That’s wot I wants to know. Strike me dead! it’s easy enough to send out poor old seadogs to be shot like bunny rabbits. I could do that. There ain’t no pluck in that, as far as I can see, though p’raps I be wrong, and if I be wrong, well, I’ll own up to it, for I don’t care bein’ put in the wrong of it when I is in the wrong of it.”

“You ain’t a-settin’ a very good example to the young men, I’m thinkin’,” said Job Mallet. “You, the oldest seaman here, and a-grumblin’ and a-gossipin’ like an old housewife. You ought to think shame on yourself, old friend.”

“Oh, well,” growled the other, “I won’t utter another blarsted word, I won’t. But if you does want to know my opinion in these ’ere proceedin’s, it’s—hell!”

“I don’t say as how I don’t agree with you,” returned Job Mallet, “but there it is and we’ve got to make the best of it. It won’t do no good a-grumblin’. We’ll make the best of a bad job, and I hopes as I for one will be able to do my duty, ’cos I don’t relish it no more than you do.”

===Chinese===
“那他怎么就不在这儿呢？我倒想问个明白。雷劈了我吧！打发可怜的老海狼出去，叫人家当小兔子似的打，这法子谁不会？我也会。依我看，这里头半点胆量也没有；也许是我错了吧，要是我错了，好，我自己认，我这个人，错在我自己身上，才不怕认这个错呢。”

“我说，你这榜样给后生们立得可不怎么样，”乔布·马利特说。“船龄数你最老，倒跟个老太婆似的又是咕哝又是嚼舌头。老伙计，你该给自己害臊才是。”

“哦，好，”那一个吼道，“我一个爆粗字也不吐了，不吐了。可你要真想知道我对这档子举动的高见，那就是——见他妈的鬼！”

“我不是说我不赞成你的话，”乔布·马利特回道，“可是事情摆在眼前，咱们总得往好里对付。咕哝顶不了用。烂摊子也得当好摊子过；我巴望我自己好歹能尽职，因为这差事我也跟你一样不稀罕。”

===Original===
“Well, strike me blind, dumb, and deaf!” thundered the seadog in a voice of emotion as he clapped Job Mallet on the back, “if I’ve been a snivellin’ powder monkey I ought to be downright ashamed of myself, and seein’ as how I be the oldest seaman here, instead—well, I’m more than damned downright ashamed. Job Mallet, thank you! You set a good example to us all, Mister Bo’sun, and I’ll stand by you for one. Damn the smugglers, and wait till I get at ’em, that’s all!”

“Thank yer,” said the bo’sun, “but you’ll greatly oblige me by keeping quiet, ’cos here be the smugglers, if I ain’t mistook.”

===Chinese===
“好，劈瞎我、劈哑我、劈聋我吧！”老海狼动了感情，一巴掌拍在乔布·马利特背上，声音都吼出来了，“方才我那副抽抽搭搭的德行，跟个哭鼻子的火药猴子没什么两样，本该臊得慌；何况在这儿数我船龄最长，本该带这个头——得，我他妈臊到家了。乔布·马利特，多谢你！你给咱们大伙儿立了个好榜样，水手长先生，我头一个跟你站到头。叫走私的通通见鬼去吧，就等老子扑上去收拾他们，就这样！”

“多谢了，”水手长说，“不过你要能赏脸消停消停，我就承你的大情了，因为要是我没弄错，走私的来了。”

===Original===
Indeed at that instant along the road came the sound of the sharp, quick steps of the pack-ponies. At present they were hidden in the mist which floated thickly about that part of the Marsh, but they could not only hear the ponies but a sound of a voice singing as well. This voice was raised in a wailing monotone and the words were repeated over and over again. They were intended for the ears of the wretched sailors who were waiting in the ditch for the attack:

“Listen, oh, you good King’s men who are waiting to shoot us from the damp ditch. We have got your kind captain here, a blunderbuss a-looking at the back of his head. If you fire on us, good King’s men, then the blunderbuss will fire at the good captain, and then:

“ ‘All the King’s horses and all the King’s men
					
	
					Could not put captain together again.’ ”

===Chinese===
果然就在这一刻，大路上传来了驮马急促细碎的蹄声。马队这会儿还隐在那片浓雾里——湿地这一带的雾气正浓得漂浮不散——可沟里的人不但听得见马蹄，还听得见一个唱歌的嗓音。那嗓音拖着一腔哀号般的单调调子，翻来覆去只把同一番话唱个没完。那是唱给蹲在沟里候着挨打的一众可怜水兵听的：

“听着，噢，你们这班好心的国王的人马，正蹲在湿漉漉的水沟里，候着朝我们放枪。你们那位仁厚的上校如今在我们手里啦，一杆喇叭口火铳正瞅着他的后脑勺。你们要朝我们放枪，好心的国王的人马，那火铳就要朝好心的上校开火，到那时——

“‘纵有国王的千军和万马
					
	
					也难把上校重新拼作一个囫囵人。’”

===Original===
Even if the words were not sufficient to explain the situation to the sailors, the first figures of the cavalcade were all sufficient. A donkey led by two jack-o’-lanterns on foot jolted out of the fog. Upon its back was a man bound and gagged, supported on either side by two devil-men. That the gagged wretch was the captain needed no words to tell, for his uniform showed by the lantern’s light, and there right behind him, sure enough, was the blunderbuss in question, pointed by a snuffy little devil called by his colleagues Hellspite, who sat hunched up on a shoddy little pony. This little group halted at a convenient distance from the sailors in the ditch, and Hellspite again rehearsed his little speech, ending up with:

“ ‘All the King’s horses and all the King’s men
					
	
					Could not put captain together again.’ ”

===Chinese===
这些话即便还不够叫水兵们明白眼下的局面，马队打头的几条人影可就把一切都说明白了。一头毛驴从雾里颠了出来，牵驴的是两个步行的鬼火儿（jack-o’-lantern）；驴背上捆着一个堵着嘴的人，左右各有一个魔卒搀扶着。那堵嘴的苦主就是上校，这不用谁费一句口舌——灯光底下，他那身制服看得清清楚楚；而他正后方，一点不差，正是那杆话里说的火铳，由一个沾着鼻烟末的小恶魔端着——同伙们都管他叫地狱煞（Hellspite），正弓着身子骑在一匹寒碜的小驽马上。这一小队人在离沟中水兵不远不近的地方停住，地狱煞又把他那套小演说念了一遍，末句是：

“‘纵有国王的千军和万马
					
	
					也难把上校重新拼作一个囫囵人。’”

===Original===
Now the poor bo’sun in command had all his life grown so used to taking other people’s orders that he didn’t know what to do for the best. He liked the captain and didn’t want to see him killed, though he knew what he must be suffering in his ridiculous position. He knew that had the captain but got the use of his speech he would have shouted, “Fire! and be damned to ’em!” But then the captain had not got the use of speech. The Scarecrow and Hellspite knew enough of the man to see to that, and as they had no great desire to be fired at, they had seen that the gags were efficient. So it was, after all, small wonder that the old grumbling seadog next to him, who possessed a rollicking vein of humour, laughed until he rolled back into the mud, for the sight was enough to make the proverbial cat laugh, much less a humorous old tar, and the rest of the men were divided into two classes, some following the example of the bo’sun and being struck stiff with amazement and powerless wrath, others joining the laughing tar in the muddy ditch and guffawing over the ridiculous situation of their captain, for he was not the build of man to sit an ass with any dignity, not being at all akin to a Levantine Jew, but very absurd in his naval uniform, with the cocked hat literally cocked right down over his nose. It was this sudden surprise that made the sailors utterly unprepared for what followed. A large party of horse swept out of the mist behind them, and when they turned to see what fresh thing was amiss there was a gallant line of terrible cavalry pulling up on their haunches a few yards in their rear. Thus they were cut off on both sides: at their back the devils with flaming faces, on horses of alarming proportions, and in front, their captain, waiting for them to shoot, to meet his own death by the little demon’s blunderbuss:

===Chinese===
可怜的当值水手长一辈子听惯了旁人的号令，这会儿全然不知怎么办才好。他喜欢上校，不愿看着他丢命，虽然也明白，落到这副滑稽田地，上校心里该是何等煎熬。他知道，上校只要还能开口，准会大喊一声：“开枪！让他们见鬼去吧！”可上校偏偏出不得声。稻草人（Scarecrow）和地狱煞深知此人脾性，早把这个关节堵得严严实实；他们自己既不大乐意吃枪子儿，自然把那团塞嘴的家什做得十分牢靠。所以话说回来，身旁那个好发牢骚的老海狼，原本就带着一身嘻嘻哈哈的诙谐劲儿，直笑得一个倒仰，翻回泥水里去，原也算不得奇怪：这景象足够叫俗话里说的那只猫都笑出声来，更别提一个滑稽的老水兵了；其余的人也分成了两路，一路学着水手长的样子，惊得浑身发僵，一腔怒火又无从发作；另一路便跟着那个大笑的老水兵，在泥沟里对着他们上校那副滑稽相放声狂笑；因为他那副身架坐在驴背上，实在端不出半点气派——他跟黎凡特（Levant）地方骑驴的犹太人全然不是一路人——一身海军制服穿在身上要多别扭有多别扭，那顶三角帽更是名副其实，端端正正一直扣到鼻梁上。正是这猝不及防的一出，教水兵们对接下来的一幕全无提防。大队人马从他们背后的雾里兜了出来；回头想瞧瞧又出了什么新鲜乱子，只见一列威风凛凛的骇人骑兵已在身后几码远收缰勒马，马匹齐齐人立而起。这么着，他们两头都叫人截断了：背后是一张张火脸的恶魔，骑着大得吓人的高头大马；面前是他们那位上校，专候着他们开枪，好叫小恶魔那杆火铳送掉他自己的性命：

===Original===
“ ‘If you fire, you good King’s men,
					
	
					Then the devil shall blarst your captain.’ ”

“And you as well, you good King’s men!” shrieked and howled the terrible demons at the back, who covered with pistols or blunderbuss every Jack Tar in the ditch.

===Chinese===
“‘你们若开枪，好心的国王的人马
					
	
					恶魔就要轰烂你们的上校。’”

“还有你们这些好心的国王的人马，也一样！”背后那些狰狞的恶魔又是尖叫又是嗥叫，一杆杆手枪、火铳，把沟里每一个水兵都罩得死死的。

===Original===
Then another rider appeared on the scene. He was tall, thin, and of ungainly countenance, and he rode a light gray thoroughbred. He was the Scarecrow, and all the devils hailed him by that name as he appeared. Behind him came the pack-ponies, some sixty or seventy in all, and on each pony was a wool pack that would have meant a human neck to the King’s hangman if only Collyer were free to work his will. The Scarecrow drew up in the road and watched the great procession of ponies pass along toward the coast. When they had all but passed he gave a signal, and the doors of Mill House barn were opened and ten more heavily laden ponies trotted out and joined the snake of illegal commerce that was wriggling away to the sea. Then like some field-marshal upon the field of battle did the Scarecrow slowly ride over a small bridge and then along the front of his demon cavalry. Jerry Jerk heard him give a short order to Beelzebub as he passed, and then saw him gallop away after the pack-ponies. And then came the ordeal for the King’s men, for they were kept in that uncomfortable position for a full two hours, or maybe even longer. Folly to move, folly to fight, there they had to stop—a foolish-looking group of fighting men, if you like, but more foolish had they attempted resistance, for they were outnumbered in men, in arms, and in wits. Once, indeed, did the bo’sun nearly lose his head, and that was when Hellspite lowered his blunderbuss and produced a clay pipe which he lit. The bo’sun saw a chance, spat in his hand, grasped his cutlass, and clambered from the dyke. But instantaneously came the ominous noise of cocking pistols, and the old seadog grabbed the bo’sun’s leg and pulled him back swearing into the mud. Hellspite chuckled and smoked his pipe, the horsemen covered every man in the ditch with cocked weapons, and so another hour passed over the curious group. Suddenly from over the Marsh came the cry of a curlew, weird and repeated seven times. Hellspite put up his pipe and muttered an order to the two devils by the donkey, and then he addressed the sailors:

===Chinese===
跟着，又有一骑上场了。来人又高又瘦，形容粗笨，胯下骑着一匹浅灰色的纯种马。他便是稻草人（Scarecrow），一现身，所有的恶魔齐声高呼他这个名号。他身后便是驮马队，总数约莫六七十匹，每匹驮的都是羊毛包——只消科利尔能遂愿行事，每一包羊毛，本都意味着国王的绞刑吏跟前的一条人命。稻草人在大路上勒住马，看着这一长列驮马浩浩荡荡往海边去。眼见得快要过完，他打了个信号，磨坊农舍谷仓的门应声而开，又有十匹满载的驮马小跑出来，汇入那条蜿蜒而去、直奔海边的非法买卖长蛇。随后，稻草人恰似战场上的一位元帅，缓缓骑过一座小桥，又沿着他那队魔骑的阵前走过。杰里·杰尔克（Jerry Jerk）听见他经过时朝别西卜短短吩咐了一句，接着便见他打马飞奔，追赶驮马队去了。接下去，轮到国王的人马受熬煎了：他们就那样别别扭扭地钉在原地，足足两个钟头，也许还不止。动是蠢，打也是蠢，他们只得停在那儿——你要说这是一伙瞧着顶蠢的战斗人员，也随你；可他们若当真试图抵抗，还要更蠢：论人数、论兵器、论心眼，他们都不占上风。的确有那么一回，水手长险些沉不住气：那回地狱煞放低了火铳，摸出一只陶土烟斗，点上了。水手长一见有机可乘，往手心里啐了口唾沫，攥住弯刀，从沟里攀了上来。可眨眼间便是一片不祥的咔嗒声，手枪扳机齐刷刷扳开了；老海狼一把攥住水手长的腿，把他拽回泥里，嘴里骂骂咧咧。地狱煞哧哧笑着抽他的烟斗，骑马的人们端着支好的枪，把沟里每一个人都罩住；就这样，这群古怪人马的头顶上又过了一个钟头。忽然，湿地那边传来一声麻鹬的啼叫，古古怪怪，一连七声。地狱煞收起烟斗，朝毛驴旁边的两个恶魔低低吩咐了一句，随后便向水兵们发话：

===Original===
“Now, good sailors, we will trouble you for your arms. Pass them up to good Job Mallet and he shall stretch his legs and lay them at my feet.”

But again Job Mallet lost his head. He arose in the ditch and sang out bravely: “You and the rest of you are damned cowards in silencing the mouth of our captain. Had he his voice you know what he’d say—‘Shoot and be damned to you!’ and well you know it. Why don’t you meet us in fair fight, you damned cowards, instead of using such devil’s tricks?”

“’Cos we ain’t so bloody-minded as the good King’s bo’sun,” answered Hellspite in a piping voice, which drew forth a great laugh from the devils.

===Chinese===
“好了，好水兵们，有劳各位把家伙交出来。递给好心的乔布·马利特，让他伸伸腿脚，把家伙摆到我脚跟前。”

可这一回，乔布·马利特又失了方寸。他从沟里直起身，放声大喝：“你们这伙人，堵住我们上校的嘴，全是天杀的懦夫。他若能开口，你们知道他会说什么——‘开枪，让你们见鬼去！’你们心里雪亮。你们为什么不出来跟我们真刀真枪干一场，你们这班天杀的懦夫，偏要玩这等魔鬼的把戏？”

“因为我们不像好心的国王的水手长那样嗜杀成性，”地狱煞用一副尖细嗓音答道，惹得恶魔们哄然大笑。

===Original===
One of the seamen, considering that all eyes were now upon the bo’sun, leaped from the ditch and made a rush for Hellspite with his naked cutlass. Five or six pistols cracked behind him and over he fell, face downward in the road. Every shot had taken effect: he was dead.

“Oh, do keep your little heads, you silly King’s men!” wailed Hellspite, “for look how we’ve spoiled that nice little man. He’s no use now to fight the French, no use at all. Oh, what a pity, what a pity, what a pity!”

Again came the cry of the curlew, seven times.

===Chinese===
一个水兵估摸着这会儿众人的眼睛都盯在水手长身上，便从沟里一跃而出，抡着出鞘的弯刀直扑地狱煞。身后五六支手枪砰然齐响，他一头栽倒，脸朝下扑在大路上。颗颗子弹都着了肉：他死了。

“唉，快收好你们的小脑袋吧，傻乎乎的国王的人马！”地狱煞哀声叹气，“瞧瞧，我们把那么可爱的小人儿给糟蹋了。他如今打法国佬是不中用喽，一点也不中用喽。哦，可惜呀，可惜呀，可惜！”

麻鹬的啼声又起，还是七声。

===Original===
“Now, then, those weapons!” ordered Hellspite sharply, “and if they don’t come along quick we’ll put this captain out of service along with his man there.”

There was nothing for it but to obey. They were in the demons’ power. The sailors had found that the smugglers were good shots and that they meant business. No, there was nothing for it but to hand over their arms to the bo’sun, who with bad grace laid them upon the roadway, whence they were picked up by the jack-o’-lanterns, who bore them into the barn.

“Now, then, my fine fellows,” said Hellspite, “we’ll plump this ’ere captain on the road. You will pick him up if you want him and take him home to bed, for the dawn ain’t far off, and as the wool packs are safe and away, we’ll bid you good repose.”

===Chinese===
“好了，快些，家伙什！”地狱煞厉声喝道，“再不痛快交上来，我们就叫这位上校跟他那边的伙计一样，退役报废。”

不服从是不成的了。他们落在了恶魔的手心里。水兵们已经领教：走私的枪法精准，而且说到做到。是了，除了把兵器交给水手长，再没有别的法子；水手长老大不情愿地把兵器撂在大路上，鬼火儿们随后拾起，搬进了谷仓。

“好了，我的俊小伙们，”地狱煞说，“我们把这位上校往大路上一撂。想要他，你们就自个儿把他捡起来，抱回家睡觉去；天眼看就要亮了，羊毛包也已经稳稳当当走远了，我们就此祝各位安歇。”

===Original===
The captain was accordingly lifted from the donkey and laid upon the road. The sailors were filed up around him, and conducted ingloriously back to the vicarage barn. Three devils, having been told off for the purpose, bore away the body of the dead seaman, so that before the dawn lit up the Marsh there was no sign of smugglers anywhere, and Jerry Jerk, after disrobing with the others at the coffin shop, was packed off home to bed by Beelzebub, where, without disturbing his grandparents, he fell immediately to sleep, and dreamed his whole adventure over again.

===Chinese===
于是上校被人从驴背上抱下来，放在了大路上。水兵们鱼贯集合到他身旁，灰头土脸地被押回牧师宅的谷仓。三个恶魔奉了专差，抬走了那水兵的尸首；所以等到天光照亮湿地，四下里早已不见半点走私贩子的踪影。杰里·杰尔克同大伙儿一道在棺材铺卸了行头，便叫别西卜打发回家睡觉；他到家没惊动爷爷奶奶，倒头便睡，一夜又把这场历险原原本本梦了一遍。

===Original===
Just as the dawn was breaking Mipps was returning from the vicarage barn, where he had deposited a bundle of weapons outside the door, when he saw a yellow-faced man creeping along the field by the churchyard wall. As he watched the figure disappear into a deep dyke he muttered: “I wonder if that there thing is real or unreal? I wonder if he did get off that reef in his body? If he did, what the blarsted hell’s he findin’ to live upon? and if he ain’t—well, God help one of us in this ’ere place!” And he scurried back to the coffin shop like a sneaking rat.

===Chinese===
天光将破未破之际，米普斯从牧师宅谷仓折返回来——他把一捆兵器撂在了谷仓门外——忽见一条汉子贴着教堂墓地墙根的田垄悄悄爬行，正是那个黄脸汉子。他望着那条身影钻进一道深沟，嘴里咕哝道：“不晓得那玩意儿到底是真是假？不晓得他那副皮囊当真从那块礁石上脱了身没有？若真脱了身，这该死的东西这阵子吃什么活着？若没脱身——那好，上帝可得救救这地方上的咱们啦！”说罢，他像一只鬼头鬼脑的老鼠，一溜烟窜回棺材铺去了。

## XXIX. Captain Collyer Entertains an Attorney from Rye / 二十九　科利尔上校款待拉伊来的律师

===Original===
It was something of a difficult position which Captain Collyer was called upon to face. That he had cut a ridiculous figure no one was more conscious than himself, and being made absurd before his own men made the situation doubly difficult. But Captain Collyer preserved his dignity in a most meritorious manner.

When the smugglers had gone and the bo’sun had freed him from his bonds, he stood up in the barn and addressed the sailors: “My men,” he began, “we have been badly beaten. Without a blow you were forced to lay down your arms, which I well know must have been a hard thing for you to do. After I had given the bo’sun orders of the night’s plan I went out to verify certain suspicions that I had formed against certain folk upon the Marsh. I was congratulating myself on how well I was succeeding, when I found myself a helpless prisoner in the wretches’ hands. I had walked blindly into a very clever trap. As you saw for yourselves, my captors made such a complete job of me that I was helpless to speak to you or give you any sign. Under the circumstances, I must thank the bo’sun for his gallant behaviour. I appreciate what he did, for he saved my life, although perhaps I could almost find it in my heart that he had acted otherwise, for a good seaman’s death is now on my hands—brave Will Rudrum, who was shot dead on the road. I also cannot find it in my heart to reprimand Joe Dickinson for his fit of laughter, because nobody saw the humour and disgrace of my position as much as I did myself. But when a man’s life is forfeited all humour slips away, and so it has for me and for you, I’m sure, who were Will Rudrum’s comrades at arms. I am very thankful that my life has been spared for this one purpose—namely, of avenging poor Rudrum’s death—and if anyone should and can avenge him, I hold myself to be that man. For this purpose I intend to take you all into my confidence. Having failed dismally so far, I do not wish to fail again; therefore, listen. In the first place, we are not a strong enough body to cope with these Marshmen. I shall therefore demand a strong body of reinforcements. There are redcoats at Dover and there are seamen at Rye. To both of these towns shall I send couriers. Also at Rye there is a remarkable old man, a wise man, an attorney-at-law. He will meet me this very day at the Ship Inn, and will undertake all the legal points with regard to the arrests which I shall make as soon as I have gathered up a few more facts. Will Rudrum was the first to fall in a good cause, for this corner of England is a very hotbed of enemies to the government. Bo’sun, you will serve out an extra allowance of rum at once, for we must drink together.”

===Chinese===
摆到科利尔上校（Captain Collyer）面前叫他应付的，着实是个有些难堪的局面。他自己出了一场多大的丑，再没有谁比他本人更心知肚明；而当着一众部下的面出乖露丑，更叫这局面难上加难。不过，科利尔上校维持体面的功夫，倒也实在是可圈可点。

走私贩子走远了，水手长替他解开了绑缚，上校便在谷仓里站起身来，向水兵们训话：“弟兄们，”他开口道，“我们结结实实吃了一场败仗。人家一枪未放，你们便被迫交了械；我深知，这必定是件叫你们难以下咽的事。我把今夜的部署交代给水手长之后，便出去查证我心中对湿地某些人存下的几点疑心。我正暗自庆幸事情办得如何顺当，却发现自己已经落在那班歹徒手里，成了动弹不得的囚徒。我是瞎着眼睛闯进了一个十分巧妙的圈套。你们都亲眼看见了，捉我的人把我收拾得那样干净利落，叫我既不能向你们喊话，也不能给你们半点暗号。事已至此，我不能不感谢水手长的忠勇之举。他的作为我心领，因为他救下了我的性命；虽说有时候，我心底里几乎情愿他当时没有那样做——因为眼下，一个好水手的性命债记在了我的账上：勇敢的威尔·鲁德拉姆（Will Rudrum），在大路上叫人一枪打死了。还有，乔·迪金森（Joe Dickinson）那阵子大笑，我也实在狠不下心去申斥，因为再没有谁比我本人更清楚我这处境的滑稽和耻辱。可是一个人性命押上了关头，一切滑稽便都溜之大吉；对我是如此，对你们——曾与威尔·鲁德拉姆并肩执械的同袍——我确信也是如此。我十分感激上天留下我这条性命，专为这一桩用场——那就是，替可怜的鲁德拉姆之死报仇雪恨；倘若有人该当替他复仇，也有人能够替他复仇，那么依我看，此人非我莫属。为着这个目的，我打算引你们大家为心腹，把底细和盘托出。迄今我一败涂地，我不愿再败；所以，都听着。第一层，凭我们这点人手，斗不过这班湿地汉子。因此我要请求调拨一支强大的增援兵力。多佛（Dover）有红衫军，拉伊（Rye）有水兵，这两座城镇我都要派出信使。另外，拉伊还有一位非同小可的老人，一位智者，一位执业律师。就在今天，他将在“船”酒馆（the Ship Inn）与我会面，把缉捕一节的全部法律事宜都承办下来——只等我再搜罗齐几件真凭实据，便即刻动手拿人。威尔·鲁德拉姆是为正义的事业倒下的第一人，因为英格兰的这个角落，正是与政府为敌之徒的一个十足的温床。水手长，即刻加发一份朗姆酒，咱们得一道喝上一杯。”

===Original===
The rum was served and the captain raised his pannikin:

“To the swift avenging of poor Will Rudrum, to the quick regaining of our dignity, and to the speedy hanging of his Majesty’s foes!”

===Chinese===
朗姆酒发了下来，上校举起他的小锡罐：

“为可怜的威尔·鲁德拉姆早日报仇雪恨，为我们早日重振体面，为国王陛下的仇敌早早绞上绞架，干杯！”

===Original===
The men drank, and then Joe Dickinson shouted: “And to our captain, God bless him, and blast them as does him dirty tricks!”

This toast was drunk greedily, and then the bo’sun led three cheers—three cheers which went echoing out of the old barn across the Marsh with a strength that made many a smuggler turn in his bed uneasily.

===Chinese===
水兵们一饮而尽，随后乔·迪金森高声喊道：“再为我们上校干一杯——上帝保佑他，叫那些给他使损招的家伙统统天打雷劈！”

这一杯众人喝得又急又贪；接着水手长领头三呼喝彩——三声欢呼冲出老谷仓，轰隆隆滚过湿地，那股声势，害得多少个走私贩子在床上辗转反侧，好不心惊。

===Original===
When they opened the barn door at daybreak to let the captain go forth, they found there a neat pile of weapons: his Majesty’s pistols and his Majesty’s cutlasses were all returned.

“Aye, but there’s some honour amongst thieves, sir!” exclaimed the bo’sun.

“Devil a bit of it!” said the captain. “The rascals know that we can soon get substitutes, and they’ve no wish to have such telltale things discovered on their premises. There’s more good sense than honour in it, I’m thinking, Job Mallet.”

===Chinese===
天光破晓，他们打开谷仓门放上校出去，只见门边齐齐整整码着一堆兵器：陛下的手枪、陛下的弯刀，全都物归原主。

“可不是嘛，长官，盗亦有道啊！”水手长嚷道。

“盗亦有道个鬼！”上校说，“这班无赖心里明白，咱们转眼就能领到新的补充，他们可不愿让这类招供的赃物在自己地面上叫人搜出来。依我看，乔布·马利特，这里头讲体面的成分少，明白事理的心思倒不少。”

===Original===
At ten o’clock that morning a coach rolled up to the door of the Ship Inn and out stepped Antony Whyllie, Esq., attorney-at-law from Rye, a man of sixty-five years, but upright and alert as any young man. He was attired in a bottle-green coat, black satin breeches, silk stockings, silver-buckled shoes, and faultless linen. His gray wig, tied concisely with a black ribbon, completed a true picture of the law: a man to desire for one’s defence, a man to dread for one’s accusation.

===Chinese===
那天上午十点钟，一辆马车辚辚停到“船”酒馆门前，走下车来的是拉伊来的执业律师安东尼·怀利（Antony Whyllie）先生，年纪六十有五，腰板之挺直、神色之机警，却不让任何一个年轻人。他上身穿墨绿呢外衣，下身是黑缎马裤，腿上丝袜，脚上银扣鞋，贴身亚麻布衫熨帖得无可挑剔。一头灰白假发用黑丝带束得干净利索，凑成一幅法律界的标准画像：这等人物，请他替你辩护是桩福气，由他出面控告你可就得当心了。

===Original===
The captain received him at the door of the inn and conducted him to the privacy of his own bedchamber.

There he unburdened his mind to the lawyer, stating all his suspicions and clearly showing how he had arrived at them. By the end of the morning they thoroughly understood each other, the lawyer returning by coach to Rye with orders to the governor of the castle to prepare accommodation for a large number of prisoners and to see to it that there were chains enough to hang ’em to. But, strange to relate, that lawyer in bottle green never reached the little town of Rye, for his coach stopped at a certain farmhouse beyond Romney. Here he alighted to make room for another lawyer, a real lawyer, a man of sixty-five, who had left Rye that very morning to consult with a certain Captain Collyer residing at the Ship Inn, Dymchurch. This lawyer had, needless to say, never arrived at Dymchurch. For at a lonely spot on the road outside Romney a strong body of men had awaited the arrival of his coach. While two or three of them removed the driver from his box to the farmhouse, where they speedily made him drunk, two or three others had entered the coach, securely gagged and blindfolded the occupant, and conveyed him also to the house, the coach immediately proceeding to Dymchurch with another coachman and another lawyer, a man in a bottle-green coat.

===Chinese===
上校在酒馆门口把他迎住，领到自己卧房里的僻静处。

在那里，他把满腹心事向律师一吐为快，将所有疑心统统摆了出来，又把这些疑心如何一步步生将出来，交代得清清楚楚。到上午收梢时分，两人已然彼此心意通透；律师乘马车回拉伊去，给城堡长官传下指令：备好房舍，收容一大批囚犯，还要置办足够的铁链，好把那班人拴挂得牢牢靠靠。可是，说来也怪，那位墨绿衣装的律师竟没能走到拉伊小城——他的马车在过了罗姆尼（Romney）的一座农舍前停住了。他在此下车让位，换上来另一位律师，一位如假包换的律师，同样是个六十五岁的人，正是当天一早从拉伊动身，要来同住在迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）“船”酒馆的某位科利尔上校当面计议的。不用说，这位律师压根也不曾到得了迪姆彻奇。原来在罗姆尼镇外大路上一处僻静的地点，一大群汉子早已候着他那辆马车的到来。其中两三个人把车夫从车座上请下来，送进农舍，三口两口便灌得他酩酊大醉；另外两三个人钻进车厢，把里面的乘客结结实实堵了嘴、蒙了眼，也押到那座农舍里去；马车则当即载着另一个车夫、另一位律师——一个穿墨绿外衣的人——径直朝迪姆彻奇驶去。

===Original===
The blindfolded lawyer had been scared out of all knowledge, especially by the sound of the voice of a certain man known as the Scarecrow. This terrible ruffian had told the lawyer that if on returning to Rye he breathed a word of what had happened they would most certainly catch him again and do away with him, adding that there was no place more convenient than Romney Marsh for the hiding of a body. So with the exception of telling his awful experience to his wife, whom he feared nearly as greatly as he feared the Scarecrow, Antony Whyllie, attorney-at-law, held his tongue, being only thankful that the rascals had let him off so easily. The coachman, who was so muddled with drink and with falling off his box at least a dozen times on the way back, never even remembered what had happened or to whose kind offices he was indebted for the privilege of becoming so gloriously drunk. So the affair passed unheeded by the public, and the gentleman in bottle green, having changed his clothes, might that very afternoon have been seen going toward the church of Dymchurch. Down into the crypt he went, and there, at a dirty table lighted by a candle set in a bottle’s neck, he aided two other men to work out certain accounts that were spread before them in a book marked “Parish Register of Deaths.” But there were no deaths registered in that book. It was full of figures accounting for cargoes of wool, full of receipts for coffins loaded with spirits.

===Chinese===
那位蒙了眼的律师早给吓得魂不附体，尤其是一个绰号稻草人（the Scarecrow）的人物的嗓音，更是叫他胆裂。这个狰狞的恶棍对律师放了话：回到拉伊之后，他若敢吐露半句这里的遭遇，他们必定再把他捉回来结果掉；还添了一句：要藏一具尸首，天下再没有比罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh）更便当的去处。于是，安东尼·怀利律师就此守口如瓶，只把这段骇人的经历讲给了自己的老婆听——而那位太太，他怕她几乎不亚于怕稻草人——此外便只有暗自庆幸，那班无赖放他放得这等容易了。至于那个车夫，一路喝得迷三倒四，回家道上从车座上跌下来少说也有十来回，压根不记得出过什么事，更不记得自己是承了哪位好人的情分，才享到这烂醉如泥的福分。因此这桩事情公众丝毫没有察觉；那位墨绿衣绅士换了行头，当天下午便有人望见他朝迪姆彻奇教堂走去。他径直下到地下墓室里，在那里，就着一支插在瓶颈上的蜡烛照亮的一张龌龊桌子，帮着另外两个人核算摊在面前的账目——那本账簿封皮上写着“教区死亡登记簿”（Parish Register of Deaths）的字样。可那本簿子上登记的并不是什么死亡：满篇都是羊毛货载的进出数目，满是装着烈酒的棺材的收条。

===Original===
Sexton Mipps and the gentleman who had worn the bottle-green coat then unlocked an old chest and took out certain money bags which they emptied on the table. The third gentleman, whom they addressed as the Scarecrow, helped them to sort the coin, French in one pile, English in another, and then referring to a list of names in the register, the three managers of the secret bank proportioned out their servants’ wages. When this was accomplished the gentleman who had worn the bottle-green coat presented his little account, which was promptly paid in golden guineas, and he left them, saying that he was very sorry that it was the last time that he would draw so many golden Georges from the bank.

===Chinese===
随后，司事米普斯和那位穿墨绿外衣的绅士打开一口旧柜，从里面取出几只钱袋，把钱尽数倒在桌上。第三位绅士——他们口口声称呼他稻草人——帮着他们把硬币分拣开来：法国钱归一堆，英国钱归一堆；然后对照登记簿上的一页名录，这三位秘密银行的管事人便把手下人的工钱一一分派停当。事情办完，那位墨绿外衣的绅士呈上他那笔小账，当即用金几尼付了清；他辞别二人，说很遗憾，这是他最后一回从这家银行支取这么多金晃晃的乔治金币了。

===Original===
“Yes, the bank closes accounts today,” said the Scarecrow, striking his name off the list, “though perhaps some day we shall open it again. Who knows?”

“Let’s hope so,” said the other, shaking hands with the Scarecrow and the sexton, “and let’s hope we meet again. Goodbye.” And he was gone, Mipps locking the door behind him.

“It’s all right to a penny,” said the Scarecrow.

===Chinese===
“是啊，银行今天停业清账，”稻草人说，一面把他的名字从名录上勾掉，“不过兴许哪一天，我们还会重新开张。谁知道呢？”

“但愿如此，”那位绅士说着，同稻草人和司事一一握手道别，“也但愿我们后会有期。再会。”说罢人便去了，米普斯随手在他背后落了锁。

“一个便士也不差，”稻草人说。

===Original===
“Hooray! I calls it,” chuckled Sexton Mipps, rubbing his hands together. “I’ll get this little lot of coinage nailed up in a coffin and sent to Calais, and old What’s-his-name wot’s just gone up the stairs has arranged with the Calais people to get it transferred to the Bank of Lyons, so you can get at it yourself from Marseilles, can’t you?”

“Yes, we’re all square now. Everything shipshape. Mother Waggetts I’ve settled with, and Imogene gets the iron-bound casket. I’ve seen to it all. But it’s time I was off. I’ve a certain gentleman to see before nightfall.”

===Chinese===
“好哇！要我说，就是这句，”司事米普斯搓着两只手，咯咯直乐，“我要把这堆小钱币钉进一口棺材里，发往加来（Calais）；方才上楼去的那位老什么爷，已经同加来那边的人讲妥了，把它转存进里昂银行（the Bank of Lyons），往后你人在马赛（Marseilles），自己就能支取，是不是？”

“对，如今咱们账账两清，样样齐整。瓦格茨大娘（Mother Waggetts）那边我已经结清；那只包铁皮的小箱子归伊莫金。样样都经我的手办妥了。可我该动身了。天黑以前，我还要去拜访一位老爷。”

===Original===
“Who’s that?” asked Mipps.

“The squire,” replied the Scarecrow, laughing as he tied up the money bags.

“And I have a gentleman to visit, too,” said Mipps.

“Who’s that?” asked the Scarecrow.

“Parson Syn, Doctor Syn, the worthy vicar,” replied Mipps, winking, at which the Scarecrow laughed and went out of the crypt.

===Chinese===
“是哪一位？”米普斯问。

“乡绅，”稻草人答道，一面笑着把钱袋扎紧。

“我也有位老爷要拜访，”米普斯说。

“是哪一位？”稻草人问。

“辛牧师，辛医生，可敬的教区牧师，”米普斯答道，还挤了挤眼；稻草人闻声大笑，走出了地下墓室。

===Original===
Mipps, after locking up the money in the chest, followed leisurely, and as he crossed the churchyard he saw Doctor Syn ringing the front door bell of the courthouse.

“Well,” murmured Mipps to himself, “I’ve met one or two of ’em in my time, but he’s a blinkin’ marvel.”
===Chinese===
米普斯把钱锁回柜里，慢悠悠地跟了出来；穿过教堂墓地的时候，他望见辛医生正在按议事厅正门的门铃。

“嗯，”米普斯暗自咕哝，“我这辈子也见识过一两个这样的角色，可他这个人，真是他娘的一个奇迹。”

## XXX. Doctor Syn Has a “Call” / 三十　辛医生有了“圣召”

===Original===
“Do you mean to say that you’re going to leave Dymchurch?”

The squire was positively angry, a thing he had never been with Doctor Syn in all the years that he had known him. “You are undoubtedly pulling my leg—that’s what you’re doing. God bless my soul, sir, there’s precious few fellows can do that, and precious few that dare try; but that’s what you’re doing, isn’t it?”

“I’m afraid not, Sir Antony. My dear squire, my good friend, I am afraid that for once in my life I am most dreadfully in earnest.”

“But what don’t you like about the place? Is it something I’ve done? Do you want your stipend raised? Damme, I’ll treble the blessed thing, if it’s that. Oh, it’s that rascally son of mine that’s been putting you out. It’s that Denis scamp, who never took to his books and never will. But I’ll make him. I’ll take my riding whip to the young whelp if he causes you pain. It is he! He’s at the bottom of it. My soul and body, I’ll give the young puppy a shaking up. He doesn’t know a good tutor when he sees one. The impertinent young popinjay! Doesn’t appreciate anything. No! God bless my soul, why he’s no more respect for me than a five-barred gate. He’s always doing something to jar me. Why, do you know, that the cool-faced young malefactor announced the other day in the most insolent manner that he was going to marry a barmaid? Yes, I assure you he did. He announced to me, sir, in the most condescending tones, as if he were conferring an inestimable favour upon my head, that he thought I ran a very good chance of having that girl Imogene for my daughter-in-law. You know Imogene, that serves and waits and does innumerable dirty jobs at the Ship Inn; and when I expostulated in fatherly tones, why, bless me, if the young spitfire didn’t fly into a passion, crying out that it was high time one of the Cobtrees introduced some good looks into the family. Said that to me, mind you—his natural father that brought him into the world. I told him that, used those very words, and what does he do but begin to bow and scrape and praise and thank me for bringing him into the world at the same period as that black-haired bargirl, just as if his mother and I had timed the thing to a nicety! Why, when I come to think of it, she’s the daughter of a common pirate, that rascally, scoundrelly Clegg, who was hanged at Rye. Isn’t she now? And she’s to be my daughter-in-law! Now, Doctor Syn, in the name of Romney Marsh, what the devil—I say, what the devil would you do if you had a son like that to deal with?”

===Chinese===
“你的意思是说，你就要离开迪姆彻奇了？”

乡绅这一回当真动了怒——相识这么多年，他还从来没跟辛医生（Doctor Syn）红过脸。“你分明是在耍弄我——你干的就是这个勾当。老天爷，先生，耍得了我的人，普天之下没有几个，敢试上一试的更没有几个；可你如今干的就是这个，是不是？”

“恐怕不是，安东尼爵士。我亲爱的乡绅，我的好朋友，只怕我活了一辈子，这一遭可是顶顶郑重、再当真不过的了。”

“可这地方到底哪儿叫你不如意啦？是我做错了什么事么？还是你想加一加你的圣俸？见鬼，真为这个，我把那份劳什子翻上三倍都使得。哦，准是我那个无赖儿子惹你生了气。就是丹尼斯那个小流氓，他从不用功念书，往后也决计不会。可我非叫他念不可。他若敢叫你受委屈，我就拿马鞭子抽这个小崽子。准是他！祸根就出在他身上。我的魂儿和身子，非把这小狗崽子狠狠摇醒一顿不可。好导师站在眼前，他都认不出来。这个放肆的小臭美鬼！什么都不领情。可不是！老天爷，他对我的敬重，还不抵一道五横栏的栅门多。他总惹我生气。嗐，你可知道，前儿个那张厚脸皮的小恶少，竟用最无礼的口吻宣布，说他要娶一个酒馆女侍？是的，我告诉你，真有这么回事。他呀，先生，用那副屈尊俯就的腔调对我宣布，倒好像他把天大的恩典赏到了我头上似的：他说，我有极大的指望，能把伊莫金（Imogene）那姑娘收作儿媳妇。你认得伊莫金，就是在‘船’酒馆（the Ship Inn）里端茶伺客、干着数不清的脏活累活的那个姑娘；我用为父的口吻才劝诫了一句，哎呀呀，那小炮筒子竟勃然大怒，嚷嚷说科布特家早该有人给这门里添几分好相貌了。想想吧，这话是冲我说的——冲他生身的老子说的。我就是这么点破他的，一字不差；可他倒好，居然打起躬、作起揖来，又夸我又谢我，谢我跟那个黑头发的酒馆女侍赶在同一个时辰把他生到了人世，倒好像他娘跟我把这事掐算得毫厘不差似的！嗐，如今我细一想，她不过是一个寻常海盗的女儿，就是那个无赖透顶、恶棍成性的克莱格（Clegg），在拉伊上的绞架。难道不是么？而她竟要做我的儿媳妇！哎，辛医生，以罗姆尼湿地的名义，你究竟——我说，你究竟会拿这样一个儿子怎么办呢？”

===Original===
The squire absolutely had to stop for breath, and Doctor Syn, who had been vainly trying to get a word in edgewise, replied: “Well, sir, I should candidly confess that my son was a lucky dog if he succeeded in getting her, and which, I should very much doubt. In fact, were I in your place, I should go so far as to bet my wig that he would never win the girl. I’m very fond of Denis, devoted to him in fact, but I’m afraid he’ll have a great difficulty in marrying Imogene.”

“I should damn well bet my eyes he will, sir! I need none to tell me that. Difficulty in marrying her? Aye, that he will. My son will marry position, sir—money, sir—and if beauty comes along of it, well, then, beauty, sir, and all the better for my son, sir.”

“And provided of course that the lady is willing,” put in the vicar.

“Willing? What minx wouldn’t be only too damned willing to marry my son—old Cobtree’s son; and not so old either, sir, eh? Why, any woman would jump at the chance! And as for a bargirl, the daughter of a dirty pirate hanged in that silly conceited little town of Rye, why, pooh-pooh, my dear Doctor! Laughable!”

===Chinese===
乡绅实在不得不停下来喘口气；辛医生白费了半天气力想插进一句话，这才得了空答道：“呃，先生，倘若我的儿子真能把她娶到手，那我就坦白承认，他是个幸运的小子——不过这一点，我很是存疑。老实说，我若是处在您的位子上，我就要押上我的假发打赌：这姑娘他一辈子也赢不到手。我很喜欢丹尼斯，说疼爱也不为过；可我担心，他要娶上伊莫金，只怕难上加难。”

“那我还敢押上我的眼珠子打赌呢，先生！这不用谁来开导我。娶她有难处？是啊，难着呢。我儿子娶的是门第，先生——是钱财，先生；相貌要能搭着一起来，好嘛，那就是相貌，先生，对我儿子只有更好，先生。”

“当然，还得那位小姐自己情愿才行。”牧师插了一句。

“情愿？哪个小妖精不肯天杀地求之不得，要嫁我的儿子——老科布特的儿子；况且也还不算老，先生，嗯？随便哪个女人都得抢着抓住这个机会！至于一个酒馆女侍，一个下流海盗的女儿，那海盗还是在拉伊那个又傻气又自命不凡的小镇上吊死的——呸呸，我亲爱的医生！笑死人了！”

===Original===
“Well, I think differently in this case, Squire,” said the Doctor. “I should call Denis a lucky dog. I might even stretch a point and, at the risk of being unfrocked, say a damned lucky dog if he succeeded in marrying that girl Imogene.”

“What?” cried the squire.

“Of course,” said the Doctor, “you mustn’t go entirely by what I say, because I hold myself very seriously gifted in the judging of attractive women.”

“And so do I, sir. I know she’s attractive. A damned fine, upstanding young woman, and if she were even a county pauper I might stretch a point and accept her, but beauty comes last on my list.”

“But Imogene possesses all the other necessaries required. Rich she is, and very rich, though she doesn’t know it, and although her mother was but a dancer in a Raratonga gambling saloon, she was descended direct from an Incan princess, and as you said ‘pooh-pooh’ to me, sir, why, I’ll say ‘pooh’ back, sir: ‘pooh’ to your Kentish ladies of quality, for when Imogene comes into her own, why, damme, she could chuck their fortunes on to every horse in the village steeplechase.”

===Chinese===
“这件事上，我的看法可不一样，乡绅，”医生说。“我要说丹尼斯是个幸运的小子。我甚至可以破个例，冒着被褫夺圣职的风险说一句：他若真能娶上伊莫金那姑娘，那他就是个天杀的幸运小子。”

“什么？”乡绅嚷道。

“当然，”医生说，“你也不可全凭我一句话，因为我自认在品鉴有魅力的女人这一门上，实在颇有些天赋。”

“这也是我的自许，先生。我知道她有魅力。一个呱呱叫、身板笔挺的年轻女人；就算她是个领郡里救济的穷丫头，我兴许也能破个例把她收下。可相貌在我的单子上，排在最末。”

“可伊莫金把旁的种种必备条件占全了。她有钱，而且极有钱，只是她自己还不知道；她母亲虽说不过是拉罗汤加（Raratonga）一家赌场里的舞女，她本人却是印加公主的嫡系后裔。您既然冲我说了‘呸呸’，先生，那好，我就回敬您一声‘呸’，先生：呸您那些肯特郡的贵妇人们！待到伊莫金名下应得的财产到了手，嘿，见鬼，她尽可以把那些太太们的家私，一把一把撒给村里越野赛马会上的每一匹马。”

===Original===
“Is she so very wealthy—that girl at the Ship Inn? Well, perhaps I am wrong in saying that the match is so very uneven. Perhaps I am.”

“Yes,” went on the vicar, “there is just the possibility that it might be brought to a successful issue, though if you’ll excuse my saying so, you are so very tactless at times, Squire.”

“What do you mean?” cried the squire hotly. “I am none too sure that I should care for my son to marry a bargirl, though she were the daughter of Croesus himself.”

“My dear Squire, calm yourself, I beg. As a barmaid I admit Imogene is below Denis as regards position, but as an Incan princess, why, my dear friend, she is as far superior to the Cobtrees of the courthouse as the reigning house of England. Why, do you know anything—but of course you do—of the pride, the magnificence, the omnipotent splendour possessed by the Incan kings? Why, the Palace of Whitehall would compare most unfavourably with their sculleries.”

“No? Really?” said the squire.

===Chinese===
“‘船’酒馆那个姑娘竟这样有钱？呃，那我说这门亲事如此不般配，许是我错了。许是我错了。”

“正是，”牧师接着说，“这门亲事兴许还真有办成的一线可能——不过恕我直言，乡绅，您说话有时候实在是毫无分寸。”

“你这话什么意思？”乡绅火辣辣地嚷道。“我很拿不准我会乐意让儿子娶一个酒馆女侍，哪怕她是克罗伊斯（Croesus）王爷的亲闺女。”

“我亲爱的乡绅，求您消消气。作为一个酒馆女侍，我承认，论门第伊莫金是配不上丹尼斯；可作为一个印加公主，嘿，我亲爱的朋友，比起议事厅里的科布特一家来，她不知尊贵到哪里去了——那份判别，恰如英格兰君临的王室比起他们来一般。怎么，印加诸王那份威仪、那份豪奢、那份无所不能的煊赫，你可知道几分——哦，你自然知道？白厅宫（the Palace of Whitehall）同他们家的洗碗间比一比，也要大大地相形见绌。”

“不见得吧？真的？”乡绅说。

===Original===
“And it’s for the wealth and fortunes of Imogene that I must leave you,” went on the cleric—“that is, leave you for a time, you understand? For although I shall bestow upon her certain things of value that I hold as her guardian, the bulk of her fortune has been lying idle, but now that she is growing into womanhood, it is high time I fulfilled my duties and lifted her money for her.”

“Then she’s your adopted child, is she?” said the squire, pushing his wig back and scratching his head.

“Well, I suppose that’s how it stands in a sense,” replied the Doctor. “When that rascal Clegg died he actually paid me a good sum of money to see that his daughter was provided for, and of course I’ve kept that money for her till she came to years of discretion. He also told me where England’s treasure was buried, and that’s what I’m off to get.”

“England’s treasure? What’s that?” asked the amazed squire.

“Clegg was a partner of England, the notorious pirate. It is said that he killed England in a quarrel, though nothing was proved of it. Anyhow, Clegg was the only man who knew of the hiding-place, and at his death he imparted the secret to me, after I had given solemn oath upon the Bible to keep it to myself.”

===Chinese===
“而我之所以不得不离开你，正是为了伊莫金的这份财帛家私，”教士接着说道——“就是说，只是离开你一阵子，你明白吗？因为，我虽要以监护人的身份，把我掌管的几件值钱东西交到她手上，她那笔财产的大头却一直闲搁着；如今她已长成大人，我正该克尽厥职，替她把那笔钱提取出来了。”

“这么说，她是你的养女了？”乡绅说着，把假发往脑后推了推，挠起头皮来。

“呃，从某种意义上说，情形也就是这样，”医生答道。“那个恶棍克莱格死的时候，当真付了我好大一笔钱，托我照料他女儿日后的生活；这笔钱我自然一直替她存着，直到她到了能自作主张的年纪。他还告诉了我英格兰的宝藏埋在何处，我这就要动身去取的，正是那个。”

“英格兰的宝藏？那是什么？”乡绅惊问道。

“克莱格是那个恶名昭彰的海盗‘英格兰’（England）的合伙人。据说他在一场争吵里杀死了‘英格兰’，只是并无实据。总而言之，藏宝之地，天下只克莱格一人知晓；他临终把这秘密传给了我——那是在我对着《圣经》郑重立誓、守口如瓶之后。”

===Original===
“God bless my soul!” said the squire, leaping to his feet; “and do you mean to say that you’ve kept the secret all this time and not fitted out a ship and gone to lift it? Why, there may be millions there!”

“There are,” said Doctor Syn. “I’m certain of that. That’s why I’ve been at pains to keep the whole matter to myself, not even telling the girl, for it will want careful handling. Once let anyone know that I am off to lift Clegg’s treasure-chests, and all the dogs in Christendom will be nosing on my trail. Clegg had the same fear of this secret being stolen and so committed the exact lie of the island to my memory, and to no artificial map, but he did it so uncommon well that I can see point, bays, lagoons, soundings, and tracks just as if I had piloted ships there all my life.”

“Then all this pious talk of wanting to go out as a mission preacher to the smelly blacks is simply balderdash, and you haven’t had a ridiculous ‘call’ at all?”

“Merely a cloak to hide my real designs.”

“Good Lord deliver us!” said the squire, pushing his wig clean off and allowing it to lie unheeded on the floor.

===Chinese===
“老天爷保佑我的老命！”乡绅霍地跳起来。“你的意思是说，这秘密你一守这么多年，竟没装备一条船去把宝藏起出来？哎呀，那里头弄不好有几百万呢！”

“有几百万，”辛医生说。“这一点我确信无疑。正因如此，我才煞费苦心，把整桩事情闷在自己一个人肚里，连那姑娘也没告诉：这事宜须小心措置。但凡有一个人知道我要动身去起克莱格的宝箱，基督教世界上所有的恶狗都要循着踪迹嗅到我这边来。克莱格也生怕这秘密被人窃去，所以不曾交给我什么人工绘制的地图，只把那座岛确确切切的地形，牢牢印进了我的记性。他又做得实在高明：岬角、海湾、潟湖、水深、航路，我闭眼便见，仿佛我这辈子都在那儿领船引水似的。”

“那么，你这一套要去给臭烘烘的黑人当传道牧师的虔诚话，通通是一派胡言，你压根儿也没得过什么可笑的‘圣召’（call），是不是？”

“不过是一件遮掩我真正图谋的斗篷罢了。”

“上帝救救我们吧！”乡绅说着，把假发整个儿推落，任它掉在地板上，看也不看。

===Original===
Just then there entered a servant who announced to the squire that the girl from the Ship Inn was outside with a note which she desired to give to the squire.

“Ask her to be so kind as to step in,” said the squire, with a touch of deference and awakened interest. Imogene accordingly came into the room. Perfectly at ease she stood there, until with almost regal grace she accepted the chair that the squire brought forward. Yes, he thought the vicar was right. Her clothes were rough indeed, but her manner would have sat well on an empress.

“You have brought a note for me, I think—Imogene?” said the squire at last. He was ridiculously uncertain whether to call her Imogene as usual, or Madame; in fact in his confusion he was as near as not saying Mistress Cobtree, which would have been awful. Imogene held out a small sealed packet, and looked at the fire, and so taken up was the squire with looking at her and thinking of the Incan millions that, if Doctor Syn had not shuffled his foot, he would have forgotten to open the letter at all. But the moment he had, the girl, the Incan millions, his anger against his son, the mission “call” of the Doctor, everything was forgotten, for he crunched the letter in his hand, threw his head back, and looking at the ceiling with the most appalled expression on his face, cried out: “If there’s a God in heaven, come down quick and wring this captain’s neck!”

“What is it?” cried the vicar.

“Read it out!” yelled the squire, flinging the crumpled paper ball upon the table. “If you love me, read it out and tell me what to do.”

===Chinese===
正在这时，一个仆人走了进来，禀报乡绅说，“船”酒馆的那个姑娘正在外面，带着一封便笺，要当面交给乡绅。

“请她赏光进来吧。”乡绅说，语气里带着几分敬重和被唤起的兴致。伊莫金于是走进屋来。她仪态从容地站在那里，直到乡绅把椅子推上前来，她才以一种近乎王家的雍容欣然落座。是啊，他想，牧师说得不错。她的衣衫着实粗陋，可那副仪态，配一位女皇也绰绰有余。

“我想，你是给我送信来了——伊莫金？”乡绅末了开口道。他那份拿捏实在可笑：不知该照旧叫她伊莫金呢，还是该称夫人；慌乱之下，他险些脱口唤出“科布特太太”来，那可就要闹天大的笑话了。伊莫金递出一个小小的封缄的纸包，两眼望着炉火；乡绅一面端详着她，一面惦着印加的百万家财，神魂颠倒，若不是辛医生把脚在地上蹭了一蹭，他简直会把拆信这件事整个儿忘掉。可信一到手，那姑娘、印加的百万家财、他对儿子的恼怒、医生那桩传道的“圣召”，霎时忘了个干净：他把信在手里攥得咯吱作响，仰头望着天花板，脸上现出惊骇已极的神色，放声喊道：“天上若真有上帝，快下来拧断这个上校的脖子！”

“怎么啦？”牧师喊道。

“念出来！”乡绅吼道，把那团皱纸球往桌上一摔。“你要是还疼我，就念出来，告诉我该怎么办。”

===Original===
Doctor Syn recovered the note, which had bounced from the table to the floor, and when he had unravelled it and smoothed it straight and flat, he read:

“Ship Inn.

“To Sir Antony Cobtree of the Courthouse, Leveller of Marsh Scotts.

“**Sir**: I beg to inform you on behalf of the British Admiralty that the person of Mister Rash, Dymchurch schoolmaster, has disappeared. I feel sure that there is somebody in power who is organizing Romney Marsh for his own ends. Somebody is running wool to France, and from the clever organization of these runs, I know that some cultured brain is directing affairs. Your attitude of utter indifference forces me to suspect you. As Leveller of the Marsh Scotts you are in a safe place to control such a scheme, and so I have taken a strong measure in attaching the person of your son, Mister Denis Cobtree. If the body of that unfortunate schoolmaster, dead or alive, is not produced before me within the next twenty-four hours, I shall take steps to force your hand.

[Signed]

**“Captain Howard Collyer**,

					“Coast Agent and Commissioner.

“P.S. There is a press gang at work in Rye who will ship your son to sea in twenty-four hours.”

===Chinese===
辛医生拾起那张从桌面弹落到地板上的便笺，把它展开抚平，然后念道：

“船”酒馆

致议事厅的安东尼·科布特爵士、湿地斯科特人的平定者

**先生**：我谨代表英国海军部奉告阁下：迪姆彻奇的学堂先生拉什先生（Mister Rash）现已失踪。我深信，有一位权势人物正为自己的图谋把罗姆尼湿地组织起来。有人在向法国偷运羊毛；从这些偷运行动调度的精妙来看，我知道，幕后必有一个有教养的头脑在主持大局。阁下全然漠不关心的态度，使我不由得疑心到您身上。身为湿地斯科特人的平定者，您正处在一个足以操纵这一图谋的稳妥位置上；因此我已采取强硬手段，扣押了令郎丹尼斯·科布特先生的人身。若在今后二十四小时之内，那位不幸的学堂先生的身子——无论死活——不押送到我的面前，我便要采取步骤，逼阁下就范。

[签名]

**霍华德·科利尔上校**，

					海岸缉私特派专员（Coast Agent and Commissioner）

又及：拉伊现有一支强征队（press gang）正在活动，不出二十四小时，他们就会把令郎抓上船去出海。

===Original===
“Now what am I to do? Press gang at Rye! Twenty-four hours! What have I got to do with that flabby-faced schoolmaster? Where’s he got to? How the devil should I know? P’raps he thinks that I have danced him off somewhere. Never heard of such a thing in my life. But what am I to do? That’s what I want to know! What am I to do? My poor Denis! Why, I wouldn’t have quarrelled with him if I’d known. Why has that schoolmaster disappeared? By what infernal right, I say, has that insignificant anæmic louse disappeared?”

Doctor Syn then briefly related the bo’sun’s story of Rash’s disappearance, which the squire listened to impatiently.

===Chinese===
“这可叫我怎么办？拉伊来了强征队！二十四小时！我跟那个肉泡脸的学堂先生又有什么相干？他上哪儿去了？我见鬼怎么会知道？没准他还当是我把他变戏法似的弄到什么地方去了呢。我活了这一辈子，没听说过这等事。可我究竟该怎么办？我要问的就是这个！我该怎么办呀？我可怜的丹尼斯！唉，早知如此，我决不会跟他拌嘴。那学堂先生为什么失踪了？凭什么天杀的道理，我说，那个无足轻重的、贫血的虱子凭什么失踪了？”

辛医生于是把水手长讲述的拉什失踪经过，扼要说了一遍；乡绅听得好不耐烦。

===Original===
“Well, sir,” the latter exclaimed at the conclusion, “as far as that schoolmaster’s concerned, I don’t mind if he’s roasting on Lucifer’s spit, for I dislike the man, but when his disappearance concerns the safety of my son, my God! he’s got to put in an appearance and be quick about it. For I’ll have him routed out of his infernal hiding-place. I’ll rouse the Marshmen and have him routed out.”

“That’s all very well, Squire, but how?”

“How, sir?” echoed that irascible gentleman. “How? Do you ask me how? Well, I don’t know! How? Yes, how?”

“That’s the question,” ruefully remarked Doctor Syn.

“Of course it is,” returned the other. “Well, how would you set about it yourself?”

===Chinese===
“好嘛，先生，”乡绅听罢嚷道。“单说那位学堂先生，他就是正叉在路西法的烤叉上烤着，我也不在乎，因为我讨厌那个人；可他这一失踪，关系到我儿子的安危，我的老天爷！他非得露面不可，还得给我快些。我非把他从他那个见鬼的藏身洞里掏出来不可。我要把湿地汉子们发动起来，非把他掏出来不可。”

“好倒是好，乡绅，可怎么掏呢？”

“怎么，先生？”那位火暴性子的绅士应声道。“怎么？你倒来问我怎么？呃，我不知道！怎么？是啊，怎么呢？”

“问题正在这儿，”辛医生苦笑着说道。

“那还用说，”对方回道。“呃，换作你自己，你会怎么下手？”

===Original===
“I’d beat the Marsh up from border to border.”

“So I will, sir, so I will!”

“And I should get that mulatto and hang him, for he’s a sorcerer, a witchman; and I believe that as long as we have such a Jonah’s curse among us that nothing will come right.”

“I’ll do that at once. But we’ve only twenty-four hours.”

===Chinese===
“我就要把这片湿地，从这头到那头，翻它个底朝天。”

“就这么办，先生，就这么办！”

“我还要把那个黑白混血儿抓来吊死：他是个施咒的术士，是个弄巫蛊的家伙；我深信，只要这约拿的诅咒还在咱们中间一日，就什么事也别想顺当。”

“这个我立刻就去办。可咱们只有二十四个钟头了。”

===Original===
Imogene stood up and looked at the squire, and in a steady voice, as if she were pronouncing a definite judgment, she said: “It is enough for me. I will undertake to find your son for you, and the schoolmaster, too.” And without waiting for a reply she swiftly passed out of the room.

“But what can we do?” stammered the squire.

“I should find that mulatto and hang him.”

“But I don’t care a fig about finding him.”

“You must,” persisted the cleric, “for he is the cause of the trouble. Find that mulatto, and leave the rest to Imogene. She has spoken, and you may be sure she’ll keep her word. But find that mulatto!”

===Chinese===
伊莫金站起身，望着乡绅，声音沉稳，仿佛在宣读一桩确定不移的判决：“这对我已经足够了。我担下这桩事，替你把儿子找回来，连那位学堂先生也一并找回来。”她不等回答，便翩然快步走出了屋子。

“可我们能做什么呢？”乡绅结结巴巴地说。

“我还是那句话：把那黑白混血儿找出来，吊死他。”

“可把他找出来，我半点兴致也没有。”

“你必须找，”教士执意道，“因为祸端就出在他身上。把那黑白混血儿找出来，其余的只管交给伊莫金。她既已开口，你尽可放心，她说到做到。可是，先把那黑白混血儿找出来！”

## XXXI. A Certain Tree Bears Fruit / 三十一　某棵树结出了果实

===Original===
Jerk was kept busy all day at the Ship Inn, for Imogene had left her post and Mrs. Waggetts, who appeared to have grave matters of her own to fuss about, kept the young potboy in command. He was sorry about this, for he was unable to visit his estate upon the Marsh, and he was eager to view his latest purchase, the gallows. But to his great satisfaction he heard it discussed by a farmer and a fisherman who sat drinking at the bar.

“I tell you that there’s a gallows erected on the Marsh nigh Littlestone Point,” the fisherman was saying. “I could see it quite plain at sunrise when we were running up on to the beach.”

“And you say that there was a man a-hangin’ from it?” said the farmer.

“Aye, that’s what I said, and I thought as how you could tell me what man it was.”

“I don’t know nothing,” replied the farmer, “except that the demon riders was out again last night, and if what you says is right, why, they’re at their tricks again, I suppose.” And the farmer gave the fisherman a knowing wink. However, this didn’t trouble Jerry, for the laugh was all on his side. Not content with an empty scaffold, he had gone out the night before, while Doctor Syn and the captain had been chatting in the sanded parlour, and collected two great sacks full of dried sticks and sand, which, with the help of a few tightly knotted lengths of twine, he had converted into the semblance of a man, and this same dummy he had hanged from the rusty chain. It had looked splendid swinging there with the mist wrapped round its feet. This indeed was playing hangman’s games with a vengeance.
