Impatient as he was to see the fruits of his labour, impatient he had to remain, for he was not released till nightfall, when Mrs. Waggetts entered the bar with Sexton Mipps. Freed at last from duty, Jerry stepped outside, pulling his hat over his eyes and tucking up his collar, for the wind was blowing up for a cold night. He was leaving the yard with a brisk step when he noticed a cloaked figure coming to meet him. It was Imogene.

===Chinese===
杰尔克这一整天都在“船”酒馆里忙得团团转：伊莫金丢下了她的活计，瓦格茨太太呢，看模样自有要紧事要操心，便把店面丢给年轻的酒保一人照管。他心里老大不情愿，因为这样一来，他就没法上湿地去瞧他的那份产业了——他正急着要看看他最新置办的家当：那座绞架。不过，令他大感受用的是，吧台边坐着喝酒的一农夫一渔夫，正谈起这件事，叫他听了个正着。

“我告诉你，湿地上立起了一座绞架，就在利特尔斯通角（Littlestone Point）跟前，”渔夫正说道。“日出那会儿我们把船往滩上拖，看得再清楚不过了。”

“你还说上头吊着个人？”农夫问。

“是啊，我是这么说的，我还当你能告诉我吊的到底是哪个人呢。”

“我什么都不知道，”农夫答道，“只知道魔骑们昨儿夜里又出动了；你说的要是真的，哼，依我看，他们又在耍他们的把戏了呗。”说罢，农夫朝渔夫心照不宣地眨了眨眼。不过这可搅不乱杰里的心境，因为这个笑话只有他一个人心里有数。他还不满足于一座空绞架：头天夜里，趁着辛医生和上校在铺沙的客厅里闲谈的工夫，他跑出门去，收拢了满满两大口袋干柴枝和沙土，再靠几截扎得死紧的细麻绳帮忙，扎出个人的模样来，又把这假人吊上了那根锈链。雾气裹着它的两脚，它在那儿荡来荡去，煞是气派。这才真叫把刽子手的营生玩了个痛快淋漓。他纵然心急火燎，想看看自己这份劳作的成果，却也只好按捺着等下去，直到天擦黑，瓦格茨太太偕司事米普斯一同走进店堂，他才得了自由。杰里总算脱开了差事，跨出门去，把帽檐压到眼睛上，竖起了衣领——风越刮越紧，看样子又是个寒夜。他正迈着轻快的步子出院子，忽见一个披斗篷的身影迎面而来，是伊莫金。

===Original===
“Jerry,” she whispered, “who put up that gallows on your plot of land?”

“It’s my gallows,” answered Jerk proudly. “I paid for it, and Mister Mipps it was wot helped me to set it up.”

“It’s a real one, Jerry,” the girl replied.

“Yes, that it is—and ain’t it fine?”

“But there’s a man, a real man hanging there.”

At this Jerk slapped his knee with enthusiasm and cried aloud: “Now by all the barrels of rum! if I ain’t fit to take in the devil hisself, wot I believes is a sexton dressed up. For that same corpse wot you’ve seed a-danglin’ from my gallows tree ain’t a corpse at all, but sticks, sand, and sacks wot I invented to look like one.”

===Chinese===
“杰里，”她低声问，“你那块地上的绞架，是谁给立起来的？”

“那是我自个儿的绞架，”杰尔克得意地答道，“我花钱置办的，还是米普斯先生帮我立起来的呢。”

“杰里，那可是座真绞架。”姑娘说。

“可不是嘛——你说气派不气派？”

“可上头吊着个人，一个真人。”

杰尔克一听，起劲地一拍大腿，高声嚷道：“嘿，凭一桶桶朗姆酒起誓！我那手艺连魔鬼他老人家都哄得过——依我看呐，那魔鬼原本也不过是个化了装的教堂司事。你瞧见吊在我绞架树上的那具尸首，压根儿不是尸首，是我琢磨出来装样子的柴枝、沙土加口袋。”

===Original===
“Are you sure, Jerry?” said the girl.

“I’m a-goin’ out there myself now; so come along and see for yourself.”

“I’ve been there once this evening, Jerry.”

“Well, come along o’ me and you shall give the old scarecrow wot’s a-swing on my gallows a good sharp tweak in the ribs.” So off they set through the churchyard and out over the Marsh.

===Chinese===
“你拿得稳吗，杰里？”姑娘问。

“我这就要亲自上那儿去；走，你自个儿瞧一瞧就明白了。”

“今晚我已经去过一趟了，杰里。”

“那就跟我一块儿去，你可以冲着吊在我绞架上那老稻草人，结结实实地拧一把他的肋骨。”于是两人穿过教堂墓地，出了门，往湿地去了。

===Original===
“Jerry,” whispered the girl presently, “there’s something queer going to happen soon. Perhaps tonight. Perhaps tomorrow night. And it’s something uncommon queer, too.”

“Now what makes you think that?” said Jerry, looking up at her.

“I believe, Jerry, that there are certain tides that run from the Channel round Dungeness that wash up the dead seamen from the deep waters, and all the time that they lie near shore waiting for the ebb to take ’em back to their old wrecked ships in the deep their spirits come ashore and roam about us. I feel that way tonight. I can almost smell death in the air.”

“Well, that’s a funny notion,” remarked the boy, turning it over in his mind, “but I dare say you are right. After all, the sea, what does look so tidy on the top, must have lots of ugly secrets underneath, and I don’t see why it shouldn’t want to wash ’em ashore once in a way. I’ve often wondered myself about the dead what moves about inside the sea, and I thinks sometimes when the high tide runs into the great sluice and near fills the dykes that perhaps it buries things it’s sick of in the mud. P’raps it’s a-doin’ it now, and that’s wot’s given you them notions.”

“Perhaps it is, Jerry.”

===Chinese===
“杰里，”走了一程，姑娘低声说道，“眼看不久就要出一桩怪事。兴许就在今夜，兴许在明儿夜里，而且是桩顶顶蹊跷的怪事。”

“你这是怎么想到的？”杰里仰头望着她问。

“杰里，我相信，有些潮水是从英吉利海峡绕过邓杰内斯流过来的，会把深水里的溺海人冲上岸来。这些人躺在近岸处，等退潮把他们送回深海里那些失事的旧船上去；这当儿，他们的魂灵便上了岸，在我们身边游荡。今儿夜里我就有这种感觉，空气里的死气，我差不多都闻得到了。”

“嗐，这个想法倒新鲜，”这孩子把这话在心里掂了掂，说道，“不过我看你说得在理。说到底，海面瞧着那样齐整，底下必定藏着数不清的丑事；隔三差五把它们冲上岸来，也合乎情理。海里头那些游来荡去的亡魂，我自己也常犯嘀咕；有时候涨潮灌进大水闸，快把沟渠灌满了，我就想，兴许它是把厌烦的东西埋进烂泥里了。兴许它这会儿正这么干着呢，你这想法，怕就是这么来的。”

“兴许正是呢，杰里。”

===Original===
Now the mist was so thick that they did not get a far view of Jerk’s gallows; indeed they had crossed the one-planked bridge over the dyke and half climbed Gallows Tree Hill before they viewed it at all. But as soon as they did Jerry sprang forward crying: “Who’s been messing about with my bag o’ sticks?”

The sacking had been torn, and from the slit appeared a hand. Jerry seized the hand and pulled. The rusty chain squeaked, and one of the rotten links gave, and the ghastly fruit of the gallows tree fell upon the young hangman, who was borne to the ground beneath the falling weight. Imogene, with a cry, pulled it from him, and Jerk scrambled to his feet. Then they both looked.

The mildewed sacking, wet with the dense mist, had severed in the fall; the threads had rent at a hundred points, and from the fragments of scattered debris the dead face of Rash looked up with protruding eyes that stared from the blood-streaked flesh.

Jerk’s gallows had borne fruit.

===Chinese===
这时雾浓极了，杰尔克那座绞架，老远根本望不见；两人过了沟渠上那条独板小桥，把绞架山（Gallows Tree Hill）爬到半坡，方才看清了它。可一看之下，杰里立刻蹿上前去，嚷道：“谁动过我这袋柴枝？”

麻袋已经破了，破口里伸出一只手来。杰里抓住那只手就拽。锈链吱嘎直响，朽坏的一环崩断了，绞架树上那颗骇人的果实便砸在年轻刽子手身上，把他压倒在地。伊莫金惊叫一声，把它从他身上拖开，杰尔克手脚并用爬了起来。接着，两人都看清了。

浸透了浓雾的霉烂麻袋在坠落时绽裂开来，断丝百处，破布碎屑之间，拉什那张死人的脸仰面朝天，一双凸出的眼睛直勾勾地瞪着，血痕道道，布满皮肉。

杰尔克的绞架，结出了果实。

===Original===
For minutes they stood looking. The cloak had fallen from the girl’s shoulders, and the shrieking wind flapped in her rough dress and tore at her streaming hair. Jerk, with his ambitions fulfilled, found himself most uncomfortably scared. For minutes neither of them spoke. They could only stare. Stare at the huddled horror and listen to the jangle of the broken gibbet chain. Suddenly Imogene remembered something which brought her back to consciousness, for she spoke:

“Jerry, after seeing that, are you afraid to return to the village alone?”

Jerry had not yet found his voice, so he shook his head.

“Then go to the courthouse and report what we’ve found to the squire, and tell him that Imogene has gone out to keep the rest of her promise.”

===Chinese===
两人怔怔地站着，望了好几分钟。斗篷从姑娘肩头滑落，尖啸的风灌进她那身粗布衣裙，撕扯着她披散的湿发。杰尔克呢，心愿虽已了却，却发觉自己怕得厉害，浑身说不出的难受。好几分钟里，谁都没有做声，只有瞪眼干瞧的份儿——瞪着那一团蜷缩的恐怖，听着断裂绞链的哐啷声响。忽然，伊莫金想起一桩事，神志这才归了窍，开口道：

“杰里，见了这个，你还敢独自一个回村去吗？”

杰里还没找回自己的声音，只摇了摇头。

“那你就上议事厅去，把咱们见着的事报给乡绅，告诉他，伊莫金已经出门，履行她其余的诺言去了。”

===Original===
Jerry got her to repeat the sentence again, and he watched her leap the dyke and disappear into the mist, and then from behind the scaffold stepped the captain.

“You’ll do nothing of the kind, potboy,” he said, seizing Jerk’s arm and leading him away from the scaffold. “I’ve other work for you to do. We’re going back to the village to make our experiment.”

As they stumbled across the Marsh, scrambling the dykes that skirted the fields, the wind got up off shore, scattering the mists and driving them across the sea toward the beacons of France. Half an hour later, as the captain and Hangman Jerk approached the vicarage, a small fishing boat, carrying no light but much sail, raced before the screaming wind toward Dungeness, and with a firm hand grasping the tiller and a great heart beating high, stood Imogene, blinded with lashing spray and her drenched streaming hair, fighting the cruel sea to keep her word to the squire.

===Chinese===
杰里请她把这句话又说了一遍，眼看她跃过沟渠，隐进雾里去了；就在这时，绞架背后走出来那位上校。

“你什么也不许去报，酒保，”他说着抓住杰尔克的胳膊，把他从绞架边领开。“我另有差事派给你。咱们这就回村去，做咱们的试验。”

两人在湿地上跌跌撞撞地赶路，翻爬着沿田边蜿蜒的一道道沟渠；这时起了离岸的风，吹散浓雾，把它们卷过海面，推向法兰西的座座灯标。半个钟头后，上校和刽子手杰尔克走近牧师宅时，一条小小的渔船不见一点灯火，却张着满帆，正乘着尖啸的狂风朝邓杰内斯飞驰。舵柄前立着的正是伊莫金：一只手牢牢把住舵柄，一颗心怦怦激跳；横扫的浪花打得她睁不开眼，湿透的长发狂乱飞舞，她正同这无情的大海搏斗，要去履行她对乡绅许下的诺言。

## XXXII. The Captain's Experiment / 三十二　上校的试验

===Original===
They entered the vicarage by the back door and found the bo’sun roasting chestnuts on the bars of the kitchen fire. There was another man there, with his back to the door, and by his black clothes and scholarly stoop Jerry recognized the vicar. So quietly had the captain opened the door that neither of the men roasting chestnuts was aware of their presence. They went on roasting the nuts, when an astonishing thing happened: The vicar, in trying to take out a hot chestnut from the bar, knocked three of the bo’sun’s into the red-hot coals, which so enraged the bo’sun that he administered with his forearm a resounding clump on the back of the cleric’s head. Jerry thought this a distinct liberty, but the vicar only laughed, and when he turned round Jerry saw that it was Morgan Walters dressed in an entire clerical suit, and not Doctor Syn at all.

Morgan Walters looked sheepish and uncomfortable when he beheld the captain, but the latter remarked that his getup was magnificent, and that his black hair, which had been carefully sprinkled by the bo’sun with flour to make it gray, so nearly resembled that of the cleric, that Morgan Walters was evidently intended by Providence to be a parson, for such a capital one did he make. Thus encouraged, Morgan Walters strutted about the kitchen, and the likeness to Doctor Syn (for he was of the same build and Doctor Syn had always the sailor’s rolling gait) was so perfect that Jerk began to laugh, but was speedily hushed by the captain.

===Chinese===
他们打后门进了牧师宅，只见水手长（bo’sun）正就着厨房炉火的炉栅烤栗子。屋里另有一人，背对着门口；瞧那一身黑衣裳，再加上那副读书人的躬背，杰里（Jerry）认出他便是教区牧师。上校开门开得那样轻悄，烤栗子的两个人竟谁也没觉察有人进来。两人只顾烤着栗子，忽然出了一桩惊人的事：牧师伸手去炉栅上拣一颗烫栗子，却把水手长的三颗碰落进烧得通红的炭火里；水手长登时勃然大怒，抡起小臂，照着那位神职人员的后脑勺“啪”地敲了一记脆响。杰里觉得这一下实在放肆无礼，牧师却只是哈哈大笑；待他转过身来，杰里才看清，哪里是什么辛医生（Doctor Syn），分明是摩根·沃尔特斯（Morgan Walters），一整套牧师行头穿戴得齐齐整整。

摩根·沃尔特斯一见上校，登时露出羞窘不安的模样；上校却夸赞他这身行头气派极了，又说他那头黑发经水手长细心撒过面粉，弄得灰白，同那位牧师的头发像到了十分，足见上天造出摩根·沃尔特斯，本就打定主意要他当牧师——瞧他扮得多在行。得了这番鼓励，摩根·沃尔特斯便在厨房里趾高气扬地踱来踱去；他与辛医生相像得毫无破绽——他两人身量相当，而辛医生走起路来又素来是水手那种摇晃的步态——杰尔克（Jerk）看得笑出了声，却叫上校立刻止住了。

===Original===
“Now remember, Walters,” the captain said, “there’s no danger in this if you do exactly as I told you, but you will have to be spry, of course.”

“If he sticks me, then I deserves to be stuck,” replied Morgan Walters. “I’ve been Aunt Sally at the county fairs afore now, and never got whacked, not once. I always could bob down in time in those days, and I didn’t have no bo’sun’s whistle to help me.”

And then began the captain’s experiment, a most curious game, and, in spite of its tragic purpose, a humorous game it was.

===Chinese===
“现在记住喽，沃尔特斯，”上校说，“只要你分毫不差地照我方才教的去做，这里头没有半点凶险；不过嘛，你自然得手脚麻利些。”

“他若扎着我，那便是我活该挨这一下，”摩根·沃尔特斯答道，“想当年县里赶集，我就扮过‘萨莉婶婶’（Aunt Sally），从没叫人打着过，一下也没有。那阵子我总能及时缩头躲开，况且那时还没有水手长的哨子帮衬我哩。”

上校的试验就此开场——一局顶古怪的把戏；它纵然包藏着悲剧的用心，玩起来却透着滑稽。

===Original===
The bo’sun, whistle in mouth, was hidden in the little front garden; the captain and Jerk crouched in the corner of the room of which the window had no view; while Morgan Walters, in all points resembling Doctor Syn, sat reading in the ingle seat by the fire—sat reading a book with his back to the window, from which the shutters had been thrown open and the broken casement set ajar. It was a weird occasion: the captain crouching down in the corner holding on to young Jerk with a warning hand, the bo’sun with his whistle hidden in the garden, and the firelight aided by one candle upon the table throwing the two wavering shadows of the pseudo parson upon the whitewashed wall. Jerk could hardly persuade himself that it was not the Doctor, so clever was the rig-out of Morgan Walters, and he could hardly forbear letting out a laugh as the crafty seaman kept turning the pages of the book. But he had ample time to control himself before anything happened; indeed, a whole hour he had to wait—an hour which seemed a lifetime; and then the occurrence was swift and terrible.

A shrill whistle sounded from the garden; down went Morgan Walters’s head; and with a thud which broke the surrounding wall plaster into a thousand powdery cracks, a great harpoon trembled in the wall, exactly one foot above the settle.

===Chinese===
水手长嘴里衔着哨子，藏在前头的小花园里；上校和杰尔克蹲在屋里望不见那扇窗的角落；摩根·沃尔特斯则浑身处处都活脱是辛医生，坐在炉边的炉角座上读书——背朝着窗读一本书；那扇窗的窗板已经敞开，破损的窗扉半支着。这一幕光景煞是诡异：上校缩在角落里，一只手按住小杰尔克，示意他万勿出声；水手长衔着哨子藏身园中；炉火的光，再添上桌上一支蜡烛，把那假牧师的两条影子摇摇晃晃地投在粉白的墙上。摩根·沃尔特斯这身行头实在精妙，杰尔克简直难以相信眼前坐的不是医生本人；眼见那狡黠的水手一页一页翻着书，他好几次险些笑出声来。不过，变故到来之前，他有的是工夫按捺自己；说实在的，他足足等了一个钟头——那一个钟头漫长得像熬了一辈子；随后，事情来得又快又骇人。

花园里响起一声尖利的哨音；摩根·沃尔特斯的脑袋应声一矮；紧接着“咚”的一记闷响，四周墙皮震得绽开千百道粉状细纹，一柄大鱼叉颤巍巍地钉在墙上，恰在长凳上方一英尺的地方。

===Original===
“Gone!” shouted the bo’sun from the garden, and he immediately tumbled up through the window, closing the shutters behind him.

“Well, sir,” said Morgan Walters, “it wasn’t the ducking I minded when it came to it, but the waiting wasn’t pleasant.”

“You did well, my man,” said the captain. “And now, potboy, after that little experiment I’ll know how to proceed, how to prescribe like an analyzing apothecary, so, as it’s Sunday tomorrow, which ain’t far off now, we’ll get back to the Ship Inn, bo’sun, and you can light us there, whilst Morgan Walters can change his clothes and get back and to sleep.”

===Chinese===
“打出去啦！”水手长在花园里喊了一声，随即从窗口翻身爬进屋来，回手掩上了窗板。

“说真的，先生，”摩根·沃尔特斯说，“真到了节骨眼上，叫人打那么一下的滋味我倒不放在心上，只是这一阵干等，实在不是滋味。”

“干得漂亮，伙计，”上校说。“好了，酒保，经过这番小小的试验，我知道该怎么行事了，知道该像药剂师析药配方那样对症开方了。眼看明天就是礼拜天，时候也不早；水手长，咱们回‘船’酒馆（the Ship Inn）去吧，你在头里照路；摩根·沃尔特斯呢，换下这身衣裳，回家睡觉去。”

===Original===
So they left him there, the bo’sun with a lantern stepping before the captain and Jerk to the door of the Ship. Just as they reached the door a horseman galloped up from the Hythe Road and, saluting, asked if any could direct him to Captain Collyer. As soon as the captain had made himself known, Jerk saw the rider hand the captain a blue paper, which the latter put carefully into his pocket. Then he led the rider and the bo’sun into the sanded parlour and gave them drinks, after which he went home to bed and slept sound.

But back in the vicarage, just as Morgan Walters was about to divest himself of his ecclesiastical robes, Mr. Mipps entered with a loaded blunderbuss and requested him to turn round, hold his hands above his head, and precede him to the coffin shop at the farther end of the village.

===Chinese===
他们便把他撂在了那里；水手长提着一盏灯笼走在头里，把上校和杰尔克一路送到“船”酒馆门口。两人刚到门前，一名骑手从海斯（Hythe）大道上飞马赶到，敬了个礼，问可有哪位能指点他找到科利尔上校（Captain Collyer）。上校一通名号，杰尔克便瞧见那骑手递给上校一纸蓝色公文，上校小心地收进了衣袋。随后他领着骑手和水手长进了铺着细沙的堂屋，请两人喝了一盅，自己这才回家安歇，一觉睡得又沉又香。

可是牧师宅那边，摩根·沃尔特斯刚要褪下他那身教士袍服，米普斯先生（Mr. Mipps）端着一管装好弹药的喇叭铳走了进来，请他转过身去，双手举过头顶，在前头带路，跟他到村子那一头的棺材铺去。

===Original===
Doctor Syn slept at the courthouse, for he did not intend to go to the vicarage any more at night. He had a dread of that sitting-room of his. The horrible whirring of a certain weapon boring a hole through the shutter was still in his ears, and he could see a terrible eye, magnified by the bottle glass of the casement, looking in at him from the darkness. No, he had had enough of that room, he told himself, and so he welcomed the squire’s invitation to pass the night in the courthouse. Complaining of fatigue, he went to his room, but the squire sat up late wondering how Imogene was faring, and whether or no she would succeed in rescuing his son, and how in the world she was setting about it. About two o’clock in the morning he detected a smell of burning. He went upstairs. The smell seemed to be coming from the room assigned to Doctor Syn, but there was only the firelight showing under the door, so thinking that the Doctor was asleep, he put his eye to the keyhole. But the Doctor was not asleep. He was dressed in shirt and breeches, and the sleeves of the shirt were turned up. He was standing by the fireplace with a red-hot poker in his hand, looking at a seared mark upon his forearm.

“What the devil’s he burning his arm for?” thought the squire. Doctor Syn then began to whistle under his breath; to whistle that old tune the words of which the squire knew so well:

“Here’s to the feet wot have walked the plank.”

===Chinese===
辛医生睡在议事厅（courthouse）里，他打定主意，夜里再不回牧师宅了。他对自己那间起坐间心存畏惧。那件凶器钻透窗板时可怕的呜呜声还在耳边萦绕；他眼前还看得见一只可怕的眼睛，经窗扉的瓶底厚玻璃一放大，从黑暗里朝他屋里窥望。不，那间屋子他受够了，他对自己说；因此乡绅邀他在议事厅过夜，他正求之不得。他推说乏了，径自回房；乡绅却坐守到深夜，心里直犯嘀咕：伊莫金（Imogene）眼下怎么样了？她到底能不能救回他的儿子？她又究竟打算从哪儿下手？约莫凌晨两点，他嗅到一股焦煳味，便上楼去。那气味像是从拨给辛医生的房间里透出来的，可门底下只映着一线火光；他想医生已睡下了，便把眼睛凑到钥匙孔上。医生却没有睡。他只穿衬衫和马裤，衬衫袖子高高挽起。他站在壁炉跟前，手里攥着一根烧红的拨火棍，正端详着自己小臂上一处烧灼的印记。

“他烧自己的胳膊，搞的什么鬼名堂？”乡绅暗自纳罕。跟着，辛医生低声吹起口哨来；吹的正是那支老调子，歌词乡绅熟得不能再熟：

“这一杯，敬那走过跳板的脚板。”

===Original===
The squire remembered certain words of the captain: Clegg’s one tattoo—the picture of a man walking the plank, executed badly upon his forearm. “Good God! Was it possible? No! Ridiculous!”

An uncanny feeling came over the squire, and he went downstairs quietly, without knocking at the Doctor’s door, as he had intended—went downstairs to the fire in the library, relit his pipe, and began to think about Doctor Syn.

So when Sunday morning broke, two more strange things had happened: Morgan Walters, for one thing, had disappeared, parson’s clothes and all, and Doctor Syn, on going to the vicarage, discovered a new ugly gash in the plaster of the wall, and he felt indeed thankful that he had passed the night at the courthouse.

The villagers had it announced to them at the morning service that, in order to undertake a great spiritual mission to the blacks, Doctor Syn was leaving Dymchurch that very night; leaving after evensong by fishing lugger which was timed to pick up a certain Spanish trader bound for Jamaica and sailing upon the next day from the port of Rye. So all that Sunday afternoon the villagers, with much sorrow in their hearts at the thought of losing their faithful shepherd and good friend, prepared great beacons along the coast seawall as far as Littlestone, in order to light and cheer their vicar on his lonely way at night.

===Chinese===
乡绅想起了上校说过的某些话：克莱格（Clegg）一辈子只刺过一回青——一幅人走跳板的图，刺在小臂上，手艺很蹩脚。“天哪！这可能吗？不会！荒唐！”

一股阴森说不出的感觉攫住了乡绅。他本想敲一敲医生的房门，结果却没有敲，蹑手蹑脚地下了楼——下到书房的炉火边，重新点着烟斗，开始琢磨起辛医生这个人来。

就这样，礼拜天清晨到来时，又出了两桩怪事：头一桩，摩根·沃尔特斯不见了踪影，连那身牧师衣裳也一并无影无踪；第二桩，辛医生一回到牧师宅，便发现墙上又添了一道狰狞的新口子，他着实庆幸自己昨夜是在议事厅里过的夜。

晨礼拜上，村民听得宣布：为肩负一项向黑人传布福音的大使命，辛医生就在当夜离开迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）；晚祷之后，他将乘一条渔船（lugger）动身，按约定时辰去接一艘开往牙买加的西班牙商船——那商船次日便从拉伊（Rye）港启航。于是，整个礼拜天下午，村民们一想到就要失去他们忠实的牧人、善良的朋友，无不满怀哀伤；他们沿海堤一路备下堆堆大篝火，直到利特尔斯通（Littlestone），好在那天夜里点亮火光，为他们牧师孤身一人的旅途壮胆送行。

## XXXIII. Adventures in Watchbell Street / 三十三　观钟街历险

===Original===
Imogene had got to Rye, and got there through the devil of a bad sea. It was Sunday morning, and by the time that the church bells were ringing for matins she had safely beached her boat with the help of two fishermen who knew her well. With these two old salts she breakfasted. A rude meal it was, served in a hut upon the shingle. Fish, bread, and hot broth were things that she liked, and she did credit to the fare, for she was hungry. She was also sorely in need of sleep, and the old fellows tried to persuade her to take a nap, but she would not hear of it, for time pressed and she had much to do.

===Chinese===
伊莫金（Imogene）总算到了拉伊（Rye），而且是顶着顶恶劣的海况到的。那是个礼拜天的早晨；教堂钟声敲响晨祷的当儿，她已在两个相熟的渔夫帮忙之下，把小船稳稳当当地拖上了滩。她跟着这两位老盐巴一块儿用了早饭。饭食粗陋，就在卵石滩上一间小屋里开的一顿：鱼、面包、热腾腾的肉汤，都是合她口味的东西，她也没辜负这顿饭，因为她饿极了。她实在还困得厉害，两位老人家劝她打个盹儿，她却说什么也不肯——时辰逼人，她要做的事多着呢。

===Original===
Before leaving Dymchurch, Mrs. Waggetts had provided her with a case of pistols and a sealed packet of papers. This packet she now examined. It contained two papers. It was fortunate, indeed, that Doctor Syn had in his charity taught her to read. One of the papers was a letter of instructions telling her the easiest way of setting about the rescue of the squire’s son, and she knew the advice to be sound, for the signature bore the great name of the Scarecrow. What’s in a name, eh? More than Mr. Shakespeare gave credit for, because as the name of Robespierre had carried terror and power in France, and as the name of Napoleon was changed to Boney for the frightening of children by tyrannical nurses in England, so the title of the Scarecrow bore the like qualities on Romney Marsh, for it meant that the power of the smugglers was behind it, and would be used to force obedience to the Scarecrow’s behests. Imogene knew, therefore, that her papers were of power, credentials that would get her a hearing, and the rest must be left to her own initiative, her wits, and her courage, and to chance. Yes, if she carried out these orders to the letter she was pretty confident that all would be well. She read the letter of instructions till she had thoroughly mastered its contents, and then burned it on the bucket of live coals outside the hut. The other letter she kept, for she had great need of that. It was addressed to one Antony Whyllie, attorney-at-law, Watchbell Street, Rye, Sussex, and read:

We find that we have further need of your help. The son of our squire is in the hands of the Rye press gang. We have accordingly dispatched to you one of our messengers, a young girl upon whom no suspicions will fall. You must see to it that you and the girl succeed in rescuing the young man. If the girl returns without him, all we have to say to you is that it will be the worse for you both; it will also be the last of you both. We would have done well perhaps to send you more help in this difficult venture, but this we cannot do, the girl being the only one of our servants available. However, you will find in her a young woman of great resource, and of high courage, and those qualities, added to your well-known ability and cunning in getting out of difficult corners, should enable you to carry out our wishes for our own convenience and for the saving of your life, which we presume affords you some interest.

[Signed]

Scarecrow.

===Chinese===
动身离开迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）之前，瓦格茨太太（Mrs. Waggetts）交给她一匣手枪和一袋火漆封口的文件。这会儿她把文件袋拆开检视。里面是两份文书。真是万幸，辛医生（Doctor Syn）发善心教过她识字。其中一份是封指示函，教她搭救乡绅公子的最省力的门路；她知道这主意错不了，因为落款处签着稻草人（Scarecrow）的大名。名号这东西算得了什么，嗯？可它的分量，比莎士比亚先生笔下认账的还要重：罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）的名字在法兰西意味着恐怖与权势；拿破仑的名字，在英格兰叫凶暴的保姆们改成了「波尼」（Boney），拿来吓唬小孩；同样，在罗姆尼湿地（Romney Marsh），稻草人这个名号也带着一样的斤两，因为它意味着走私贩子的全部势力都在它背后撑腰，并且会被动用来逼人服从稻草人的号令。所以伊莫金明白，她手里这两纸文书是顶用的权柄，是能叫人洗耳恭听的凭证；剩下的事，就得凭她自己的魄力、她的机变、她的胆量，再添上几分运气了。是的，只要她一字不差地照着指令办，她满有把握一切都会顺当。她把那封指示函读到烂熟于心，便拿到屋外一桶火炭上烧了。另一封信她贴身收着，因为那封信她大有用处。信是写给萨塞克斯（Sussex）郡拉伊镇观钟街（Watchbell Street）的一位律师安东尼·怀利（Antony Whyllie）的，信文如下：

我等发觉，尚有一事，须再借重君的援手。我等乡绅的公子已落入拉伊强征队（press gang）之手。为此我等特遣我等的一名信使前往君处，是个年轻姑娘，绝不会惹人起疑。君务必设法，同这姑娘一道把那年轻人救出。倘若姑娘独自归来而人未救出，我等只有一言奉告：那于你们二人都是大大的不妙；那也将是你们二人的末路。这桩艰险的营生，我等本宜多派人手助君一臂，只是无从办到，姑娘乃我等手中唯一可供差遣的仆人。不过，君自会发现，她是个机变极多、胆色极高的年轻女子；凭这些长处，再加上君那出了名的脱困本事与狡黠，想必足以成我等之愿——此既是为我等行个方便，也是为搭救君自己的性命；想必君对此多少还有些兴致。

[签署]

稻草人

===Original===
With this useful letter tucked away in her blouse in company with one of Mrs. Waggetts’ pistols, Imogene, after bidding farewell to the two fishermen, struck out from the beach across the mile or so of flat country that lies in front of the little rising town of Rye. It is a fortified town, an ancient stronghold against whose walls the sea at one time used to beat but has long since receded. Her heart beat high as she looked up at the great battlements and the quaint little houses that clustered in all shapes and sizes around them, higher and higher, until they reached the church tower, the highest point of all.

She did not enter the town by the north gate, but skirted the wall and ascended the long irregular step-way that rises from the river wharf—a long ladder of stone that climbs the surface of rock zigzag till you find yourself at the top of the wall and standing upon the cobbled roadway of Watchbell Street—a thoroughfare made green with moss and with rank grass and rendered vastly attractive by the picturesque houses that flank its little pavements. To one of these little houses Imogene made her way, a little white house with a quaint little white front door. She pulled the brass chain, and in response to the bell a serving-maid announced that Mr. Whyllie was not then at home, having gone to church with his wife. So perforce she had to wait until the master and mistress returned from the morning service. A quaint old lady was the wife of the starchy old lawyer. She was dressed in highly flowered brocades, with a curious bonnet, under which her round little face shone out with much animation. A clever little face it was, with a queer little pursed-up mouth, and a tiny little nose with an upward tilt, and her eyes were lively. It was the face of a clever eccentric. Imogene saw them coming and gave them a profound courtesy as they drew near to their front door.

===Chinese===
伊莫金把这封顶用的信揣进怀里，同瓦格茨太太的一把手枪作伴；与两个渔夫道过别，她便打海滩动身，穿过拉伊这座渐次高起的小镇跟前那约莫一英里的平川。那是一座设防的市镇，一座古老的要塞；当年海潮曾拍打它的城墙，如今早已退远了。她仰头望着巍峨的雉堞，望着雉堞四周高高低低、大大小小挤作一团的奇巧小屋——屋子越摞越高，一直摞到教堂钟楼跟前，那是全镇最高的所在——她的心也随之怦怦跳得欢了。

她没有从北门进城，而是贴着城墙绕行，从河码头拾级而上，登那条曲折错落的长石阶——好一座石头长梯，之字形攀在岩面上，待你爬到尽头，人已在墙头之上，站在了观钟街的鹅卵石路面上——这条街教苔藓和蔓生的野草染得一片青绿，两侧人行小道旁立着一幢幢如画的房屋，愈发显得可亲可爱。伊莫金径直朝其中一幢小屋走去：一幢白色小屋，配一扇别致的小白前门。她拉动铜链，女仆应铃声出来回话，说怀利先生此刻不在家，同太太上教堂去了。她只好候着，等男女主人做完晨礼拜回来。这位古板老律师的太太，是位古怪有趣的老太太。她一身大花的织锦，头上扣着顶希奇古怪的帽子；帽子底下，一张团团的小脸神采奕奕。那是张精明的小脸：噘噘的小嘴，一只微微上翘的小鼻子，一双滴溜溜有神的眼睛。一张精明怪人的脸。伊莫金望见他们回来了，待两人走到自家前门跟前，便深深屈膝行了一礼。

===Original===
“Lord love you, Mister Whyllie,” the old lady exclaimed, “and what’s the pretty wench bobbing at us for?”

“It may be that she would speak to you, my dear,” replied the lawyer to his wife.

“Then why doesn’t she, sir?” answered the little lady, raising her glasses and quizzing Imogene from head to foot. “A handsome face she has. Mister Whyllie, a handsome face indeed, refined yet rough, but then again rough yet refined, take it how you will, but Lord love you again, Mister Whyllie, she has positively the most obnoxious clothes you could wish for to meet, and no shoes, neither has she stockings, sir, but shapely legs, sir, good legs indeed, though you need not embarrass the child by quizzing them, Mister Whyllie.”

Mr. Whyllie looked away awkwardly and, raising his hat, inquired whether Imogene wished to speak to them.

“I have come to speak to you, sir, on most grave business.”

“To do with one of my cases, I suppose,” he answered, by way of explanation, to his wife, for he had no wish that she should suspect him of having any dealings with such a handsome wench.

===Chinese===
「哎哟老天爷，怀利先生，」老太太嚷道，「这么标致的姑娘家，冲咱们又是躬身又是屈膝的，这是做什么呀？」

「兴许她有话要跟你讲呢，亲爱的，」律师回答他太太说。

「那她干嘛不讲呢，先生？」小个子太太应道，一面举起眼镜，把伊莫金从头到脚打量了个遍。「好一张标致脸蛋，怀利先生，实实在在标致：又秀气又带点野，可再一琢磨呢，又野里透着秀气，随你怎么品吧。不过，哎哟老天爷，容我再说一遍，怀利先生，她这一身衣裳，可是你一辈子也难撞见的顶顶惹人嫌的一身；脚上没鞋，先生，也没袜子，先生；可两条腿生得匀称，先生，确确实实是两条好腿——不过你也犯不上盯着人家瞧，臊着这孩子，怀利先生。」

怀利先生好不尴尬地把脸扭开，举了举帽子，问伊莫金可是有话要同他们讲。

「先生，我是为一桩顶要紧的事，来同您讲话的。」

「想必是我手头哪桩案子上的事吧，」他答道——这话是讲给太太听的，权作解释，因为他决不愿让她疑心自己同这样一个标致姑娘有什么瓜葛。

===Original===
“Which case?” snapped the suspicious little wife.

“Well, really, now, I cannot say off hand,” faltered the lawyer. “Probably the Appledore land claims, but I wouldn’t swear to it, for it could quite equally be something to do with the Canver squabble. In fact, more likely to be, quite likely to be. Probably is, probably is. It might so very well be that, mightn’t it, my love?”

“Yes, and it might *not* be that,” returned his wife with scorn. “Why don’t you ask the girl if you want to know, instead of standing there like the town idiot? Being a lawyer, I naturally suppose you to have a tongue in your head.”

“I have, my dear,” exclaimed the lawyer desperately, “but dang it, ma’am, you will not let me wag it.”

“You blasphemous horror!” screamed the lady, sweeping past him into the house, for the serving-maid was holding the front door open for them.

===Chinese===
「哪桩案子？」起了疑心的小个子太太紧逼一句。

「呃，这个，一时还真说不上来，」律师支吾道，「多半是阿普尔多尔（Appledore）那几桩田产讼案，可我也不敢打包票，因为兴许同坎弗（Canver）那场纠纷也脱得了干系。说实在的，倒更像是那桩，很有可能是那桩。多半就是，多半就是。非常可能就是那桩，不是吗，亲爱的？」

「是啊，兴许*不*是那桩呢，」他太太带着讥诮回敬道。「想知道，你问一句姑娘不就得了，杵在那儿活像全镇的大傻子做什么？你一个当律师的，我自然当你嘴里长着舌头呢。」

「我是长着舌头，亲爱的，」律师急得叫起来，「可该死，太太，你死活不肯让我摇一摇它呀。」

「你这亵渎神明的骇人东西！」太太尖叫一声，一拂袖从他身旁进了屋——女仆正替他们撑着前门呢。

===Original===
It was, by the way, a good thing for Antony Whyllie that his house was situated in a quiet corner of Watchbell Street, a very good thing, for these sudden squalls would repeatedly burst from his wife, regardless altogether of publicity.

With a sigh the attorney begged Imogene to follow him, and led the way into a little breakfast-room whose latticed windows looked out upon the street. It was a panelled room, but the panels were enamelled with white paint, which gave to the place a most cheerful aspect. Upon each panel hung a mahogany framed silhouette portrait of some worthy relative and over each panel was hung a brass spoon or brazen chestnut roaster, each one polished like gold and affording a bright contrast to the black portraits below, which stood out so very severely against the white panelling. There was in one corner of the room an embrasure filled with shelves, the shelves in their turn being filled with china. A round mahogany table, mahogany chairs, and a heraldic mantelpiece made up the rest of the furniture of this altogether delightful little room into which Imogene followed the lawyer, who placed a chair for her and shut the door. He then sat down by the fire and awaited her pleasure to address him. Imogene handed him the paper which had been prepared for her, and as he began to read she drew the silver pistol from her blouse and held it ready beneath a fold of her dress. That the lawyer was greatly startled was only too plain, for as he read the letter he turned a terribly pallid colour in the face.

===Chinese===
顺带说一句，对安东尼·怀利来说，他的宅子坐落在观钟街一处僻静的角落，实在是件好事，天大的好事，因为他太太这类晴天霹雳动不动就炸将出来，全然不顾有旁人听见。

律师叹了口气，请伊莫金随他来，领路进了间小早餐室，格子窗正临着街。屋子是镶板墙面的，不过板壁都涂了白瓷漆，满屋便透出一派明快的气象。每块板壁上挂着一幅红木框的剪影像，都是哪位体面亲戚；板壁上方又挂着一把铜匙或一只黄铜栗子炉，件件擦得金亮，同底下的黑色剪影恰成鲜亮对照——那些黑影像在白漆板壁的映衬下显得何等肃穆。屋角有一处凹室，安着搁架，架上层层摆满瓷器。一张红木圆桌、几把红木椅子，再加一座带纹章的壁炉台，便是这间处处可喜的小屋其余的家当。伊莫金跟着律师进了屋；律师替她安放一把椅子，掩上了门，然后在炉边坐下，静候她开尊口。伊莫金把那份替她备好的文书递了过去；趁他展读的工夫，她从怀里抽出那把银柄手枪，藏在裙褶底下握着待发。律师这一惊可着实不小，这瞒不过人的眼：他读着信，脸色刷地一下白得怕人。

===Original===
“God bless me! but it’s monstrous,” he said, starting up, with his eyes still on the paper. “Not content with holding up my coach, commandeering my horses, and making me look extremely ridiculous, they now force me, a lawyer, an honest lawyer, to break those very laws that I have sworn to defend. It’s monstrous! Utterly monstrous! What am I to do? What can I do? My wife must know of this! My wife must read this letter,” and accordingly he took a step toward the door. But Imogene was too quick for him. With her back against it and the pistol levelled at his head, the lawyer was entirely nonplussed.

“If you please, sir,” she said, “I had orders that you were not to leave the room, indeed that you were not to leave my sight, until I was quite satisfied that you would carry out the Scarecrow’s orders.”

“No, really?” exclaimed the lawyer.

“Yes, indeed, sir,” replied the girl, and then added in a frightened voice: “If you disobey the Scarecrow, it is just as well that I should shoot you here, for all the chance you will have to get away from the penalty, and for myself—well, the consequences would be as fatal to me in either case, so you see if you do not help me by obeying the letter you will not only be killing yourself but me, too.”

===Chinese===
「上帝保佑！这真正是岂有此理，」他嚷道，一跃而起，眼睛却还钉在信纸上。「拦我的马车，征用我的马，害我出尽了洋相，这还不算，如今竟逼我——我一个律师，一个规规矩矩的律师——去破坏我宣誓要捍卫的法律。岂有此理！真正岂有此理！我该怎么办？我又能怎么办？这件事必须让我太太知道！这封信必须让我太太读一读！」说着他便朝门口迈了一步。可伊莫金比他手快得多。她背靠着门，手枪平平端起，正对他的脑袋；律师登时没了辙。

「劳驾，先生，」她说，「我领的吩咐是：在我没有完全确信您会照稻草人的令旨办事之前，您不许离开这间屋子，甚至不许离开我的视线。」

「哦，真的吗？」律师叫出声来。

「千真万确，先生，」姑娘答道，跟着又用发颤的声音添上一句：「您若违抗稻草人，还不如就让我在这儿把您打死，反正您想逃过惩罚，也是一点儿指望没有；至于我自己嘛——唉，两种下场对我都是死路一条。所以您瞧，您若不肯遵信行事、帮我的忙，那就不光是害死您自己，连我也一块儿害死了。」

===Original===
The lawyer looked blankly at Imogene, and then, retreating from the close and unpleasant proximity of the pistol, sank into his armchair.

“Put it down, girl! Put that pistol down for heaven’s sake, for how can I think whilst I am being made a target of?”

Imogene lowered the weapon.

“I really don’t know what to say,” went on the wretched old man. “I am entirely fogged out of all vision. Muddled, muddled—entirely muddled. I wish you would let my wife come in. Oh, how I do wish you would! Whatever her faults may be, she is really most excellent at thinking out difficulties of this kind. In fact, I must confess that she does all my thinking work for me. Women sometimes, you know, have most excellent brains—quick brains. They have, you know. Really they have. Quick tongues, too. My wife has. Oh, yes, really, you know, she’s got both, and the tongue part of her is developed to a most astonishing degree. But give her her due. Give her her due. So’s her brain. So’s her brain. A most clever brain—most clever. Very quick; exceptionally alert. As clever as a man, really she is. In fact, she’s absolutely cleverer than most. She’s cleverer than me. Oh, yes, she is. I confess it. I’m not conceited. Why, she does all my work for me—so there you are. It proves it, don’t it? Writes all my speeches for me. Really, you know, I am utterly useless without her. She guides me—absolutely guides me, she does. Why, alone I’m hopeless. How on earth do you suppose that I can get a young man out of the hands of the Rye press gang? They’re the most desperate of ruffians. The most desperate set of good-for-noughts that you could possibly wish to meet.”

===Chinese===
律师直勾勾地瞪了伊莫金半晌，然后从那杆逼得人浑身不自在的手枪跟前退开，一屁股跌进扶手椅里。

「放下，姑娘！看在上天的份上把枪放下，我让人当靶子瞄着，还怎么动脑筋想事情？」

伊莫金把枪放低了。

「我真不知道该说什么好了，」可怜的老头儿接着说。「我整个儿叫雾罩住了，一点头绪也瞧不见。糊涂，糊涂——一塌糊涂。求你让我太太进来吧。哦，我真是求之不得！她纵有千般不是，替人盘算这路难题，可真是顶顶出色的好手。说实在的，我得招认，我那些动脑筋的活儿全是她替我干的。女人家有时候，你晓得，脑子好使得很——转得飞快。真是这样，你晓得。千真万确。舌头也快。我太太便是。哦，没错，真的，你晓得，她两样都占，舌头那一项，更发达到了叫人吃惊的地步。可也得给她个公道。给她个公道。脑子也是。脑子也是。一副顶精明的脑子——精明透顶。转得飞快，机灵得出奇。跟男人一般聪明，真的。说实在的，她绝对比大多数人强。她比我强。哦，没错，她就是比我强。我招认。我不自大。喏，我那些活儿全是她替我干的——这不结了。这不就证明了吗？我的讲稿也全是她替我写的。说真的，你晓得，没有她我就是个地道的废物。她指引我——千真万确指引我。是啊，单靠我自己就完蛋了。你倒说说，我凭什么本领，能从一个拉伊强征队手里把一个年轻小伙子弄出来？那是一伙顶顶亡命的恶棍，是你们一辈子也难碰上的顶顶亡命、顶顶不成器的一伙东西。」

===Original===
The handle of the door turned suddenly, but Imogene’s foot was not easily shifted.

“There’s something in the way of the door, you clumsy clodhopper!” called the voice of Mrs. Whyllie from outside.

“I know there is, my love,” faltered the husband, and then to Imogene he said: “Oh, please let her come in. She will be quiet, I’m sure.” Then in a louder tone: “You will be quiet, won’t you, my love?”

“Antony,” called the voice of the spouse, “are you addressing yourself to that handsome girl? Are you calling her your love?” Then in a tone of doom: “Wait till I get in!”

“Oh, dear, oh, dear, she’s misunderstanding me again. Don’t let her come in now, for heaven’s sake!” But Imogene had already opened the door and in had burst the little lady, and without heeding Imogene she rushed across the room and administered with her mittened hand a very resounding and sound box upon her husband’s ear.

“Now perhaps you will behave yourself like a respectable married man, like an old fogey that you are, like everything in fact that you ought to be, but aren’t and never will be! Will you behave yourself now, you truly terrible old man?”

===Chinese===
门柄忽然转动起来，可伊莫金的脚却不是那么好挪开的。

「门口有什么东西堵着哪，你这笨手笨脚的庄稼汉！」怀利太太的声音在门外喊。

「我知道有东西堵着，亲爱的，」做丈夫的支吾着，又转身对伊莫金说：「哦，求你放她进来吧。她一定安安静静的，我担保。」跟着又提高嗓子：「你会安安静静的，对不对，亲爱的？」

「安东尼，」太太的声音喊道，「你是在跟那个标致姑娘说话吗？你管她叫亲爱的？」跟着换了宣判一般的腔调：「等我进来再说！」

「哦，天哪，天哪，她又误会我了。看在上天的份上，这会儿千万别放她进来！」可伊莫金早已把门打开，那位小个子太太已经冲了进来；她全然不理会伊莫金，一阵风似的抢过屋子，戴着连指手套的手抡圆了，结结实实赏了丈夫一记响亮的耳光。

「这下子，你总可以规规矩矩像个体面的有妇之夫了，像个你这样的老古板了，像一切你本来该是、却不是、也永远成不了的样子了！如今你肯规矩了吧，你这真正骇人的老东西？」

===Original===
“Certainly, my love,” meekly replied the lawyer, “but do look at this young lady.”

“Sakes alive!” she exclaimed when she did look at Imogene, “for if she hasn’t got a pistol in her hand, you’re no fool, Antony!”

“She has got a pistol in her hand, my love, and I’ll not only be a fool, but a dead fool, if you don’t find some way out of the difficulty.”

“And what is the difficulty, pray?” she asked, looking from her terrified husband to the extraordinary girl. “Oh, keep that pistol down, will you, my dear? for there is no immediate danger of my eating you. Just because I keep this fool of a husband of mine in his place, you mustn’t think me an utter virago.”

“I am afraid it is me that you will be thinking a virago,” answered the girl, still feigning fear in her voice, “but indeed I cannot help myself. This unpleasant situation has been forced upon me.”

===Chinese===
「一定规矩，亲爱的，」律师服服帖帖地答道，「不过您倒是瞧瞧这位小姐呀。」

「我的老天爷！」她这才朝伊莫金一望，失声叫道，「她手里要是没攥着一杆手枪，那你就不算傻子了，安东尼！」

「她手里实实在在攥着一杆手枪，亲爱的；你要是再不想个法子解这个围，我不光要做傻子，还要做死傻子了呢。」

「那么请问，到底是什么难题呀？」她问道，目光从吓坏了的丈夫移到这个出格的姑娘身上。「哦，把枪放低些好不好，亲爱的？我又不会立刻把你一口吞了。我不过管着家里这个傻瓜丈夫，不让他越出本分，你总不至于就把我当成个十足的悍妇吧。」

「只怕被当成悍妇的该是我才对，」姑娘答道，声音里仍装着害怕，「可我实在是没有法子。这难堪的局面是硬逼到我头上来的。」

===Original===
But the old lady cut in again with: “I beseech you both to cease making melodramatic idiots of yourselves and tell me calmly and clearly what all this to-do is about. Now, Antony, speak up and tell me all about it. Come along, sir, make haste and tell me if you have any ideas left in that silly head of yours. No doubt you’ve been getting yourself into another pretty mess. Isn’t it enough for you that you go out, sir, a-driving and get robbed of your coach and cattle? I should really have thought that had been quite enough to keep you out of mischief for a day or two. But no! Here you are in trouble again. No doubt you have quite forgotten the little lecture I read to you upon that occasion?”

“No, my dear, I cannot forget it, I assure you. It is still very vivid to me, I promise you.” For indeed the little old man was still very conscious of a strange feeling of slippers whenever he chanced to sit down.

“Oh, yes, you have forgotten it,” went on the irrepressible lady. “You must have done so. Now tell me what on earth have you been doing to make this handsome girl behave in such a ridiculous fashion?”

===Chinese===
老太太却又插进话来：「我求你们两位，别再演这出闹剧耍宝了，安安静静、清清楚楚地告诉我，这一通折腾到底为的什么。好了，安东尼，大声说，一五一十都讲给我听。来吧，先生，趁你那傻脑袋里还剩点主意，赶快讲。不用问，你又给自己惹出一场好乱子来了。先生，你出门坐了趟车，叫人把马车连同马匹都劫了去，这还不够你受用的吗？我实在以为，单凭这一桩，总够你安分个一天两天的了。可不成！你瞧你，又惹上麻烦了。不用问，那天我念给你听的那番小小训话，你准是忘得一干二净了吧？」

「没有忘，亲爱的，我断不敢忘，我向你担保。至今历历在目，我向你起誓。」的确，这小老头儿每回不当心往椅子上一坐，总还分明觉出一阵拖鞋加身的怪滋味。

「哦，得了吧，你就是忘了，」那压不住阵脚的太太接着说。「你准是忘了。现在你老实讲，你到底干了什么好事，叫这么标致的姑娘做出这等荒唐的举动来？」

===Original===
With one hand still rubbing his boxed ear and with the other holding out to his wife the terrible letter, the lawyer explained as coherently as possible the whole situation. He told the facts in a timid voice, for he was greatly troubled as to how his wife would take it, but her manner was the most shocking surprise to him, it was so entirely different from anything he might have expected, for when she heard about the press gang, she clapped her little mittens together, and, laughing aloud, urged her husband to go on with the tale which she found the most refreshing she had heard for a month of Sundays, and at the conclusion she gave way to the most extraordinary capers of excitement, literally tripping round and round the table, exclaiming that nothing could have been more fortunate. “La, sir,” she cried, “this little affair is truly a Godsend to me.”

“In whatever way?” asked the amazed lawyer.

“Why, you disproportionate dullard! Who is head of the press gang, eh? Answer me that now, and you’ve got it.”

“Captain Tuffton, isn’t it, my love?” said the lawyer.

“Captain Tuffton, of course it is,” said his wife. “Captain Tuffton of a truth. That insufferable coxcomb, that atrociously obnoxious scent-smelling profligate on whom I shall now be able to pay off old scores.”

“Old scores, my love? Old scores?”

===Chinese===
律师一只手还揉着挨了耳光的耳朵，另一只手把那封可怕的信递给太太，竭力把事情原原本本、条理清楚地讲了一遍。他陈述情由时声音发怯，因为他大费踌躇，不知太太听了会作何反应；谁知太太的态度却叫他大吃一惊，跟他所能预想的任何情形都全然不同：一听强征队三个字，她把两只小连指手套一拍，放声大笑起来，还催丈夫往下讲——在她听来，这是她多少个礼拜以来听过的顶顶痛快的一桩故事；听到末了，她更是乐得忘乎所以，当真绕着桌子一圈又一圈地旋转雀跃，嘴里直嚷天底下再没有比这更凑巧的事了。「哎呀，先生，」她叫道，「这桩小小的差事，真正是老天爷赏给我的恩典！」

「怎么就恩典了？」律师愕然问道。

「嗐，你这蠢得离谱的榆木脑袋！强征队的头儿是谁，嗯？你回答我这一句，答案就有了。」

「是塔夫顿上尉（Captain Tuffton），对吧，亲爱的？」律师说。

「塔夫顿上尉，当然是塔夫顿上尉，」他太太说。「千真万确是塔夫顿上尉。那个叫人忍无可忍的花花公子，那个浑身香水气、可恶透顶的浪荡子——这一回，我可要跟他清算老账了。」

「老账，亲爱的？什么老账？」

===Original===
“La, sir, have you utterly forgotten how he snubbed me at Lady Rivers’s card party and again at his lordship’s water picnic? Has that slipped your memory, too? How he got that appallingly painted besom of a Parisian actress to imitate me to my face? Lord love you, Mister Whyllie, I have long sworn to get even with that young idiot. Why, it was only this morning that I was puzzling out a thousand schemes all through church for his undoing, and here comes a direct answer to my prayers, and you seem to have covered yourself with the blues about it. Why, Mister Whyllie, here is not only a chance to humble him to the dust, but a most admirable occasion for his disgrace as well.”

“I am truly glad to hear you say so,” was the husband’s comment. “But I’m danged if I can see how you are to set about it.”

“Through the help of this girl here, stupid, and by the bewitching charms of your handsome niece from India, who has returned to England with her large fortune inherited from the British East India Company.”

The lawyer stared at his wife blankly, then genuine concern for that lady’s health getting the better of his amazement, he said: “Can I fetch you your salts or anything, my love? Your pounce box or your vinaigrette? for I declare that you are wandering in your mind, my poor dear. I never had a niece in all my life, my love, and as for the British East India Company—well, I have heard of it, of course, but little else indeed—very little else.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，先生，他在里弗斯夫人（Lady Rivers）的牌会上冷落我，又在勋爵大人的水上野宴上冷落我，你忘得精光了吗？这个也从你记性里溜了？他找来那个脂粉涂得吓死人的巴黎戏子，当着我的面学我的模样，这个也忘了？老天爷在上，怀利先生，我早就赌下咒来，非跟那个小浑蛋扯个平不可。怎么了，就在今天早晨，整场礼拜的工夫，我还在心里千条万条地盘算着整治他的计策；如今祷告的回音应声而至，你倒好，为这事把自己裹了一身愁云惨雾。怎么了，怀利先生，这不光是叫他低头认栽的机会，还是叫他当众出丑的绝妙良机呢。」

「听你这么说我真高兴，」丈夫如此评道。「可我到死也看不出，你打算从哪儿下手。」

「靠眼前这位姑娘帮忙呀，笨蛋，再靠你那位印度回来的漂亮侄女的迷人丰姿——她刚从英国东印度公司（British East India Company）继承了一大笔家财，回英格兰来了。」

律师直瞪瞪地望着太太，跟着，对太太贵体安康的真切关切压过了他的惊愕，他说道：「我去把您的嗅盐拿来可好，亲爱的？或是您的香粉盒、香嗅瓶什么的？因为我敢说，您这是神志错乱了，我可怜的亲爱的。我这一辈子从没有什么侄女，亲爱的；至于英国东印度公司嘛——呃，我自然听说过，此外就实在所知无多了——实在所知无多。」

===Original===
“Well, for today you will have to know a good deal about it,” said Mrs. Whyllie, “so you had better step into the library and read up its history, and as to your niece, your favourite niece, you will please do me the favour of remembering that you possess her, too, sir. Now, then, Mistress,” addressing Imogene, “as soon as this husband of mine has taken himself off, I’ll tell you your part in this affair.” Taking the hint, the lawyer beat a retreat to the library, gladly leaving the difficult business in the hands of his wife. “Now, girl,” she went on when they were alone, “I suppose I shouldn’t be very far wrong if I surmised that you are head over ears in love with this young man that the press gang has taken, eh?”

“Yes, I love him,” said the girl quietly.

“Ah!” sighed the lady, “that’s all right, and I suppose I’m also not far out if I suppose that you would do a good deal to save him from being shipped off to the wars, eh?”

“I will do anything to save him from that danger,” said the girl.

“Good!” replied the old lady. “Then come upstairs with me.”

===Chinese===
「哼，那从今天起，你就得好好地知道它一通了，」怀利太太说。「你还是进书房去，把它的历史好好读上一读；至于你那位侄女，你最心爱的侄女，也请你赏个脸记住：她也是你的，先生。好了，姑娘，」她转向伊莫金说道，「等我这个丈夫一走开，我就告诉你，你在这桩事里要扮演什么角色。」律师会意，退避三舍进了书房，乐得把这桩难缠的事交到他太太手里。「好了，姑娘，」屋里只剩她们两人时，她接着说，「要是我猜得不算太离谱，你跟强征队抓走的那个年轻人，是深深爱着，爱得没顶了，嗯？」

「是的，我爱他，」姑娘平静地说。

「啊！」太太叹道，「这就对了。那么我再猜一句：为了救他，不叫他给装上船送去打仗，你什么都肯干，嗯？」

「为了救他脱离那个险境，我什么都肯干，」姑娘说。

「好！」老太太答道。「那就跟我上楼来。」

===Original===
Out of the room and across the little hall they went, and so up the broad white staircase to the dearest little bedroom imaginable, with a small four-posted bed with chintz frills and hangings, and a dressing-table set with bright silver ornaments.

“Now this room is for you, my dear, for my handsome niece from India, you understand? And now I must ask you to change your clothes and get into some pretty frock or other, and I must have you to know, my dear, that I have been married twice, and by my first marriage I must tell you, my dear, that I had a daughter, a really beautiful daughter. This was years ago, of course, but she was just about your age as I remember her—By the way, what is your age, my dear?”

“About sixteen, or I might be seventeen perhaps,” said Imogene.

===Chinese===
她们出了屋子，穿过小门厅，走上宽宽的白楼梯，来到一间任你怎么想也想不出的顶可爱的小卧房：一张小小的四柱床，垂着细棉布的褶边和帐幔；一张梳妆台，摆着亮晶晶的银饰。

「这间屋子就是给你的了，亲爱的，也就是给我那位印度来的漂亮侄女的，明白了吗？现在我要请你换换衣裳，换上随便哪件漂亮裙衫；还有一层，你得知道，亲爱的：我结过两回婚。头一婚，我得告诉你，亲爱的，我有过一个女儿，一个真正美丽的女儿。那自然是多年以前的事了，可在我记忆里，她正是你这个年纪——对了，你今年多大，亲爱的？」

「十六岁上下，兴许有十七了，」伊莫金说。

===Original===
“Ah, well, my daughter was just nineteen when she died,” went on the old lady. “She was all I had in the world, for her father had died when she was quite a child. Yes, she was all that I had to love for fifteen years, and when she was taken I was so desperately lonely that in a weak moment I married that foolish Mister Whyllie, who is really very kindhearted and quite a good man, but, oh! how dull! Indeed, my dear, he would never have been in the position he is now if I hadn’t pushed him there. You see, my dear, he hasn’t much brain. Why, he cannot boast a third of my power, but on the whole I am glad that I married him, because he has given me such a lot to do helping him deceive other people that he isn’t a born fool. But I really must not talk such a lot, for we have a deal to do, my dear. But I must just explain this: I spent a good deal of money upon pretty frocks for my daughter, and, oh! how sweet she used to look in them. Well worth the money it was, my dear, to see her look so pretty. Now every one of these dresses I have kept, and kept carefully, too. If the sweet child came back to me now, she would find all her things as well cared for, as clean, and as fresh as when she left me, for this was her room (this house belongs to me, my dear, not to that fool downstairs), and in these chests and in that oaken tallboy there I have kept everything that reminds me of my darling. See!” And taking a key from a casket upon the chimneypiece she unlocked the tall oak cupboard, displaying to Imogene’s gaze a sight to make her stand entranced. The daintiest dresses were there, and in the brassbound coffer at the end of the bed the most costly laces and fine linen, and all kept sweet and pure in a strong scent of lavender. From these sacred treasures the old lady made selections, and by the time that the gong had sounded for the three o’clock dinner, instead of the handsome, dashing fisher-girl, there sat before the mirror, having the finishing touches put to her beautiful hair, done in the height of the fashion then existing, a beautiful young girl in a gown of country splendour, jewels glistening in her hair, and a diamond brooch of great beauty clasped into a lace fichu which set off her shapely neck to great advantage.

===Chinese===
「唉，可惜，我女儿死的时候刚满十九岁，」老太太接着说。「她是我在人世上仅有的一切，她父亲在她还很小的时候就故去了。是的，十五年光阴里，她就是我全部的疼爱；她一被上帝召了去，我孤苦得没了着落，一时软弱，就嫁了怀利那个傻先生。他心肠倒是真热，人也不坏，可是，哦！多么乏味！说实在的，亲爱的，我若不把他推到如今这个位子上，他一辈子也坐不上去。你要明白，亲爱的，他脑子有限。他浑身的本事，还夸口不上我的三分之一；不过大体说来，嫁了他我也不悔，因为他给了我那么多营生——帮着他去哄骗旁人——足见他还不是个天生的傻瓜。可我真不该这样絮叨，亲爱的，我们要办的事多着呢。只是有一样，我必须说明白：当年我在女儿的漂亮裙衫上，花过好大一把钱；她穿起来，哦，多么标致！瞧着她的俊模样，亲爱的，那钱花得值。这些裙衫，如今我一件也没丢，件件都细心收着。要是那可爱的孩子如今回到我身边，她会看见她所有的东西都保管得好好的，干干净净、清清爽爽，同她离开我的时候一模一样；因为这间屋子就是她的屋子（这房子是我的，亲爱的，不是楼下那个傻子的），这些箱匣里、还有那边那口栎木高柜里，凡是能叫我想起我心肝的东西，我都收在里面。瞧！」她从壁炉台上一只小匣里取出一把钥匙，开了那口高大的栎木立柜；眼前展现在伊莫金面前的景象，叫她看得出了神：那里头是顶顶精致的裙衫；床尾一只包着铜皮的小柜里，是最名贵的花边和细亚麻布；件件东西都让浓烈的薰衣草香熏得芬芳洁净。老太太从这些珍爱的宝物里拣选起来；等三点钟正餐的锣声敲响，坐在镜前、由人替她一头梳得入时极了的秀发做最后梳理的，已不是那个俊俏利落的渔家姑娘，而是一位美丽的少女：身上一袭乡间华服，发间宝光闪烁，一枚绝美的钻石胸针扣着蕾丝三角胸巾，把她那秀美的脖颈衬托得分外动人。

===Original===
While she had been dressing the girl the old lady had with great tact got all of Imogene’s history out of her, at least as much of it as she knew, and just before they stepped from the room, as she surveyed her protégée with admiration, she held up her little quaint face and requested Imogene to kiss her, which she did.

“And now, my dear, we will go down to dinner, and the while we are eating I will tell you exactly what we are to do, and,” she added with enthusiasm, “if that squire’s son, whom I regard as a fortunate young fellow, does not marry you—well, I’ll horsewhip him myself, aye, both him and his father, and adopt you as my own daughter, for what a relief it would be to have you in the house to look at, for you know, my dear, you are vastly prettier than my foolish Mister Whyllie,” saying which she tripped lightly down the stairs followed by the dazzling Imogene.

===Chinese===
老太太替姑娘装扮的这阵工夫，言语间手段高明，已把伊莫金的来历套问了个一清二楚——至少她自己所知道的那一部分。临出房门，她带着满心的赞赏端详着自己一手栽培的人，仰起那张希奇的小脸，要伊莫金亲亲她；伊莫金依言亲了。

「好了，亲爱的，咱们下楼用饭去。吃饭的工夫，我就一五一十地告诉你，咱们该怎么动手。还有，」她兴致勃勃地添上一句，「那位乡绅公子——我看他是个有福气的年轻人——他若不娶你，哼，我亲自拿马鞭抽他，没错儿，连他老子一块儿抽；再认你做我的亲闺女。有你这样的人才在屋里养眼，该是多舒心的事；要知道，亲爱的，你比我家怀利那个傻先生俊多了。」说罢，她轻快地一步三跳下了楼，光彩照人的伊莫金跟在后面。

===Original===
Had Imogene been in reality the old lady’s daughter, returned to her from the dim side of the veil, she could not have been shown more kindly love and attention. Even Mr. Whyllie got a happy time of it, for the little old lady was in the best of tempers, entirely at peace and lighthearted. Indeed at the conclusion of the meal the lawyer found himself pushed into a comfortable chair with a small table at his side upon which stood a fine old bottle of port, and to his utter astonishment his wife standing near with a churchwarden pipe filled with tobacco and a lighted paper spill all ready for him. So he also began to bless the coming of his niece from India, wishing that she had been invented sooner and that she was going to remain in the house to the end of the proverbial chapter.

Then Mrs. Whyllie, over a dish of tea with Imogene, unfolded her plan of campaign for the rescue of young Denis, and the manner in which this plan was carried out is set forth in a following chapter.

===Chinese===
即便伊莫金真是老太太的亲女儿，从幽冥纱幕暗淡的那一侧回到她身边，她也受不到更多更亲的怜爱了。连怀利先生也过了好一阵舒心日子：小老太太心情好到了十分，心平气和，眉开眼笑。果然，饭快吃完的时候，律师发觉自己被人推进一张安乐椅，手边一张小桌，桌上立着一瓶陈年上好的波特酒；更叫他诧异到极点的是，太太就站在近旁，手里一支教区长长柄烟斗，装满了烟丝，纸捻也点着了，一切齐备，单等他来享用。于是他也开始祝祷印度侄女的大驾光临，只恨这样一位侄女怎么不早点儿发明出来，又巴望她长长久久住在这宅子里，一直住到老话儿里说的最后一章。

随后，怀利太太同伊莫金对坐用茶，把营救年轻的丹尼斯（Denis）的作战方略和盘托出；至于这方略如何施行，且听下一章分解。

## XXXIV. A Military Lady-Killer Prepares for Battle / 三十四　军人猎艳客整装备战

===Original===
That insufferable coxcomb Captain Tuffton was in the act of sprinkling his lace handkerchief with the scent that old Mrs. Whyllie found so atrociously obnoxious when his valet entered the room with a note. The insufferable one went on with his sprinkling and languidly inquired who the note was from.

“I really cannot say, sir,” returned the valet.

“Cannot say?” repeated the insufferable, lifting his pencilled eyebrows into the higher regions of astonishment. “Indeed, my good Transome — and you call yourself a valet, don’t you now? It is not a bill, I trust, strayed in upon the Sabbath out of cunning, for I have not seen a bill these many years now, and the sight, I feel convinced, might upset my stomach.”

“I think, sir, that there is no valet in Europe so quick to smell out a bill or so nimble at tearing them up as your humble servant.” Transome could be tremendous upon occasions and he certainly was when he added: “And under your livery, sir, I venture to suggest that my practice of bill nosing has been unlimited.”

===Chinese===
那个叫人受不了的花花公子塔夫顿上尉（Captain Tuffton），正往他的花边手帕上洒香水——就是老怀利太太（Mrs. Whyllie）闻了要嫌恶到极点的那种香水——这当口，他的贴身男仆拿着一张字条走了进来。那位受不了的人物洒香水洒个不停，懒洋洋地问字条是谁写来的。

“小的实在说不上来，先生。”贴身男仆答道。

“说不上来？”那位受不了的人物把描过的眉毛朝惊愕的高处一挑，重复道。“可不是嘛，我的好特兰索姆（Transome）——你还自称是个贴身男仆呢，是不是？我谅它总不是账单吧——账单还会狡猾地溜进安息日里来？我这许多年没见过账单了，那玩意儿一见，我敢说，准要搅得我倒胃口。”

“依小的看，全欧洲再没有第二个贴身男仆，能像小的这样鼻子灵，一闻便嗅出是账单，也没有谁撕起账单来有小的这般利索。”特兰索姆偶尔也极有气势，此刻便是一例，他又添上一句：“在您这身号衣底下，先生，小的斗胆进言，小的这手嗅账单的营生，向来是大有用武之地的。”

===Original===
“Now, come, my good Transome, you disrespectful dog. I’ll not have you chiding me, upon my soul I won’t, for I have a most damned head on me this forenoon. I generally do get a damned bad head on me o’ Sundays. All abuzz, I declare, and it’s those damned exasperating church bells. I never met anything so persistent in my life. They go on, they go on, and there’s no stopping them, now is there? As plentiful as bills are church bells and just as taxing to the nerves. If ever I have to oblige the blasted Parliament by sleeping in it, I shall endeavour to keep awake to vote for the abolishment of church bells.”

“And you might, sir, at the same time do away with bills. It would be most convenient, wouldn’t it, sir?”

“Well, I suppose it would. If I ever do get in, which I think extremely unlikely, for which I most heartily thank my Maker, knowing how unutterably bored I should become, but if ever I do get in, I will most certainly abolish bills and bells, and if there should be any other little thing that you think might sensibly be abolished, why, you must jog my memory, Transome, and jog it hard, won’t you, my dear fellow, for you know what a memory I have? Damned bad, upon my soul it is!”

“Ah, sir,” sighed the valet, “you will become a great orator, a very great orator.”

===Chinese===
“得啦得啦，我的好特兰索姆，你这没有规矩的狗东西。我可不许你数落我，凭我的灵魂起誓，决计不许，因为我今天上午这颗脑袋疼得要命。每逢礼拜天，我这脑袋总要疼得死去活来。嗡嗡直响，我敢说，全是那些可恶的教堂钟闹的。我一辈子没见过这么缠人的东西。它们敲呀，敲呀，怎么也停不下来，是不是？教堂钟跟账单一样多，也一样磨人的神经。哪一天我要是不得不赏那该死的议会一个脸、钻进去睡觉的话，我一定竭力撑着别睡，好投上一票，赞成把教堂钟统统废掉。”

“那您兴许还能顺手把账单也一并废掉，先生。那可就方便多了，是不是，先生？”

“唔，我想是会方便的。我若真能进去——我看这事绝无指望，为此我打心底里感谢造物主，我知道自己准会闷得没法说——可我若真能进去，账单和教堂钟我是一准要废的；此外若还有什么小玩意儿，你认为废了于事有补，那你就得提醒我这副记性，特兰索姆，还得狠狠地提醒，行不行，我的好朋友？你知道我这记性是什么德行？糟得要命，凭我的灵魂起誓，就是糟得要命！”

“啊，先生，”贴身男仆叹了口气，“您将来必成了不起的演说家，了不得的大演说家。”

===Original===
“I might, my dear fellow, I really might, although I am positive that I shan’t, because, you see, I know that I shall go most damnably to sleep. I shan’t be able to help myself.”

“You must really make an effort, sir, to keep awake, for the sake of your country, you really must, sir, for you will make as great a statesman as you have a soldier. You cannot help it, sir. Talent such as yours, genius such as yours, is like murder, sir — it will out.”

“No, I am a lazy good-for-nought, upon my soul I am, and a statesman I shall never become, for even if I do get pushed into a seat, what shall I lay on my sleeping in it all the time? A pack o’ dogs, sixteen fighting cocks, and a blasted nag? Will you take me?”

“Against what, sir?”

“Against nothing, you damned, disrespectful dog! Upon my honour, against nothing but my sleeping. What are you flashing that deuced silver tray about for? It catches the light in a most exasperating manner and causes the most acute suffering to my wretched eyesight. Have you no feeling at all, my good Transome, or have you lost it as well as your respect? Have you never suffered the spasms of the damned? I declare that my poor wretched head is executing positive manoeuvres this morning. Musket drill and cavalry charges are going on inside it the whole time. Oh, dear, oh, dear! How I wish you would open that note, instead of flourishing it about again. You surely don’t expect me to open it, do you?”

===Chinese===
“兴许会的，我的好朋友，真兴许会；不过我拿得准自己成不了，因为，你瞧，我知道自己准会睡得一塌糊涂。我实在管不住自己这双眼。”

“您可真得费把子力气撑住别睡，先生，为您的国家着想，您真得这样，先生；您做起政治家来，准会跟您做军人一样伟大。这是挡也挡不住的，先生。您这等才干，您这等天才，就跟人命案子一样，先生——终究是包不住的。”

“不成不成，我是个懒骨头、没出息的东西，凭我的灵魂起誓，就是这么个人，政治家我是永远做不成的。就算人家硬把我塞进一个议席，我拿什么打赌，赌我不会自始至终都在里头睡觉？一帮猎狗、十六只斗鸡，外加一匹该死的驽马？你接不接我这注？”

“跟什么赌，先生？”

“跟什么都不赌，你这该死的、没有规矩的狗东西！凭我的荣誉起誓，什么赌注也不押，就赌我睡觉这一桩。你把那托盘晃来晃去做什么？那鬼银子反起光来，最惹人气恼，叫我这双倒霉眼睛难受得钻心。你难道一点体贴心肠都没有吗，我的好特兰索姆？还是说，你的体贴跟你的规矩一并丢了？你可曾尝过地狱囚徒那种抽搐的滋味？我敢说，今天上午我这颗可怜的倒霉脑袋，正在操演着规规矩矩的队列呢。燧发枪操练、骑兵冲锋，一刻不停地在里头进行。哦，天哪，天哪！你倒是把那张字条拆了呀，别又举着它晃来晃去。你总不至于指望我亲自动手拆吧，啊？”

===Original===
Accordingly the valet opened the letter and announced to his master that it was a lady’s handwriting.

“Then you had better give it to me,” drawled the captain with a resigned air, “for if you pry into the contents of the poor thing’s soul, it will be all over the town in an hour or so, and another woman’s reputation will have disappeared. Why, Lord love us,” he added as he glanced at the note in question, “if it isn’t from that she-dragon herself, that most terrible and alarming Missus What’shername, Missus — Missus — oh, what the devil is her name, eh?”

The valet suggested humbly that the lady in question would most probably have signed her name at the end of the letter.

“Oh, yes, of course, what a downright sane fellow you are, to be sure. Now with all my brain power I should never have thought of that. Perfectly ridiculous of me, I know, but I really shouldn’t have, you know. Ah! I remember who the woman is now, without looking. She’s the wife of that perfectly idiotic lawyer fellow who always fastens up his fat stomach in a white waistcoat a cut or two too small, but I’m blamed if I can remember even his name, so you see we are not much nearer to it, are we now?”

===Chinese===
贴身男仆于是把信拆开，禀报主人说，这是一位女士的手笔。

“那你还是把它给我吧，”上尉拖着长腔，一副听天由命的神气说道，“因为你要是窥探了那可怜人家的心底事，不出个把钟头就得传遍全城，又一位太太小姐的名声就要完蛋。哎呀，老天爷，”他瞥了一眼手边那张字条，又道，“这不是那条母龙亲自写来的吗？就是那位顶顶可怕、叫人心惊肉跳的什么什么太太——太太——哎呀，她到底叫什么名字来着，嗯？”

贴身男仆恭恭敬敬地提了一句：那位太太多半在信的末尾签了名。

“哦，对，自然，你可真是个头脑顶清醒的家伙，一点不假。我这满脑子的智慧，就愣是想不到这一层。我真是可笑透了，我知道，可我实在就是想不到，你知道。啊！不用看，我也想起这女人是谁来了。她就是那个糊涂透顶的律师的太太——那家伙老把他那肥肚皮勒在一件小了两号的白色马甲里。可要命的是，我连他的名字也想不起来，所以你瞧，咱们还是没弄明白，是不是？”

===Original===
Again the valet repeated the brilliant suggestion of looking to the end of the letter, and the master, having graciously accepted his suggestion, announced to the valet that the mystery was solved at last and that the name was nothing more nor less than Whyllie.

“And I wonder what the devil she can want with me, Transome?”

The valet again made a brilliant suggestion that if he would take the pains to read the letter he would in all likelihood discover. So with a very bored air the perfumed soldier read the note right through, and threw it down upon the dressing-table with a great smile of self-complacency.

“She desires me to wait upon her this afternoon, my good fellow. She wishes positively to let bygones be bygones, and desires that I will bury all past differences by partaking of an hour’s hospitality from their house. She also states that she has a wealthy niece but just returned from India, and she desires that this same niece may have the privilege of meeting the cream of the Rye bachelors. My dear fellow, what a truly terrible age we do live in! I have never heard of such daring and unblushing matchmaking. Well, I suppose it is a thing that we must expect in a Godforsaken little hole of a place like this, where the available bachelors are few indeed and possess not the smallest knowledge of how to decently deport themselves, much less their clothes.”

===Chinese===
贴身男仆又把那条妙计重说了一遍——看看信末就是。主人宽宏大量地采纳了，随即向男仆宣布，谜底总算揭开了，那名字不是别的，正是怀利。

“可她找我，到底有什么见鬼的事呢，特兰索姆？”

男仆又献上一条妙策：只消他肯费神把信读上一遍，多半便见分晓。于是这位香喷喷的军人摆出一副百无聊赖的神气，把字条从头到尾读了一遍，随即将它往梳妆台上一撂，满脸都是自鸣得意的笑。

“她请我今天下午去拜望她，我的好朋友。她一心巴望旧事一笔勾销，还指望我到府上叨扰一个钟头，吃她一顿，好把从前的种种龃龉一并埋葬。她还说她有个外甥女，家财豪富，才从印度回来；她盼着让这位外甥女有幸会一会拉伊（Rye）单身汉里的头挑人物。我的好朋友，咱们活的这是个什么年头！这样大胆又不知害臊的保媒拉纤，我从来没听说过。唔，我想，在这种老天爷都不照看的小窟窿地方，这也是意料中事：能挑的单身汉实在少得可怜，又不懂得怎样把举止弄得体面些，更不必说衣裳了。”

===Original===
“Besides, sir,” the valet ventured to remark, “the red cloth of the military has a great attraction for matchmakers. It is always so very respectable, and it carries a most remarkable tone with it, to be sure, sir.”

“Well, I think I will go, at all events,” went on the insufferable, “and throw my eye over the niece, though I really cannot expect much in the beauty line, for she will probably be forty if she’s a day, judging by the ancient aunt. However, it will not be such bad sport leading her on a bit. Have you ever practised the amusing art of exciting elderly spinsters? If not, do, my dear fellow, for it has its humour, and, really now, humour is about all that is left to us nowadays, isn’t it? Hurry up, my good fellow! No, you dolt, I am not on duty. What do I want my sword for? Swords get most damnably between your legs at the wrong moment. They really are positively useless lumber. I cannot think why they are not abolished. Damned clanky things, always in the wrong place, and tripping one up when least on one’s guard. I’ll take my cane. No, no, you positive Judas, the one with the scarlet tassle of course. And my perfume box — no, no, that’s a snuffbox. I hate snuff. You know that I always endeavour to leave it behind whenever possible, for it has a most damnable habit of getting up my nose and bringing on the most acute attacks of sneezing. Now my hat and — no, perhaps not the cloak. A cloak, my good fellow, has a most annoying habit of hiding the curve of the waist. And I really do think that even my most bitter detractors must own that my waist curve is entirely and absolutely right. Now how are we, eh? Has the most criticising valet in the world got anything to remedy, anything to suggest? I think we can do little else with the cravat?”

“It would be passed by Mister Brummel himself.”

===Chinese===
“况且，先生，”男仆斗胆插了一句，“军人的红呢子衣裳，对媒人们向来有极大的魔力。它瞧着就那么体面，还自带一股顶了不起的气派，这可是真的，先生。”

“好吧，反正我看我还是去，”那位受不了的人物接着说，“去瞧一瞧那位外甥女。虽说论姿色我实在不敢指望什么——瞧那位老姑母的成色，外甥女少说也有四十了。不过，逗她一逗，倒也不失为一桩消遣。逗弄老处女的那门有趣的艺术，你实践过没有？没有的话，不妨练练，我的好朋友，这里头自有它的滑稽；说实话，如今留给咱们的，差不多也就剩下滑稽了，是不是？赶快，我的好朋友！不，你这蠢货，我今天不当值。我要剑干什么？剑这东西，偏偏在最不凑巧的时刻可恶地绊人的腿。它们分明是一堆毫无用处的累赘，我不明白怎么还不把它们废掉。叮叮当当的鬼东西，永远搁不对地方，专趁人最不留神的时候把人绊个跟头。我带手杖。不对，不对，你这十足的犹大，当然是带那根绯红穗子的。还有我的香盒——不对，不对，那是鼻烟盒。我讨厌鼻烟。你知道我只要能不带上它就尽量不带，它顶讨嫌的毛病，就是往人鼻子里钻，惹得人喷嚏打得死去活来。好了，我的帽子，还有——不，斗篷就免了吧。斗篷这东西，我的好朋友，顶招人烦的毛病，就是遮住腰身的曲线。而我真的认为，哪怕是最恨我的人，也不得不承认，我这腰身的曲线完完全全、彻头彻尾地标准。好，现在怎么样，嗯？世界上顶爱挑剔的贴身男仆，可还有什么要补救的、要建议的？我看这领巾也就只能收拾到这个地步了吧？”

“就是布鲁梅尔先生（Brummel）本人过目，也会让它通过的。”

===Original===
“Then we are ready, are we? Au revoir, therefore, my estimable friend! Keep your spirits up, and don’t forge my name to a check in my absence!” With which piece of jocular raillery Captain Tuffton, the military lady-killer, swaggered out of the room, swinging the red-tasselled cane, and humming in well-modulated tenor a Spanish love song in very bad Spanish; but that didn’t matter, as nobody was any the wiser, and literally tripping into Watchbell Street, he approached the little white front door behind which were waiting three good people, preparing a most superb ambuscade for the insufferable captain to walk into, an ambuscade that was going to very effectually put an end to the military swagger of this scent-breathing officer. He rang the bell languidly, little thinking it a tocsin of battle and of sudden death.

===Chinese===
“那么，咱们齐备了，是不是？那就再会吧（Au revoir），我这位可敬的朋友！精神头儿提起来，别趁我不在，冒签我的名字去开支票！”说了这番打趣的俏皮话，塔夫顿上尉——那位军人猎艳客——便大摇大摆地走出门去，甩着那根红穗手杖，用圆润调匀的男高音哼着一支西班牙情歌，西班牙话却说得蹩脚透顶；不过这不要紧，反正没人听得出来。他迈着轻飘飘的步子，一路拐进了观钟街（Watchbell Street），朝那扇小小的白漆前门走去。门后正候着三位好心人，给这位受不了的上尉布下了一座顶顶壮观的埋伏，专等他自己一头走进去；这座埋伏，就要十分干净利落地结果了这位满身香气的军官的军人威风。他懒洋洋地拉响了门铃，全然没有想到，这一声响，原是催战的警号、暴死的凶铃。

## XXXV. Scylla or Charybdis / 三十五　斯库拉还是卡律布狄斯

===Original===
Captain Tuffton could certainly not complain of his reception, for the lawyer was positively nervous in his endeavours to please, while Mrs. Whyllie, in her anxiety to let bygones be bygones, positively basked in the sunshine of his glory, and as to Imogene—well, she at least had the speedy satisfaction of knowing that her appearance had caused havoc in the heart of the lady-killer.

“And so you are back from India?” he said to the beautiful niece.

“So it appears, sir,” answered Imogene, with a roguish smile.

“Ah, yes. Of course it is only too obvious,” answered the military one, “for here you are, aren’t you now? It’s a beastly place out there, I suppose, now isn’t it? I never could abide elephants or snakes!”

“La, sir, then you must not venture there, for they abound most vastly,” answered Imogene. Mrs. Whyllie by this time was tittering behind her fan, and old Whyllie looked greatly troubled at the whole proceedings.

===Chinese===
塔夫顿上尉（Captain Tuffton）实在挑不出人家待客的礼数有什么错处：那位律师一个劲儿地巴结讨好，紧张得实在可以；老怀利太太（Mrs. Whyllie）一心要把旧账一笔勾销，更是沐浴在这位上尉荣耀的光辉里；至于伊莫金（Imogene）嘛——嘿，她至少很快就称心如意地得知，自己这一露面，已经把这位猎艳老手的心搅得七颠八倒。

“这么说，您是从印度回来了？”他对那位标致的侄女说。

“看样子是回来了，先生。”伊莫金答道，唇边挂着一丝狡黠的笑。

“啊，可不是。这自然再明显不过，”这位行伍出身的人物答道，“您这不是人就在这儿嘛，对不对？那地方糟透了，我猜，是不是？我一辈子就见不得大象和蛇！”

“哎呀，先生，那您可千万别去，那儿这两样东西多得了不得。”伊莫金答道。这工夫，老怀利太太已经在扇子后头吃吃笑个不停，老怀利瞧着这一整套把戏，愁得不行。

===Original===
“A devilish climate, too, for the complexion, isn’t it?” stroking his smooth, weak chin.

“La, sir, indeed if you say that, I must take it as a poor compliment to myself.”

“Do not mistake me, I beg,” urged the officer, “for in your case the Indian sun has been most gentle. He has kissed you with a light hand—er—a light mouth, indeed. Lucky sun, lucky sun!”

“You are being vastly gentle with my complexion, sir, but I perceive you to be a most accomplished courtier and a turner of beautiful compliments.”

“Madam, I speak from my heart, I assure you.”

“Whoever heard of Captain Tuffton possessing one?” tittered Mrs. Whyllie.

===Chinese===
“那地方的气候对皮肤也顶伤的，是不是？”他说着，摩挲着自己那光滑疲软的下巴。

“哎呀，先生，您既然这样说，那我只好把它当作对我的一句不高明的恭维话啦。”

“求您千万别误会，”军官急急地说道，“就您而言，印度的太阳一直是顶温柔的。它亲您的时候下手极轻——呃——该说是嘴唇极轻。走运的太阳，走运的太阳！”

“先生，您待我这副肤色未免太手下留情了；不过我看得出，您是位顶老练的朝臣，编起漂亮恭维话来是位圣手。”

“夫人，我句句发自肺腑，我向您担保。”

“塔夫顿上尉有肺腑？谁听说过！”老怀利太太吃吃笑道。

===Original===
“You wrong me, Madam, I assure you,” declared the glorious one with conviction. “My poor heart is too large for my scarlet tunic, I assure you. It was an empty shell this morning, I confess, but the beauty of your accomplished niece, which it has been drinking in with rapture, has filled that poor receptacle and made it swell and stretch with the very throes of deep emotion.”

“La, sir, how prettily you turn the English tongue! How the Indians would adore you, sir!”

“Pooh-pooh, indeed,” said Mrs. Whyllie with a great show of decorum, “you must not take for gospel what the captain says. He is a very prince of dandies; indeed, he is second only to the Regent and Mister Brummel in all manners of deportment. I never trust dandies myself entirely.”

“Oh, Madam, pray, pray, make me the exception.”

“No, Captain, for you are not only a dandy, but a soldier, and soldiers are another class I distrust.”

===Chinese===
“您错怪我了，夫人，我向您担保，”这位神气活现的人物一本正经地声明，“我这颗可怜的心太大，我那身猩红短上衣都盛它不下。不瞒您说，今儿早上它还是个空壳子；可令侄女那份才貌双全的美，叫它如痴如醉地饮了个饱，把这可怜的容器灌得满满的，胀得它、撑得它，随着深深的情潮阵阵悸动。”

“哎呀，先生，您的英语说得多么漂亮！印度人见了您，先生，准得把您捧上天！”

“哼，得了吧，”老怀利太太拿出一副顶讲礼数的架势说，“上尉的话，您可别当成真经。他是花花公子里的亲王；说真的，论种种做派仪表，除了摄政王和布鲁梅尔（Brummel）先生，就数他了。我自己是从来不怎么信得过花花公子的。”

“哦，夫人，求您了，求您了，就让我做那个例外吧。”

“不成，上尉，您不但是个花花公子，还是个军人；军人是另一类我信不过的人。”

===Original===
“Ah, Madam,” lisped the officer, “you are cruelty itself.”

“I cannot help it, my dear sir. Soldiers are not to be trusted, and well you know it. They walk about with gay apparel, appearing the most gentle of creatures, but we know how dangerous they are, aye, dangerous both morally and physically, with their minds full of most terrible conquests planned against poor women, and their pockets stuffed to the bursting point with explosives and weapons.”

“La, Madam, you are mistaken, upon my soul. Take my case now as an example: I came here, I confess it, with thoughts of conquest in my mind, but I am conquered, I am vanquished, I am beaten most damnably myself. The eyes of your niece have sown my very foundations with salt.”

“Indeed, sir, that’s bitter!” exclaimed Imogene, blushing.

“And as to the belief that soldiers—officers, that is—are loaded with explosives and weapons, why, pish! Madam, it is a fallacy, I assure you. We leave explosives to the sergeants and our weapons to our orderlies. It is not only most damnably dangerous to carry firearms on our person, but it is most damnably damaging to the set of one’s clothes. Indeed, I declare that the cream of the army would retire if carrying weapons was insisted upon.”

===Chinese===
“唉，夫人，”军官咬着舌儿说，“您可真是残酷的化身。”

“我没法子呀，亲爱的先生。军人信不得，这层道理您心里最清楚。他们穿着花哨衣裳四处游逛，瞧着像天底下顶温良的生物，可我们知道他们有多危险，是啊，无论品行还是身子都危险：满脑门子净是对付可怜女人的可怕征服勾当，两只口袋塞满了炸药武器，都快要撑破啦。”

“哎呀，夫人，您可冤枉人了，我拿灵魂起誓。就拿我自己的事打个比方吧：我承认，我来这儿的时候，脑子里是打着征服的算盘的；可如今我被征服了，我被击溃了，我被杀了个一败涂地，败得要多惨有多惨。令侄女的那双眼睛，已经把盐撒在了我心田的地基上。”

“哎呀，先生，这话可真够苦的！”伊莫金红着脸嚷道。

“至于说军人——我是说军官啦——个个浑身绑满炸药武器，呸！夫人，那是无稽之谈，我向您担保。爆炸物留给中士们去操心，武器交给勤务兵去背。把火器带在身上，不但是要命地危险，更要命地毁衣裳的板型。真的，我敢说，要是硬逼着军人随身佩带武器，军中的精华立马就得告老还乡。”

===Original===
“And you mean to say, sir, that you, a captain, walk abroad in your uniform unarmed?”

“And with the place infested with French spies?” added Imogene, shuddering.

“Why, yes, Madam, I assure you it is so. When I walk abroad I rely entirely for my personal safety upon my tasselled cane, and I venture to suggest that I could put up a very pretty fight with it.”

“But it would not be of much service against pistols, would it, Captain?” asked Mrs. Whyllie.

“Perhaps not, Madam, but who would want to put a pistol to my head?”

===Chinese===
“您的意思是说，先生，您一位上尉，穿着军服出门，身上竟不佩一件武器？”

“何况这地方还满地是法国奸细？”伊莫金补了一句，打了个寒噤。

“哎，正是如此，夫人，我向您担保，千真万确。我出门在外，人身安全全托付给我那根带穗子的手杖；我斗胆放一句大话，凭它，我准能打出一场顶漂亮的架来。”

“可碰上手枪，它就顶不了大用啦，是不是，上尉？”老怀利太太问。

“兴许是吧，夫人。可有谁乐意拿手枪指着我脑袋呢？”

===Original===
“You must have many enemies surely, Captain,” suggested the old lady, “for are you not in command of the press gang?”

“Yes, and a poor job it is for an army officer,” said the soldier. “I take no interest in the sea at all, and the authorities are endeavouring to transfer me to the marine service.”

“The press gang does most cruel work, too, I hear,” went on the old lady.

“Well, you see, that really cannot be helped, Madam. War with France is a certain thing, and if our navy is not able to smash Napoleon on the sea—well, we shall not be able to sing ‘Rule Britannia’ any more, now shall we? And if young men won’t join the navy—well, we have to make ’em, you see, and that’s what the press gang’s for, don’t you know? If you cannot get a thing done for love, you know, you must get it done by force. Do you follow me?”

“Perfectly, my dear Captain,” said Mrs. Whyllie. “That little maxim of yours is most admirable, I declare, and we shall put it to most instant practice.” Thereupon the old lady got up from her chair and pointed a pistol at the captain’s head. “And it’s most fortunate, I vow, that your tasselled cane is reposing safely in the hall.”

===Chinese===
“上尉，您的仇家一定少不了，”老太太点拨道，“强征队（press gang）不就归您统带吗？”

“是啊，对一名陆军军官来说，这差事实在寒碜，”军人说。“海上的事我半点兴味也没有，上头还在千方百计要把我调进海军陆战队去呢。”

“听说强征队干的营生也顶残忍的，”老太太接着说。

“唉，您瞧，这实在是没有法子的事，夫人。同法国开战是板上钉钉的了，我们的海军若不能在海上把拿破仑砸个稀烂——那我们就再也唱不成《统治吧，不列颠尼亚》（Rule Britannia）了，是不是？年轻人要是不肯投海军——那我们就只好逼他们投，您明白，强征队就是干这个使的，您说是不是这个理儿？凡事好说好商量办不成，您知道，就只能来硬的把它办成。您听明白了吗？”

“明白得透透的了，亲爱的上尉，”老怀利太太说。“您这条小箴言实在高明，我敢说，我们这就把它付诸实践。”说着，老太太从椅子上站起身，一支手枪直指上尉的脑袋。“而且我发誓，顶万幸的是，您那根带穗子的手杖，正安安稳稳躺在门厅里歇着呢。”

===Original===
“What does this mean, Madam?” spluttered the captain. “Are you joking?”

“My dear niece,” said the old lady, “this admirable captain really asks us if we are joking.” The captain turned his terrified eyes to Imogene only to discover that she also held a pistol at his head.

“What is the cause of this terrible behaviour?” he stammered.

“You are going to pay your debts, my dear Captain,” said the old lady. “To pay your debts in full. You have owed me apologies for a long time which you have taken no pains to tender to me. You made me a laughingstock in public—well, I am now going to return the compliment, and heaven shield you from the scorn of your brother officers, the anger of your superiors, and the scathing and greedy wits of the neighbourhood. I say, heaven shield, for I shan’t. Antony, my dear, get the paper out of the drawer in the desk there.”

===Chinese===
“这算什么意思，夫人？”上尉结结巴巴地问。“您在开玩笑吧？”

“我亲爱的侄女，”老太太说，“这位可敬的上尉居然当真问我们，是不是在开玩笑。”上尉把吓坏了的眼珠转向伊莫金，却发现她也举着一支手枪，正对着他的脑袋。

“你们这样可怕，究竟为了什么缘故？”他口吃着问。

“您该还债了，亲爱的上尉，”老太太说。“连本带利地还。您欠我的道歉欠了这么久，却从没费心送到我面前来。您当众叫我成了笑柄——好，如今我也要礼尚往来，回敬您一份；愿上天保佑您躲得过同僚军官的讥嘲、上峰的震怒，还有四邻八舍那些又刻薄又贪婪的口舌。我说的是愿上天保佑，因为我本人是不打算手下留情的。安东尼，亲爱的，把写字台抽屉里那份文书取出来。”

===Original===
Old Mr. Whyllie moved behind the captain and went to the desk. The captain moved toward Mrs. Whyllie.

“Stay where you are!” she ordered. “If you move again I shall fire.”

“A likely tale!” he spluttered. “You wouldn’t dare!”

“I can easily contradict you on that score,” quickly remarked the old lady, and she pulled the trigger. The captain fell back upon the sofa, his pale face blackened with powder, his eyes blinded with smoke, and a sharp, pricking sensation in his left shoulder.

“My God!” he cried. “You’ve hit me.”

“And shall do so again if you give me any more trouble,” said the old lady, “and,” she added, “next time I may aim to kill,” and she took up another pistol from the mantelpiece. “You see, sir, we were quite prepared for you.”

===Chinese===
老怀利先生绕到上尉身后，走向写字台。上尉却朝老怀利太太那边挪动。

“站在原地不许动！”她喝道。“再挪一步我就开枪。”

“说得好听！”他结结巴巴地嚷。“您不敢！”

“这一层，我轻而易举就能证明您错了，”老太太立刻说道，随即扣动了扳机。上尉仰面跌在沙发上，苍白的脸叫火药熏得漆黑，眼睛被硝烟迷得睁不开，左肩上一阵针刺般的锐痛。

“我的天哪！”他大叫。“您打着我了。”

“您再敢给我添乱，我还照样打，”老太太说，“而且，”她添了一句，“下回我可就要往要害上瞄了。”说着，她又从壁炉台上抄起一支手枪。“您瞧，先生，我们等着您，可是准备得十足周到。”

===Original===
Then the lawyer set a table before him with pen and ink and requested him to sign a certain paper that he had already drawn up. This paper was addressed to the petty officer in charge of the press gang, and commanded that the young man of the name of Denis Cobtree should be driven immediately in a hired coach to the house of Antony Whyllie, attorney-at-law, Watchbell Street, who would give them further commands. To this paper Captain Tuffton signed his name. Indeed, he could do nothing else; and a servant was sent off to the castle to deliver it.

In half an hour or so the noise of a coach was heard rattling over the cobblestones, and Antony Whyllie left the room to see if Denis was safe. In the meantime the captain had signed another paper declaring Denis free to return over the Sussex border into Kent, and this paper having been shown to the petty officer and a guinea piece having been put into his dirty hand by the lawyer himself, the seadog saluted respectfully and swung off down Watchbell Street whistling a tune. The lawyer explained the situation hurriedly to Denis and then went in to take Imogene’s place as guard over the wretched soldier. But the captain was suffering acute spasms in his left shoulder, and this being his first experience of bullet wounds, he was nearly unconscious at the horror of it. So Mrs. Whyllie was able for a moment to lower the pistol in order to kiss Imogene, and having recommended her to Denis’s care, bade them urge the coach quickly out of Rye and into Kent.

===Chinese===
随后，律师在他面前摆上一张桌子，备好笔墨，请他在一份早已拟好的文书上签字。这文书是写给统带强征队的那名海军军士（petty officer）的，命令他立即雇一辆马车，把那个名叫丹尼斯·科布特（Denis Cobtree）的年轻人火速送到观钟街（Watchbell Street）执业律师安东尼·怀利（Antony Whyllie）的宅邸，怀利自会再对他们下达进一步的指令。塔夫顿上尉便在这份文书上签了名。说实在的，他也再没有第二条路可走；于是打发一名仆人送文书到城堡去了。

约莫半个钟头后，听得一阵马车轱辘声辘辘碾过鹅卵石路面，安东尼·怀利离开屋子，去看丹尼斯是否平安。这工夫，上尉已在另一份文书上签了字，声明丹尼斯可以自由越过萨塞克斯边界回肯特去；那名海军军士把文书验看过了，律师又亲手把一枚几尼金币塞进他那只脏手，这老海狗便恭恭敬敬行了个礼，大摇大摆下了观钟街，一路吹着口哨。律师匆匆把眼下的局面向丹尼斯交代了一遍，随后进去顶替伊莫金，看守那可怜的军人。可是上尉的左肩一阵阵痉挛剧痛，他又是头一回领教枪伤的滋味，吓得几乎不省人事。于是老怀利太太得空把枪放低片刻，好亲一亲伊莫金，把她托付给丹尼斯照料，又叮嘱他们催车快行，赶出拉伊（Rye），直奔肯特。

===Original===
“Shall I change my clothes first or send them back to you?” asked Imogene.

“Neither, my love,” answered the old lady, again levelling the pistol at Captain Tuffton’s head; “for when we have packed this ridiculous soldier back to his place in an hour or so, I am going to see to it that Mr. Whyllie draws up all legal forms for adopting you as our daughter—that is, providing of course you raise no objection—but I shall do myself the honour of calling upon Sir Antony Cobtree himself within the week,” saying which she dismissed the young people to the coach, and when the driver had received a handsome fee from the lawyer and been promised a further one if he made good pace for Dymchurch, he touched up the horses, and with great rattling clattered the cumbersome coach through the great gate of Rye and so out on the smooth high road, where the long whip cracked and the wheels began to spin. But for a whole hour the wretched captain stayed a prisoner in the white house until he beseeched the old lady to let him go home and have the surgeon dress his wound. So at last she consented, and another coach having been hired, he was lifted into it and in a few moments reached his rooms, where the most criticising valet in the world pulled from his shoulder a steel pin. With the exception of this deep pin prick, there was no mark of a wound, as indeed why should there have been? for Mrs. Whyllie had fired only a blank charge, and the old lawyer, according to careful instructions, had got behind the captain and dug in the pin at the crucial moment.

And while the valet administered brandy as a restorative, a boy and a girl sat hand in hand in a great old coach which swayed and jolted as they dashed along the Romney Road toward Dymchurch. Useless, indeed, to follow that coach from Rye, for the necks of the four horses were stretched in tensioned gallop, the harness pulling near to breaking-point, the wheels tearing round the axles, and the busy driver’s long whip cracking like pistol shots above the pounding thunder of the swift-flying hoofs.

===Chinese===
“我是先换下这身衣裳呢，还是回头给您送回来？”伊莫金问。

“都不必，亲爱的，”老太太答道，又把手枪端平，对准了塔夫顿上尉的脑袋；“等个把钟头把这位可笑的军人打发回他该待的地方去，我就要看着怀利先生拟好全部法律文书，收你做我们的义女——这自然以你不反对为前提——不过一礼拜之内，我要有幸亲自登门拜会安东尼·科布特爵士（Sir Antony Cobtree）本人。”话音一落，她便打发两个年轻人动身上马车；车夫从律师手里领了一笔丰厚的赏钱，又被许下诺言：若肯为去迪姆彻奇（Dymchurch）的一路赶出好脚程，还有一份；他便扬鞭催马，赶着那辆笨重的马车辘辘隆隆地穿过拉伊的大城门，驶上平坦的大道，长鞭噼啪炸响，车轮开始飞转。而那可怜的上尉，足足一个钟头都关在那座白房子里做囚徒，直到苦苦哀求老太太放他回家，好让外科大夫替他裹伤，她这才总算点头应允。于是另雇了一辆马车，他被人搀了上去，不几分钟便到了自己的寓所；世上顶挑剔的贴身男仆从他肩头拔出了一根钢针。除了这一针扎出的深刺痕，身上再没有半点伤痕——说实在的，又哪能有什么伤痕呢？老怀利太太装的不过是空包弹，而老律师按着细致周到的嘱咐，早已绕到上尉背后，在节骨眼上把针扎了进去。

贴身男仆忙着灌白兰地给他定神的时候，一个少年和一个姑娘正手拉着手，坐在一辆高大的旧马车里，沿着罗姆尼大路（Romney Road）朝迪姆彻奇一路飞驰，车身摇摇晃晃、颠簸不平。说实在的，想从拉伊追上那辆马车，是白费劲：四匹马伸长了脖子，绷足了劲儿飞奔，挽具拽得几乎要断，车轮绕着车轴呼呼地转，忙活的车夫那条长鞭，在疾驰马蹄的隆隆轰响之上，炸响得恰似一声声手枪。

## XXXVI. Holding the Pulpit / 三十六　把住讲坛

===Original===
Never was there such a great congregation as upon that night in the old dim church. The news that Doctor Syn was to leave immediately after the service brought everybody to bid him farewell, and Mipps had great difficulty in packing them all into the old pews. In fact, full half an hour before the vestry prayer the pews were all choked, and latecomers began to perch themselves upon the high oak backs. Benches were even arranged across the aisles, and boys climbed up on to the window ledges; in fact, every available place in the church capable or not capable of supporting a human being was utilized. Jerry Jerk perched himself without ceremony upon the font cover, much to the indignation of the sexton, who in his capacity of verger tried to signal him off. But Jerk, knowing well that Mipps could not get at him over the benches that crowded the aisles, remained where he was. Right under the pulpit, immediately opposite to the squire’s pew, sat Captain Collyer, and two pews behind that some half-dozen sailors fumbled with hymnbooks under the large eyes of the bo’sun. Once Captain Collyer turned round to see if his men were there, and Jerk noticed the corner of a blue paper bulging from his pocket. Doctor Syn conducted the service from the top box of the three-decker pulpit, with Mipps below him carefully following the printing on the great Prayer Book with a dirty thumb running backward and forward. Now Doctor Syn, although appearing to the congregation to be wrapped up heart and soul in the farewell service, had found occasion to notice two things: the blue paper in the captain’s pocket and the swinging lanterns of men outside the church. He alone could see them, for from the great height of the three-decker he had a good view through the window, and the flashes from the lanterns had revealed one important thing: the red coats of soldiers. The church was surrounded with soldiers, every door was barred and every window watched; and upon the face of Captain Collyer appeared a look of triumph. But none of these things hindered the service, which continued with great spirit. The sea salts in the choir bellowed the hymns louder than usual, although there was no schoolmaster to start them off on the fiddle. The hymn before the sermon was just finishing. Doctor Syn closed the great Bible upon the red cushion and placed it upon the shelf below. The “Amen” was reached and the congregation clattered back into their seats. Then the vicar leaned over the pulpit side and addressed his flock for the last time:

===Chinese===
